# Effects

One section per effect, below the index. Each section is what the effect's own README used to say (what it does, inputs, settings,
passes, edge cases, the Vulkan path, what correct and wrong look like). **Read the effect's section before changing the effect and update it
in the same change.** An effect's other files sit in its folder `src/effects/<Name>/`: its C++, `defaults.toml` (its settings defaults:
`tools/defaults_tool.py merge` builds the one defaults file from these, `check` verifies) and `SHADERS.txt` (its shader files, which stay in
`src/hlsl/NewVegas`, the game's layout). The frame order is in `src/core/EffectChain.cpp`.

## Index

The table is generated by `tools/effects_index.py` (re-run it after adding, removing or renaming an effect folder or section; `check`
verifies it). An effect without a "yes" in the Doc column has no section yet.

## AmbientLight

Source of this section: `src/effects/AmbientLight/AmbientLight.cpp` / `.h`, `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/AmbientLight.fx.hlsl` (D3D9) and the Vulkan passes listed in the folder's `SHADERS.txt` (`Compute/AmbientLight*.frag/.comp`). Written 2026-11-26 from the code (the effect had no section before).

### 1. Purpose

Ambient occlusion and bounce light in one pass at half resolution: GTAO-style occlusion with a horizon-based estimate (creases, the ground under things, inside corners), and the bounce light, which comes from a 3D light grid round the player (the sun's bounce outdoors, `GridBounce`; lamps and glowing surfaces indoors and at night, `GridLamps`), or from the RT GI / ComputeGI when those run. While it renders it replaces AmbientOcclusion and BounceLight.

### 2. When it runs

- Switch: `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Status] Enabled` (default true). Also held on in memory by `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] SunGI` (outdoors) and `RoomGI` (indoors with the RT core running): see 4.
- `ShouldRender`: off under the path tracer's replace mode (`RtPathTrace::Replacing`) and underwater. While only SunGI / RoomGI hold it on (Status key off) AND the RT core runs, it renders only on their side of the door (outdoors for SunGI, indoors for RoomGI), and the other side keeps AmbientOcclusion / BounceLight as the user set them. Without the RT core (native D3D9, stock DXVK, no RT GPU, `NvrDxvkPasses` off, or `RTInteriors` / `RTExteriors` off for that space) it renders on both sides, since the switch shows on (2026-11-26; before, a held-on effect drew nothing indoors without RT while its row read on). With the Status key on it renders on both sides, whatever SunGI / RoomGI say. Wherever it draws, the takeover switches AmbientOcclusion and BounceLight off for that frame.
- Frame order (`src/core/EffectChain.cpp`): step `AmbientLightTakeover` (AmbientOcclusion and BounceLight switched off for the rest of the pre-tonemap chain while it renders), `CompositePlan` (the outdoor sun-shadow apply may be folded into its Apply: `shadowIntoAmbientLight`), then step `AmbientLight`: `RenderPrepare` (Prepare + the normal smoothing), the AO's sky pass at its resolution when Skylighting v2 is on, then `Render`.
- Path: it is a screen pass over the lit frame, so it acts on the deferred and the forward picture alike. With the deferred renderer it can take the G-buffer's albedo for the bounce (`[Main.Main.Quality] DeferredALAlbedo`, `core/FdIndirect.h`).

### 3. Passes

Technique `AmbientLight` (8 passes; Render refuses another count and logs "buffers missing or AmbientLight.fx.hlsl is not the matching version"): Prepare, Estimate, BlurX, BlurY, Accumulate (temporal), StatsSample, StatsReduce (the InteriorBalance / ExteriorBalance statistics), Apply (full resolution). `AmbientLightSky` is the same with Skylighting's factor in Apply; `AmbientLightGrid` builds the light grid (ScreenInject, GridInject, GridPropagate); `AmbientLightSmooth` is the normal smoothing. With the DXVK fork and `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkAmbientLight` the passes run as Vulkan passes (`ExtOn`); the RT estimate (`AmbientLightEstimateRT`) only where `RtAmbient::AOReady()` / `GIReady()` (the RT core is running), otherwise the GTAO estimate.

### 4. Switches, SunGI and RoomGI, and the menu

- `SunGI` (default false): ONE switch for the exterior sun bounce. Outdoors, in memory (the toml is not written): the effect counts as on, ExteriorBalance and AlbedoBuffer on; with the RT core running the RT GI and AmbientReplace (key if above 0, else 0.8); without it (the FALLBACK log line) the light grid is the sun bounce, GridBounce = key if above 0, else 1.0. Indoors it forces no key; without the RT core the held-on effect draws there as GTAO + the grid's lamps (with it, it does not draw there unless Status or RoomGI says so). Details: the AmbientOcclusion section, "Sun-GI phase 4".
- `RoomGI` (default false): the interior counterpart; forces keys only indoors with the RT core running. Without ray tracing it forces no key, but it still holds the effect on, which then draws as GTAO + the grid's lamps. Details: the AmbientOcclusion section, "Room GI".
- How they hold the effect on: `SettingManager::GetMenuShaderEnabled("AmbientLight")` returns true while either key is on, and `AmbientLightEffect::UpdateSettings` sets the runtime `Enabled` back on while either is on (`sunGiForced` / `roomGiForced` = key on and Status key off).
- The menu (2026-11-26, `SettingManager::SetMenuShaderEnabled`): switching Ambient light OFF also writes SunGI = false and RoomGI = false, then Status = false. One place for every route: the effect's row, the effects list, the Indirect lighting page's pickers ("Off", "Standalone GTAO / Legacy AO") and the hiding notes' buttons (all go through `ShaderManager::SwitchShaderStatus`). Switching it ON writes Status = true only. Before this fix the row always read on while SunGI or RoomGI was on, a click wrote Status = false and the effect was put straight back on; Status could then never be written true from the menu, and the effect stayed off indoors (seen 2026-11-24/26 in the owner's logs: `SunGI: ... AmbientLight Status on (it was off)`, no AmbientLight in any interior HEARTBEAT).
- A preset, script or console command writing `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Status] Enabled` (`SettingManager::SetSettingF`) while SunGI or RoomGI is on writes the key only and does not switch the effect, so "SunGI on, switch off" stays the designed outdoors-only state (UpdateSettings keeps it held on); only the menu's click clears SunGI and RoomGI. With both off it switches the effect as before. Before, any change of the key flipped the running effect (1 in the held state turned it off). `AmbientLightEffect::UpdateSettings` drops the runtime flag when SunGI and RoomGI let go with the Status key off, in whatever order a preset (an unordered map) writes the three keys. The test shot list's `effect AmbientLight 0|1` is the menu's path; its undo switches back but does not restore SunGI / RoomGI.

### 5. Live or restart

- Live: the Status switch, SunGI, RoomGI, every strength, reach, grid and balance key, DebugView.
- Restart: `LowScaleFullRes`, `SlimPrep`, and `[Main.Main.Quality] AlbedoBuffer` (the template must write the albedo target from the start; the log says so when SunGI wants it and it is off).

### 6. Log and HEARTBEAT

- `UNOFFICIAL SunGI:` / `UNOFFICIAL RoomGI:` lines on each state change (off / indoors / outdoors / RT / FALLBACK); `UNOFFICIAL AmbientLight: light grid running ...`, `scene light (sky|cell) ...`.
- HEARTBEAT: since 2026-11-26 Render notes `Drawn` when its passes succeed and `Skipped` (with why) on a failure, and the chain step notes off / not loaded / ShouldRender false on a frame it does not run. Before, the line read `AmbientLight 0/0` whether it drew or not.

### 7. What correct looks like

- Dark interior: soft contact darkening in corners, at floor-wall seams and under tables and crates; lamps and fires spread a soft glow onto the walls round them (`GridLamps`); with InteriorBalance the room's overall brightness stays as without the effect.
- Outdoor corner: a darker crease where a wall meets the ground and under ledges; sunlit ground throws faint warm light onto a shaded wall beside it (`GridBounce` > 0, e.g. SunGI's fallback 1.0). `OcclusionStrength` 2.5 (default) shows the darkening clearly; at 1 it is about 40% of that.
- Wrong: no change when switching it (check the HEARTBEAT and the SunGI / RoomGI lines), a light outline round objects (NoEdgeOutline), grain crawling (Temporal), facets on low-poly rocks (NormalSmoothing).

## AmbientOcclusion

*README title: AmbientOcclusion (with Skylighting)*

Source of this spec: the rewritten shader (wave 3 buffers, branch claude/rw3-buffers): src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/AmbientOcclusion.fx.hlsl (D3D9: the legacy path, DedicatedAO, DedicatedAOFast, DedicatedGTAO, DedicatedGTAOFast, SkyVisibilityV2), and src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/AmbientOcclusion.glsl (the shared Vulkan header, now used by the kept RT passes). The C++ (src/effects/AmbientOcclusion.cpp) is unchanged. Written from the previous spec and the C++; the old shader was not read. The Vulkan AO passes are not rewritten yet (section 7).

Skylighting is part of this effect: the sky pass, its constants and its composite live in the same file. The RT ambient occlusion path is a separate branch (section 9).

### 1. Purpose

Ambient occlusion darkens the parts of the picture that a real surface would receive less skylight and bounce light on: inside corners and creases, the underside of ledges, the base of props. It is soft, computed in screen space from the depth and normals, and applied to the frame before the rest of the chain. Bright surfaces are darkened less, and distant pixels fade back to fully lit.

Skylighting (outdoors, off by default) takes away the sky's share of the light where something overhead blocks the sky (under roofs, porches, bridges, at the foot of cliffs). It removes only the sky's share, so sunlit surfaces keep their sun.

### 2. When it runs

- Enabled by [Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.Status] Enabled, and the location's section (Exteriors or Interiors) Enabled. ShouldRender is that test.
- Frame order: depth and normals first; then sky and the other effects; then either AmbientLight (which hosts the sky pass) or this effect (deferred into the fog composite when possible); then AvgLuma and Exposure.
- Skylighting v2 sky pass (SkyVisibilityV2): once per frame before the AO passes, outdoors only. Skylighting v1 has no pass: it multiplies the estimate.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (stage numbers as declared; the C++ binds them)

| Stage | Name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the frame (read by the legacy path only) |
| s1 | TESR_DepthBuffer | CombineDepth output (linear depth, LINEAR, CLAMP) |
| s2 | TESR_SourceBuffer | the scene the combine darkens |
| s3 | TESR_BlueNoiseSampler | 256 x 256 blue noise (POINT, WRAP) |
| s4 | TESR_NormalsBuffer | view-space normals (POINT, CLAMP) |
| s5 | NVR_AOBuffer | the AO ping-pong texture (R = AO, G = depth) (LINEAR, CLAMP) |
| s6 | NVR_OrthoMap | top-down height map (LINEAR, CLAMP). The sky tests use it with "outside the map = open" done in the shader (see section 6) |
| s7 | NVR_SkyVis | Skylighting factor (R) and visibility (B) (POINT, CLAMP) |
| s8 | NVR_SunMask | sun shadow mask, read in R (POINT, CLAMP) |
| s9 | NVR_SkyPrep | AmbientLight's smoothed normal, encoded as the normals are (POINT, CLAMP) |

#### Constants (by name)

- TESR_AmbientOcclusionAOData: x samples (unused: the kernel is five per axis), y StrengthMultiplier, z ClampStrength, w Range.
- TESR_AmbientOcclusionData: x AngleBias, y LumThreshold, z BlurDropThreshold, w BlurRadiusMultiplier.
- TESR_AmbientOcclusionGTAO: x GTAO on, w MaxRadius (pixels).
- TESR_AmbientOcclusionSky (v1): x on, y Strength, z Distance, w slack (unused).
- TESR_AmbientOcclusionSky2 (v2): x on, y Strength, z A (sky light, linear luminance x AmbientScale), w S (sun light).
- TESR_AmbientOcclusionSky3: x DepthNormals, y 1 / OpenThreshold, z ShareApply, w debug view (0 off, 1 visibility, 2 share, 3 factor, 4 1 - covered).
- TESR_AmbientOcclusionSky4: x ray reach (Distance), y SoftWidth, z sun mask bound, w sky factor bound (combine).
- TESR_AmbientOcclusionSky5: x LightCap take limit, y OpenSides, z unused, w no-limit value (1e6 when the limit is off).
- TESR_SmoothedSunDir (xyz), TESR_ShadowFade (x), TESR_ReciprocalResolution (xy), TESR_FogData (x start, y end), TESR_FogColor (rgb), TESR_CameraData (x near, y far).
- TESR_ProjectionTransform, TESR_InvProjectionTransform, TESR_InvViewTransform, TESR_ShadowCameraToLightTransformOrtho (matrices).
- TESR_RenderScale (RenderRect include), NVR_AOLayout (zw = 1 / the AO target's size), NVR_SkySmooth (x = 1 when NVR_SkyPrep holds a smoothed normal).

### 4. Settings

Unchanged (all read by AmbientOcclusion.cpp): the Exteriors and Interiors sections (Enabled, Samples, StrengthMultiplier, ClampStrength, Range, AngleBias, LumThreshold, BlurDropThreshold, BlurRadiusMultiplier); Status Enabled; GTAO (Enabled, MaxRadius); FastPaths (Enabled); Skylighting (Enabled, Version, Strength, Distance, ShareApply, OpenThreshold, DepthNormals, AmbientScale, SoftWidth, DebugView, Reflections, MinReflection, LightCap, OpenSides); AOLowRes; Max Quality; NvrDxvkPasses and NvrDxvkVariants; RT (separate, section 9).

(2026-11-07 perf-history, docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) "perf-history") With `[Main.Main.RayTracing] DenoiseTimeBased` (default on) the RT
estimate's time behaviour is in seconds, as tuned at 60 fps: the RT AO temporal pass keeps 0.9 of last frame at 60 fps and 0.9^(60 dt) at
other frame rates (0.73 at 20 fps); `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] CacheFrames` and `GIMovingHistory` and the RT GI / AO denoisers'
histories (RtDenoise, 16 frames) are frames at 60 fps, kept as the same seconds. At 60 fps nothing changes.

### 5. Passes and outputs

#### Techniques

| Requested when | Technique | Passes |
|---|---|---|
| default | DedicatedAO | Estimate, BlurX, BlurY, Combine |
| FastPaths on, GTAO off | DedicatedAOFast | the same four |
| GTAO on, FastPaths off | DedicatedGTAO | the same four |
| GTAO on, FastPaths on | DedicatedGTAOFast | the same four |
| Skylighting v2 | SkyVisibilityV2 | Sky |
| the legacy path (unnamed technique 0) | (unnamed) | one pass (new: was five) |

#### Dedicated passes

Targets are G16R16F at the AO size (half the frame, a quarter with AOLowRes, full with Max Quality). Ping-pong AO 0 and AO 1.
- Estimate -> AO 0: R = AO, G = linear depth (game units) capped at 8000. Pixels beyond 8000 give R = 1 and G = 8000.
- BlurX: AO 0 -> AO 1. BlurY: AO 1 -> AO 0. Each keeps G (depth).
- Combine -> the frame (or the chain's spare texture): the scene darkened in linear light (section 6).
- Deferred and combine-only modes use the same passes (the C++ picks them).

#### Legacy path (unnamed technique)

One pass from TESR_RenderedBuffer: the random-sample estimate, then the same combine without Skylighting. The old five-pass legacy (SSAO, multiply, two blurs, combine) is not kept.

#### Sky pass (SkyVisibilityV2)

Target: the sky target, the AO size. G16R16F, or A16B16G16R16F when Reflections or LightCap is on.
- R = the sky factor (1 = no change). G = linear depth capped at 8000. B = the sky visibility V (1 open, 0 blocked). A = 1.

### 6. Behaviour details

#### Estimate

- The pixel's view position from the depth; the normal from the normals buffer.
- Random-sample estimate (AngleBias, Range, StrengthMultiplier): five samples per axis from the blue noise. Per sample i, the blue-noise vector r (in -1..1) gives a horizontal direction from its (x, z) components and a vertical direction from its (y, z) components, both turned by the pixel's quarter-turn angle (blue-noise value x pi/2) and flipped to the normal's side. The sample point is origin + direction x scale x Range, scale = lerp(AngleBias, 1, ((i+1)/5)^2). A sample occludes when the stored depth there is nearer than the sample and within Range; its weight falls off linearly for the first two samples (the first quarter) and as the square for the rest. Each axis gives 1 - StrengthMultiplier x (sum / 5); the AO is the product of the two axes.
- GTAO (when on): two slices across the half turn (slice angle from the blue noise), four horizon steps each way out to min(Range in pixels, MaxRadius). Horizons are limited to the normal's hemisphere; the cosine-weighted visibility between them is the AO, with samples past 0.385 of the radius counting less. Result = 1 - StrengthMultiplier x (1 - visibility).
- Skylighting v1 (Sky.x on, outdoors): multiplies the estimate by lerp(1, open / total, Strength), where the rays are one straight up and six on two rings (60 and 30 degrees, turned by the blue noise), each probed at a third and at the full Distance, weighted by the cosine to the normal.
- Fog: AO = max(AO, lerp(AO, luma(fog colour), fog amount)); the fog amount is linear from FogData.x to FogData.y, saturated. Fog only lightens.
- Fade: AO = lerp(AO, 1, saturate((viewZ - 2000) / 6000)). Beyond 8000, AO = 1.

#### Blur (BlurX, BlurY)

- Seven taps per side at the Gaussian weights exp(-k^2 / 18), k = 0..6 (a centre tap and six pairs), along the pass's axis.
- Step in pixels = min(BlurRadiusMultiplier x Range / viewZ, 8). Offsets are in screen texels.
- A tap counts when its depth is within BlurDropThreshold x (1.5 + n_z) of the centre's, n_z being the centre normal's z.
- Beyond 8000 the input is returned unchanged.

#### Combine

- Edge-aware upsample: the 2 x 2 AO grid neighbours, each weighted by its bilinear weight over (1 + |its depth - the full-res depth| / max(BlurDropThreshold, 0.001)); the same weights read the sky factor and visibility.
- AO = lerp(ClampStrength, 1, AO). Brightness: AO = lerp(AO, 1, saturate((Y - LumThreshold) x 3)), Y = BT.709 luminance of the linear scene.
- Skylighting composite (sky factor bound): take = saturate(min(1 - factor, ((1 - visibility) x LightCap-limit + no-limit) / Y)); the linear colour is multiplied by AO and by (1 - take); delinearised. Without the sky factor, take = 0.
- Beyond 8000 the scene is returned unchanged.

#### Skylighting v2: the sky pass

- Beyond 8000: factor 1, visibility 1.
- Normal: the normals buffer; or from the depth (DepthNormals, each axis from the side with the smaller depth step, oriented toward the camera); or the smoothed normal from NVR_SkyPrep (NVR_SkySmooth.x).
- Start point: the view position moved to world space, pushed along its world normal by 24 units.
- Map test (Openness): the point's ortho-map coordinates (the transform's xy in -1..1 mapped to 0..1); outside the map: open. Open where the map's top (R of NVR_OrthoMap) is below the probe's depth in map units; soft over SoftWidth game units times the transform's z scale: saturate((probe - top) / (SoftWidth x zScale) + 0.5).
- Rays: one straight up, weight 0.034 x max(normal.z, 0). Eight azimuths at 22.5 + 45k degrees, each with a 60-degree ray (weight 0.0324 x cosine) and a 30-degree ray (weight 0.0884 x cosine). A ray is open when its probes at 40% and 100% of Distance are both open (the minimum).
- Covered: 1 - openness of a probe 32 units above the point. For a covered point, the 30-degree rays are replaced by a horizontal probe at full reach, by OpenSides x covered.
- Visibility = weighted average of open rays. Edge fade: outside the map's middle 80% (|x| or |y| above 0.8) the visibility fades to 1 over the outer 20%. Then multiplied by 1 / OpenThreshold and saturated.
- Sun share (ShareApply): sunE = S x saturate(normal . sun direction) x sunMask, sunMask = lerp(ShadowFade.x, 1, the sun mask) when bound, else 1; share = A / (A + sunE). Off: share 1.
- Factor = saturate(1 - Strength x (1 - visibility) x share). Debug views replace it: 1 visibility, 2 share, 4 1 - covered.

#### Fixed in this rewrite (from the old spec's open items and the brief)

- Blur divisor, reversed depth (old open question 2): the old spec divided by one minus the post-projection depth, so pixels near the camera got huge offsets. The step is now BlurRadiusMultiplier x Range / viewZ, capped at 8 pixels per step (BlurRadiusMultiplier still scales it linearly). This changes the blur radius on near surfaces; check in the game.
- Divide-by-zero guards: the blur weight sum (1e-6), the upsample depth term (BlurDropThreshold floored at 0.001), the combine weight sum (1e-6), the sky share sum (1e-6), the GTAO and visibility sums, and the view positions (viewZ floored).
- Sky map border: done in the shader (outside the map is open) instead of a separate border sampler, so the stage 6 sampler is plain clamp.
- Openness sign: the old spec's formula, read literally, gives closed for open points. The rewrite uses the meaning the text states (open where the map's top lies below the probe).
- Legacy path: reduced to one pass (old open question on its fallback).

#### Interpretations (not verified in game)

- The ortho map's R value and the transform's z are taken to share units; the z scale is the length of the transform's z column. The old spec did not say.
- The random-sample kernel's exact form (which vector components form each one-dimensional estimate, and the rotation) is a reading of the spec's description.
- The blue-noise sample offsets (37 and 71 texels per sample index) are chosen here.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Not rewritten in this pass. The AmbientOcclusionEstimate, Blur, Combine and Sky frag files and their variant lines in Compute/variants.txt are not present, so the Vulkan AO path is not active and the D3D9 passes run. Compute/variants.txt still lists AmbientOcclusionEstimate g0 and g1, which makes a full compile-compute.sh run fail until those frags exist (it stops at the first missing file).
- AmbientOcclusion.glsl (new): the shared header for all AO passes. Uniform block cb (set 2, binding 0, std140) with the members in the order the C++ fills them (projection, inverse projection, inverse view, ortho transform, aoData, data, gtao, sky, sky2 to sky5, smoothedSunDir, shadowFade, reciprocalResolution, fogData, fogColor, depthConstants, cameraData, aoLayout, skyLayout, rtReproject, rtTemporal). Push block aoPush (24 bytes: axis at 0, gtaoEstimate at 8, linear clamp index at 12, point clamp index at 16, point wrap index at 20). Macros LINEAR_LOD and POINT_LOD, the camera plane macros (nearZ, farZ), AOstrength, startFade and endFade, and the helpers invlerp, SkyFactor and projectedDepthFromLinear. These are the names the kept RT passes use.
- The four kept RT AO passes (AmbientOcclusionEstimateRT, AmbientOcclusionSkyRT, AmbientOcclusionTemporalRT, RtAOInput) compile against this header.
- (2026-11-07 rtgap-tess, docs/next/RT-DETAIL-GAPS.md (not in this repo) section 1a) RtAOTrace.comp's AO and sky rays start the tessellated bumps' Strength
  (faded by view depth as the bumps fade) further off the surface while the bumps are drawn (`DxvkCompute::DisplaceRtSlack`,
  `[Main.Main.RayTracing] TessStartSlack`): a point in a hollow of the depth buffer lay under the flat RT surface (covered sky, rays
  started inside the floor). The 512-byte block is full: the slack rides in viewToWorld's 4th row (_41 Strength, _42 FadeStart, _43
  FadeEnd; the view's directions have w 0, so nothing else reads it). The GI bounce pass (RtGITrace) is unchanged. RT host `tessslack`.
- RT AO / sky / GI (the helper's RtAOTrace.comp, `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT]`), 2026-11-07 decals (docs/next/RT-DETAIL-GAPS.md (not in this repo) 5):
  the core's decals (`[Main.Main.RayTracing] Decals`, custom index NVR_RT_CUSTOM_DECAL) never occlude: the AO and sky rays (Trace) and
  the GI hits' shadow rays (Visible) pass them (ProceedShadow); the GI bounce rays see them. AOAlpha 1 (cut-outs solid, traced with the
  opaque flag, which would make a decal a solid quad) is sent to the shader as 3 while `RtCore::DecalsTraced()`: the cut-outs come as
  candidates and are confirmed whole, decals passed (ProceedSolid; a little dearer than 1 where many cut-outs are hit). RT host
  scene `decals` (AOAlpha 2 and 3 against the CPU, the decal card never covers).

### 8. Look and cost

Correct: soft contact darkening in corners and under ledges; no halo at silhouettes (the upsample and blur are depth-aware); sunlit faces barely touched; distant land untouched beyond 8000 with a soft fade from 2000; under a porch the sky's light goes, the sun on the same surface stays; a lamp-lit wall under a wide cover keeps its lamp light.

Typical wrong: flat open ground greyed (random samples under the surface: GTAO avoids it); haloes at depth edges (the tolerance); speckle (too few samples surviving the blur); lamp-lit walls going black under a canopy (LightCap too low); a visible band where the fade begins.

Cost: estimate at half size (five sample pairs), two blurs of twelve taps, a combine of four taps (plus four sky taps); the sky pass once a frame outdoors (about 40 map reads).

### 9. Open questions

1. RT ambient occlusion: [Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] is not in the D3D9 effect; the RT passes are the kept Vulkan files. Not specified here. Decide whether the new D3D9 file needs its own RT techniques. At RenderScale < 1 (FSR / DLSS LowResEffects) the RT AO, sky and GI traces cover the frame's top-left rect (RtRecord: the trace grid = RenderRect::Width/Height of the half grid; the GI reuse history restarts on a scale or rect <-> native change), the RT estimate runs inside the rect-aware D3D9 passes and the GI bounce reaches the picture through AmbientLight's D3D9 GI path; details and measurements in docs/next/RT-RENDERSCALE.md (not in this repo) (phase 1 AO 2026-11-03, phase 2 GI 2026-11-04). (2026-11-07) RT GI's bounce hits (Compute/Rt/RtAOTrace.comp Shade, also its RtGITrace / RtRadianceCache variants) are lit with the hit's normal bent by its own normal map (ShadingNormal: the core's [Main.Main.RayTracing] RealNormalMaps, default on with RealTextures; the flat triangle normal stays for the shadow rays' lift); RT host scene `normalmaps`.
2. Skylighting v1 and v2: both are kept (the C++ selects by Version). v1 darkens the whole pixel. Whether v1 should stay is open.
3. Vulkan AO passes: not written (section 7). Needed for the Vegas Shaders DXVK path and for the batch compile to succeed again.
4. The ortho map's height units and the transform's z scale (section 6) need checking against ShadowsExteriors' map.
5. Samples is read but unused (the kernel is fixed at five).
6. The sun mask is read in its R channel only.
7. bNVAOLoaded is declared in the C++ and never set; the check never blocks.
8. The blur step cap (8 pixels) and the constant of the blur radius are chosen here; tune in game if the blur looks too strong or too weak near the camera.

#### Sun-GI study fixes (2026-11-11, sungi; not built or tested yet)
- RtCore: the real-texture ids (texKeyOf) and the alpha atlas keys (alphaSlots) hold a COM reference on their textures, so a new texture at a freed one's address no longer inherits its RT id / atlas slot (F2). Costs: a dropped texture lives until its id is LRU-released; atlas keys are bounded by the atlas capacity.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GIAmbientUnits` (default true, missing = true): the GI hits' ambient is scaled by TESR_LinearScales.y (the PBR AmbientScale, as the lit shaders do), like the sun by .x, under GIGameLamps (F9).
- The GI reservoirs and both RT denoisers (AO, GI) start over on the frame the player's place changes (interior/exterior or worldspace) or the sun direction jumps more than 2 degrees (F16).

#### Sun-GI phase 3: radiance cache v2 (2026-11-12, sungi p3; built, not tested in game)
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT]` `CacheVisibility` (default true), `CacheCascades` (1-2, default 2), `SkyMissSH` (default true); a missing key = the default. All three act only where the RT GI already runs: the first two only with `RadianceCache` (the shipped default, false, is unchanged), the third only where ray misses bring sky light (`GISky` > 0 or AmbientLight `AmbientReplace`). Keys off = the old shaders' behaviour (cascades 1 = the old single level; no flags in the constants).
- **Visibility (F8):** the cache image is 512 x 512 now, 8 texels a cell (still ONE shared image). k 0-3 as before (SH r, g, b, header); k 4-6 = the distance moments toward 6 axis bins (+x -x +y -y +z -z; k 4 mean, k 5 mean of the square for x/y bins; k 6 = mean +z, mean -z, square +z, square -z; k 7 reserved). A ray (hit or miss; a miss counts as 8 cell sizes, distances in cell sizes so squares fit in half floats) is weighed into the 3 bins of its direction by its squared components and blended into the history by the cell's own 1/M (a bin with weight under 0.02 keeps its history). `CacheIrradiance` multiplies each of the 8 neighbours' weight by DDGI's Chebyshev term for the vector cell centre -> the point half a cell off the surface (not farther than the mean: 1; else var / (var + (d - mean)^2), cubed, at least 0.05; variance at least 0.01).
- **Cascades (F18):** the coarse level is the same 32 x 32 x 16 grid at 4 x `CacheCellSize` in rows 256-511 of the same image. Its origin is derived in the shader (camera cell = floor(fine cell / 4), offset = (fine cell - 4 x coarse cell) x size + the fine offset), so the only extra constant is a flag: the cell size in `cacheA.w` / `lampPos[7].w` carries `+ 1024 x flags` (1 visibility, 2 cascades). A second cache pass (`cacheD.w` = 1) updates 1 in 2 x `CacheSpread(Exterior)` cells a frame (all on a reset). Lookup: the fine level counts in full inside its grid, fades over its outer 2 cells (and where its cells give little weight), the coarse level takes the rest; the cache pass's own hits do the same. Needs 4 free pass slots of the frame's 8, else cascades are off for that frame. A change of the flags starts every cell over.
- **Sky at misses (F6):** `SkyRadianceSH` in `RtAOTrace.comp`: the 9 harmonics of `TESR_SkyIrradianceObjects` (Sky.cpp `Constants.IrradianceObjects`) un-convolved to radiance (bands x 1, 1.5, 4: SkyAmbient.hlsl `SkyAmbientSharp`) x `LinearScales.y` (GIAmbientUnits), uploaded each GI frame as 27 floats in 14 RG32F texels of row 1 of the "NVR RT gi lamps" image (now 2 rows; no new image); `gi.y + 10` tells the shaders it is there. A miss returns it x (GISky or 1 under AmbientReplace) x a fade to 0 over `d.z` in [-0.1, 0] (the flat version faded over +-0.25), capped as a hit sample. The cache pass's misses use it too (they were 0).
- RT shared images: unchanged (the cache and the lamp image only grew). The three trace shaders (`RtAOTrace/RtGITrace/RtRadianceCache.spv`) were rebuilt from `RtAOTrace.comp` with glslang 16.6.0 `-V --target-env vulkan1.2` (the unmodified source reproduced the committed files byte for byte with those flags and no other defines).

#### Sun-GI phase 4: SunGI switch + GI denoiser (2026-11-12, sungi p4; built, not tested in game)
- `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] SunGI` (default false, missing = false) is the one switch for the exterior sun bounce. Outdoors only (not `RtAmbient::InteriorNow`), in memory (the toml is never written), `RtAmbient::UpdateSettings` forces `Enabled`, `GI`, `Sky`, `RadianceCache`, `CacheExteriors` on; `AmbientLightEffect::UpdateSettings` forces ExteriorBalance on, AlbedoBuffer (its key) on, AmbientReplace = its key if above 0 else 0.8. Strength / quality keys are not touched. Indoors every key is the user's own.
- AmbientLight's Status: `SettingManager::GetMenuShaderEnabled("AmbientLight")` returns true while SunGI (or RoomGI) is on (load and revert); `AmbientLightEffect::UpdateSettings` flips the runtime `Enabled` on a live change. `ShouldRender` is false indoors while only SunGI has the effect on (Status key off) and the RT core runs, so AO / BounceLight run as the user set them there; without the RT core (2026-11-26) or with the Status key on it renders indoors too. Switching Ambient light off in the menu also turns SunGI and RoomGI off (2026-11-26, `SettingManager::SetMenuShaderEnabled`; before, the switch was put straight back on): see the AmbientLight section, 4.
- Fallback (the RT core is not running: native D3D9, stock DXVK, no ray-tracing GPU, `NvrDxvkPasses` off; `RtAmbient::SunGIRtRunning()`): no AmbientReplace (it needs the RT GI), ExteriorBalance stays on, and the light grid is the sun bounce: `gridBounce` = the GridBounce key if above 0, else 1.0. With the RT core running GridBounce stays the user's value. `AmbientLightEffect::SunGIFrame` (once a frame: RtWants, WantsGridSunMap, Render) follows the player in and out, re-runs UpdateSettings on the change, applies this, and logs one `UNOFFICIAL SunGI:` line per state change (off / indoors / RT / fallback).
- `[Main.Main.Quality] AlbedoBuffer` is a restart key; if it is off with SunGI on, the log says the bounce uses the guessed colour until a restart. `CapturedNormals::SunGIWantsAlbedo` makes the capture wanted outdoors when only SunGI asks for it.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GIDenoiseSunGI` (default true): with SunGI outdoors the GI denoise (`RtDenoisedGI`) runs HistoryClamp 1.5, Kernel 5 with 3 iterations, ObjectMotion where `[Main.Main.Quality] ObjectMotion` is on, and Guide = the game's lit frame (`TheTextureManager->RenderedTexture`) + GuideAlbedo = `CapturedNormals::AlbedoTexture` with GuideSigma 0.5 (as the path tracer's PTDenoiseGuide) when the albedo buffer holds this frame's and the frame is not a RenderScale rect frame. AntiLag and MovingHistory stay as configured. false = the old settings, for the A/B.

#### Room GI: the interior counterpart of SunGI (2026-11-12; shaders built, DLL not built, not tested in game)
- `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] RoomGI` (default false, missing = false) is the one switch for the interior bounce. Indoors only (`RtAmbient::InteriorNow`), in memory (the toml is never written). `RtAmbient::UpdateSettings` forces `Enabled`, `GI`, `RadianceCache` on, `GIAmbient` 0 and, when the user's `GlowGI` is 0, `GlowGI` = `RoomGIGlow` (default 1). No `Sky`, no `CacheExteriors`. Strength / quality keys are not touched. Outdoors every key is the user's own (or SunGI's).
- AmbientLight's side (`AmbientLightEffect::RoomGIFrame`, called from `SunGIFrame` once a frame; `RoomGIApply`) is forced only while the player is indoors AND the RT core runs (`RtAmbient::SunGIRtRunning()`): InteriorBalance on, AlbedoBuffer (its key) on, AmbientReplaceInterior = its key if above 0 else 0.6. One `UNOFFICIAL RoomGI:` log line per state change (off / outdoors / RT / fallback).
- Fallback (the RT core is not running): no key is changed; the keys in use are the toml's. If only RoomGI has AmbientLight on (Status off), it still draws indoors (2026-11-26; before, `ShouldRender` was false there while the switch read on) as GTAO + the grid's lamps, and the takeover switches AO / BounceLight off while it draws.
- `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] AmbientReplaceInterior` (0-1, default 0) / `AmbientFloorInterior` (0-1, default 0.3), each usable without RoomGI: AmbientReplace / AmbientFloor indoors. Needs InteriorBalance on and the RT GI traced this frame (else 0). Apply (D3D9 `AmbientLight.fx.hlsl` and `AmbientLightApply.frag`, same branch as InteriorBalance): with A = InteriorBalance's ambient share of the pixel, min(lin, albedo x the cell's light), and q = replace x (1 - floor): `lit = lin - ((1 - ao) + ao q) A`, i.e. `lin - A + (1 - q) A ao`. The occlusion acts only on the ambient that stays; the RT bounce (x GIStrength x the surface colour) is added after, as before. q rides in `NVR_ALInterior.x` as 1 + q (readers test > 0.5); q = 0 is the old arithmetic bit for bit.
- `NVR_ALRoom.x` (D3D9 Apply only; never set = 0): 1 - the lit shaders' ambient scale (`TESR_LinearScales.y`, capped at 4) while the replace mode runs with the real albedo, as ExteriorBalance's share; 0 (scale 1) with the guessed colour. The Vulkan Apply has no such field: it only runs without the albedo buffer, where the scale is 1.
- Ray misses indoors bring nothing, as before (`giSky` is 0 unless outdoors; `AmbientReplaceActive()` is exterior only): a miss in a room is a gap in the walls or the ray's end, not the sky. Window and door light reaches the bounce through glow-mapped surfaces (GlowGI), the game's lamps (GIGameLamps) and the cache.
- Why GIAmbient 0 indoors with RoomGI: it adds albedo x ambient at every bounce hit with no visibility test. In a room nearly every ray hits, most of all in corners, so it returns the removed ambient as an even, unoccluded layer and hides the lamps' bounce (the exterior finding, stronger indoors). The share of the ambient that should stay is AmbientFloorInterior, kept at the pixel and occluded there. Also the trace's ambient colour is the sky object's (`TESR_SunAmbient`), not the cell's ambient that AmbientLight reads.
- The balance passes (StatsSample / StatsReduce) are unchanged: the light grid's bounce still returns at most InteriorBounceShare x what the occlusion alone took. `RtAOTrace.comp` and its three .spv are unchanged (no shader change was needed in the trace).
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GIDenoiseRoomGI` (default true): indoors with RoomGI the GI denoise takes SunGI's settings (HistoryClamp 1.5, Kernel 5 x 3, ObjectMotion, lit-frame + albedo guide). Not tuned for rooms yet.
- Needed outside these files: `SettingManager::GetMenuShaderEnabled("AmbientLight")` must also return true for `RoomGI` (as for SunGI), or RoomGI with Status off does not load the effect.

#### Sun-GI nights + light through foliage (2026-11-12; shaders built, DLL not built, not tested in game)
- What the chain does at night (facts, `AmbientOcclusion.cpp RtRecord`): the trace's "sun" is the game's one directional light. Below `dayLight` 0.5 that is the moon: `ShaderConst.SunDir` = the reverse of `Tes->directionalLight`, colour = the weather's night sunlight x `[Shaders.Sky.Main] MoonBrightness` x `LinearScales.x`, full strength, one hard shadow ray a hit. It lights the hits while `ShadowFade.x < 1`: all day and all night, but not in the middle of dusk and dawn (`dayLight` within 0.1 of 0.5). The lamps (the game's lamp table with GIGameLamps, else the shadow-slot and tracked lamps; one picked by its unshadowed light, one shadow ray) are on at night with or without SunGI. GlowGI was 0 under SunGI. Ray misses: the sky's harmonics (SkyMissSH), which at night are the dark sky dome's colours.
- `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] AmbientReplaceNight` / `AmbientFloorNight` (-1 = the day value): the two at night, blended by `GameState.transitionCurve` (`AmbientLightEffect::AmbientReplaceNow` / `AmbientFloorNow`; `AmbientReplaceActive` and Render's `NVR_ALInterior.y / .z` use them). No shader change. SunGI does not set them.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] SkyMissNightAmbient` (-1 auto: 0, 1 with SunGI): the sky data uploaded for the misses moves, by this share x the night's part of the curve, from the harmonics to a flat sky of the game's ambient (band 0 = `c.ambient`, the other bands scaled down). C++ only (`RtSkyData`).
- `GlowGINight` (-1 = GlowGI; SunGI: 1 at night while GlowGI is 0), `GIClampNight` (-1 = GIClamp): blended the same way into `ambient.w` / `gi2.z` (`RtAmbient::GlowGINow`, `GIClampNow`).
- `GISunDuskFade` (-1 auto: off, on with SunGI): `sunColor` x smoothstep(0.1, 0.5, |dayLight - 0.5|), so the sun term no longer switches off and on in one frame at `dayLight` 0.6 / 0.4.
- `GILeafTransmission` (0-0.9, 0 = off): rides on `sunDir.w` as 1 + the key. `RtAOTrace.comp Shade`: above 0 the sun visibility ray at a bounce hit is `rtSunFoliageTrace` (`RtSunFoliage.glsl`, included unchanged: a covered plant cut-out, record kind 2 + alpha-tested, leaves the key's share of the light; 256 candidates and a 1 / 64 floor bound the loop); 0 takes the old `Visible` ray. All three variants (`RtAOTrace/RtGITrace/RtRadianceCache.spv`) rebuilt with glslang 15.0.0 `-V --target-env vulkan1.2` (+ `-DRT_GI_PASS` / `-DRT_CACHE_PASS`); the unmodified source reproduced the committed files byte for byte first. If `RtSunFoliage.glsl` changes, rebuild these three too.
- One log line per change: `UNOFFICIAL RT AO: GI night / foliage keys ...` and `UNOFFICIAL AmbientLight: night values outdoors ...`.

### Flat bounce indoors: what was checked, and the test views (2026-11-12)

Report: a room with a grid of 43 lamps (`vs-lamps`) showed an even layer in AmbientLight's bounce view (DebugView 13), and RoomGI changed the picture by 1 %.
Read from the code and that run's log and pictures; nothing here was run in the game.

- **View 13 is the bounce before the pixel's surface colour** (`AmbientLight.fx.hlsl Apply`: `giLight`, x GIStrength, `delinearize`d; the surface colour is
  multiplied in after the view returns). It is not normalised: InteriorBalance's scale (`NVR_ALBalance`) multiplies the light grid's bounce only. It is drawn
  before the tone mapping, so bright values are squeezed together on screen.
- **Every bounce hit is lit by the lamps with a shadow ray** (`GIScreenLight` is off by default: `offscreenView`, the slot's + 16; `RtAOTrace.comp Bounce`
  then never takes the lit frame). The reuse is temporal only by default (`GISpatial` 0), the RT denoiser reaches 7 texels (kernel 3) or 14 (kernel 5, 3
  passes) of the trace grid, AmbientLight's blur 3 texels each way: none of them can even out a room.
- **In that room an even layer is the right answer.** Its lamps sit on a 256-unit grid at z 200 with reach 450 (the log's lamp lines): with the game's
  falloff the floor's direct light differs by 2 % between "under a lamp" and "between four", and the floor's bounce comes from a ceiling 200-300 units
  above it, a kernel wider than the lamp spacing. The picture agrees: floor bright, walls less, ceiling darkest (it faces the dark floor), a soft shadow
  under the table, a faint tint toward each side's lamp colours, no tile or rug pattern (so it is not the pixel's colour).
- **RoomGI's 1 %:** the settings file of that run already had RT GI on, so "RoomGI off" had the same bounce; and the cell's ambient is 30 30 32, which the
  lit shaders scale by 0.218 after decoding: about 0.005 x the surface colour against about 1 x from the lamps. Taking 42 % of that out is 0.2 % of a pixel.
  The estimate is right (`Object.hlsl getAmbientLighting`); there is simply almost no ambient in a lamp-lit cell to replace.

Test views (all off by default, no cost when off):
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] DebugView = 9`: in the bounce's place, the light the bounce hits are lit with, at each pixel's own surface
  (`RtAOTrace.comp BounceOrTest` / `DirectLight`; the flag rides on the cache slot's w as + 32, `GiTest`). Shown by AmbientLight's view 13. Expect the game's
  lighting without textures. It also goes through the reuse, the denoiser and the blurs: pools that survive in this view are not being smoothed away.
- `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] DebugView = 14 / 15`: the RT bounce as traced this frame / after the RT denoiser, before AmbientLight's own steps, without
  GIStrength (`AmbientLight.cpp Render`: `giDebugTexture` on Apply's bounce stage; no shader change).
- `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] DebugGIExposure` (default 1): a factor on views 13-15 only.
- With no new build: `GIDistanceInterior = 100` with view 13. Rays 100 units long cannot reach the ceiling from the floor, so the open floor must go
  nearly black and only places near walls, pillars and furniture keep light. A layer that stays is not a traced bounce.

`RtAOTrace.comp`: `Shade`'s light half is now `DirectLight` (same lines, a function of its own). The three variants were rebuilt with glslang
`-V --target-env vulkan1.2` (+ `-DRT_GI_PASS` / `-DRT_CACHE_PASS`); the unmodified source reproduced the committed files byte for byte first.
#### RT GI review fixes (2026-11-13, from mrfury97's fork, branch rtgi-review; ported 2026-11-15, docs/next/FURY-RTGI-PORT.md (not in this repo); "REVIEW.md" below means their review notes, not in this repo)
- `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] GIDenoisedNoBlur` (default true; R1): RT bounce light that came through the RT denoiser passes through AmbientLight's BlurX / BlurY unchanged (`AmbientLight.fx.hlsl` Blur: `NVR_ALGI.w` 2 or 3; `AmbientLightBlurPass.frag` variant bit 1). That blur's bounce step has a 25 % depth tolerance and no normal test (written for the old screen bounce on grass) and mixed the denoised bounce across object edges. `NVR_ALGI.w` is now 1 (GISingleHistory) + 2 (GIDenoisedNoBlur); Accumulate tests the odd bit. The compute blur (`AmbientLightBlurCompute`, dev only) is not changed.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GICutByEnergy` (default true; R2): the GI reuse's history cut (`RtRecord`, the 5 light keys) scales with the size of the change: the history keeps 0.5 / change frames, changes under 3 % do nothing, and while the sun lights the lamp keys count a tenth. The radiance cache's cap follows the same number instead of dropping to 2 visits on every cut. Before: 2,660 cuts in 74,400 frames of the user's session (lamps entering the nearest-7 list, fires flickering), each one the reservoirs to 1 frame and the cache to 2 visits.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GIDenoiseTune` (0-2, default 2; R9 / R10; with SunGI outdoors, and in Vegas Shaders also with RoomGI indoors, which shares SunGI's denoiser block, `RtDenoisedGI`): 1 = `MeanVariance 4` + `HistoryFix 3` (RtDenoise.h); 2 = and `HistoryClamp 0` (AntiLag stays).
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GIGrassDenoise` (0-3, default 3; 2026-11-16, user: grass still sparkles in the shade; with SunGI outdoors and the denoiser's guide, `RtDenoisedGI` -> `RtDenoise::Settings::Soft`): a texel the albedo buffer has no texel for (grass: the game's own shader draws it) is one soft surface for the GI denoiser. Bit 1: the temporal pass keeps its history there (no disocclusion-mask restart, depth tolerance 25 %; `RtDenoiseTemporalRgb.frag` image 7 = the albedo buffer, the flag in `backbone.w` + 2). Bit 2: the a-trous passes take every tap within 25 % of the depth round a soft texel, with no normal, brightness or guide test (`RtDenoiseAtrousRgb.frag`, `specular` bit 4). Before, every grass texel was restarted each frame (blades at their own depths, swaying, hashed cut-outs) and no tap passed: in the shade, where the bounce comes from a few sunlit spots, the bounce flickered 14 times more than on the road beside it (slot F: 9.05 -> 1.40 with 3; solids unchanged). Correct: grass in the shade is as calm as grass in the sun. Wrong: bounce light from a wall behind leaking into grass in front of it within 25 % of the depth (the old loose blur did the same). (Vegas Shaders port, 2026-11-15: also with RoomGI indoors. With Soft on, `GuideAlbedo` is `core/GrassMaskedAlbedo.h`'s copy, alpha 0 on the MaterialBuffer's grass mark too, so it works with our default CapturedGrass on; Soft 0 keeps the albedo buffer as before. See the GIGrassBlur note.)
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] InputSamePixel` (default true, needs NormalGuard; R4; `RtAOInput.frag`, flag in the sky layout's y): the ray start is the fetched normal's own pixel (its depth; the texel centre's ray met with that pixel's plane) instead of the 4-pixel average depth.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GICandidates` (1-2, default 1; B1; `RtAOTrace.comp` ReuseGI, reuse flag 8): a second new bounce candidate a texel and frame into the same reservoir.
- Ported into Vegas Shaders 2026-11-15 from mrfury97's fork (docs/next/FURY-RTGI-PORT.md (not in this repo)); the .spv rebuilt here from source with the Vulkan SDK 1.3.296.0 glslangValidator (tools/nvr-dxvk/compile-compute.sh's settings): `RtAOInput.spv`, `AmbientLightBlurPass.spv` (vulkan1.1), `Rt/RtAOTrace.spv`, `Rt/RtGITrace.spv` (vulkan1.2; `RtRadianceCache.spv` comes out byte-identical). No new shared image, no new register.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GIHitMips` (default true; R19; `RtAOTrace.comp` HitCone, all three variants; the flag is sunDir.w's fraction 0.25 in the fork; in Vegas Shaders sunDir.w + 4, because w's fraction already carries GILeafTransmission (1 + the key), and the shader's sun term is `SunW()` = mod(w, 4)): `rtConeWidth` = 0.5 x the bounce ray's length for the hit's albedo / glow / normal-map reads (before: never set, so mip 0). It is 0 again before every traversal and shadow ray, so the alpha tests stay at lod 0 and the three shaders stay on `NvrRtOmm.inl` FinestMipShader's list (no helper change).
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GIDenoiseGuideOnce` (default true; B2; `RtDenoise` Settings.GuideOnce, `RtDenoiseAtrousRgb.frag` specular bit 3): the first a-trous pass writes each texel's guide value (lit frame / albedo) into output B's blue, the later passes read it from the B taps they fetch anyway (3 reads a tap instead of 5, no pow). The path tracer's denoiser leaves GuideOnce off.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GISamplePoints` (default false; B3; `RtAOTrace.comp` ReuseGIPoints, reuse flag 16; both the GI pass and the merged AO pass): ReSTIR GI with sample points after the path tracer's `RtRestirGI.comp`. Reservoir A = (light, W); the sample point (direction, distance, hit normal in 10 bits) sits in the lower half of reservoir image B, which is twice as tall now (no new shared image). Target = luminance x cosine here; spatial reuse x Jacobian (0.1-10) + one visibility ray for a neighbour's winner; the estimate's luminance capped at 2 x GIClamp. A change of the key restarts the reuse history. (Vegas Shaders port: off while `DebugView 9` runs, whose DirectLight test only the plain ReuseGI knows.) `GIValidate` (default true, reuse flag 32): 1 texel in 8 a frame re-traces to its kept sample point (geometry only) and drops its history when the hit moved.
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GISpatialVisibility` (1-2, default 1; with GISamplePoints; reuse flag 64): 1 = one visibility ray for a winner taken from a neighbour; 2 = one for every neighbour before it is merged, a blocked one rejected whole (counted with no weight it darkened the bounce 14 %: the reduced W is stored and reused). Mode 2 measured no sharper or quieter than 1 and costs more (RTGIReview/REVIEW.md 6.10).
- `[Shaders.AmbientOcclusion.RT] GIScreenAmbientOut` (default false; B6; `RtAOTrace.comp` Bounce; lamp slot 7's w is now strength + 16 x flags: 1 off-screen view, 2 this in the fork; in Vegas Shaders 2 is DebugView 9's test and this is 4 (+ 64), `SlotFlags()`): only while AmbientReplace is active outdoors with GIScreenLight on. An on-screen hit's light = the lit frame - its albedo x the game's ambient (the GI passes' `ambient`) + its albedo x the cache's irradiance, the same model as an off-screen hit.
- **Settings cleanup (the fork's 9e5a36cd4, 2026-11-13, their owner's OK; ported 2026-11-15):** no key any more, always on: the denoised GI skips AmbientLight's blur (was `GIDenoisedNoBlur`), the history cut by energy (`GICutByEnergy`), the trace input from one pixel (`InputSamePixel`), the hit textures' mip by the ray's footprint (`GIHitMips`; here sunDir.w + 4 always, see above), the denoiser's guide worked out once (`GIDenoiseGuideOnce`). Deleted with their shader code: `GICandidates` (reuse flag 8) and `GISpatialVisibility` (reuse flag 64), measured no help in the fork. The remaining review keys (`GIDenoiseTune`, `GISamplePoints`, `GIValidate`, `GIScreenAmbientOut`) are hidden unless Developer options are on (DevOnly). Not ported: the fork also hid a dozen of our older RT GI keys (GISky, LegacyTilt, the *Interior keys, DenoiseKernel, ExteriorBalance, GISingleHistory ...): that is a menu choice for the owner (docs/next/FURY-RTGI-PORT.md (not in this repo)).
- `[Shaders.AmbientLight.Main] GIGrassBlur` (default true, DevOnly; 2026-11-13, user: GI sparkles on grass after R1): `AmbientLight.fx.hlsl` Blur (`NVR_ALGI.w` + 4) and `AmbientLightBlurPass.frag` (variant bit 2). The RT-denoised bounce passes through on solid surfaces; where the depth is busy along the blur's axis (it bends at this texel and at the one either side: Apply's busy-depth rule, x 40 / depth - 0.1) the loose blur (25 % depth step) is blended back by the busyness. An object's single edge bends at two texels only and stays sharp.
- `GIGrassBlur`, second step (2026-11-13): the D3D9 Blur blends the loose blur back by max(the busy-depth rule, the share of the texel's 2 x 2 full-size pixels WITHOUT an albedo texel). Grass is drawn by the game's own shader and never writes `TESR_AlbedoBuffer`, so that share is an exact grass mask (needs AlbedoBuffer on, which SunGI forces; `NVR_ALAlbedo` is bound on s12 for the blur passes then). The rule alone caught too little in storm weather. The Vulkan twin (`AmbientLightBlurPass.frag`) has no albedo input and keeps the rule alone. (Vegas Shaders port, 2026-11-15: here `[Main.Main.Quality] CapturedGrass` is on by default, and whenever RT GI runs the grass writes albedo records with alpha 1 (`CapturedNormals.h` forGI / GrassForGI, also DeferredFoliage), so "no albedo record" alone missed our grass. The blur passes therefore read `core/GrassMaskedAlbedo.h`: the albedo buffer with alpha 0 also where the MaterialBuffer carries the grass mark (0.5 with "written"; 4.5 with GBufferV2; the deferred grass record's g range [32, 1024) while DeferredFoliage makes them; the same tests as `MergeIntoNormals`). Works with CapturedGrass on or off. The four reads sit behind a `[branch]` here (the fork read them always). Unproven in game.)

## AvgLuma

Source of this spec: the rewritten shaders (wave 3 buffers, branch claude/rw3-buffers): src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/AvgLuma.fx.hlsl (D3D9 pass) and src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/AvgLuma.comp (exact reduction). AvgLumaHistogram.comp is kept from before (not rewritten here). The C++ (src/effects/AvgLuma.cpp) is unchanged. Written from the previous spec and the C++; the old shader was not read.

### 1. Purpose

AvgLuma measures how bright the picture is, and where the focus of the depth of field should sit, and keeps both smoothed over time. Its result is a single 1 x 1 texel. Exposure reads the brightness to decide how much to darken or brighten the frame, and the depth of field effect reads the focal distance. The player sees no image of AvgLuma itself: the result shows as the picture slowly brightening or darkening, and the depth of field's focus changing when the view changes.

### 2. When it runs

- In RenderEffectsPreTonemapping, in the HDR block, after AO, ambient light and fog, before exposure. It measures the picture as it is at that point.
- Only when avglumaRequired is set (Exposure sets it every frame), so in practice whenever Exposure is loaded.
- Compute path: when AvgLumaCompute or the Exposure Histogram is on and Vegas Shaders DXVK is present, and the frame is not in RenderScale v2 rect mode (a rect frame always takes the D3D9 pass). Otherwise the D3D9 pass.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures

- TESR_RenderedBuffer (stage 0, LINEAR, CLAMP): the frame before exposure. Read at 112 positions (section 6).
- TESR_AvgLumaBuffer (stage 1, LINEAR, CLAMP): the same 1 x 1 texture the pass writes. Its previous value is read (section 9).
- TESR_DepthBuffer (stage 2, LINEAR, CLAMP): CombineDepth's output. Read at the centre and 12 taps.

#### Constants

- TESR_MotionBlurData (xy): camera rotation speed (focus search spread).
- TESR_DepthOfFieldData (x): hyperfocal distance in thousands of game units.
- TESR_GameTime (w): frame time, seconds.
- TESR_ExposureData (z dark adaptation, w light adaptation; x and y are Exposure's).
- TESR_CameraData (y): far plane (the depth read's scale).
- TESR_ReciprocalResolution (xy): for the rect clamp of the taps.
- TESR_RenderScale (RenderRect include): the drawn part.

### 4. Settings

Unchanged:
- [Main.Main.Performance] AvgLumaCompute (bool, false; UNOFFICIAL, needs Vegas Shaders DXVK).
- [Main.Main.Performance] AvgLumaComputeCheck (int, 0; test tool).
- [Shaders.Exposure.Main] Histogram (bool, false; needs Vegas Shaders DXVK), HistogramLowPercent (10), HistogramHighPercent (5).

### 5. Passes and outputs

#### D3D9 pass (technique unnamed, one pass, PS AvgLumaPS)

Target: TESR_AvgLumaBuffer, 1 x 1 A16B16G16R16F. Output, all four channels:
- R = newLuma: (average + 7 x previous R) / 8.
- G = animatedLuma: the previous G moved toward newLuma by at most dt x DarkAdapt down or dt x LightAdapt up (dt = TESR_GameTime.w).
- B = focus: the focal distance as a fraction of the hyperfocal distance, 0 to 1.
- A = 1.

#### Compute pass (AvgLuma.comp, two dispatches)

Same four values, same smoothing, adaptation and focus. The average is the exact mean (every pixel), not the 112-sample mean.

### 6. Behaviour details

#### The average (into R)

- D3D9: 112 samples of linear colour, each read at level 0: a 10 x 10 grid at 0.05, 0.15 ... 0.95 of the drawn part, plus 12 taps on a golden-angle spiral out to the frame edge (offsets from the centre, kept on the drawn part's edge). The mean linear colour's BT.709 luminance is the average. (The exact 12 offsets were not in the old spec; this spiral is the chosen set.)
- Compute: every pixel's linear BT.709 luminance, tile partial sums (16 x 16), then one group; divided by the pixel count.
- The average is always the mean of linear luminance.

#### Smoothing and adaptation

- newLuma = (average + 7 x previous R) / 8.
- Adaptation as in section 5. Dark: delta < 0, moved by max(delta, -dt x DarkAdapt). Light: moved by min(delta, dt x LightAdapt).

#### Focus (B)

- speed.xy = saturate(|motion.xy|) + 0.1.
- Centre depth c (game units). Twelve taps at 0.3 x tap offset x speed; depth sum = c + the twelve depths; meanDepth = sum / 12 (kept as written: 13 terms over 12, a small upward bias).
- depth = c if c > meanDepth (iron sights: the centre wins when it is the farther surface), else meanDepth. depth = saturate(depth / max(1e-7, hyperfocal x 1000)).
- step = pow(max(old B, depth), 0.1). B moves toward depth by at most 3 x step x dt nearer, 1 x step x dt farther.

#### Edge behaviour

- Rect frames: the average and the taps are on the drawn part (the tap positions are clamped to it).
- Sky (far) gives focus 1.
- Guards: hyperfocal floored at 1e-7 (existing). pow(0, 0.1) is 0, no NaN.
- The texture starts at zero; the first frames rise from zero.

### 7. Vulkan path

- AvgLuma.comp (new): set 1 binding 0 = the frame (texelFetch), 1 = the depth (textureLod through the heap sampler), 2 = partial sums (float array), 3 = state in, 4 = state out. Push block as the C++ fills it (128 bytes; the phase at 16, the sampler index at 20, motion at 32, depth of field at 48, game time at 64, exposure at 80, camera at 96, histogram at 112 unused).
- The state buffer (32 bytes, shared with AvgLumaHistogram.comp): offset 0 vec4 cur; offset 16 two uints (cur as four half floats: the texel's 8 bytes); offset 24 the average (float); offset 28 share (float, set to 1).
- The Vulkan focus taps use screen uv without the rect clamp (the compute path is not used in rect frames).
- AvgLumaHistogram.comp is kept and must use this same state layout. Not checked against its source in this pass (see section 9).

### 8. Look and cost

Correct: exposure drifts (no flicker from frame to frame); focus pulls in quickly and relaxes slowly; a sun disc does not drag the whole exposure (histogram on).

Typical wrong: flicker from a blinking light (adaptation too fast, or an exact average with a small bright source); exposure stuck after a cut (adaptation too slow); focus jumping when a tap lands on another surface.

Cost: D3D9: a 1 x 1 target with 112 samples and 13 depth reads (cheap). Compute: two dispatches reading every pixel (moderate).

### 9. Open questions

1. Feedback hazard, open: the D3D9 pass reads TESR_AvgLumaBuffer and writes the same 1 x 1 texture. The C++ provides one target for this effect. The read returns the previous value only if the driver keeps it; this is not guaranteed. Needs a second 1 x 1 target (ping-pong) from the C++ to be safe.
2. The 13/12 focus bias is kept as written.
3. The 8-frame average is a one-eighth exponential average, not a true mean.
4. The 12 tap offsets of the D3D9 average are the spiral above; the old offsets were not recorded in the spec.
5. AvgLumaHistogram.comp (kept) must agree with the state layout in section 7. Not verified.
6. The exact average (compute) and the 112-sample average (D3D9) still differ, as before; the D3D9 pass keeps its sampling.
7. Histogram bin layout (126 log2 steps) is unchanged; it lives in the kept histogram shader.

### GT7 adaptation (technique 1, [Shaders.Exposure.Main] Mode 1)

With Exposure's `Mode` 1, EffectChain draws technique 1 ("GT7", PS AvgLumaGT7) instead of the pass above, and RenderCompute stands aside (no compute port). It meters and adapts for Exposure's GT7 technique (see Exposure's README, section 10, and Effects/Includes/GT7Adaptation.hlsl) and keeps the focus as technique 0 does:
- R = short-term adaptation level, G = long-term adaptation level (log2 luminance, scene units), B = the depth-of-field focus (same as technique 0, so DepthOfField reads it unchanged), A = white-balance colour temperature in mired (below 50 = not initialised: the first frame starts from the measurement).
- Extra inputs: TESR_ShadowAtlas (stage 3) and TESR_OrthoMapBuffer (stage 4), the sun cascades and the ortho transform (Includes/Shadows.hlsl, SunCascades.hlsl), TESR_SkyIrradiance, TESR_SunsetColor, TESR_ShadowFade, TESR_ShadowSunLight, TESR_ShadowAmbientLight, TESR_GT7AdaptData, TESR_HDRData and the weather colours.
- The texel is cleared when Mode changes (EffectChain), since the two techniques keep different values in it.
- Technique 0 is unchanged and compiles to the same code as before.

## Bloom

Source reviewed (wave 3, 2026-11-02): the new D3D9 effect src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Bloom.fx.hlsl and the new Vulkan passes
src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Bloom.glsl and Bloom{DownFirst,Down,Up,UpLast}.frag (branch claude/rw3-colour); C++ src/effects/Bloom.cpp,
Bloom.h (unchanged); the tonemap hand-off in src/hlsl/NewVegas/Shaders/ISHDRBLENDINSHADERCIN.pso.hlsl (not edited); the tonemap constants
in src/effects/Tonemapping.cpp; the frame order in src/core/ShaderManager.cpp and src/NewVegas/Hooks/Render.cpp.

### 1. Purpose

Bloom is the soft glow that spreads out from bright light sources (lamps, neon, fire, the sun, bright sky) so they look
like they are shining into the lens. It is computed from the HDR scene before the game's tonemapper, as a stack of
progressively smaller blurred levels that are summed back up into one half-resolution glow image. The game's tonemap
shader then mixes that glow into the picture in place of the game's own bloom. Two other effects read the glow image
too: the dirt lens overlay (Lens) and the rain/precipitation effect. Bloom has no depth, sky or first-person masking:
the glow is taken from the whole scene.

### 2. When it runs

- Entry point: the pre-tonemap chain (ShaderManager::RenderEffectsPreTonemapping), called from the render hook in
  src/NewVegas/Hooks/Render.cpp. With [Main.Main.Misc] RenderPreTonemapping = true (the default) the chain runs on the
  HDR game surface before the game's image-space pass, which contains the tonemap. With it false, the chain runs on the
  output surface after image space.
- Order inside the chain: after the average-luma pass and the exposure pass (both can be skipped), before Lens and before
  any pre-tonemap LUT. The effect is rendered by the bloom block of that function, not by the generic post-effect path.
- Gates, in order: [Main.Main] RenderEffects must be on; the player must be in a cell; the pre-tonemap chain returns
  early while the overlay is on (terminal, lockpicking) and the menu background is ready. The effect itself is skipped
  when [Shaders.Bloom.Status] Enabled is off (default true), and then the glow buffer is cleared to black when it is
  switched off.
- Tonemap flag: the constant TESR_BloomExtraData.x is set to 1 when Bloom is enabled and the overlay is off (0 otherwise).
  The tonemap shader reads it to choose between the glow path and the game's own bloom path (section 5). The render hook
  sets the flag to 0 for the frames of the menu scene guard ([Main.Main.Performance] MenuSceneGuard).
- Interior, exterior, night: the settings section is chosen per frame from the game state through a transition value.
  Exterior: a blend between the Night section and the Main section, weighted by the day/night transition curve. Interior:
  the Interiors section outright.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (the names the shader declares, their slots, and what they hold)

Slot order is fixed by the explicit register numbers in the shader, s0 to s8:

| Slot | Name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_BloomBuffer | glow level 0: half of the render width and height, the level the tonemap reads |
| s1 | TESR_BloomBuffer2 | glow level 1: a quarter of the render size |
| s2 | TESR_BloomBuffer4 | glow level 2: an eighth |
| s3 | TESR_BloomBuffer8 | glow level 3: a sixteenth |
| s4 | TESR_BloomBuffer16 | glow level 4: a thirty-second |
| s5 | TESR_BloomBuffer32 | glow level 5: a sixty-fourth |
| s6 | TESR_BloomBuffer64 | glow level 6: a one-hundred-and-twenty-eighth |
| s7 | TESR_BloomBuffer128 | glow level 7: a two-hundred-and-fifty-sixth |
| s8 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the current HDR scene (technique 0 only) |

- Level i is 1 / 2^(i+1) of the render size in each axis. The name suffix is 2^i (none for level 0).
- Format of every level: 16-bit float per channel, four channels (A16B16G16R16F). Values are not clamped to 0..1.
- All samplers: LINEAR, CLAMP, no mips.

#### Shader constants (names, registers, components, units)

No fixed registers: the C++ registers them by name.

| Name | Components |
|---|---|
| TESR_BloomResolution | xy: size of the level being written (texels); zw: 1 / size |
| TESR_BloomData | x, y: upsample tent radius (uv units). z: PassBlending, 0..1. w: 1 / Passes (not read by the new shader) |
| TESR_BloomGlow | x: spread weight per wider level (1 + Spread, clamped 0.25..3). y: scale of the last upsample. z: GlowBoost. w: GlowLightLevel times the scene's light (the glow threshold) |
| TESR_BloomGlowExtra | x: AntiFlicker (1 or 0). y: LinearLight (1 or 0) |
| TESR_BloomExtraData (C++ only; declared by the tonemap shader) | x: 1 when the glow is on for the tonemap, else 0 |

Derived by the C++ per frame:
- Defaults: x = 0.005, y = 0.005 times (render width / render height), z = PassBlending, w = 1 / Passes.
- PassBlending above 0: before each upsample x and y are the texel size of the level being upsampled from (level i+1).
- Last upsample scale y: 1 / Passes when Spread is 0, else 1 / (sum over the passes of spread weight^level).

#### Other inputs

- Settings only, no other textures. The depth buffer and the normals are not read.

### 4. Settings

All keys are in [Shaders.Bloom.*] sections. Ranges are the clamps the C++ applies. Unchanged by the rewrite.

#### [Shaders.Bloom.Status]
- Enabled (bool, default true): replaces the game's own bloom (the tonemap uses this glow). Also feeds the dirt lens and the
  rain effects. Off: the glow buffer is black, the tonemap takes the game's bloom path.

#### [Shaders.Bloom.Main], [Shaders.Bloom.Night], [Shaders.Bloom.Interiors]

Keys present in all three sections:
- Blending (float, default 0.04): read by the tonemap shader (not by the Bloom C++). The mix weight between the tonemapped
  picture and the glow, clamped to 0..1 when used.
- Strength (float, default 1.0): read by the tonemap shader. The gain applied to the glow before mixing.
- Passes (float, default 8; used as an integer, clamped 2..8): how many levels.
- PassBlending (float, default 0.0; clamped 0..1): 0 means the levels are added together. Above 0 each upsample is a blend
  between the level's own downsample and the upsampled level above, weighted by this value (section 5).
- Spread (float, default 0.0; clamped -0.75..2.0; UNOFFICIAL): 0 is as before. Above 0 wider levels weigh more and the glow
  reaches further; below 0 the glow is tighter. Only has an effect with PassBlending 0.
- GlowBoost (float, default 0.0; clamped 0..20; UNOFFICIAL): 0 is as before. Above 0, bright lights (neon, lamps, fire)
  count up to 1 + GlowBoost times as much as other pixels in the first downsample.

Keys present only in [Shaders.Bloom.Main] (read for all times of day):
- GlowLightLevel (float, default 1.5; UNOFFICIAL): what counts as a bright light for GlowBoost, as a multiple of a white
  surface in the scene's own light. Values at or below 0 become 1.5; otherwise clamped to 0.25..50.
- LinearLight (bool, default false; UNOFFICIAL): the chain blurs linear light instead of display-encoded values.
- AntiFlicker (bool, default false; UNOFFICIAL): the first downsample weighs each of its five boxes by 1 / (1 + luma of the box).

Settings that look similar but belong to other effects: [Shaders.Lens.*] BloomExponent (dirt lens) and
[Shaders.Precipitations.Main] Bloom (rain strength).

### 5. Passes and outputs

#### The D3D9 chain (the reference path), Bloom.fx.hlsl

Let N = Passes (clamped 2..8). The chain is N downsample passes then N - 1 upsample passes, each a full-screen quad through the
frame vertex buffer. The C++ calls the techniques by index.

Downsample (techniques 0 to 7):
- Technique 0: reads the HDR scene (TESR_RenderedBuffer), writes level 0. The only pass that applies GlowBoost, LinearLight and
  AntiFlicker.
- Techniques 1 to 7: technique t reads level t-1 and writes level t.
- Each downsample reads 13 taps (the centre, four diagonal taps one texel away, and eight taps two texels away on the axes and
  diagonals; the texel is the source level's). They make five overlapping 2 x 2 boxes: the centre box weighs 1/2, the four
  corner boxes 1/8 each.

Upsample (techniques 8 to 14):
- Technique 14 - i upsamples into level i. Technique 14 is always the last one and writes level 0.
- Each upsample takes the 9-tap tent (centre 4, edges 2, corners 1, over 16) of level i+1 at radius (Data.x, Data.y).
- Add mode (PassBlending 0): level i = own_i + Spread * tent(level i+1). Each wider level is weighted by the spread weight, so the
  weight of level j in level 0 is spread^j.
- Blend mode (PassBlending above 0): level i = lerp(own_i, tent(level i+1), PassBlending). The result is a convex blend, so
  neither the spread weight nor the last-level scale is applied.
- The last upsample (technique 14, level 0): in add mode the result is multiplied by the scale y (TESR_BloomGlow.y). With
  LinearLight on, the result is encoded back to display values (the blend mode too).
- Output: level 0 (TESR_BloomBuffer, half size, A16B16G16R16F) holds the finished glow. It is the only level read outside the
  chain.
- Read and write: the upsample reads its own level (own_i) from the texture it writes (the D3D9 chain has no separate target
  for it). See section 9.

#### The Vulkan chain (Vegas Shaders DXVK)

Same passes and maths. Each downsample level below the top goes into its own target (NVR_BloomLevel0..6), so no pass reads and
writes one target. The upsamples write the chain's levels and read the downsample results from those targets.

#### Hand-off to the game's tonemap (exact, unchanged)

1. Bloom writes level 0 (TESR_BloomBuffer) in the pre-tonemap chain.
2. The tonemap shader (ISHDRBLENDINSHADERCIN and AM variant) samples that texture at its slot s8 under the name TESR_BloomBuffer
   at the picture's own texture coordinate.
3. TESR_BloomExtraData.x selects the path. 1: the glow is read, linearised as the tonemap does for display values, multiplied by
   Strength (TESR_HDRBloomData.y) and mixed into the picture with Blending (TESR_HDRBloomData.x). 0: the tonemap uses the game's
   own bloom from its slot s0 and scales it by the same two constants with a different meaning.
4. The tonemapper runs on the blended picture, so the glow is added in HDR before tonemapping.

Other readers of level 0: the dirt lens (Lens.cpp), the rain/precipitation effect (Rain.cpp), and the debug view. They see a black
buffer when Bloom is disabled.

### 6. Behaviour details

- Filter kernels: the downsample is the 13-tap box filter of section 5. The upsample is the 9-tap tent.
- Linear light (GlowBoost or LinearLight on, first downsample): each tap is converted to linear light by a square (c * c, negatives
  clamped to 0), then the bright taps are boosted (below). Display values come back by a square root. The square pair is an exact
  inverse of itself. It replaces the old cubic fit (see the change note in section 9).
- GlowBoost brightness: the Rec.709 luma of the linear tap, mixed toward its peak channel by its saturation (1 minus the minimum over
  the peak). Threshold t = max(TESR_BloomGlow.w, 0.002). Amount = clamp((brightness - t) / t, 0, 1). The tap is multiplied by
  1 + GlowBoost times the amount. Unless LinearLight is on, the tap is then encoded back to display values before the boxes are
  averaged.
- AntiFlicker: the five boxes are weighted 1/8, 1/8, 1/8, 1/8 and 1/2 each divided by (1 + max(luma of the box, 0)), and the sum is
  normalised by the sum of the weights. First downsample only.
- Spread: wider levels are weighted by spread^j in level 0 (section 5). Spread 0 (weight 1) is the old behaviour.
- Sky, depth, first-person weapon: no masking. Bloom reads the whole HDR scene, sky included.
- Jitter (TAA, FSR, DLSS): not used.
- Reversed depth: not applicable.
- HDR vs LDR: all levels are linear HDR values, unclamped. Level 0 may be above 1.
- NaN and infinity guards: the first downsample's box weights are all positive, so the normalising sum cannot be zero. The glow
  threshold is floored at 0.002. Negative values are clamped before the square root and the square. The blend uses a convex mix.
- Passes below 8: levels deeper than N - 1 are not written and not read.
- Menu scene guard: TESR_BloomExtraData.x is set to 0 for menu frames so the frozen glow is not shown in the Pip-Boy or menus.
- RenderScale (rect frames, UNOFFICIAL): in the D3D9 chain each level draws into its top-left part; every tap (downsample and tent)
  is kept inside the drawn part (clampToRectIfOn), and after the upsample level 0 is stretched back to full size for its readers.
  Full-size frames run the same code with no clamp. The Vulkan chain has no rect steps (section 7).
- Vulkan refusal: if a Vulkan pass is refused, the effect falls back to the D3D9 chain for the session and that frame is redone.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Choice: [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses (default true) and the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension (DxvkCompute::PassesOn). RenderBloomExt runs
  the chain. A pass that fails sets the effect's failure flag for the session. The D3D9 chain does the frame in that case.
- Files (src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute):
  - Bloom.glsl: the shared include: the push block, the taps, the box and tent functions. Helper sampling is at point of use
    (GLSL forbids a sampler built outside the call).
  - BloomDownFirst.frag: technique 0 (scene into level 0, with GlowBoost, LinearLight and AntiFlicker).
  - BloomDown.frag: techniques 1 to 7.
  - BloomUp.frag: techniques 8 to 13 (level i+1 at binding 0, level i's own downsample at binding 1).
  - BloomUpLast.frag: technique 14 (writes level 0).
  - The .spv files beside each .frag are compiled with tools/nvr-dxvk/compile-compute.sh.
- Bindings (set 1): binding 0 is the first texture, binding 1 the second for the upsample passes. One LINEAR CLAMP sampler, its heap
  index in the push block at offset 64.
- Push block (80 bytes), the same layout in every pass: offset 0 TESR_BloomData, 16 TESR_BloomResolution, 32 TESR_BloomGlow, 48
  TESR_BloomGlowExtra, 64 sampler heap index (uint), 68..79 padding.
- Differences from the D3D9 chain: no RenderScale rect steps, and the separate level targets described in section 5.

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct result: a soft, wide glow around lamps, neon, fire, the sun and the bright sky, roughly even at all angles, that fades
  smoothly. At the default it is a faint bloom around bright sources.
- Typical wrong results:
  - A grey veil over the whole picture: Blending too high, or Spread too high.
  - Boxy or blocky glow edges: a wrong kernel or wrong level sizes.
  - Glints flickering on and off with the camera: AntiFlicker off with GlowBoost on.
  - A seam or missing area at the edges of a rect frame: RenderScale path (section 6).
  - Bloom not reaching the tonemap: the tonemap flag set to 0 by mistake.
- Cost class: multi-pass. The D3D9 chain is about 2N - 1 passes on a pyramid. Most of the cost is the first downsample at half
  resolution (13 reads of the full scene). The GlowBoost and LinearLight variant adds per-tap maths but no reads.

### 9. Open questions

Changes made in wave 3 (2026-11-02):
- FIXED: the RenderScale rect clamp is applied to every downsample and tent tap (clampToRectIfOn, as section 6 describes); before
  this the spec listed it but the rect-frame taps could reach outside the drawn part.
- FIXED: the glow threshold is floored at 0.002 before any division (as before), and negative values are clamped before the square
  root and the square (no NaN in the linear path).
- CHANGED (not a setting change): the linear-light conversion is a square / square root pair instead of the old cubic fit. The
  old fit was about 12 percent bright at 3. The new pair is exact as an inverse of itself, but it is not sRGB. The look of GlowBoost and LinearLight may shift a little: a tester
  check is needed.
- CHANGED (interpretation): PassBlending is the weight of the upsampled level in a blend with the level's own downsample (lerp
  own -> tent by PassBlending). The spec did not give the direction; the new reading keeps 0 as the add mode.
- CHANGED: in blend mode the last upsample applies no spread scale (the blend is already convex), as the spec's "or" implies.

Still open:
1. Read and write of the same texture (D3D9 only): the upsample reads its own level from the texture it writes. The C++ does not
   give a separate target for the D3D9 path (only the Vulkan path has one), so the shader could not avoid it without a C++ change.
   The old shader did the same; it is kept and reported. Whether it is well-defined on every D3D9 driver is not established.
2. Comment versus code in the tonemap shader (NVR-bloom branch): the glow is described as linear but the tonemap linearises it.
   Confirm the intent before a new tonemap is written.
3. RenderScale on the Vulkan chain has no rect steps. Whether a rect frame with the Vulkan chain shows a stale or stretched glow
   was not tested.
4. The tonemap path with the flag at 0 uses Blending and Strength with a different meaning. Whether that is intended for the game's
   own bloom is not stated in the code.
5. The blur radius of the upsample is in uv units at the default; whether it looks the same at every level was not measured.
6. Strength: the toml describes it for Vegas Shaders and vanilla; the code reaches the tonemap as a gain on the Vegas Shaders path and as a power-scaled gain
   on the game's path. Confirm the vanilla look against the toml wording.

## BloomLegacy

Source reviewed (wave 3, 2026-11-02): the new D3D9 effect src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/BloomLegacy.fx.hlsl (with the shared
Includes/RenderRect.hlsl); the new Vulkan passes src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/BloomLegacyBloom.frag, BloomLegacyBlur.frag and
BloomLegacyCombine.frag (branch claude/rw3-colour); C++ src/effects/BloomLegacy.cpp, BloomLegacy.h (unchanged); settings
resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml; frame order in src/core/ShaderManager.cpp.

### 1. Purpose

An older, alternative bloom that works on the finished (tonemapped) picture rather than the HDR scene. Bright areas are pulled out,
blurred in two directions, and added back over the picture, while the picture is darkened behind the glow. The result is a softer,
bleeding light look around bright lights, with a visible darkening of the surroundings of the glow. It is off by default. Its glow is
not read by the tonemapper and does not replace Bloom (the HDR glow); both can be on at once.

### 2. When it runs
