- Position: post-tonemap, in the weather block of RenderEffects (ShaderManager.cpp). The order is: rain, snow, GPU particles, then
  BloomLegacy, then heat haze, volumetric smoke, barrel haze, coloring and LUT, and so on.
- Enabled: [Shaders.BloomLegacy.Status] Enabled (bool, default false).
- Gates: [Main.Main] RenderEffects on, a player cell present, overlay off. No ShouldRender override.
- Interior and exterior: the section is chosen per frame. Exteriors section when outdoors (day and night share it), Interiors section
  otherwise. No transition blend: the switch is immediate.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures

| Slot | Name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the current picture (the output of the previous pass, or the picture for the bright pass) |
| s1 | TESR_SourceBuffer | the picture as it was before this effect ran (read by the combine pass) |

Both LINEAR, CLAMP, no mips.

#### Constants (registered by name, no fixed registers)

- TESR_BloomLegacyData: x Luminance, y MiddleGray, z WhiteCutOff (the current category).
- TESR_BloomLegacyValues: x BloomIntensity, y OriginalIntensity, z BloomSaturation, w OriginalSaturation.
- TESR_ReciprocalResolution: 1 / width and 1 / height of the render (texels).

### 4. Settings

[Shaders.BloomLegacy.Status]: Enabled (bool, default false).

[Shaders.BloomLegacy.Exteriors] and [Shaders.BloomLegacy.Interiors] (same keys; defaults in brackets). Unchanged:
- Luminance [0.06]: the bright pass multiplies the picture by MiddleGray / (Luminance + 0.001).
- MiddleGray [0.18]: the mid-grey level the bright pass maps to.
- WhiteCutOff [0.8]: the boost in the bright pass is (1 + v / WhiteCutOff^2); a smaller value boosts bright values more.
- BloomIntensity [1.4]: gain of the glow when added back.
- OriginalIntensity [1.0]: gain of the picture the glow is added to.
- BloomSaturation [1.2]: saturation of the glow (1 unchanged, 0 grey).
- OriginalSaturation [1.0]: saturation of the picture before the glow is added.

No night category.

### 5. Passes and outputs

One technique (index 0) with four passes. Each pass reads the current picture and writes into the frame chain; the last pass may write
the game target directly when it is the chain's final effect.

1. Bright pass. Per channel: v = c * MiddleGray / (Luminance + 0.001); v' = v * (1 + v / WhiteCutOff^2); output (v' - 5) / (10 + max(v', 0)).
   Negative for most of the picture (about -0.5 for a dark value), zero at v' = 5, approaching 1 for very bright values. Not clamped.
2. Horizontal blur. Reads the bright-pass result. A 13-tap separable blur along x (offsets -6..6 texels), weights a Gaussian of sigma 2.1
   texels, normalised by their own sum. The centre weight is about 0.19 and the outer taps about 0.003.
3. Vertical blur. The same kernel along y, reads pass 2.
4. Combine. Reads the blurred glow (the chain's current picture) and the source picture (TESR_SourceBuffer):
   - glow g = saturate(blurred), so negative blur values become 0 and values above 1 are capped at 1;
   - glow colour = lerp(luma(g), g, BloomSaturation) * BloomIntensity;
   - original colour = lerp(luma(o), o, OriginalSaturation) * OriginalIntensity, where o is the source pixel;
   - darkening = max(max(o, 1) - g, 0) per channel;
   - output = original colour * darkening + glow colour, alpha 1.

Output: the combined picture in the chain (or the game target), same size as the render, float format of the chain. Opaque.

### 6. Behaviour details

- Negative values are part of the blur on purpose: dark areas give negative values in pass 1, which the blur averages with the bright
  ones, so a dark surround lowers the glow near bright sources. Clamping before the blur would change the look.
- The darkening uses max(o, 1), so a picture above 1 is darkened by its own brightness; on a normal LDR picture it is 1 - g.
- No sky, depth, first-person or TAA-jitter handling.
- HDR vs LDR: the input is the tonemapped picture. Pass 1 has no upper clamp; the combine caps the glow at 1.
- NaN and infinity: pass 1 divides by (10 + max(v', 0)), at least 10. The Luminance term carries +0.001. WhiteCutOff is clamped to at
  least 0.001 before it is squared and divided by (wave 3 fix).
- RenderScale (rect frames, UNOFFICIAL): every blur tap is kept inside the drawn part (clampToRectIfOn with the full-frame texel), so a
  tap cannot read the stale part of the target (wave 3 fix; D3D9 only, see section 7).
- Enabling this with Bloom on adds the two glows; nothing sets one off when the other is on.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Choice: [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses (default true) with the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension. The effect's ExtRender runs the four passes
  when the flag is on and the effect has not failed. All pipelines are made before any pass is drawn, so a refused pipeline leaves the
  frame to the D3D9 technique. The Vulkan path needs technique 0 and the source buffer to be the chain's source surface.
- Files (src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute): BloomLegacyBloom.frag (pass 1); BloomLegacyBlur.frag (passes 2 and 3, direction from the push block);
  BloomLegacyCombine.frag (pass 4; binding 0 the blurred glow, binding 1 the source picture).
- Push blocks:
  - Bright pass: 0 TESR_BloomLegacyData (vec4); 16 sampler heap index (uint).
  - Blur pass: 0 TESR_ReciprocalResolution (vec4); 16 offset mask (vec2: (1, 0) or (0, 1)); 24 sampler heap index (uint).
  - Combine pass: 0 TESR_BloomLegacyValues (vec4); 16 sampler heap index (uint).
- The blur weights and the combine are the same maths as the D3D9 passes.
- No RenderScale rect steps on the Vulkan path.

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct result: bright lights bleed a soft, slightly saturated halo over nearby surfaces, and the surface right around the light is
  slightly darker than before. At the defaults the halo is noticeable but not a haze over the scene.
- Typical wrong results:
  - A grey or dark ring around bright lights: the darkening is too strong (OriginalIntensity or BloomIntensity mismatch).
  - A glow over dark areas: MiddleGray too high or Luminance too low.
  - Hard glow edges: the taps wrong (they must be one texel apart).
  - A glow that never reaches far: WhiteCutOff too high.
- Cost class: multi-pass at full resolution. Pass 1 is one read; passes 2 and 3 are 13 reads each; pass 4 is two reads. Likely the heavier
  of the two blooms per frame; not measured.

### 9. Open questions

Changes made in wave 3 (2026-11-02):
- FIXED: the blur taps are clamped to the drawn part in a rect frame (RenderScale).
- FIXED: WhiteCutOff is floored at 0.001 (no divide by zero).
- CHANGED (look may shift a little): the blur kernel is a normalised Gaussian of sigma 2.1 texels, not the old kernel's values. The spec's
  shape (symmetric, peak about 0.2, ends about 0.002, sum about one) is matched at the centre (0.19) and the ends are about 45 percent
  higher (0.0032); the exact old values
  were not copied. A tester look check is needed.

Still open:
1. Both Bloom and BloomLegacy can be enabled; no check stops it, and no note says what the intended combination is.
2. Night: there is no night section; the Exteriors values are used at night. This is by design in the code, but the toml does not say so.
3. Vulkan path: no rect steps (section 7). Not checked in a rect frame.

## Cinema

Rewritten 2026-11-02 (wave 3, branch claude/rw3-screen, commit 9f541fcc). New shaders: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Cinema.fx.hlsl (D3D9,
both techniques) and src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Cinema.frag (Vulkan, both techniques via push dither). The C++ is unchanged.
Changes against the old shader: the aspect of zero no longer divides by zero (section 9, item 3, FIXED); the dirt-lens term is removed
because the C++ never sets it, so it could never act (item 2, REMOVED); the sky dither uses the same pixel grid in both paths (item 8,
FIXED). Everything else is as described below.


### 1. Purpose

Cinema gives the picture a film look. It adds black letterbox bars at the top and bottom of the screen for a wide aspect ratio, a faint lens colour split (red and blue pulled slightly apart towards the edges), animated film grain at 24 frames a second, a slight overlay boost of colour and contrast, and a darkening vignette round the edges. The letterbox bars are optional by context: always, only outside conversations, only during conversations, or never. With the default settings (Mode 3) the player sees the grain, colour split, contrast and vignette all the time, and never the bars (Mode 3 was Oblivion's "only during persuasion"; New Vegas has no persuasion menu).

### 2. When it runs

- **Switch:** `[_Shaders.Cinema.Status] Enabled`, default **true**.
- **Frame position:** in the post chain after Sharpening and before ImageAdjust (`ShaderManager.cpp`, the Cinema block). It therefore takes Sharpening's output. It is in the chain's "last effect" candidate list.
- **Two techniques:** technique 0 (`Cinema`, the normal pass) and technique 1 (`CinemaSkyDither`, the same look with the sky dither added in the same pass). `ShaderManager` uses technique 1 only when all of these hold: Cinema and SkyDither both render this frame, ImageAdjust does not render, Bodycam does not render, and the loaded file has the `CinemaSkyDither` technique. Otherwise the SkyDither effect runs as its own pass, and Cinema uses technique 0.
- **Letterbox context (`Mode`):** the bars are shown only in the chosen game situations (see section 4). When the situation is not met the aspect is treated as 1 (no bars) for that frame, but the grain, colour split, overlay and vignette still apply.
- **Interior / exterior / night:** no separate rule. The same values apply everywhere. Letterbox bars follow `Mode`, not the cell type.
- **Menus:** no special rule; it runs whenever the post chain runs and the switch is on.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (slot order; Vegas Shaders binds samplers by declaration order)

| Slot | Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | `TESR_RenderedBuffer` | The picture arriving at Cinema (after Sharpening) | Linear, clamp |
| s1 | `TESR_DepthBuffer` | CombineDepth's combined depth: first channel = linear depth divided by far plane distance | Linear, clamp |
| s2 | `TESR_BlueNoiseSampler` | The game file `Effects\bluenoise256.dds`, a 256 x 256 blue-noise texture (from `ResourceName`) | **Point, wrap** (no filtering, repeats) |

On the Vulkan path, if the blue-noise file is not loaded the pass does not run and the D3D9 technique is used instead.

#### Constants

| Name | Components and meaning |
|---|---|
| `TESR_GameTime` | z = game time in seconds (used for the grain animation; multiplied by 25 to get the grain's frame count) |
| `TESR_ReciprocalResolution` | xy = 1 divided by picture width and height; z = width / height (the aspect of the picture, used for the letterbox size) |
| `TESR_CinemaData` | x = aspect ratio of the visible picture (1 = no bars); y = vignette radius (darkening starts at distance 1 minus this, see section 6); z = vignette darkness (how much the edge darkens); w = overlay strength (blend of the self-overlay, 0 = none) |
| `TESR_CinemaSettings` | x = dirt-lens opacity (**never set by the C++; always 0 in practice**, see section 9); y = film grain amount (scaled by 0.01 in the shader); z = chromatic aberration strength (in pixels at the edge); w = letterbox depth threshold (game units) |
| `TESR_SkyDitherData` | Used only by technique 1. x = dither step size (one 8-bit step, 1/255, typical); y = 1 for "sky only", 0 for all; z = frame offset for the dither pattern. Supplied from SkyDither's own constant, or zeros if SkyDither is absent. |
| `TESR_CameraData` | y = far plane distance in game units (used by the depth helper for the letterbox depth test) |

The D3D9 side registers `TESR_CinemaData` and `TESR_CinemaSettings` by name. The C++ fills `TESR_CinemaData` from `Constants.Data` and `TESR_CinemaSettings` from `Constants.Settings`.

### 4. Settings

All keys are read in `Cinema.cpp` (`UpdateSettings`).

| Section | Key | Type | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Main]` | `AspectRatio` | float | 2.35 | Width/height of the visible picture. The bars are sized so the picture has this ratio. 1 = no bars. Only applies when the context (Mode) allows bars. |
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Main]` | `Mode` | int | 3 | When bars show. See the table below. |
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Main]` | `VignetteRadius` | float | 0.6 | Sent as `TESR_CinemaData.y`. Darkening starts at distance (1 minus this value) from the centre (section 6). Larger = darkening starts nearer the centre. This is the reverse of how the toml comment reads (section 9). |
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Main]` | `VignetteDarkness` | float | 1.2 | Strength of the edge darkening. Sent as `.z`. 0 = none. Above 1 darkens the edges beyond black at the corners. |
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Main]` | `OverlayStrength` | float | 0.1 | Blend amount of the self-overlay (picture overlaid on itself to raise contrast and colour). Sent as `TESR_CinemaData.w`. 0 = none. |
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Main]` | `FilmGrainAmount` | float | 0.3 | Grain strength. Sent as `TESR_CinemaSettings.y`; the shader scales it by 0.01. 0 = none. |
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Main]` | `ChromaticAberration` | float | 1.0 | Colour split at the edges, in pixels. Sent as `TESR_CinemaSettings.z`. 0 = none. |
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Main]` | `LetterBoxDepth` | float | 0.0 | Depth threshold for the bars: only pixels farther than this (game units) are blacked out. Sent as `TESR_CinemaSettings.w`. 0 = every pixel with any depth is blacked out, so near objects such as a first-person weapon are covered too. Raising it keeps near objects (in the letterbox band) visible. |
| `[_Shaders.Cinema.Status]` | `Enabled` | bool | true | Turns the effect on or off. |

**`Mode` values (as the code reads them):**

| Mode | Bars are shown when |
|---|---|
| 0 | always (when AspectRatio is not 1) |
| 1 | not in a dialogue |
| 2 | only in a dialogue |
| 3 | never (the **default**; it was Oblivion's "only in persuasion", and New Vegas has no persuasion menu) |
| 4 | only in a dialogue (it was "in a dialogue or in persuasion") |

The toml comment lists only 0, 1 and 2. Modes 3 and 4 exist in the code (see section 9). Any other value falls into "always" for the bars in the code's chain of tests, but the spec should state only the five values above.

### 5. Passes and outputs

- **Technique 0 (`Cinema`, unnamed in the file):** one pass, full-screen, writes the picture with letterbox, colour split, grain, overlay and vignette applied. Output alpha is 1. Writes the chain's output target at the picture's resolution, 8-bit LDR.
- **Technique 1 (`CinemaSkyDither`):** the same pass, then the sky dither is added to the result (section 6). One pass.
- **Vulkan path:** the C++ asks for the Vulkan pass named `Cinema` for technique 0 and 1 (the push block selects the dither). Same output target.

### 6. Behaviour details

**Letterbox.** When the aspect is not 1, the bar height is calculated from the picture's aspect (from `TESR_ReciprocalResolution.z`, the render target's own width / height) and the wanted aspect: the bar takes the part of the height that the wanted ratio does not use, split evenly top and bottom. Pixels in the bar band are blacked out (alpha 1) only if their linear depth is greater than `LetterBoxDepth`. The depth read happens only when the aspect is not 1 (a branch), so normal gameplay with bars off pays nothing for it.

- The bars use the render target's aspect, not a render rectangle's. In a RenderScale frame this may be wrong (section 9).

**Colour split (chromatic aberration).** The picture is sampled three times. Red is taken from a position moved slightly towards the screen centre, blue from a position moved slightly away from it; green is not moved. The offset grows with distance from the centre and is `ChromaticAberration` pixels at the edge (the offset is the reciprocal resolution times the strength, scaled by the distance from centre). Nothing happens at the exact centre.

**Film grain.** Two samples of the blue-noise texture are taken at positions moved by the animated time (game time x 25, so the grain moves at 24 frames a second when the game time runs in seconds), and the product of their two channels is turned into a value near 0.5. That value (scaled by grain amount) drives a standard overlay blend of the grain onto the picture, so the grain brightens and darkens without changing the average brightness much. The grain is stronger where the picture is dark (the overlay blend does this). Point filtering and wrap addressing on the noise texture keep the grain sharp and repeating.

**Overlay.** The picture is blended with itself by an overlay blend and mixed back in by the overlay strength. This raises contrast and colour saturation a little.

**Vignette.** The distance from the centre is measured on a scale where the middle of each screen edge is 0.5 and the corners are about 0.71. A smooth falloff (smoothstep) gives a value of 1 up to the distance `1 - VignetteRadius` and falls to 0 at distance 1. The colour is multiplied by `1 - darkness x (1 - falloff)`: no change where the falloff is 1, darkening by up to the darkness setting where it is 0. With darkness above 1 the multiplier goes below zero at the far edge and the colour clips to black there. With the defaults (radius 0.6, darkness 1.2) darkening starts at distance 0.4, so the middle of the screen edges is already partly darkened.

**Sky dither (technique 1 only).** After the colour is computed, pixels whose depth is "sky" are dithered: if the sky-only flag is set and the raw depth (not linear) is below 0.996 (a non-sky pixel), the pixel is left alone. Otherwise the pixel gets a noise value in the range of about one 8-bit step (the x value of the dither constant), built from two interleaved gradient noise samples at the pixel position plus the frame offset, minus 1. The noise removes banding in the sky gradient.

**First-person weapon.** No special handling. The weapon is near, so it falls outside the bar band only if its depth is at or below `LetterBoxDepth`, and it is affected by grain, colour split and vignette like everything else. This is the current behaviour.

**Sky and depth.** Only the letterbox and the sky dither read depth. The sky is treated as depth at the far end (raw value near 1), which is how the dither decides "sky".

**RenderScale / RenderRect frames.** The C++ supplies the full-target size. The Vulkan path is off in render-rectangle frames (the same as Sharpening), so the D3D9 path runs. The letterbox and vignette use the full picture.

**Jitter (TAA).** No handling; Cinema runs on the displayed picture.

**Reversed depth.** Not relevant: depth is Vegas Shaders' linear combined depth, and sky is recognised by the stored value near far.

**HDR vs LDR.** LDR only, 8-bit chain picture. Overlay and grain are applied in display space.

**NaN and inf.** The grain and overlay are bounded. Vignette and letterbox use clamped values. The letterbox's division by the wanted aspect is safe only while the aspect is not 0; the C++ passes the setting through, so an aspect of 0 gives a division by zero (see section 9).

**Things that would look wrong if missed:** bars that cover the first-person weapon when `LetterBoxDepth` is 0 (may be intended); grain that does not animate at 24 fps (the time factor); a colour split that goes the wrong way (red inward, blue outward); sky banding if technique 1's dither is left out; the bars appearing during normal play when `Mode` is 0 (intended only for "always").

### 7. Vulkan path

- **Files:** `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Cinema.frag` (SPIR-V `Cinema.spv` in the same folder).
- **Selection:** `CinemaEffect::ExtRender` is used only when Vegas Shaders DXVK is loaded, `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses` is on, and the frame is not a render-rectangle frame. Technique index 0 or 1 is handled; any other index returns "not drawn". If the pass fails, the D3D9 technique runs for the session.
- **Bindings (descriptor set 1):** binding 0 = picture (`t_rendered`), binding 1 = combined depth (`t_depth`), binding 2 = blue-noise texture (`t_noise`).
- **Samplers:** the picture and depth use the linear clamp sampler, taken from the push block at offset 96. The blue-noise texture uses the point wrap sampler, taken from the push block at offset 100.
- **Push constants (112 bytes):**

| Offset | Type | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | vec4 | `TESR_CinemaData` |
| 16 | vec4 | `TESR_CinemaSettings` |
| 32 | vec4 | `TESR_GameTime` |
| 48 | vec4 | `TESR_ReciprocalResolution` |
| 64 | vec4 | `TESR_CameraData` (y far plane distance) |
| 80 | vec4 | `TESR_SkyDitherData` |
| 96 | uint | linear clamp sampler index |
| 100 | uint | point wrap sampler index (noise) |
| 104 | uint | dither: 0 = technique 0, 1 = technique 1 |
| 108 to 111 | uint | padding |

- The Vulkan shader matches the D3D9 shader except for the sky dither pixel position, which uses the framebuffer pixel coordinate rather than the screen position register.

### 8. Look and cost

- **Correct:** a clear film feel. Bars at the top and bottom at the wanted ratio when the chosen context is on, otherwise the full picture. Grain fine and moving slowly at a film rate, not crawling or flickering per frame. A small colour fringe at the edges, red and blue apart, green in the middle. Corners a little darker. No bars over conversation menus unless the chosen Mode says so. The sky shows smooth gradients with no banding.
- **Typical wrong result:** bars at the wrong ratio or on the wrong axis; bars showing in normal play under Mode 3 (should not); a colour fringe that is strong in the centre; grain much too coarse or moving at the frame rate instead of 24 fps; banding in the sky when the sky dither is missing; black corners when the darkness setting is above 1 (may be wanted).
- **Cost class:** single full-screen pass. Three colour reads, two noise reads, one overlay, cheap arithmetic. The depth read happens only when the letterbox is on. Technique 1 adds one depth read. Cheap.

### 9. Open questions

1. **Mode 3 and 4 are missing from the toml.** Since the persuasion tests were removed (2026-11-05), Mode 3 (the default) means "never" and Mode 4 the same as 2; New Vegas never had a persuasion menu, so neither changed on screen. The toml describes only 0, 1 and 2. Decide whether the default should stay 3 and update the comment.
2. **Dirt lens (REMOVED 2026-11-02).** The shader term read TESR_CinemaSettings.x, which the C++ never sets, so it was always zero. The new shader does not read it; the constant stays registered with x unused.
3. **Aspect of zero (FIXED 2026-11-02).** An aspect of zero (or below) now means no bars, instead of dividing by zero. The valid range (the toml gives none) is still not stated.
4. **Letterbox in RenderScale frames.** The bars use the render target's aspect (`TESR_ReciprocalResolution.z`). Confirm that is right when a render rectangle is active.
5. **Ranges not stated** for `VignetteRadius`, `VignetteDarkness`, `OverlayStrength`, `FilmGrainAmount`, `ChromaticAberration`, `LetterBoxDepth`. Only `AspectRatio` and `Mode` have a description. Ranges should be written in the spec once known.
5a. **VignetteRadius direction.** The toml comment says it is "distance to the centre for the start of the Vignette effect", which reads as: larger = darkening starts farther out. The code does the reverse (larger = darkening starts nearer the centre). Decide which is wanted, then fix the code or the comment.
6. **Grain timing.** The shader multiplies game time by 25 to get the grain's frame count. Confirm the intended grain rate (the comment says 24 fps) and whether game time is in seconds.
7. **Sky dither threshold.** The raw depth threshold of 0.996 is a hard number in the source. Confirm it is the intended sky test for the sky dither, especially at the far plane settings the user runs.
8. **Vulkan dither pixel position (FIXED 2026-11-02).** Both paths take the pixel as floor(uv x size), so the grid is the same.

## Coloring

Source reviewed (wave 3, 2026-11-02): the new D3D9 effect src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Coloring.fx.hlsl; the new Vulkan pass
src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Coloring.frag (one file for both techniques) (branch claude/rw3-colour); C++ src/effects/Coloring.cpp,
Coloring.h, LUT.h (unchanged); settings in resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml and the section reader in
src/core/SettingManager.cpp; the frame order and the combined-LUT choice in src/core/ShaderManager.cpp (line ~2826).

### 1. Purpose

Coloring is the colour grade of the picture: the overall look of a place (saturation, contrast, gamma, a bleach-bypass style
overlay, colour curves and a split tone between shadows and highlights). It works on the tonemapped picture. Each cell, or
worldspace, can have its own grade through a settings section, with a Default fallback. When the LUT effect is also running,
Coloring can apply the LUT in the same pass.

### 2. When it runs

- Position: post-tonemap, in the screen-space colour block of RenderEffects (ShaderManager.cpp). The order is: rain, snow,
  BloomLegacy, heat haze, volumetric smoke, barrel haze, then Coloring, then LUT (unless the LUT is combined into Coloring or runs
  before tonemapping), then depth of field, motion blur, lens effects, DitherBuster, SMAA, FXAA, sharpening, cinema, image adjust,
  bodycam, sky dither and debug.
- Enabled: [Shaders.Coloring.Status] Enabled (bool, default false).
- Gates: [Main.Main] RenderEffects on, a player cell present, overlay off. No ShouldRender override.
- Combined with LUT: the C++ asks for technique 1 (ColoringLUT, looked up by name in ShaderManager.cpp) when Coloring and LUT both
  render this frame and the LUT is not set to run before tonemapping. In that case the LUT is not run as its own pass.
- Per-location settings (the section is chosen per frame, unchanged): the section named after the player's current cell editor name;
  when outdoors only, the worldspace editor name; then the section named Default. Any [Shaders.Coloring.<name>] section counts except
  Status.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures

| Slot | Name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the current picture (tonemapped) |
| s1 | TESR_LUTDayBuffer | day LUT (technique 1 only) |
| s2 | TESR_LUTNightBuffer | night LUT (technique 1 only) |
| s3 | TESR_LUTInteriorBuffer | interior LUT (technique 1 only) |

All LINEAR, CLAMP, no mips.

#### Shader constants (registered by name; no fixed registers)

| Name | Components |
|---|---|
| TESR_ColoringColorCurve | x ColorCurve (the whole grade's S-curve); y, z, w ColorCurveR, G, B (the tone mask's S-curves) |
| TESR_ColoringEffectGamma | x EffectGamma (tone mask); y, z, w EffectGammaR, G, B |
| TESR_ColoringData | x Strength; y BaseGamma; z Fade; w Contrast |
| TESR_ColoringValues | x Saturation; y Bleach; z BleachLuma; w Linearization |
| TESR_LUTData, TESR_LUTBlend | technique 1 only: the LUT effect's values (see the LUT spec, section 3) |

### 4. Settings

All keys are floats under [Shaders.Coloring.<section>]. Defaults are the [Shaders.Coloring.Default] values. Unchanged.

- Strength (0.5), BaseGamma (1.6), Fade (0.0), Contrast (0.8), Saturation (-0.1), Bleach (0.1), BleachLuma (0.2), ColorCurve (1.2),
  ColorCurveR (0.9), ColorCurveG (1.0), ColorCurveB (1.0), Linearization (1.1), EffectGamma (0.9), EffectGammaR, G, B (1.0).
- [Shaders.Coloring.Status] Enabled (bool, default false).

### 5. Passes and outputs

Two techniques, one pass each, both full-screen quads, writing the frame chain's target (or the game target when final).

- Technique 0 (Coloring; the C++ asks for index 0): the grade of TESR_RenderedBuffer. LDR output, alpha 1.
- Technique 1 (ColoringLUT; the C++ looks it up by name): the same grade, then the LUT grade (day, night, interior, strength,
  HDR compatibility) in the same pass, reading slots s1 to s3.

### 6. Behaviour details

The grade (ten steps, in order). The old spec gave these steps in words only; the formulas below are the new shader's (wave 3):

1. Overlay: the picture is blended toward Overlay(picture, Rec.709 luma of the picture) (the standard overlay: dark base 2 b l, light
   base 1 - 2 (1 - b)(1 - l)). The blend weight is BleachLuma times smoothstep(0.45, 1, luma). The overlay result is capped at 65504.
2. Linearization: sign-preserving power by Linearization.
3. Contrast: the picture is blended toward a floor of 0.01 by Contrast (Contrast 1 is unchanged).
4. Tone mask: m = max(dot(c, (-0.9689, 1.8758, 0.0415)), 0) (the green row of the XYZ-to-RGB matrix; the spec's "not Rec.709" luminance).
   Per channel m_c = SCurve(m, ColorCurve_c), then m^(1/EffectGamma), then m -> lerp(m, 1 - m, Bleach), then m^(1/EffectGamma_c). Values
   are clamped to 0..1 before the inverse.
   SCurve(x, k) = (s(x) - y0) / (y1 - y0) with s(x) = 1 / (1 + exp(-k (x - 0.5))), y0 = s(0), y1 = s(1). It is the identity at zero
   steepness (|k| below 0.001), which avoids the 0 / 0 of the normaliser.
5. BaseGamma: sign-preserving power by 1 / BaseGamma. The picture is kept as beforeSplit for step 10.
6. Split tone: for each channel, a value below the mask 0.5 moves toward its square, one above toward its square root, by an amount that
   grows with the distance of the mask from 0.5 (linear: amount = 2 |m - 0.5|, clamped). Sign is kept. Blended over the picture by Strength.
7. The whole-grade S-curve (ColorCurve) per channel.
8. Channel mixing: Saturation pulls each channel toward the mean of the three (lerp to the mean by Saturation; negative values separate the
   channels). Fade then mixes green and blue toward their mean by Fade (clamped 0..1).
9. Second split tone with the mask set to the Rec.709 luma of the mixed picture, then the sign-preserving power 1 / Linearization.
10. Output: beforeSplit (step 5) blended toward the step 9 result by Strength. Not clamped.

Edge cases the shader handles:
- ColorCurve and the channel curves at zero: identity (the NaN bug of the old grade, fixed in the old code and kept fixed here).
- Gamma settings (EffectGamma, EffectGammaRGB, BaseGamma, Linearization) are clamped to at least 0.01 before a division or a power, so a
  zero setting cannot divide by zero (wave 3 fix).
- Negative values: powers and the split tone keep the sign. The tone mask uses max(0).
- The overlay result is capped at 65504 before the blend.
- Output is not clamped at the end. Values outside 0..1 are passed to the next stage.

Other behaviour:
- Per-location: the grade changes instantly on entering or leaving a cell or worldspace. No blend between sections.
- Sky, depth, first-person weapon, TAA jitter: not handled.
- HDR vs LDR: the tonemapped (LDR) picture.
- RenderScale (rect frames): no rect handling (the grade is per pixel; there are no neighbour reads).
- Technique 1 relies on the LUT effect's constants and textures being set for the frame.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Choice: [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses (default true) with the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension. Both techniques use the same file, and the
  push flag lutPass selects the LUT (1 for ColoringLUT). If the pass is refused, the D3D9 technique runs for the session.
- File: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Coloring.frag (the same maths as the D3D9 effect, line for line).
- Bindings (set 1): binding 0 = the picture; bindings 1, 2, 3 = the day, night and interior LUT textures, read only when lutPass is 1.
  Missing LUT textures are bound to the picture.
- One LINEAR CLAMP sampler, its heap index in the push block.
- Push block (104 bytes): 0 TESR_ColoringColorCurve; 16 TESR_ColoringEffectGamma; 32 TESR_ColoringData; 48 TESR_ColoringValues; 64
  TESR_LUTData; 80 TESR_LUTBlend; 96 sampler heap index (uint); 100 lutPass (uint: 1 = ColoringLUT, 0 = grade alone).

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct result: a subtle grade of the whole scene: slightly desaturated at the default, slightly lower contrast, softened shadows and
  highlights. Strength 0 gives the original picture exactly.
- Typical wrong results:
  - A flat, grey or washed look: Contrast or Saturation in the wrong direction, or Bleach too high.
  - Black or NaN pixels: a zero curve or gamma (guarded in wave 3).
  - Banding in shadows: Linearization or BaseGamma too far from 1 for an 8-bit output.
  - Strong colour shifts between two places: a missing per-location section, so Default applies.
- Cost class: single pass, ALU-heavy (several exponentials and powers per pixel); technique 1 adds three LUT samples and their strip
  maths. No extra render targets.

### 9. Open questions

Changes made in wave 3 (2026-11-02): the formulas of steps 1, 4, 6, 8 and 9 are the new shader's choices, because the old spec gave
them in words only. The look is therefore re-derived. A tester look check is needed before this replaces the old grade. The changed
choices are:
- Overlay: standard overlay of the picture with its luma (weight BleachLuma times a smoothstep above 0.45).
- S-curve: the normalised sigmoid in section 6, step 4.
- Split tone: the amount is linear in the mask's distance from 0.5.
- Fade: green and blue are moved toward their mean (not swapped).
- FIXED: divide-by-zero guards on the gamma settings.

Still open:
1. Output range: the grade is not clamped; the next stage decides. Not checked.
2. Per-location sections: the table is read when settings load. Whether a section added while playing is picked up without a restart was
   not checked.
3. Interior cells with no matching section do not try the worldspace name (by the code). Not checked against intent.
4. Two luma definitions are used: Rec.709 for the overlay and the XYZ green row for the tone mask. Both kept, from the old spec.
5. Technique 1 needs the LUT's constants and textures set for the frame. The order of the LUT update relative to the Coloring pass is in
   ShaderManager.cpp and was not traced again here.
6. Strength is applied twice (steps 6 and 10). Kept; whether the double use is intended was not established.
7. The toml section [Shaders.Coloring.Status] has no description for Enabled.

## CombineDepth

Source of this spec: the rewritten shader (wave 3 buffers, branch claude/rw3-buffers): src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/CombineDepth.fx.hlsl (techniques CombineDepth and DepthNormals), src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/CombineDepth.frag (Vulkan separate pass). The C++ (src/effects/CombineDepth.cpp) is unchanged. Written from the previous spec and the C++; the old shader was not read.

### 1. Purpose

CombineDepth makes the picture's depth buffer that almost every Vegas Shaders effect reads. The game's own depth (the world, and the first-person weapon and hands drawn in a separate depth pass) is turned into one linear depth per pixel, in game units, so that the effects can measure distances and rebuild positions without caring which pass the surface came from. The player sees nothing directly: its quality is what makes the other effects' results right (shadows, ambient occlusion, reflections, fog, depth of field and so on).

### 2. When it runs

- Every frame where Vegas Shaders' effects run: first step of the depth and normals stage in RenderEffectsPreTonemapping, before any other Vegas Shaders post-process.
- Enabled: no settings section; on whenever the effect loaded. ShouldRender is the default (true).
- Two ways it runs:
  - Separate (the default, MergedDepthNormals off): technique CombineDepth renders the depth target alone; then Normals renders the normals target from it.
  - Merged (MergedDepthNormals on, the normals would run, no ReShade depth binding kept): technique DepthNormals writes the depth and the normals in one draw. Falls back to separate passes if the merged path is not allowed or fails, or if its one-off check finds the draw was dropped.
- RenderScale v2: reads are clamped to the drawn part (section 6).
- Interiors, exteriors, night: the same.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (bound by name, not written by the effect)

- TESR_DepthBufferWorld (stage 0, POINT, CLAMP): the world depth, INTZ raw values (reversed in FNV).
- TESR_DepthBufferViewModel (stage 1, POINT, CLAMP): the first-person depth; 0 where nothing was drawn.

#### Constants

- TESR_DepthConstants: x = view-model near plane (JohnnyGuitar clip distance when above 0, at least 0.3; else the camera near plane); z = 1 when the depth is reversed, else 0.
- TESR_CameraData: x = world near plane, y = far plane.
- TESR_ReciprocalResolution: 1 / the full frame size (the rect clamp's texel, and the merged path's neighbour step).
- TESR_InvProjectionTransform: the merged path's view-space positions.
- TESR_ProjectionTransform: declared, not read.
- TESR_RenderScale (RenderRect include): the drawn fraction, for the read clamp.

### 4. Settings

Unchanged:
- [Main.Main.Performance] SlimDepthBuffer (bool, true; UNOFFICIAL): R32F target (default) instead of G32R32F. Read at target creation; restart to change.
- [Main.Main.Performance] MergedDepthNormals (bool, false; UNOFFICIAL, experimental): the merged draw (the C++ key is DisableMergedNormals, its inverse).
- [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses (bool, true): the Vulkan passes.

### 5. Passes and outputs

#### Separate pass (technique CombineDepth, index 0, one pass, PS CombineDepthPS)

Target: the full-frame depth target. Format R32F (SlimDepthBuffer) or G32R32F.

Output, per pixel (what every reader depends on):
- R = viewZ / farZ: viewZ is the view-space distance along the camera's forward axis in game units. Readers multiply by far to get game units.
- G (only with G32R32F; always written) = the reversed-Z post-projection depth from viewZ with the world near plane: near x (far - viewZ) / (viewZ x (far - near)). Readers recompute it from R, so G is redundant.
- B = 1, A = 1.

Conversion of a raw depth d to viewZ, with near and far (the view model uses its own near plane):
- Reversed (TESR_DepthConstants.z > 0.5, FNV): viewZ = near x far / (near + d x (far - near)). d = 1 gives near, d = 0 gives far.
- Standard (z = 0): viewZ = near x far / (far - d x (far - near)). d = 0 gives near, d = 1 gives far.
- The world depth is used unless the view-model depth is above 0; then the view-model depth is converted with the view-model near plane (no blending: the weapon's edge is a hard edge).
- Sky and nothing drawn: d at the clear value, so viewZ = far and R = 1.

#### Merged pass (technique DepthNormals, one pass, PS DepthNormalsPS, two targets)

- COLOR0 = the depth target, same encoding as above.
- COLOR1 = the normals target, same encoding as the Normals spec (n x 0.5 + 0.5, view space, +z away from the camera, the Normals spec's neighbour rule).
- The neighbour depths are recomputed from the two raw depth textures (CombinedViewZ), at one and two texels to each side, clamped to the drawn part.

### 6. Behaviour details

- Reversed depth: the flag (TESR_DepthConstants.z) now selects the conversion (section 5). With FNV's reversed buffer the output is the same as before; with a non-reversed buffer the output is now correct instead of wrong.
- Guards: the viewZ conversion is floored at 1e-6, the view-model near plane at 1e-4 (no division by zero). A raw depth of 0 (reversed) is the far plane, R = 1.
- RenderScale v2: reads are clamped to the drawn part with the shared rect clamp (clampToRectIfOn). Output is written across the target; the viewport limits the drawn part.
- Merged normals of a degenerate neighbourhood (zero-length cross product, e.g. at a flat edge clamp) return (0, 0, -1), facing the camera. No NaN.
- Depth is point sampled; no mips.
- The merged path's one-off check, the failure flag and the ReShade rule are C++ behaviour, unchanged.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Separate pass: Compute\CombineDepth.frag (technique 0 line for line): set 1 binding 0 = world depth, binding 1 = view-model depth, POINT CLAMP heap sampler at push offset 32; push offset 0 = depth constants, offset 16 = camera data. Same encoding. No RenderScale clamp (the push block has no rect data; open, see section 9).
- Merged pass: Compute\DepthNormals.frag. The file is kept from before wave 3 (not in the removed list) and includes Compute\Normals.glsl for its maths. The new Normals.glsl does not provide the function it calls (viewNormalFromTaps), so DepthNormals.frag does not compile now. Until it is rewritten, the C++ merged Vulkan path fails and the D3D9 merged path (or separate passes) runs.

### 8. Look and cost

Correct: no visible output of its own. Smooth distances, no weapon halo in depth-based effects, sky counted as far.

Typical wrong: a dark outline or halo round the weapon in SSAO, shadows or fog (view-model near plane or convention mismatch); sky counted as near (wrong convention); flicker on edges (a depth test on a filtered texture).

Cost: one full-screen pass (separate) or one pass with two targets (merged). A few depth reads per pixel.

### 9. Open questions

1. Whether FNV can run with a non-reversed buffer is still unconfirmed (the stock game is assumed to clear to 0). The shader now handles both, so the question no longer changes the output.
2. Whether any reader uses the G channel directly (the shared depth helper reads R only).
3. The view-model depth at a different size or filter (RenderScale, DLSS) is not checked.
4. The JohnnyGuitar clip distance when absent is assumed to be 0.
5. Vulkan separate pass has no RenderScale clamp; add it if rect frames show stale depth on the Vulkan path.
6. DepthNormals.frag (kept file) must be rewritten against the new Normals.glsl, or the Normals.glsl function it calls restored. Until then the batch compile-compute.sh run stops at it.
7. SlimDepthBuffer's restart requirement is as the previous spec wrote it; not re-verified in the settings loader.

## Debug

Rewritten 2026-11-02 (wave 3, branch claude/rw3-screen, commit a47cd42b). New shader: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Debug.fx.hlsl (D3D9 only, two
techniques: the overlay (unnamed) and ClipView). The C++ is unchanged. There is still no Vulkan pass for this effect.
FIXED: a light with radius zero gives no glow instead of dividing by zero (section 6, NaN and inf).
REMOVED: TESR_ShadowRadius (declared, never read) is no longer declared (section 9, item 2).
NEW OPEN (from the rewrite): the average-luminance swatch reads the red channel of the average-luma buffer; the spec does not name
the channel (DepthOfField reads blue for focus), so this is a guess to confirm. The light marker keeps a constant angular size
(cos above 1 - 4e-6); the spec phrase "closer for lights far away" is not implemented as a size rule.


Two separate things live in this effect and must be specified separately:
- **The Debug overlay** (the main technique, switch `[_Shaders.Debug.Status] Enabled`): diagnostic panels drawn over the picture.
- **The clipping view** (the `ClipView` technique, switch `[_Shaders.Debug.Main] ClipView`): marks clipped colour channels. It works even when the overlay is off.

### 1. Purpose

Debug is a developer and fault-finding tool. With the overlay on, the player (or tester) sees a row of small panels across the top and a column of colour swatches down the right side: the normals buffer, the depth buffer, the shadow buffer, the bloom buffer, the SMAA edge and blend buffers, the sun, ambient, fog, horizon, sky and water colours, the average brightness, and the sun and light directions. Light sources are marked on the picture as coloured spots and glows. With the clipping view on, pixels that have lost colour to a clipped (full-brightness) channel are marked with stripes, so the tester can see where detail or colour is lost and whether the tonemapper or a colour effect caused it. Neither is meant for normal play; both are off by default.

### 2. When it runs

- **Overlay switch:** `[_Shaders.Debug.Status] Enabled`, default **false**. When off, the overlay technique is not drawn.
- **Clipping view:** `[_Shaders.Debug.Main] ClipView`, default 0. Values 1, 2 and 3 turn on a mode (section 6). The view is drawn whether or not the overlay is on. Changes apply immediately.
- **Average brightness dependency:** when the overlay is on, `ShaderManager` also forces the screen's average-luminance measurement on (it is needed for the average-luma swatch). That is a side effect the new code must keep.
- **Frame position:**
  1. At the start of the post chain (straight out of the tonemapper, before the first post effect), the C++ `CaptureClipStart` copies the game target into a helper texture, but only when ClipView is 2 or 3 (section 6).
  2. Late in the chain, after Coloring, the LUT, Cinema, ImageAdjust, Bodycam and SkyDither (and the sun-depth debug view, off by default), the overlay (technique 0) is drawn into the chain target.
  3. Immediately after the overlay, the clipping view (technique `ClipView`) is drawn into the same target. When the clipping view is active, Debug is named as the chain's last effect so its output writes the game target directly (the clipping view then covers everything, as the last layer).
- **Menus and interiors:** no special rule. The panels draw wherever the post chain runs.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (slot order; Vegas Shaders binds samplers by declaration order)

| Slot | Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | `TESR_RenderedBuffer` | The picture at the Debug step | Linear, clamp |
| s1 | `TESR_SourceBuffer` | The chain's source buffer (declared; the overlay does not read it) | Linear, clamp |
| s2 | `TESR_DepthBuffer` | CombineDepth's combined depth (linear depth over far distance, first channel) | Linear, clamp |
| s3 | `TESR_NormalsBuffer` | The view-space normals buffer | Point, clamp |
| s4 | `TESR_AvgLumaBuffer` | The average-luminance buffer (read at its centre for the average swatch) | Point, clamp |
| s5 | `TESR_PointShadowBuffer` | The point-light shadow buffer (red and green channels read) | Point, clamp |
| s6 | `TESR_BloomBuffer` | The bloom buffer | Point, clamp |
| s7 | `TESR_SMAA_Edges` | SMAA's edge buffer | Linear, clamp |
| s8 | `TESR_SMAA_Blend` | SMAA's blend-weight buffer | Linear, clamp |
| s9 | `TESR_ClipStartBuffer` | Clipping view only: a copy of the picture as it came out of the tonemapper (see section 6). Declared last, so it stays last. | Point, clamp |

The overlay's samplers s0 to s8 must stay in this declaration order; the C++ binds by it. The clipping-view sampler at s9 is registered by name and created on first use (section 6).

#### Constants

| Name | Components and meaning |
|---|---|
| `TESR_SunAmbient` | Sun ambient colour (rgb) |
| `TESR_SunColor` | Sun colour (rgb) |
| `TESR_FogColor` | Fog colour (rgb) |
| `TESR_WaterShallowColor` | Water shallow colour used by the game |
| `TESR_WaterDeepColor` | Water deep colour used by the game |
| `TESR_HorizonColor` | Horizon colour (rgb) |
| `TESR_SkyColor` | Sky colour (rgb) |
| `TESR_ViewSpaceLightDir` | Light direction in view space (xyz) |
| `TESR_SunDirection` | Sun direction (xyz) |
| `TESR_DebugVar` | The four developer values from `[Main.Develop.Main]` (`DebugVar1` to `DebugVar4`, x y z w). **Declared, but the overlay does not read it.** Kept for shader experiments. |
| `TESR_ShadowRadius` | Declared; the code that used it is commented out. Not read. |
| `TESR_LightPosition[12]` | Twelve light positions: xyz = world position, w = light radius |
| `TESR_ShadowLightPosition[12]` | Twelve shadow-casting light positions, same layout |
| `TESR_ClipView` | x = clipping view mode (1, 2 or 3; 0 is never drawn) |
| `TESR_ReciprocalResolution` | xy = 1 divided by picture size (used by the clipping view) |
| `TESR_CameraPosition`, `TESR_CameraData` | Used through the helpers for world-position reconstruction and far-distance scaling |

The C++ fills the colour and light constants from the game through the shared registrations; this effect's own C++ registers only `TESR_DebugVar` and `TESR_ClipView`.

### 4. Settings

| Section | Key | Type | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[_Shaders.Debug.Status]` | `Enabled` | bool | false | Turns the overlay on or off. |
| `[_Shaders.Debug.Main]` | `ClipView` | int | 0 | Clipping view mode: 0 off, 1 finished picture, 2 tonemapper output, 3 who clipped it. Any value outside 0 to 3 is treated as 0. |
| `[_Main.Develop.Main]` | `DebugVar1`, `DebugVar2`, `DebugVar3`, `DebugVar4` | float | 0.0 | Developer values sent as `TESR_DebugVar`. Not read by the overlay (section 3). |

The `Main.Develop` section also holds unrelated developer keys (debug mode, shader trace, GPU labels, RenderDoc key). They are not used by this effect.

### 5. Passes and outputs

- **Overlay (technique 0, unnamed):** one full-screen pass drawing the picture with the panels and markers on top. Writes the chain target, 8-bit LDR, output alpha 1.
- **Clipping view (technique `ClipView`):** one full-screen pass. Writes the chain target. It is drawn by `RenderClipView` after the overlay. That call switches the effect on for that one call, so the clipping view does not need the overlay switch.
- The overlay and clipping view both use the D3D9 path only (see section 7).

### 6. Behaviour details

**Overlay layout (UV space, origin top-left).** Each panel shows its source buffer scaled into a box; pixels outside the box are left as the picture.

| Panel | Box (x, y) | Size | Shows |
|---|---|---|---|
| Normals | 0.10, 0.05 | 0.15 x 0.15 | Normals buffer, as stored |
| Depth | 0.30, 0.05 | 0.15 x 0.15 | Depth as a grey value: linear depth divided by far distance, raised to the power 0.3 (near = dark, far = light) |
| Shadows | 0.50, 0.05 | 0.15 x 0.15 | Point shadow buffer: red x 0.5 plus green |
| Bloom | 0.70, 0.05 | 0.15 x 0.15 | Bloom buffer, as stored |
| SMAA edges | 0.10, 0.25 | 0.15 x 0.15 | SMAA edge buffer |
| SMAA blend | 0.30, 0.25 | 0.15 x 0.15 | SMAA blend buffer |

**Colour swatches (right edge, x 0.90 to 0.95).** Each band is a solid colour, the raw value of one constant. These are not gamma-corrected; the value is written as it is.

| y band | Shows |
|---|---|
| 0.15 to 0.20 | Sun colour |
| 0.20 to 0.25 | Sun ambient |
| 0.25 to 0.30 | Fog colour |
| 0.30 to 0.35 | Horizon colour |
| 0.35 to 0.40 | Sky colour |
| 0.40 to 0.45 | Water shallow colour |
| 0.45 to 0.50 | Water deep colour |
| 0.55 to 0.60 | Average luminance (sampled at the centre of the average-luma buffer), as grey |
| 0.65 to 0.70 | View-space light direction, as a colour (values outside 0 to 1 clip on write) |
| 0.70 to 0.75 | Sun direction, same rule |

**Light markers.** Each light is reconstructed in the world from the depth of each pixel. For each light the shader does two things:
- If the view direction from the camera points at the light (within a very small angle, closer for lights far away), the pixel is painted solid in the marker colour. This marks the light's position.
- Otherwise the pixel gets a soft glow round the light's world position, scaled by the light's radius (its w value) and faded to nothing by a rational falloff, added at one fifth of the marker colour.

The 12 shadow-casting lights (`TESR_ShadowLightPosition`) use green for the first seven and blue for the rest. The 12 ordinary lights (`TESR_LightPosition`) use red. Lights are drawn in that order, so red sits above green and blue.

**Depth from the overlay.** The depth panel and the light markers read depth from CombineDepth; sky pixels are at the far end and show as light grey in the depth panel.

**Clipping view: what counts as clipped.** The post chain's buffers are 8 bits per channel, so a channel is clipped when it reaches 254.5 / 255 or above. A pixel with one or two clipped channels has its colour shifted (for example orange turns yellow); a pixel with all three clipped is burnt to white.

**Clipping view: output.** Unmarked pixels are shown dimmed: 25 percent colour and 75 percent luma, times 0.4 (dark and nearly grey). Marked pixels are drawn in a flat colour with a diagonal stripe: pixels whose position sum (x + y, in whole pixels) falls in the first half of each 8-pixel period take the full colour, the rest take 60 percent of it.

| Mode | Marked as |
|---|---|
| 1 (finished picture) | Magenta where one or two channels are clipped in the final picture; cyan where all three are clipped. |
| 2 (tonemapper output) | The same marks, using the copy taken at the start of the chain (the picture as the tonemapper gave it). |
| 3 (who clipped it) | Red where the tonemapper output is already clipped (too much light: the flashlight, sun, exposure or tonemapper settings). Yellow where it is clipped only by the colour effects after it (any channel clipped in the final picture but not in the tonemapper output). Red takes priority over yellow. |

Colour values: magenta (1, 0, 1), cyan (0, 1, 1), red (1, 0, 0), yellow (1, 1, 0).

**Clipping view: the start copy (modes 2 and 3).** At the start of the post chain, the picture in the game target is copied into a helper texture (`TESR_ClipStartBuffer`) of the same size and format. The helper is created on first use and re-created if the target size or format changes. If creating it fails, the modes 2 and 3 are unavailable and the failure is logged once. The copy is taken only when mode 2 or 3 is selected. When the mode is changed to 0 or 1 the helper is released.

**Clipping view: when it draws.** It draws only when the mode is not 0, a clipping-view technique exists in the loaded file, and (for modes 2 and 3) the start copy exists. When active, Debug is named as the chain's last effect, so the clipping view is the last layer over the whole frame (after the sky dither and after any debug overlay), and it covers every other effect's result.

**First-person weapon.** No special handling.

**Sky.** The depth panel shows the sky as the far end. No other special handling.

**RenderScale / RenderRect frames.** The overlay and the clipping view use the full target. No special handling.

**Jitter (TAA).** None.

**Reversed depth.** Not used; depth is the Vegas Shaders combined depth.

**HDR vs LDR.** LDR only (8 bits). The clipping view's whole purpose is the 8-bit limit; it is meaningless for an HDR chain.

**NaN and inf.** The depth panel's power and the light falloff are written to avoid divide by zero within their ranges, but the light falloff divides by the light's radius (its w value). A light with radius 0 gives a divide by zero. The swatches write raw values, so negative or large values are clipped on write.

**Things that would look wrong if missed:** the overlay's samplers out of order (the panels show the wrong buffer); the average-luma measurement not forced on when the overlay is on (the average swatch is wrong); the clipping view marking everything because the clip level is taken in the wrong range; the start copy missing so modes 2 and 3 show the final picture.

### 7. Vulkan path

- **None.** `DebugEffect` does not override the Vulkan entry point, so it always runs as a D3D9 technique, even when `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses` is on.
- The clipping view's start copy and draw use the D3D9 device calls directly (a copy of the target, then the technique). They do not use the Vulkan extension.
- Any new Vulkan version would need its own pass and the same push-constant layout for the overlay constants and for `TESR_ClipView`. Nothing of that exists today.

### 8. Look and cost

- **Overlay correct:** the panels show clean, undistorted copies of their buffers in their boxes, with the colour swatches on the right and the light spots on the picture. Standing still should keep the panels steady. Lights mark where the game thinks they are.
- **Overlay typical wrong result:** every panel showing the same buffer (samplers bound in the wrong order); a black depth panel (the far scale wrong); light spots in the wrong places (the light positions or the depth reconstruction wrong); the average swatch stuck (the average-luma measurement off).
- **Clipping view correct:** a dim, nearly grey picture with coloured stripes only where a channel is at its maximum. Mode 3 separates the tonemapper's clipping (red) from the colour effects' clipping (yellow). Standing still should not change the marks.
- **Clipping view typical wrong result:** the whole picture stripped (the clip level too low); no marks in bright scenes (the level too high, or the start copy missing in mode 2 or 3); mode 3 showing everything yellow (the start copy not taken at the chain start).
- **Cost class:** the overlay is one pass with about nine buffer reads, a short loop over 24 lights (each with a few vector operations), and a depth read. Moderately heavy but only while on. The clipping view is one pass with one or two reads, and the start copy is one full-target copy, only in modes 2 and 3.

### 9. Open questions

1. **`TESR_DebugVar` is never read.** The four `DebugVar` settings in `[_Main.Develop.Main]` reach the shader but the overlay ignores them. Decide whether they are for future experiments only; if so, say so in the spec.
2. **`TESR_ShadowRadius` (REMOVED 2026-11-02).** It was declared and never read; the rewrite no longer declares it.
3. **Light radius zero (FIXED 2026-11-02).** A zero radius gives no glow (the falloff is guarded).
4. **Clip level.** The clip threshold is 254.5 / 255. Confirm that matches what testers mean by "clipped" (a channel at full brightness, not one just below).
5. **The swatches are raw, not gamma-corrected.** Sun, fog and sky colours are shown exactly as stored; the new version should say whether the swatches should match what the player sees.
6. **The overlay and the clipping view both use the D3D9 path only.** Confirm that is intended for a developer tool.
7. **Panel layout is fixed in the shader.** The panel positions are hard-coded fractions of the screen; a different screen aspect changes the panel shape. Confirm that is acceptable.
8. **Ranges not stated** for `ClipView` (it accepts only 0 to 3; any other value is set to 0) and for `DebugVar1` to `DebugVar4` (no range given).

## DepthOfField

Rewritten 2026-11-02 (wave 3, branch `claude/rw3-screen`, commit 05647249) from the spec of the same name. The C++ (`src/effects/DepthOfField/DepthOfField.cpp`,
`.h`) is unchanged. The new shaders: `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/DepthOfField.fx.hlsl` (D3D9), `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/DepthOfField.glsl`
(shared push block and helpers), `DepthOfField.frag` (distant pass), `DepthOfFieldFocus.frag`, `DepthOfFieldMosaic.frag`,
`DepthOfFieldBokeh.frag`, `DepthOfFieldCombine.frag`, `DepthOfFieldCombineBokeh.frag` (Vulkan). The bokeh scatter and resolve
(`DepthOfFieldBokehScatter.comp`, `DepthOfFieldBokehResolve.frag`) are unchanged.
Fixed bugs are marked **FIXED**; behaviour kept on purpose (or not yet changed) is marked **KEPT** or **OPEN**.

### 1. Purpose

The effect blurs parts of the picture that are out of focus, the way a camera lens does. Near objects that are closer than the focus range
blur into the foreground, and far objects beyond the focus range blur into the background. The focus follows what is at the screen centre
(autofocus), so the blur moves as the player looks around. A second, optional blur softens the far distance (terrain LOD and draw
distance), so that distant geometry does not pop. With the optional bokeh mode on, bright lights that are out of focus are drawn as
hard-edged shapes of the lens aperture (a disc or a polygon) instead of soft smudges.

### 2. When it runs

**Master switch.** `[Shaders.DepthOfField.Status] Enabled`. The effect is skipped when its compiled shader is not loaded.

**Which view.** Per frame: vanity camera uses `VanityView`; third person (camera not first person) uses `ThirdPersonView`; otherwise
`FirstPersonView`.

**Per-view switch and mode.** Each view has `Enabled` (autofocus on) and `Mode`: 0 always; 1 off in dialog; 2 only in dialog; 3 always off;
4 (file only) always off. (3 and 4 were Oblivion's persuasion tests; New Vegas has no persuasion menu, and they were removed
2026-11-05 with the same result.)

**Techniques.** Technique 0 (unnamed, autofocus, six passes) when the view's autofocus is on. Technique 1 (`DistantBlurOnly`, one pass) when
autofocus is off and `DistantBlur` is on. Otherwise nothing is drawn.

**Distant-only skip.** When autofocus is off and the camera far plane is at or below `DistantBlurStartRange`, the pass is skipped (interiors).
The C++ does this; **KEPT**.

**Frame order** (ShaderManager): TAA and the particle / haze effects, Coloring, LUT, then DepthOfField, MotionBlur, lens effects, low-HF,
dither buster, SMAA, FXAA, Sharpening, Cinema, ImageAdjust, Bodycam, SkyDither, Debug.

### 3. Inputs

#### 3.1 Textures (declaration order = slot order; all linear, clamp, one mip)

| Slot | Name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | The chain's current image at each pass (the picture for the distant pass; the blur amounts after pass 1; the packed quadrants after pass 2; the blurred packed image after passes 3 to 5). |
| s1 | TESR_DepthBuffer | Combined linear depth / farZ (one channel). Multiply by TESR_CameraData.y for game units. |
| s2 | TESR_SourceBuffer | The image before this effect (a copy the chain makes). Used by the autofocus technique only; the declaration still makes the copy each frame (see OPEN in section 9). |
| s3 | TESR_AvgLumaBuffer | 1 x 1. Blue = focus distance as a fraction of the hyperfocal distance, read at (0.5, 0.5). |

#### 3.2 Constants

| Name | Source | Use |
|---|---|---|
| TESR_ReciprocalResolution | shared | xy: 1 / full target size |
| TESR_DepthOfFieldBlur | effect | x distant switch (zero or not), y start, z end (game units), w base blur radius (texels) |
| TESR_DepthOfFieldData | effect | x focus scale (BlurFallOff x enabled), y radius (texels, plain blur), z diameter range (bokeh threshold), w near cut-off (game units) |
| TESR_DepthOfFieldJitter | effect (JitterFix) | xy depth jitter in UV, subtracted from every depth read paired with the picture's pixel |
| TESR_CameraPosition, TESR_CameraData, TESR_ViewTransform, TESR_ProjectionTransform, TESR_RenderScale | shared (Includes/Depth.hlsl, RenderRect.hlsl) | camera position and height, far Z, view ray and rect helpers |

#### 3.3 Vulkan push block (224 bytes, `DofPush` in DepthOfField.cpp, `Compute/DepthOfField.glsl`)

view matrix (0), projection (64), reciprocal resolution (128), blur (144), data (160), camera position (176), camera data (192),
sampler heap index (208), useBokeh (212), JitterFix offset as two floats (216). The bokeh Combine writes half-resolution size at 216; the
shader does not read it.

### 4. Settings

Unchanged, all in `resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml`. Keys: `Enabled`, `Mode`, `BlurFallOff`, `BaseBlurRadius`, `Radius`,
`DiameterRange`, `NearBlurCutOff`, `DistantBlur`, `DistantBlurStartRange`, `DistantBlurEndRange` per view; `JitterFix` in Status;
Bokeh block: `Bokeh`, `BokehShape`, `BokehThreshold`, `BokehIntensity`, `BokehSize`, `BokehRotation`, `BokehMaxSprites` (clamps in the C++).
The settings meanings are kept exactly as before (see section 9 for the two that read differently from their file comments).

### 5. Passes and outputs

#### 5.1 DistantBlurOnly (technique 1, one pass)
Reads the picture (s0) and depth (s1). Depth at the picture's pixel minus the JitterFix offset. Distant amount = a ramp from the start to the
end range (linear, complete at the end), times a height factor (full at or below 5000 game units of world height, none at or above
100000), so high points and the sky are left alone. Where the amount is zero the picture is returned unchanged. Otherwise four diagonal
taps at radius `BaseBlurRadius x (1 + amount)` texels, each weighted by how close its depth is to the centre's (full within 7 %, none
beyond 22 %); the blur is mixed with the centre by the amount.

#### 5.2 Autofocus (technique 0, six passes, each reads the chain's current image)
1. **Focus.** Depth at the picture's pixel (JitterFix). H = max(BlurFallOff x 1000, 0.0001); f = AvgLuma.b x H; near limit and far limit
   from the standard hyperfocal form (guarded, section 6.2). Output: red = near amount, green = far amount (the distant amount is added to
   green), blue = alpha = 1.
2. **Mosaic.** Quadrants of the full target, each holding the whole picture at half resolution (a 2 x 2 box of full-resolution pixels):
   top left and top right = the sharp image (SourceBuffer); bottom left and bottom right = the blur amounts (red near, green far).
3. **Bokeh blur (bokeh select).** Top quadrants blurred with their amount's radius (top left: near amount from bottom left, top right: far
   amount from bottom right); bottom quadrants unchanged. 12 disc taps (30 degree steps, alternating outer and inner ring), radius
   `BaseBlurRadius x amount` texels, taps outside the pixel's quadrant ignored. Output: the brightest valid tap when its luma is above
   `DiameterRange`, else the average of the valid taps.
4. **Bokeh blur (bokeh select)**, the same as pass 3 on its output.
5. **Plain blur.** The same taps with the plain average and radius `Radius x amount`.
6. **Combine.** Per pixel: the sharp image, mixed toward the top-left (near) blur by the near amount, then toward the top-right (far) blur
   by the far amount. Writes the frame (directly into the game target when this is the last effect).

#### 5.3 Bokeh sprites (Vulkan only; fork-only)
Unchanged scatter and resolve (`DepthOfFieldBokehScatter.comp`, `DepthOfFieldBokehResolve.frag`). The bokeh Combine adds the resolved sprites
in linear light: `1 - (1 - colour) x exp(-sprites)`, clamped to white, then back to display space.

### 6. Behaviour details

#### 6.1 Depth and sky
Depth is linear (fraction of far Z). Sky pixels are at the far plane (1.0). **FIXED (2026-11-02 rewrite):** the autofocus far weight is
zero on sky pixels (depth at or above 0.9999), so the sky is not blurred by the focus; the distance blur still follows the height rule.
The height rule is kept: world height is camera Z plus the view ray's Z times the depth.

#### 6.2 Focus geometry (guarded)
- near limit = f (H - 0.01) / max(H + f - 0.02, 0.0001), itself at least 0.0001 (**FIXED**: no divide by zero).
- far limit = f (H - 0.01) / (H - f) when H - f exceeds 0.0001; otherwise 10^6 (focus at or beyond the hyperfocal distance has no far limit;
  **FIXED**: the old form gave a negative or infinite limit there). At least 0.0001.
- near weight = saturate(1 - depth / near limit) x near cut-off attenuation; far weight = saturate((depth - far limit) / far limit) x 0.8,
  then plus the distance amount, saturated.

#### 6.3 Near cut-off attenuation (first and third person)
Factor = 0.5 x (1 - smoothstep(0, c/2, d)) + smoothstep(c/2, c, d), with c = NearBlurCutOff. It is 0.5 at the camera, 0 at c/2, 1 from c on.
**FIXED**: c <= 0 (vanity default) returns 1 instead of dividing by zero. The dip near the camera is **KEPT** (see section 9).

#### 6.4 Blur radii
Bokeh passes use `BaseBlurRadius` (TESR_DepthOfFieldBlur.w); the plain pass uses `Radius` (TESR_DepthOfFieldData.y). Both are scaled by the
amount of the pixel's quadrant. **KEPT**: this is what the code did before; the settings file comments say the reverse (section 9).

#### 6.5 Tap guards
Every tap that is read with a rect (RenderScale) frame is clamped to the drawn part (`clampToRectIfOn`), and the autofocus taps stay inside
their quadrant, half a texel from the edge. Added in the rewrite: the rect clamp keeps reads inside the drawn part in render-rectangle frames, and the
quadrant bounds stop one quadrant's taps reading another. Distant-pass weight division uses a floor of 0.0001.

#### 6.6 Bokeh sprite detection (unchanged, compute)
Half-resolution candidates above `BokehThreshold` that stand out from their surroundings; radius below one pixel dropped; weight and energy as
in the scatter file. Depth stored with a sprite is the half-resolution depth sample.

#### 6.7 Bokeh splat and combine (unchanged, compute and Combine)
Shape coverage with an anti-aliased edge, energy kept over the shape's area, fixed-point adds capped at 4.0 per pixel. Far-field sprites hidden
by a nearer in-focus pixel. Combine: section 5.3.

#### 6.8 JitterFix
Every depth read paired with the picture's pixel subtracts `TESR_DepthOfFieldJitter` (focus pass, distant pass and its taps, Vulkan copies).
Zero when JitterFix is off. The bokeh scatter does not (see section 9).

### 7. Vulkan path

Files in `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute`, all compiled with `tools/nvr-dxvk/compile-compute.sh`:

| Pass | Source | Pipeline |
|---|---|---|
| DepthOfField (technique 1) | DepthOfField.frag (+ DepthOfField.glsl) | 2 textures, sampler |
| DepthOfFieldFocus | DepthOfFieldFocus.frag | depth, AvgLuma, sampler |
| DepthOfFieldMosaic | DepthOfFieldMosaic.frag | blur amounts / chain, source, sampler |
| DepthOfFieldBokeh (passes 3 to 5, push useBokeh) | DepthOfFieldBokeh.frag | chain, sampler |
| DepthOfFieldCombine | DepthOfFieldCombine.frag | chain, source, sampler |
| DepthOfFieldCombineBokeh | DepthOfFieldCombineBokeh.frag | chain, source, sprites, sampler |
| DepthOfFieldBokehScatter / Resolve | unchanged | compute / graphics |

The Vulkan passes are native-frame only (no render-rectangle maths). Each file is the D3D9 pass line for line. Compile check: all six
`.spv` build and pass spirv-val.

### 8. Look and cost

**Correct look.** Distant geometry soft and even with no flicker along roofs and skylines (JitterFix on). The first-person gun and body stay
sharp (NearBlurCutOff). Focus follows the centre without pumping. Sky not blurred by the focus. Bokeh on: out-of-focus lights become
aperture shapes.

**Wrong results to watch for.** Blocks along roofs with TAA, FSR or DLSS at native size and JitterFix off. A dark halo around thin objects over
the sky (the distant weight prevents it). Quadrant seams in the autofocus look (the taps now stay inside their quadrant).

**Cost.** Distant pass: one sample on most pixels. Autofocus: six full-screen passes at 12 taps per blur pass, plus the bokeh dispatches when on.
The D3D9 chain also copies the source image each frame while the effect declares TESR_SourceBuffer (OPEN, section 9).

### 9. Open questions

1. **Radius and BaseBlurRadius (KEPT).** The settings file says `Radius` is the bokeh radius and `BaseBlurRadius` the overall strength. The
   code (and this rewrite) uses `BaseBlurRadius` for the bokeh passes and `Radius` for the plain pass. Which one is meant? Changing it changes
   what the setting does, so it was not changed.
2. **Near cut-off dip (KEPT).** The attenuation is 0.5 at the camera, the file comment says it is meant to reveal the gun. The dip near the
   camera is kept as written; confirm it is intended.
3. **Bokeh scatter ignores JitterFix (OPEN).** The sprite detection reads the sharp image at the picture's pixel, but the depth for the sprite
   and the far occlusion test is read at the half-resolution pixel without the offset. Fixing it needs one more field in the scatter's push
   block (C++ change), which this rewrite did not make.
4. **Bokeh passes select even when Bokeh is off (KEPT).** The C++ always runs passes 3 and 4 with the bokeh select (push 1, 1, 0), in both
   paths. "Bokeh off = exactly the old DoF" is therefore not literally true for the brightest-tap choice; left as the C++ and the Vulkan path
   already do.
5. **Mode 3 and 4 (KEPT).** Always off in New Vegas, as before.
6. **Source copy (OPEN).** The effect declares TESR_SourceBuffer for the whole file, so the chain copies the picture before the distant pass
   too, although that pass does not read it. Removing it needs a separate effect file or a C++ change.
7. **Slider ranges.** Not checked; the C++ does not clamp the view values.
8. **Sprite drop order.** Atomic counter, so which sprites are dropped at the budget is not deterministic (accepted by design).

## DitherBuster

Source reviewed (wave 3, 2026-11-02): the new D3D9 effect src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/DitherBuster.fx.hlsl with its shared include
src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Includes/DitherNoise.hlsl and Includes/RenderRect.hlsl; the new Vulkan passes
src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/DitherBuster.frag and DitherBusterStable.frag (branch claude/rw3-colour); C++ src/effects/DitherBuster.cpp,
DitherBuster.h (unchanged); settings in resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml; frame order in src/core/ShaderManager.cpp.
The toml comments describe an edge-blending pass (MaskPower, Radius) that no technique calls. The new shader does not implement it.

### 1. Purpose

DitherBuster removes the "dither" artefact that alpha-tested geometry (grass, foliage, fences and similar) produces under DXVK:
single pixels that flicker between the object's colour and the background in a checkerboard-like pattern. Each such isolated pixel
is blended toward the average of itself and its four neighbours, so the dithered coverage becomes solid. Ordinary edges are left
alone, and SMAA later in the chain handles them. An optional soft gate and a de-dither judgement reduce frame-to-frame twinkle on
still pictures.

### 2. When it runs

- Position: post-tonemap, in the lens group of RenderEffects (ShaderManager.cpp): after the blood lens, water lens and low
  high-frequency effect, and before SMAA, FXAA and sharpening.
- Enabled: [Shaders.DitherBuster.Status] Enabled (bool, default true).
- Gates: [Main.Main] RenderEffects on, a player cell present, overlay off. No ShouldRender override.
- Technique choice (made each frame by the C++, TechniqueIndex): technique 1 when the soft gate is above 0, or DeDither is on, or
  DebugView is not 0; otherwise technique 0. With all three options off the plain pass runs.
- Interior and exterior: no separate sections.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures

| Slot | Name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the current picture (LDR, tonemapped, after the earlier post effects) |

LINEAR, CLAMP, no mips. The depth sampler the old shader declared at slot s1 is gone (no technique read it; the C++ never bound it).

#### Constants (registered by name; no fixed registers)

- TESR_ReciprocalResolution: 1 / width and 1 / height of the render (texels).
- TESR_DitherBusterData (float4): x Strength (used clamped to 0..1); y MaskPower (not read by either technique); z DitherThreshold
  (0 turns the isolation step off); w Radius (not read by either technique).
- TESR_DitherBusterGate (float4; technique 1 only): x GateSoftness (0..1); y DeDither flag (above 0.5 on); z DebugView flag (above
  0.5 on); w unused.
- TESR_ToneMappingClip (float4; technique 1 only; set by the tonemap effect): y the tonemap's dither size (Sky Dither strength / 255,
  0 when off); z its frame counter. x and w are not read.

### 4. Settings

[Shaders.DitherBuster.Main] (no night or interior sections). Unchanged by the rewrite:
- Strength (float, default 1.0): how far an isolated pixel moves toward its neighbourhood average.
- MaskPower (float, default 0.5): NOT USED by either technique (section 9).
- DitherThreshold (float, default 0.08; 0 disables): the isolation threshold in luma units.
- Radius (float, default 1.0): NOT USED by either technique (section 9).
- GateSoftness (float, default 0.0; clamped 0..1): 0 is a hard step at DitherThreshold; above 0 the weight ramps smoothly from
  DitherThreshold * (1 - softness) to DitherThreshold * (1 + softness).
- DeDither (bool, default false): judge the isolation test on the picture with the tonemap's dither noise taken out (the decision
  only). Needs Sky Dither with the AtToneMap option; without it the noise size is 0 and DeDither changes nothing.
- DebugView (int, default 0): 1 shows the picture with the recomputed tonemap noise removed, for checking DeDither.

[Shaders.DitherBuster.Status]:
- Enabled (bool, default true): "Reduces dithering artifacts via luma-edge anti-aliasing."

### 5. Passes and outputs

Two techniques, one pass each. Full-screen quads through the frame vertex buffer, writing the frame chain's target (or the game
target when this is the chain's final effect). Output is the same size as the render, alpha 1.

- Technique 0 (DitherOnly; the C++ asks for index 0): the plain isolated-pixel pass (section 6).
- Technique 1 (DitherOnlyStable; the C++ asks for index 1 when the soft gate, DeDither or DebugView is on): the same pass with the
  soft gate, the de-dither judgement and the debug view.

### 6. Behaviour details

Live pass, in words (both techniques share the test):

1. Five taps: the centre and its four direct neighbours (left, right, up, down), one texel away. Each tap is kept inside the drawn part
   of a rect frame (clampToRectIfOn).
2. Luma: Rec.709 weights (0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722).
3. Isolation: the sum over the four neighbours of |centre luma - neighbour luma|, minus four times the length of the central-difference
   gradient ((right - left) / 2, (up - down) / 2 in luma), floored at 0. On a step edge the two cancel (about 0); on a one-pixel
   checkerboard the gradient is 0 and the sum stays large, so the measure picks out dither and not edges.
4. Decision: with DitherThreshold above 0 and the isolation above it, the pixel is blended toward the five-tap mean by Strength (clamped
   0..1). Otherwise the pixel is written unchanged. Blending a pixel toward a mean of values already in the picture keeps the range.

Technique 1 only:
- Soft gate: with GateSoftness above 0 the weight is a smoothstep of the isolation from DitherThreshold * (1 - softness) up to
  DitherThreshold * (1 + softness). With softness 0 it is the hard step of technique 0. The blend amount is Strength times the weight.
- De-dither: the isolation is judged on the luma with the tonemap's dither noise removed at each tap's own whole-pixel position (the
  shared include DitherNoise.hlsl: the noise is the sum of two gradient noises minus one, times the dither size, removed in proportion
  to saturate(luma * 128)). Only the decision uses the corrected luma; the blend still uses the dithered picture.
- Debug view: the output is the centre with the recomputed noise removed (same proportion), clamped to 0..1. Blend and gate are not used.

Edge cases:
- DitherThreshold 0: the isolation step is off; the picture is unchanged (apart from the debug view).
- Strength 0: no blend.
- Thin bright or dark features (wires, grass blades) can have high isolation without being dither. The threshold, the soft gate and
  de-dither are the controls for this.
- The sky's own dither is one luma step (about 0.004), far under the default threshold.
- First-person weapon, TAA jitter, reversed depth: not handled.
- HDR vs LDR: LDR (tonemapped) input only.
- NaN and infinity: the isolation uses the length of a vector; the smoothstep is only used with an upper edge above the lower edge
  (threshold above 0 and softness above 0).

### 7. Vulkan path

- Choice: [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses (default true) with the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension present. Technique 0 uses DitherBuster.frag,
  technique 1 DitherBusterStable.frag. A refused pass makes the D3D9 technique run for the session.
- Bindings (set 1): binding 0 = the picture. One LINEAR CLAMP sampler, its heap index in the push block.
- Push block for technique 0: 0 TESR_ReciprocalResolution (vec4); 16 TESR_DitherBusterData (vec4); 32 sampler heap index (uint).
- Push block for technique 1: 0 TESR_ReciprocalResolution; 16 TESR_DitherBusterData; 32 TESR_DitherBusterGate; 48 TESR_ToneMappingClip;
  64 sampler heap index (uint).
- Pixel position is floor(gl_FragCoord.xy), the same whole-pixel position as the D3D9 pass.
- No RenderScale rect steps on the Vulkan path (no rect constant in the push block).

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct result: alpha-tested grass and foliage edges look solid instead of speckled; straight edges, text and geometry edges are
  unchanged by this pass (SMAA does those). Strength 1 gives a full blend of isolated pixels.
- Typical wrong results:
  - Thin wires or fine detail softened: DitherThreshold too low.
