  - Shimmering on a still scene: GateSoftness 0 with DeDither off (the hard step flips at the threshold).
  - Dither still visible: DitherThreshold too high, or Strength too low.
- Cost class: single pass, five texture reads per pixel, cheap. Technique 1 adds the noise recomputation for the five taps when DeDither
  is on (the toml records about 0.05 ms at its test size).

### 9. Open questions

Changes made in wave 3 (2026-11-02):
- FIXED: the neighbour taps are clamped to the drawn part in a rect frame (RenderScale), so no tap reads the stale part of the target
  (D3D9 only; see section 7).
- CHANGED: the unused depth sampler is no longer declared (no visible change; the C++ never bound it).

Still open:
1. MaskPower and Radius are not used by either technique. The toml describes an edge-blending pass that is not called by any technique.
   Neither value changes the picture. They are kept in the settings as they were and are not read by the new shader.
2. The toml recommends GateSoftness 0.5 for the grass case while the default is 0. Whether the default should change is a look decision.
3. The de-dither noise must match the tonemap's noise exactly. The shared include DitherNoise.hlsl is the reference; the tonemap spec
   should confirm the same constants.
4. The Vulkan passes have no rect steps (section 7). Not checked in a rect frame.
5. SMAA also has a DeDither option that uses the same tonemap noise; that effect is outside this spec.

## Dream

A "fun" look, off by default. **Ported from the author's own DreamSim shaders** (DreamSim `godot/shaders/post.gdshader` and
`godot/shaders/mix.gdshader`, with the timings and curves of its `src/main.gd`). The HLSL was written fresh from that GLSL; it uses only
the normal effect boilerplate (the full-screen `FrameVS`, the `TESR_` constants and samplers) and none of the shared Vegas Shaders
helper code (no includes, no other effect's functions). The port is one way: nothing from Vegas Shaders goes back into DreamSim.

Left out on purpose: DreamSim's bodycam part (Vegas Shaders has its own `Bodycam` effect) and its geometry tricks (PS1 vertex
wobble, corridor twist, edge texture melt), which need the world's shaders rather than a post pass.

### 1. Purpose

The picture drifts into a trip. One dream level (0-1) drives everything: the middle of the picture twists slowly and the whole image
wobbles, red and blue split apart, colours drift round the colour wheel and grow stronger, anything that moves leaves tracers, and the
edges darken with a coarse 15-bit dithered colour. The level breathes by itself around the value set. Now and then the scene melts
into a Julia fractal for a few seconds; glitch bursts break the picture into posterised macroblocks and smeared datamosh blocks; and
holding the view still for a few seconds makes it fall into itself (a swirl, then a hexagonal kaleidoscope zooming in for ever).
Optionally, low health deepens the dream.

### 2. When it runs

- **Switch:** `[_Shaders.Dream.Status] Enabled`, default **false**.
- **Menu:** the sidebar's effects list, group **Extras** (second last, above Developer), entry **Dream**. Every setting is live.
  With Advanced off the page shows a short list (dream level, breathing, tracers, stare tunnel, fractal melts, deeper when hurt;
  `kSimpleRows` in `src/core/ImGuiManager.cpp`); Advanced shows every key below.
- **Frame position:** post chain, after Bodycam and before SkyDither (`src/core/EffectChain.cpp`, step `Dream`). It sees the finished
  picture (after anti-aliasing, Cinema, ImageAdjust and the body camera). Cinema's fused sky dither is not used while Dream draws
  (it sits between them).
- **Skip rule (`ShouldRender`):** nothing is drawn while the dream level this frame is 0 (Level 0 with Breathing off, or the
  breath below 0 at a low level) unless a melt is still running. With LowHealth on, low health alone can start it.
- **Clock:** the effect's own seconds, advanced once a frame while the switch is on (`UpdateConstants` is called twice a frame; the
  second call returns at once).

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (declaration order = binding order)

| Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|
| `TESR_RenderedBuffer` | the picture arriving at Dream | linear, clamp |
| `TESR_DreamHistory` | the effect's own history: last frame's mix (in the mix pass), this frame's mix (in the post pass) | linear, clamp |

The history is two screen-size `A16B16G16R16F` render targets, made the first time the mix pass runs (none before: a 4 x 4 stand-in
`NVR_DreamStandIn` is bound). Half floats so a fading tracer does not leave its last 8-bit step stuck on screen. It is not the TAA
history. Remade if the picture's size changes; if it cannot be made, tracers, tunnel and datamosh stay off (logged once).

#### Constants

| Name | Components |
|---|---|
| `TESR_DreamLevel` | x dream level now (0-1), y seconds, z hue angle (radians), w warp (level² x Warp) |
| `TESR_DreamTrails` | x tracer mix (0-0.95), y tunnel (0-1), z datamosh block chance, w macroblock chance |
| `TESR_DreamMelt` | x melt (0-1), y fractal variant (1-4), z fractal zoom, w colour split (uv) |
| `TESR_DreamFinish` | x vignette, y dither, z extra saturation, w unused |
| `TESR_DreamGrid` | xy the low-res grid (screen size / pixel size), z macroblock seed, w datamosh seed (frame-rate-free counters, 13 and 9 a second) |

### 4. Settings (`[_Shaders.Dream.Main]`, all live)

| Key | Default | Range | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Level` | 0.5 | 0-1 | the dream level |
| `Breathing` | true | | the level breathes: `(0.05 + 0.1 Level) (0.65 sin(0.23 p) + 0.35 sin(0.61 p + 1.7))`, p = seconds x BreathingSpeed |
| `BreathingSpeed` | 1 | 0-5 | how fast it breathes |
| `Warp` | 1 | 0-3 | twist and wobble (x level²) |
| `ColourSplit` | 1 | 0-3 | the split: `0.0008 + 0.004 level²` of the screen width, times this |
| `HueDrift` | 1 | 0-3 | hue angle `1.2 level^1.5 sin(0.07 t)`, times this |
| `Saturation` | 1 | 0-3 | extra colour `0.8 level`, times this |
| `Tracers` | 1 | 0-1.5 | history mix `0.6 level^1.5`, times this (at most 0.95) |
| `Tunnel` | 1 | 0-1 | the stare tunnel's strength |
| `TunnelDelay` | 4 | 0-120 s | stillness before it starts; it opens over 20 s and eases out at 0.4 a second |
| `Melt` | 1 | 0-1 | the fractal melt's strength |
| `MeltInterval` | 7 | 0-60 min | mean time between melts (DreamSim: 4-10 min, divided by 1 + level); 0 = never |
| `Macroblocks` | 1 | 0-3 | block chance during a glitch: 0.22 x this (green flash: 0.06 x this) |
| `Datamosh` | 1 | 0-3 | block chance during a glitch: 0.3 x this |
| `GlitchInterval` | 85 | 0-3600 s | mean time between glitch bursts (45-125 s divided by 1 + level, as DreamSim's lag spikes); 0 = never |
| `Vignette` | 1 | 0-1.5 | darkening `0.55 smoothstep(0.35, 0.85, r)`, times this |
| `Dither` | 1 | 0-1 | blend towards 31 steps a channel with a 4 x 4 Bayer dither |
| `PixelSize` | 0 | 0-32 | screen pixels per DreamSim pixel for the blocks and the dither; 0 = screen height / 240 |
| `LowHealth` | 0 | 0-1 | added to the level: this x smoothstep from half health (0) to a tenth (1); needs the player alive |

The constant parts (vignette, dither, the 0.0008 base split) fade in over the first quarter of the level, so a level near 0 is
close to the untouched picture (in DreamSim they are always on).

### 5. Passes

- **Technique 0 `DreamMix`** (only when tracers, the tunnel or datamosh are live): this frame mixed with the history (tracers), the
  tunnel blended over it, datamosh blocks from the history; into the other history target, which then becomes `TESR_DreamHistory`.
  After a gap (not drawn for 0.25 s, a menu, a load) or a camera cut, and on the history's first frame, the tracer mix and datamosh
  are 0 for that frame.
- **Technique 1 `DreamPost`**: warp, macroblocks, colour split, melt, hue, saturation, vignette, dither; from the mix just written
  into the chain (it may be the last effect and write the game target).
- **Technique 2 `DreamPostDirect`**: the same from `TESR_RenderedBuffer`, when no mix pass runs (the history is then marked stale).
- D3D9 only: no Vulkan (DXVK extension) path and no SM6 build; `ExtRender` is not overridden.

### 6. Behaviour details

- **Melt:** in 4 s, held 2-4 s, out 3.5 s, eased twice; one of four Julia sets (variant drawn at random); the view zooms towards the
  set's repelling fixed point at `exp(0.42 (t - 2))`. Pixels slide from their place to their fractal place, then take the set's
  palette (more where the orbit escaped). The first melt comes one interval after the effect starts drawing.
- **Glitch bursts:** 0.4-1.8 s each; DreamSim triggers its glitch by hand, here they come at random.
- **Stare:** "still" = turning under about 7 degrees a second and moving under 20 units a second (player rotation and position).
  Reset under the game's menus (Pip-Boy, VATS, dialogue, terminals and other full-screen menus); the Vegas Shaders menu does not stop it.
- **Not ported:** DreamSim's stare also raises the level by itself, and its field of view breathes; both left out (one is a setting the
  player owns, the other is camera geometry).
- **RenderScale / RenderRect frames:** no special handling (post-tonemap, after any upscale).
- **HDR output:** works on the display values like the rest of the post chain; the dither assumes 0-1.

### 7. Look and cost

- **Correct:** at Level 0.5 the picture twists gently, colours swim and are richer, motion leaves soft trails, edges are darker and the
  colour slightly banded with a fine pattern. Stand still 4 s: the middle starts to swirl and after ~15 s turns into nested hexagons.
- **Wrong:** trails that never clear (history not flipped or continuity broken), a frozen picture (post pass reading a stale history),
  the tunnel starting while walking (stillness test), black blocks (history stand-in read by datamosh).
- **Cost:** post pass ~380 instructions (8 texture reads; the melt's 48-step loop only during a melt); the mix pass ~300 (18 reads,
  the tunnel's work only while staring). Two extra half-float screen targets once tracers or datamosh have run.

## DustMotes

*README title: DustMotes - effect spec*

NEW 2026-11-03 (branch claude/dust-motes). FIXED the same day after the first bench (saloon16, int23: nothing visible): see section 10. Seen in game since (bench crops, saloon16); the cost below is still worked out, not measured.

### 1. Purpose

Sparse, tiny specks of dust drifting in the air, visible only where light falls on them: round lamps, and (optionally) in the sun's shafts. A speck between you and a light glints brighter. Specks in unlit air are invisible. The effect is procedural: no particles are placed in the world, so it works in every cell, modded ones included, with no setup.

It only adds light (additive, in linear light, before the tonemap, so the specks are exposed and bloomed with the scene). It never darkens anything.

### 2. When it runs

All of these must hold:
- `[Shaders.DustMotes.Status] Enabled` (default true).
- Indoors `Interiors`, outdoors `Exteriors` (both default true); never under water.
- `Density` and `Brightness` above 0.
- Some light can reach a speck: at least one of the frame's tracked lamps has its reach within `MaxDistance` of the eye, or the sun counts this frame (outdoors, `SunShare` above 0, and the volumetric light's froxel grid was built this frame: `[Shaders.VolumetricLight.Main] Froxels` on and the shafts drawn, which needs the sun's shadows; scaled by the shafts' own fade, so none in rain or with sun shadows off).
- The copy-free effect chain is active (always, in practice). Without it the effect logs once and draws nothing.

Frame order (src/core/EffectChain.cpp, pre-tonemap table): step `DustMotes`, right after `VolumetricLight` (so after the fog and the sun shafts: the specks sit in front of the lit air) and before `Sandstorm`, `GodRays` and the exposure / bloom step.

### 3. How it works

The air is cut into cubic cells of 40 game units (57 cm) that drift slowly with the "air" (about 5 units a second at Drift 1, sideways and rising a little). A cell holds one speck or none: a hash of the cell's number, against `Density`. The speck sits at a hashed spot in the cell (0.2 to 0.8 of the way across on each axis) and wobbles round it (0.13 of the cell each way, at 1, 2 or 3 times a slow clock). So the specks are world-anchored: they do not swim with the camera. The pattern repeats every 64 cells (2560 units) on each axis (the hash is of the cell number modulo 64); the C++ side gives the shader the camera's place in that repeat (in doubles), so every position the shader works with is small and the drift can wrap without any speck jumping.

Pass 0, Specks (half resolution, into `TESR_DustMotesBuffer`, RGBA16F), per pixel:
1. The view from 24 units (closer specks would be big, fast blobs, and the weapon is there) to the first surface or `MaxDistance`, whichever is nearer.
2. Cut to the stretch some light reaches: the union of the up-to-8 lamps' spheres along the view; all of it when the sun counts. Nothing lit on this view: the pixel ends here (most pixels of a room far from lamps).
3. The cells that stretch crosses are visited in order (3D DDA, at most 48). In each cell with a speck: the speck's distance from the view line gives its cover of this pixel, a Gaussian disc whose width is the speck's size on screen held between 0.8 and 1.25 buffer pixels (about 1.6 to 2.5 screen pixels). A speck smaller than that is drawn that wide but dimmer by the ratio of the sizes (not of the areas: that was physically fair but left nearly every speck invisible), so it fades out with distance and does not flicker as it moves across pixels. A speck must be in front of the surface (depth test) and past the near limit. The speck covering the pixel most is kept.
4. That speck is lit: each lamp's colour (linear, times its dimmer) with a falloff to zero at the lamp's reach, `1 - x^2` with x = distance / reach (close to the game's own lamp falloff), through a Henyey-Greenstein phase with g = `Glint` (times 4 pi, so 1 = scattering evenly; capped at 8); plus, when the sun counts, the sun's colour times its lit share of the air at the speck's depth (read from the froxel grid, section 5) times `SunShare` times the same phase towards the sun.
5. Times `Brightness`, the cover, a slow twinkle (0.55 most of the time, a short flash up to 1.75 now and then, per speck), and a fade in past the near limit and out over the last 30 % of `MaxDistance`.
6. Written as the difference it makes to the gamma-encoded HDR frame at this spot: `delinearize(linearize(frame) + light) - frame`. So the light is added in linear light, and the next pass does not need to read the frame.

Pass 1, Composite (full resolution, into the chain's current image in place): the buffer read bilinearly (that is the upsample), pixels with nothing to add discarded (`clip`), the rest added with additive blending (One, One). Colour channels only: the frame's alpha (the material capture's) is kept. No frame copy, no ping-pong: only the specks' own pixels are written.

### 4. Inputs

Textures (D3D9, slot order = declaration order):

| Slot | Name | Holds | Used in |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the frame (gamma-encoded HDR) | Specks (to work out the difference); unbound in Composite (it is the target) |
| s1 | TESR_DepthBuffer | linear depth | Specks |
| s2 | TESR_VolumetricLightGrid | the volumetric light's froxel grid (running sum of lit air, front to back) | Specks, only while the sun counts |
| s3 | TESR_DustMotesBuffer | this effect's half-resolution result | Composite; unbound in Specks (it is the target) |

Constants of its own (DustMotes.cpp):

| Name | Components |
|---|---|
| TESR_DustMotesData | x Brightness (0 = off this frame), y Size (radius, units), z MaxDistance, w Density |
| TESR_DustMotesLook | x Glint, y cell size (40), z wobble clock (cycles, wraps at 1), w lamps in use |
| TESR_DustMotesOrigin | xyz the camera in the drifting cells' frame (wrapped at 2560), w radians per buffer pixel (from the projection and the drawn rows; RenderScale-aware) |
| TESR_DustMotesSun | x SunShare times the shafts' fade (0 = no sun this frame) |
| TESR_DustMotesLampPosition[8] | xyz camera-relative, w radius (0 = empty) |
| TESR_DustMotesLampColor[8] | rgb linear colour times dimmer |

Read from others: TESR_CameraData, TESR_ViewTransform, TESR_ProjectionTransform, TESR_RenderScale (Depth.hlsl), TESR_SunColor, TESR_SunDirection, and while the sun counts TESR_VolumetricLightData (z density, w grid depth), TESR_VolumetricLightGridLayout, TESR_VolumetricLightGridData.

Lamps: picked on the CPU each frame in the effect's step (after the shadow pass has run GetNearbyLights) from the same list LampHaze and AmbientLight's grid read: every shadow slot's lamp (`ShaderManager::BatchLightPosition / BatchLightColor`, all 64 slots) and the 12 tracked unshadowed ones (`LightPosition`, `LightColor[12..23]`). Their w is the game's reach times `[Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main] LightRadiusMult`; the specks divide that back out, so they are lit as far as the lamp lights the walls. A lamp in both lists counts once. A lamp counts if its reach overlaps the drawn distance round the eye and its colour is positive; the 8 that look brightest (linear luminance times how much of the drawn distance they cover) are used. Negative ("darkness") lamps light nothing.

### 5. The sun

Only outdoors, only with `SunShare` above 0 (default 0), and only on frames the volumetric light built its froxel grid. The grid holds, per column (16 x 16 pixels) and slice (64, spaced by the square of the slice number out to the shafts' MaxDistance), the running sum of each slice's lit air weighted by the air's transmittance. The lit share at the speck's depth is that sum over the speck's slice and one either side, divided by what the same slices would hold fully lit. So the specks are sun-lit exactly where the shafts are, at the grid's resolution (soft edges). With the march path instead of the grid (Froxels off), or at a RenderScale below 1 (the grid is not built in rect frames), the sun does not light specks.

Why the sun is off by default: by day nearly all the outdoor air is sunlit, so every pixel's view would be walked the whole `MaxDistance` (the effect's worst cost), and the specks mostly show as glitter when looking towards the sun, against bright surroundings where they add little. Lamps outdoors (street lights, fires, at night) light specks with Exteriors on.

### 6. Settings (`[Shaders.DustMotes.Main]`, all live)

| Key | Default | Range | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| Density | 0.5 | 0-1 | chance a 40-unit cell holds a speck (about 2.7 specks per cubic metre at 0.5, 5.4 at 1) |
| Size | 0.15 | 0.01-0.5 | speck radius, game units; mostly sets how far specks stay visible (drawn 1.6 to 2.5 screen pixels wide) |
| Brightness | 1.0 | 0-10 | light scattered, times the lamps' light where the speck is (1 = about a white surface lit by the same lamp, before the phase and twinkle) |
| MaxDistance | 250 | 100-1000 | how far out specks are drawn (fade over the last 30 %); the main cost |
| Drift | 1.0 | 0-5 | drift, wobble and twinkle speed; 0 = frozen |
| Glint | 0.45 | 0-0.9 | forward scattering g |
| SunShare | 0.0 | 0-2 | sun's light on specks outdoors (section 5) |
| Interiors | true | | specks indoors |
| Exteriors | true | | specks outdoors |
| DebugView | 0 | 0-2 | test only: 1 = the half-resolution buffer drawn over the picture (blue: lamp reaches the view crosses, faint blue wherever specks could be, so a blue screen proves the pass reaches the frame; green: share of MaxDistance walked; red: the speck found); 2 = every speck white, lit or not (the walk covers the whole view) |

Fixed in the code (DustMotes.cpp, DustMotes.fx.hlsl): the cell size (40), the near limit (24), the drift direction and speed at Drift 1 (5 units/s), the wobble size (0.13 of a cell) and clock (0.08 cycles/s at Drift 1), the twinkle shape, at most 8 lamps.

No setting needs a restart (the buffer is made at start, at half the screen size, full with Max Quality).

### 7. Cost (estimated, not measured)

Worked out from the compiled shader (D3DX, ps_3_0: Specks about 600 instruction slots in all; the cell loop about 28 slots per cell plus about 50 in cells that hold a speck; the lamp lighting about 200 slots, run only where a speck covers the pixel) and the walk's length. The GPU timer is "Dust motes" in the profiler.

- Specks pass, 1440p (1280 x 720 buffer): a view walked the full 250 units crosses about 1.6 x 250 / 40 + 1, about 11 cells: about 800 slots, about 1500 scalar operations a pixel; over 0.92 M pixels that is about 1.4 G operations, about 0.4 ms on a GTX 1070 (about 3.2 G scalar operations per ms) if EVERY pixel were walked the full distance. Indoors most views end at a wall within a few metres and only the stretch inside a lamp's reach is walked, and pixels with no lamp on their view stop after the lamp test (about 140 slots): expected about 0.1 to 0.25 ms in a lamp-lit room. With SunShare above 0 outdoors by day the worst case applies.
- Composite pass: 9 slots a pixel, one bilinear read of a half-resolution buffer, writes only where specks are: about 0.03 to 0.05 ms at 1440p. No frame copy.
- Nothing when no lamp reaches the drawn distance and the sun does not count (the step returns before any pass); the CPU side is a sort of at most 24 lamps once a frame.

### 8. Edge cases and limits

- Lamps' shadows are not read: a speck inside a lamp's reach is lit even if a wall stands between them. The reach is short and the depth test hides specks behind surfaces, so this shows only as a few specks in a lamp's shaded corner.
- Indoors the sun never lights specks (the froxel grid is an exterior pass); a sunlit interior gets lamp-lit specks only.
- One speck per pixel is lit (the one covering it most); two specks overlapping in one buffer pixel show as one.
- The speck's difference is worked out against the frame at the buffer pixel's centre: on a sharp edge (a bright bulb against a dark wall) a speck's added light can be a little off. It never darkens.
- MaxDistance near 1000 at 1080p: the farthest specks' discs can reach about 2 units, close to the 2.8-unit margin the wobble leaves inside a cell, so a far speck may rarely show a clipped edge.
- First-person weapon: specks nearer than 24 units are not drawn, and the depth test keeps specks behind the weapon hidden.
- RenderScale (FSR / DLSS LowResEffects): the passes draw into the rect like every pre-tonemap effect; the pixel size follows the drawn rows. The sun term is off in rect frames (no grid).
- Under DXVK (with or without the Vegas Shaders extension) the effect draws through D3D9 like any effect with no Vulkan port (no ExtRender), which DXVK translates; nothing extra is needed.

### 9. What correct / wrong looks like

Correct: in a lamp-lit room, a few dozen faint specks hang in the air near the lamps, drifting slowly sideways and up, each wobbling; looking towards a lamp they glint noticeably, with the lamp behind you they are faint. Walking does not drag them along (they are fixed in the air). Away from lamps there are none. Off (Enabled false) the frame is unchanged.

Wrong: specks moving with the camera (the origin or drift is broken); specks in dark air; specks that pop or jump every few seconds (a wrap not hidden); blocky or flickering specks; a visible boundary at MaxDistance; specks drawn over the weapon or through walls; any darkening; anything at all with no lamp near.

Log: every 5 seconds while the effect is on for the cell type, one line `UNOFFICIAL DustMotes: drawn | not drawn (...)`: lamps seen and picked, MaxDistance, Brightness, Density, the sun share, DebugView, the buffer pixel's angle, whether the chain is on, and each picked lamp's distance, reach and linear colour.

### 10. The first bench (2026-11-03): nothing visible, and the fix

Bench slot C, 1920 x 1080, saloon16 and int23, shots off / defaults / strong (Density 1, Brightness 3): the strong shot pixel-identical to off. Debug run (DebugView 1 and 2, the log line above):
- The passes were fine: DebugView 1 showed the lamp reaches over the whole room (the half-resolution pass runs and the full-resolution pass reaches the frame), DebugView 2 showed white specks.
- The specks were almost black. Three factors multiplied: a speck smaller than its minimum width lost light by the square of its size ratio (at 1 to 2 m, 0.06-unit specks kept 5 to 20 %); the lamp falloff `(1 - x^2)^2 / (1 + 4 x^2)` gave a few % in most of a lamp's reach (0.04 at 80 %); and the bilinear upsample spreads a one-buffer-pixel speck over four screen pixels.
- Fix: the light lost goes by the size ratio (not the area ratio), the minimum width is 0.8 buffer pixels, the falloff is `1 - x^2` (about the game's own), Size defaults to 0.15. Also: the lamps now come from the full GetNearbyLights list (all 64 shadow slots + the tracked ones, as LampHaze), with the reach the walls get, instead of the 12-entry TESR_ShadowLightPosition (the debug run logged 30 lamps seen, 8 picked, in saloon16).
- After it (same scenes): saloon16 shows a few subtle warm specks at the defaults and clear glinting ones at strong; int23 (camera looking down at a bright casino floor, short views) shows only a few faint ones even at strong: low contrast against bright surfaces, as expected.

### Idle targets (2026-11-12)
With `[Main.Main.Performance] TargetReleaseSeconds` above 0 (default 20) the texture manager keeps 1 x 1 stand-ins in place of this effect's render targets while it is off (not made at start-up when it starts off; released that many seconds after it is switched off; made again, zero filled, before the frame draws when it is switched on). Which targets and why that is safe here: its buffer is drawn and read only in RenderFrame, behind the switch. A new reader or writer of these targets outside the effect's own switch, or a copy of their pointers kept past a frame, breaks this: then answer false in `TargetParkable` (the header). See `core/TextureManager.h`.

## Exposure

Source of this spec: the rewritten shaders (wave 3 buffers, branch claude/rw3-buffers): src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Exposure.fx.hlsl (D3D9) and src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Exposure.frag (Vulkan). The C++ (src/effects/Exposure.cpp) is unchanged. Written from the previous spec and the C++; the old shader was not read.

### 1. Purpose

Exposure is Vegas Shaders' automatic brightness correction. It measures how bright the frame is (through the AvgLuma result) and darkens or brightens the whole picture toward a pivot brightness, so that a dark interior, a bright exterior at noon or a night scene do not stay at the same level. The picture settles gradually; the correction is one multiplication of the frame's linear colour, so colours keep their hue.

### 2. When it runs

- In RenderEffectsPreTonemapping, in the HDR block, after AvgLuma and before bloom, lens and the LUT.
- Enabled: [Shaders.Exposure.Status] Enabled (default true).
- Skipped when SkipIdentityExposure is on and MaxBrightness (TESR_ExposureData.y) is exactly 0 (the usual night case). The C++ skips the draw; nothing changes in the shader.
- Each frame, UpdateConstants marks AvgLuma as required; the four constants are recomputed on a settings or day-light change.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures

- TESR_RenderedBuffer (stage 0, LINEAR, CLAMP): the frame before exposure.
- TESR_AvgLumaBuffer (stage 1, LINEAR, CLAMP): the 1 x 1 texel from AvgLuma. Only its G channel (the animated average luma) is read.

#### Constants

- TESR_ExposureData (float4): x = MinBrightness (the pivot), y = MaxBrightness (the strength), z and w = adaptation speeds (read by AvgLuma, not here).

### 4. Settings

Unchanged: [Shaders.Exposure.Main] MinBrightness (0.2), MaxBrightness (0.5), DarkAdaptSpeed (0.2), LightAdaptSpeed (0.2), Histogram (false), HistogramLowPercent (10), HistogramHighPercent (5); [Shaders.Exposure.Night] (MinBrightness 0.3, MaxBrightness 0); [Shaders.Exposure.Interiors] (MinBrightness 1.0, MaxBrightness 0.3); [Shaders.Exposure.Status] Enabled; [Main.Main.Performance] SkipIdentityExposure.

### 5. Passes and outputs

#### Pass (technique unnamed, one pass, PS ExposurePS)

- Target: the chain's spare texture or the render target, full frame, A16B16G16R16F.
- Output: RGB = the corrected colour, delinearised (the input is linearised first, the correction is applied in linear, and the result is delinearised again). Alpha = 1.

### 6. Behaviour details

#### The correction

Let A = AvgLuma's G, m = MinBrightness, M = MaxBrightness, L = the linear colour.
- A and m are first floored at 1e-6 (new guard; see section 9).
- up = max(ln(A / m), 0) + 1, down = max(ln(m / A), 0) + 1, F = up / down.
- Output (linear) = lerp(L, L / F, M). Then delinearised. Alpha 1.

A screen brighter than the pivot is darkened by a factor that grows with the log of the ratio; a darker one is lifted the same way. The factor is the same for R, G and B, so the hue is kept.

#### Guards (changed)

- Divide by zero and ln(0): a black frame drives A toward zero. A is now floored at 1e-6, so ln and the division stay finite and the output is finite (black stays black). Before, the shader had no guard.
- A pivot of 0 (not a default) is floored the same way.

#### Adaptation

Not in this shader: AvgLuma's G moves toward the new average by at most dt x speed per frame.

#### Interaction

Bloom, lens and the LUT run after it. Fog, AO and the sky effects run before it.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Exposure.frag: the same formula line for line, with the same floors. Set 1 binding 0 = the frame (texture size from textureSize), binding 1 = the AvgLuma texel (read at (0.5, 0.5)). Push: offset 0 = exposure data (x pivot, y strength), offset 16 = the sampler heap index (linear, clamp).
- The C++ skip (MaxBrightness 0) applies before either path.

### 8. Look and cost

Correct: a dim room lifts toward the interior pivot without a flash; a bright sky is pulled down gently; hue kept; changes take a second or two.

Typical wrong: pumping (adaptation too fast, see AvgLuma); a black or grey patch after a long black screen (the floor keeps it black now); washed-out colours (strength too high).

Cost: one full-screen pass, two reads per pixel. Skipped at night with the default MaxBrightness 0.

### 9. Open questions

1. Fixed: the black-frame guard (floor 1e-6 on A and the pivot). Not checked on a GPU.
2. Constant refresh on an interior/exterior switch with no day-light change: not traced in this pass (C++).
3. The defaults file describes MinBrightness and MaxBrightness as thresholds; the code and this shader use a pivot and a strength. Kept as the code does.
4. TESR_DebugVar is not read.
5. No clamp after the correction; a large negative exposure could exceed the half-float range. Not checked.
6. Night MaxBrightness 0 disables the pass; kept.

### 10. GT7 adaptation ([Shaders.Exposure.Main] Mode 1)

An alternative to the classic correction above, chosen with `Mode` (0 = classic, the default; 1 = GT7). Everything in sections 1-9 is Mode 0 and is unchanged: Mode 1 draws technique 1 ("GT7") of both AvgLuma.fx and Exposure.fx instead of technique 0.

#### What it does

Auto-exposure and auto white balance after the design Polyphony Digital presented in "Driving Toward Reality: Physically Based Tone Mapping and Perceptual Fidelity in Gran Turismo 7" (SIGGRAPH 2025, slides 188-222):
- Two adaptation stages: short-term (pupil and neural gain: a few EV in seconds) inside the range of a long-term one (receptor sensitivity: minutes). Light adaptation is faster than dark adaptation.
- A light adaptation limit: scenes brighter than it get no correction, so full sun stays as the Tonemapping Exposure set it ("mildly glaring"). The limit is defined after the Tonemapping Exposure (display-referred) and converted back with the Exposure in use, so it follows every setup's calibration. The dark end is 3 EV below it, so nights stay dark.
- Metering of the light reaching the eye from the whole field of view outdoors (cosine-weighted), not only the screen: the rendered sky dome (Sky's spherical-harmonic projection, TESR_SkyIrradiance), whether the sky is open around the camera (the top-down height map Skylighting uses, TESR_OrthoMapBuffer), the sun shadow at the camera and the lit and shaded levels seen on screen (TESR_ShadowAtlas, one cascade lookup). Falls back to the screen plus the sky on screen where those maps are missing (interiors, sun shadows off).
- Auto white balance toward D65 from luminance-weighted references (the frame and the weather's sun, sky and ambient colours), steadied by a faint virtual light, partly blended with the ideal light and limited to a camera-like range (2500-10000 K, small tint).
The slides give the structure, not the numbers: every value marked ESTIMATE in Effects/Includes/GT7Adaptation.hlsl is this port's choice.

#### Settings (per Main / Night / Interiors, blended as the other values)

- `AdaptExposure` [0-1], 1: auto-exposure strength.
- `AdaptWhiteBalance` [0-1], 1: white balance strength.
- `AdaptWeatherLight` [0-1], 1 outdoors / 0 indoors: how much the weather's lights count in the white balance against the screen's colour.
- MinBrightness, MaxBrightness and the adapt speeds are not used in Mode 1. Calibrate the Tonemapping Exposure once in full midday sun.

#### Constants

- TESR_GT7AdaptData (float4): x AdaptExposure, y AdaptWhiteBalance, z AdaptWeatherLight, w Mode.
- Also read: TESR_HDRData.y (the Tonemapping Exposure, for the limit), TESR_SunColor, TESR_SkyColor, TESR_SunAmbient, TESR_SunDirection.

#### Pass (technique GT7, PS ExposureGT7PS)

Reads the frame and AvgLuma's GT7 state (r: short-term level, a: colour temperature in mired); white-balances the linear colour (Bradford, toward D65, tint from the weather lights), then multiplies it by 2^(max(limit - r, 0) x AdaptExposure). Delinearised, alpha 1. Until the state is initialised (a below 50) the colour passes unchanged.

#### C++

- EffectChain draws Exposure::Technique() for both effects; SkipIdentityExposure skips the pass in Mode 1 only when both strengths are 0 (Exposure::IsIdentity).
- When Mode changes, the AvgLuma texel is cleared so neither technique reads the other's state (it re-initialises on the next frame).
- Vulkan: no port; Exposure::ExtRender returns false for technique 1, so the D3D9 pass draws it under NvrDxvkPasses too. AvgLuma::RenderCompute stands aside in Mode 1 (AvgLumaCompute and Histogram are not used).

#### Look and cost

Correct: full sun unchanged; shade under open sky brightened a little, held back by the sky; walking indoors brightens by about 2 EV within seconds and up to 3 EV over minutes; walking out gives a short glare; nights brighten at most 3 EV; blue moonlight and warm interiors partly neutralised.
Cost: AvgLuma's GT7 pass is one pixel (about 1070 ps_3_0 slots: 100 screen samples, 32 field directions with three height-map lookups each, a few shadow lookups); the Exposure pass is one full-screen pass, about 200 slots.

#### Open questions

1. Not tested on a GPU in this form (written against 1.0.2, both effects compile with d3dcompiler_47 for fx_2_0 / ps_3_0, technique 0 compiles to the same code as before).
2. Debug's view of AvgLuma reads r as a luma; in Mode 1 it is a log2 level.

## Flashlight

Restructured 2026-11-04. The old post-process flashlight (Flashlight.fx and its Vulkan passes), the FlashlightBeam shaft, the
MaterialLight relight (NewVegas/Hooks/MaterialPass.cpp) and the separate Interiors look are gone; their code and spec are in
VegasShaders-backups/before_flashlight_restructure_20261104.

What it is now:
- `Flashlight.cpp` (FlashlightEffect) draws nothing. Each frame, while the Pip-Boy light's magic effect is on, it places the light (camera,
  head in third person, or the weapon: `AttachToWeapon`, always while aiming), colours it with the game's "Pipboy" system colour (the
  Pip-Boy HUD colour of the display settings) and publishes it as the spot list ShadowManager draws the shadow map for. Its
  `Flashlight.fx` is a stub so the effect loads and keeps its menu switch.
- `core/FlashlightDeferred.h` + `Shaders/FlashlightDeferred.hlsl`: the spot light on the G-buffer (cone, `Falloff`, PCF shadow map,
  cookie), `Dimmer` its brightness and `Distance` its reach.
- `effects/Grass`: the grass's own beam (card and GPU grass) with its shadow; grass also casts into the flashlight's shadow map.
- `core/FlashlightLamp.h` (`GameLight`): the Pip-Boy light moved onto the beam, so the game lights what the G-buffer does not hold, with a
  per-pixel cone in the object shaders; `core/EnvLightFade.h` shapes the env-map shine of light-fade assets per pixel.

- Hair and eyes (`HairAndEyes`, 2026-11-12): `Shaders/Includes/FlashBeam.hlsl` gives the hair shaders (`SM3002.pso`, `SM3003.pso`, their
  vertex shader `SM3003.vso`) and the eye variant (`ObjectTemplate.hlsl` NVR_EYE) the same beam; see the section below.

Settings: `[Shaders.Flashlight.Main]` Dimmer, Distance, AttachToWeapon; `[Shaders.Flashlight.Deferred]` the light pass's options;
`[Shaders.Flashlight.Status]` Enabled.

### Hair and eyes (2026-11-12, `[Shaders.Flashlight.Deferred] HairAndEyes`, default on, live)
Why: the game's own hair, brows and beards are blended without an alpha test, so the game draws them in its sorted blended list,
after the main list and so after the light pass, through none of the per-object hooks. The pass never lights them, and the game lamp
reached them unshaped (full strength, no cone, no shadow, on every head in its reach) or not at all (GameLight off, the lamp not among
the hair's three lamps, path tracing). Eyeballs do write records, and the pass lit them like a smooth object: a flat veil in the
light's colour over iris and pupil.
What it does (`core/FlashlightDeferred.h` BeamBegin / BeamShow, constants `TESR_FlashBeam[5]`):
- the hair and eye shaders add the beam themselves: reach, Falloff, cone, NearFade, HotspotLimit, the shadow map (16 point taps) and
  for hair the lens pattern (Cookie); hair with its own lamp terms (diffuse + strand highlight), the eye with its wrapped diffuse;
- the hair shader leaves the flashlight's game lamp out of its lamps while the beam is on (the lamp in the beam's own direction);
- the eye marks its record's distance as "far" (60000), which the pass skips by its reach test; hair drawn in the main list
  (alpha-tested hair of a hair mod) writes the same mark over the record under it (`core/CapturedNormals.h` BeamMarkers);
- (upstream 1.1.2, Character Kit Remake 4844 one-bit alpha-test hair) hair drawn WITHOUT blend in the main list also clears the
  albedo record (a = 0, unwritten) and the material record (model 0) under it and writes the "far" mark, with the beam on or off, so
  DeferredSun, the lamp pass and the skin passes no longer light the scalp's skin record over the hair; the deferred skin's blur and
  highlight-share fills (`core/DeferredSkin.h` FillBlurTargets) skip pixels whose albedo a is below 0.5. The SM3002/SM3003 hair
  shaders already write 0 to COLOR1/COLOR2; no shader change;
- the beam is there from the main list's start to the end of the world's pass; never in reflections, the first-person pass, a
  scope's camera, with the light or the key off, without the G-buffer, or while path tracing replaces the picture.
Not done: the eye has no lens pattern; SoftEdges (the pass's wrap) is not applied to hair or eyes; under the game's MSAA (DXVK) a
marked pixel next to an unmarked one resolves to "far" as a whole (one pixel of rim, not checked in game); nothing changes under path
tracing. Log: `UNOFFICIAL FlashlightDeferred: HairAndEyes on ...` once, `CapturedNormals: HairAndEyes: ...` with the 600-frame lines
(hair shader binds before / after the pass, marks, eye draws).
Correct: hair in the beam is lit and shadowed like the head it sits on, dark outside the cone; an eye keeps its iris and pupil.
Wrong: hair twice as bright as the skin beside it (the game lamp was not left out), a black ring round an eye, unlit eyes.

### Ray tracing without the path tracer (2026-11-07, perf-flashlight)
No RT lamp-shadow pass traces the flashlight: its game lamp is left out of every NVR lamp list (GetNearbyLights, so no cube slot and no
place in the RT lamp table; ManyLamps' table; the RT reflections' hit lamps; and since perf-flashlight ManyLights' / ExteriorManyLights'
table, PointShadowForward::NoteManyLamp), and the light pass's shadow is its own spot map, which leaves the player out. So
FirstPersonBody, SameFrame and the RT shadow denoiser change nothing on the beam (host scene `flashhead`: a lamp in the hidden head is
traced as if the body were absent anyway). What RT does change: RT reflections replace the Fresnel share of a shiny surface's lit colour,
the beam's light included, with a reflection whose off-screen hits are lit without the flashlight (mirrors, chrome, gold: the spot fades
there). Checks: DebugView 1 / 2 here, `[Shaders.Reflections.RT] Enabled = false` for the reflections. docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo)
"Team perf-flashlight".

### Path tracing (2026-11-07)
Under PTRealtime / PTReplace the path tracer lights the picture itself; the flashlight is passed to it as lamp 0 with its cone
(core/RtPathTrace.cpp Lamps, Compute/Rt/RtPathTrace.comp LampSpot; docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) section 53). Before, it showed only on
the few pixels where the path-traced picture falls back to the game's frame.

## GearWeather

Code: `src/core/GearWeather.h` (no folder in `src/effects`; called from `src/core/DeferredFirstPerson.h`), shader
`src/hlsl/NewVegas/Shaders/GearWeather.hlsl` (loaded at startup by `GearWeather::Load`, no template). Settings `[Main.Main.Quality]`
GearWeather* in `resource/defaults/Core.toml`. Test only; needs `DeferredFirstPerson` at startup. The header comments of both files are the
full reference (the id stencil, the gear-local pattern, the cost design); this section is the summary.

### 1. Purpose
Weather on the player's first-person gear (gun, hands, sleeves) in the deferred path: rain wets it, snow frosts it, sandstorms and dry wind
dust it, running through puddles muddies it. Deferred: the pass changes the view model's G-buffer (albedo, roughness, metal, shading normal)
after the first-person records and before the first-person sun and lamp passes, so every light shades the changed surface.

### 2. Layers and timing (`GearWeather::Step`, mirrored by `tests/fd_firstperson_math.py` section 4)
- wet: up in about 20 s of full rain (or at once under water); dries in about 3 min outdoors, 90 s indoors.
- frost: up in about 60 s of a snow weather; melts in 45 s indoors, 4 min outdoors.
- sand: up in about 30 s of a full sandstorm, slowly in a strong dry wind; fades over 5 min outdoors, 10 min indoors.
- mud: up in about 45 s of running through wet ground once the rain has stopped (the puddles); dries over 10 min (6 min indoors).
- Rain (2026-11-26 rain fix): while rain falls outdoors, frost, sand and mud do not build and are rinsed off in about 10 s, so wet gear shows
  the wet layer alone. Under water all three wash off in about 5 s.

### 3. Look (the shader)
- Frost, sand and mud lie in blotches of `GearWeatherScale` units (value noise in the part's own space).
- Wet (2026-11-26 rain fix) is one even film: albedo x 0.55 (porous) to x 0.95 (metal), roughness down to 0.28 (skin 0.3) and never raised,
  the normal leaning 0.35 toward the smooth one. Small beads sit on it: at most one drop per 0.4-unit cell of the part's space (0.1-0.22
  units across, the same size whatever `GearWeatherScale`), in up to 60 % of the cells at full wet (30 % on skin); inside a drop the
  roughness goes to 0.1 and the normal bulges outward from its middle (tilt up to 0.45 at the rim). Skinned parts read the beads from
  their nearest bone.
- Why (2026-11-26, proven on the bench): each dry layer's cover added a noise term that did not scale with its level, so mud, sand and
  frost at level 0 still covered the spots where the noise is high. The pass runs only while some layer is above 0, so dry gear looked
  clean, but rain (wet > 0) switched the pass on and those spots showed as big pale tan and grey camo blotches (bench run
  20261126-145648-slotA, `GearWeatherForce 1`: the blotches with wet alone). Fix: each dry layer's cover x saturate(level x 4). The old wet
  layer also followed the 3-unit noise with roughness 0.07 (mirror glints in patches), and sand faded slowly in rain (40 s); both changed
  as above.
- Brightness: the pass never touches COLOR0 (the record's ambient and sky light), so under sky light wet gear is exactly as bright as dry.
  The sun's and the lamps' diffuse only lose light (darker albedo; at a lower roughness a smaller diffuse keep and more Fresnel); their
  highlight gathers the same light into a tighter lobe (a sheen at 0.28, small glints on the beads), so a highlight can be brighter than
  the dry one where it lands, nowhere else.

### 4. Settings
GearWeather (off), GearWeatherStrength, GearWeatherWet / Frost / Sand / Mud (each layer's strength), GearWeatherSpeed (rates),
GearWeatherSkin (bare skin's share of the dry layers), GearWeatherScale (dry blotch size, 3 units), GearWeatherForce (test: 0 auto, 1 wet,
2 frost, 3 sand, 4 mud, 5 all at 0.6), GearWeatherDebug (1: the covers as colours: red mud, green sand, blue frost, grey wet, white beads).

### 5. Checking it
- Offline: `tests/fd_firstperson_compile.py` + `template_compile --flow --device --batch` (GearWeather native and DXVK define lists);
  `tests/fd_firstperson_math.py` (the timings above).
- Bench / in game: `GearWeatherForce 1` outdoors on an overcast day: the gun and gloves evenly darker with a soft sheen and small bright
  drops, no pale patches; `GearWeatherDebug 1`: an even grey over the parts with small white dots. `GearWeatherForce 5`: the dry layers'
  blotches over the film. With the F10 GPU timers the log's `GEAR WEATHER` line gives the amounts and inputs every 240 frames.

### 6. Upstream 1.1.5 merge (2026-11-27)
- Upstream's dry layers are taken: an even `float3 GWField`, splatters, grains, ice and flakes, plus the `GW_Anim` constant (c76). Our wet
  film and beads stay as the wet layer (no mirror look: 88c9108 / 38de485 removed it on purpose). On the upstream mud and sand layers we added
  `saturate(level x 4)` fades, our 11-26 rain-fix rule (no blotches while the rain rinses them).
- ps_3_0 slot count of `GearWeather.hlsl`: 1918 (main 980, upstream 1737). Proven offline in the merge; not run in game, so the look of the
  new layers on gear is unchecked.

## GodRays

*README title: Effect spec: GodRays*

light-shaft effect after the sandstorm and before exposure and bloom.

### 1. Purpose

When the sun is up in an exterior and bright sky (or the sun's own disc and glare) sits near or beside the view, soft shafts of light
fan out from the sun's screen position across the picture. The shafts only come from bright sky pixels, never from geometry, and they
are added to the lit scene in linear light, tinted by the sun colour (warmer near the horizon). The player sees a haze of light
beams that is strongest when the sun is low and bright, and gone when the sun is out of view under a dim sky.

### 2. When it runs

Frame order (src/core/ShaderManager.cpp, the frame function around the god rays block at about line 2617):
- It runs after the volumetric light composite (volumetric fog and the sun-lit volumetric light block) and after the sandstorm block.
- It runs before the DLSS Ray Reconstruction point (TAA RayReconstruct), and before the exposure, bloom, lens, LUT and tonemap steps.
- It is called unconditionally from the frame function with: target = the frame's current render target, scene copy = the game's
  RenderedSurface (TheTextureManager->RenderedSurface), SourceBuffer = the source surface, technique index 0, clear = true. The effect
  decides for itself whether to draw. Its GPU timer is "God rays" and its pass timers are "GR sky mask (half)", "GR light mask
  (half)", "GR blur 1 (half)", "GR blur 2 (half)", "GR blur 3 (half)", "GR combine (full)"; a reduction timer is "GR max reduce".

Enable and skip rules (all in the effect's own render entry):
- The effect must be loaded (its .fx compiled) and enabled. Enabled comes from the menu switch of the effect: the key
  [Shaders.GodRays.Status] Enabled (default true), the menu's "Status" kind.
- The per-frame gate (exterior and day only): exterior cell (not interior) AND not underwater AND day light above 0.5. Nothing is drawn
  in interiors, underwater, or when the day light is 0.5 or less (night, and the dark part of dawn and dusk).
- No weather or menu rule is in the GodRays code itself. The menu-open case was not traced beyond the frame call (see section 9).
- Rect frames (RenderScale, TESR_RenderScale / RenderRect active): the dedicated D3D9 path still runs, with its passes limited to the
  rect; the packed (legacy) path draws nothing in a rect frame; the Vulkan path is off in rect frames (section 7).
- Once a skip decision is made the frame is left as it is (input = output), and no frame-chain commit happens for that frame.
- SkipIdle (section 5 / 6): with the sun well outside the view and the sky known to be empty, the effect draws nothing for that frame.

Day light: the game-driven day/night value (0 to 1, a linear ramp across each sunrise and sunset window, plus a smoothstep curve used
for blending, transition curve power [Main.Transitions.Main] TransitionCurvePower, default 1.0).

### 3. Inputs

#### 3a. Textures (D3D9 effect, explicit registers; the register is the slot)

| Name | Register | What it holds | Filter / address |
|---|---|---|---|
| TESR_RenderedBuffer | s0 | the current scene at render-target size (the frame's RenderedSurface copy). Read by the sky mask, the light mask and the combine. | linear, clamp |
| TESR_DepthBuffer | s1 | the combined linear depth (CombineDepth): first channel holds view depth divided by the far distance | linear, clamp |
| TESR_SourceBuffer | s2 | the scene copy passed in as the source surface. Read only by the legacy (packed) technique. | linear, clamp |
| TESR_AvgLumaBuffer | s3 | declared, not read by any active code (only in a comment) | linear, clamp |
| NVR_GodRaysBuffer | s5 | the ray buffers (half, or quarter with GodRaysLowRes). Written by the sky mask, light mask, blurs; read by the next pass and the combine. Bound by the C++ per pass. | linear, clamp |
| NVR_GodRaysMax | s6 | the light-mask maximum reduced to 1 x 1 (SkipEmpty), or a reduction step; also the sky-mask maximum for SkipIdle. | point, clamp |

The register numbers are explicit in the D3D9 source, so declaration order is not what binds these. s4 is unused.
On the Vulkan side the same textures are set 1, binding index = slot index (section 7).

#### 3b. Constants (registered by the C++ with the shader constant table; the shader reads these names)

| Name | Components (meaning, units) |
|---|---|
| TESR_ReciprocalResolution | x, y: 1 / scene width and 1 / scene height (texels). z: used as an aspect factor for the blur direction (see open questions). w: unused. |
| TESR_GameTime | declared, not read by GodRays. |
| TESR_SunColor | rgb: the sun's colour (linear-light conversion applied by the shader). |
| TESR_GodRaysRay | x: intensity (RayIntensity). y: ray length multiplier (RayLength). z: RayDensity, copied in but read by no shader (dead). w: SunsetGlareFix flag, 1 = on, 0 = off (section 4). |
| TESR_GodRaysRayColor | rgb: custom ray colour (Coloring RayR, RayG, RayB). w: Saturate, 0 to 1, how much of the custom colour replaces the sun colour. |
| TESR_GodRaysData | x: LightShaftPasses, copied in, read by no shader (dead; the sample count is fixed at 10). y: Luminance threshold. z: the per-frame day/night strength (computed each frame, see 3c). w: TimeEnabled flag, 1 = ray attenuation depends on the sun's height. |
| TESR_ViewSpaceLightDir | xyz: the sun's direction in view space. Not necessarily unit length (the code notes 0.707 at 45 degrees). Used unnormalised in the glare and the ray attenuation, and times the far distance for the sun's projected position. w: unused. |
| TESR_SunDirection | xyz: the sun direction used for the sun colour (the sun's disc position while exterior and day, else the light direction). |
| TESR_SunPosition | xyz: the sun disc's unit position in world space. z is negated when not (exterior and day light above 0.5); the effect's own maths uses z as the sun's height above the horizon. w: 1. |
| TESR_SunAmount | x: day time factor (0 at night, 1 by day) used by the sun colour. |
| TESR_SunsetColor | rgb: the sunset colour boost ([Shaders.Sky.Main] SunsetR, SunsetG, SunsetB; red by default). |
| TESR_CameraData | x: near distance, y: far distance (world units). |
| TESR_ProjectionTransform, TESR_InvProjectionTransform | 4 x 4 matrices (the depth and view-space helpers use them). |
| TESR_DebugVar, TESR_ShadowFade | declared, not read by GodRays. |

Shader-local constants the C++ also sets (NVR_*, registered only by the GodRays C++):
- NVR_GodRaysSkip: x = 1 when SkipEmpty is on and the light-mask reduction ran this frame (else 0); y = 1 when SkipIdle is on (so the
  combine returns the input exactly where the rays are zero); z = SkipIdle's limit (0.8 times the light-mask threshold, see 6).
- NVR_GodRaysReduce: xy = 1 / source size of the current reduction step.
- NVR_GodRaysLayout: xy = half-resolution extent / full extent (ratio of the ray buffer to the full target), zw = 1 / ray buffer
  dimensions. Lets one technique serve both the half and the quarter buffers.

#### 3c. Per-frame values the C++ computes

- TESR_GodRaysData.z = lerp(NightMultiplier, DayMultiplier, transitionCurve). transitionCurve is the day/night curve from the
  game's day light (section 2). So the strength moves from the night value to the day value across the sunrise and sunset windows.
- Sun height in the effect's maths: 1 minus the sun disc's elevation, clamped at zero from below (so 1 at the horizon, 0 at the
  zenith). "Sunset factor" = that height to the power 8: 1 at the horizon, near 0 with the sun high.

### 4. Settings

All keys read from resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml (the defaults file writes these sections with a leading underscore
in the headers; the code uses the name without it). Defaults are the toml values, which are what a fresh install gets.

| Section | Key | Type | Default (toml) | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shaders.GodRays.Status | Enabled | bool | true | the effect's on/off in the menu |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | DayMultiplier | float | 1.0 | strength by day (multiplies the combined rays). The ImGui look overlay also writes this key (it scales the GodRays value). |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | NightMultiplier | float | 0.5 | strength at night; applied through the day/night curve (see 3c, section 6) |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | RayIntensity | float | 1.0 | brightness of the bright sky pixels that cast rays (light-mask gain) |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | RayLength | float | 1.0 | how far each blur reaches (scales the blur step) |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | RayDensity | float | 1.0 | DEAD: copied to TESR_GodRaysRay.z, read by no shader |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | RayVisibility | float | 50.0 | DEAD: read into a C++ member, used nowhere |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | LightShaftPasses | float | 8 | DEAD: copied to TESR_GodRaysData.x, read by no shader ("Not used" in the defaults file) |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | Luminance | float | 0.8 | threshold for sky brightness that casts rays; lower = more sky casts rays. Sets the light-mask ramp width (about 15 times this value in luma). |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | SunGlareEnabled | bool | true | DEAD: read into a C++ member, used nowhere (the defaults file says it scales with the weather sun glare; no code does that) |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | TimeEnabled | bool | false | when on, ray attenuation grows with the sun's height (0.2 at the horizon to 4 high; blend by the sun's height to the power 4) instead of a constant 1 |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | SunsetGlareFix | bool | true | when on, the sunset colour boost is applied to the glare near the horizon only (see 6). Off: the boost is applied with the sun high and not at sunset (red tint all day). A missing key counts as on. |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | SkipEmpty | bool | false | exact speed-up: the light mask's maximum is reduced to 1 x 1; if zero, the blur passes return black at once |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | SkipIdle | bool | true | speed-up that is not exact in one case, using a check that is one or more frames old: draw nothing while the sun is well out of view and the sky is dim (section 6). Its own comments say release default off; the toml value is true. |
| Shaders.GodRays.Main | ProbeLog | int | 0 | diagnostic, development builds only; any value above 0 forces the D3D9 path (no Vulkan) |
| Shaders.GodRays.Coloring | RayR, RayG, RayB | float | 1.0 each | custom ray colour (linear-light conversion applied) |
| Shaders.GodRays.Coloring | Saturate | float | 0.0 | 0 = use the sun colour; 1 = use only the custom colour |
| Main.Main.ReducedQuality | GodRaysLowRes | bool | false | rays computed at quarter instead of half resolution (4 times fewer pixels; softer rays, shimmer in thin foliage) |
| Main.Main.Performance | NvrDxvkPasses | bool | true | the Vegas Shaders DXVK Vulkan route for the passes (section 7); also used by other effects |
| Main.Main.Quality | MaxQuality | bool | false | Max Quality: the ray buffers are made at full resolution (divisor 1) instead of half (or quarter). Needs restart. |
| Main.Transitions.Main | TransitionCurvePower | float | 1.0 | shape of the day/night curve used for the ray strength |
| Shaders.Sky.Main | SunsetR, SunsetG, SunsetB | float | (sky values) | the sunset colour boost (through TESR_SunsetColor) |

Keys hidden from the menu (2026-11-12): SunGlareEnabled and NvrDxvkPasses are shown only with Developer options (the DevOnly list
in the defaults file). SkipEmpty, SkipIdle, SunsetGlareFix, ProbeLog and LightShaftPasses are Internal (the Internal list there):
never shown, unless `[Main.Menu.Developer] ShowInternal = true` is typed into the settings file. Neither list is an on/off switch
by itself: hidden keys keep their saved values and work as before.

### 5. Passes and outputs

Render targets are created by the effect's texture setup (all A16B16G16R16F, i.e. 16-bit float RGBA):
- NVR_GodRaysBuffer0 and NVR_GodRaysBuffer1: half size of the scene (each side divided by 2, rounded up; 1 with Max Quality).
- NVR_GodRaysBufferLow0 and NVR_GodRaysBufferLow1: quarter size (divided by 4); used when GodRaysLowRes is on (and all four exist).
- Reduction chain NVR_GodRaysMax0 to NVR_GodRaysMax3 (half) and NVR_GodRaysMaxLow0 to NVR_GodRaysMaxLow3 (quarter): each step's
  side is the previous side divided by 8 (rounded up), until 1 x 1. At most 4 steps. A screen too large for 4 steps has no reduction
  (SkipEmpty and SkipIdle then do nothing).

Technique DedicatedGodRays (the main path, 6 passes, in this order; pass 0 is "SkyMask" etc.):

| Pass | Name | Reads | Writes | Size | Job |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | SkyMask | s0 scene, s1 depth | ray buffer 0 | half | the scene in sky pixels plus the sun glare, zero everywhere else (section 6) |
| 1 | LightMask | ray buffer 0 | ray buffer 1 | half | keeps only bright sky (threshold) and scales it up (section 6) |
| (SkipEmpty only) | technique GodRaysMaxReduce, pass Reduce | the light mask (ray buffer 1) | NVR_GodRaysMax chain | 8:1 steps | maximum of the light mask down to 1 x 1 |
| 2 | Blur1 | ray buffer 1 (and NVR_GodRaysMax as a skip) | ray buffer 0 | half | radial blur toward the sun, step 1/10 |
| 3 | Blur2 | ray buffer 0 | ray buffer 1 | half | radial blur, step 1/100 |
| 4 | Blur3 | ray buffer 1 | ray buffer 0 | half | radial blur, step 1/1000; its alpha = ray attenuation (section 6) |
| 5 | Combine | s0 scene, ray buffer 0 | the frame's render target | full | adds the rays to the scene in linear light, re-encodes (section 6) |

Each blur pass takes 10 taps along the line from the pixel to the sun's projected screen position (section 6 for the exact tap rule).

Technique GodRaysMaxReduce, pass "Reduce": 8 x 8 texels per output texel, output = largest of the three colour channels. Run after the
light mask when SkipEmpty is on (and, once, for the SkipIdle check).

Technique GodRaysIdleTest, pass "Test": one pixel inside an occlusion query (see section 6). Discards the pixel unless the sky
maximum is at least the limit (or is NaN); the query counts survivors.

Legacy technique, unnamed (technique index 0; used when the dedicated path cannot run): 6 passes in the same order (sky mask, light
mask, three blurs with the same steps and storeAttenuation 0, 0, 1, combine). It works in the top-left half of the target (packed)
with a copy into the scene copy for each pass. It requires exactly 6 passes. Used only as a fallback.

### 6. Behaviour details

Sky and depth:
- Sky test: a pixel counts as sky when its linear depth (combined depth divided by the far distance) is above 0.9. Only sky pixels
  pass the sky mask; every world pixel is exactly black there, so geometry never casts or receives rays from itself.
- The dedicated sky mask reads the depth first and returns black for world pixels, or when the horizon factor is 0, without reading
  the scene. Must match the legacy result on sky pixels.
- Glare: the sun glare is the dot product of the view-space sun direction and the normalised view direction of the pixel, raised to
  the 180th power, times a glare power (0.1 by day, 8 at the horizon, interpolated by the sunset factor), times the sun colour, added to
  the scene colour in linear light before the sky test.
- Sun colour in the glare: the sun colour (linear) plus the sunset colour boost weighted by the sunset factor. With SunsetGlareFix on
  the boost is weighted by the sunset factor (strong only near the horizon). With it off the boost is weighted by (1 minus the sunset
  factor): strong with the sun high, none at sunset. That is the old red tint.
- Horizon factor: the sky mask and the blur attenuation both use the sun's height. See open question 2: the arithmetic gives the
  horizon factor 1 when the sun is below the horizon, while the comments say black; the attenuation (below) is what stops the rays.

Light mask:
- Five texel-centred taps (the pixel and its four diagonal neighbours, one half-resolution texel away), averaged.
- Threshold: 2 with the sun at the zenith, falling to 0 at the horizon (the sun's height to the power 8 weights the fall). Rays only
  come from pixels whose luma passes the threshold, with a soft ramp of width 15 times Luminance above it.
- The result is multiplied by 100 and the intensity, clamped to 0 to 1 (so the light mask is 0 or close to 1 for bright sky).
- Consequence: at a high sun a dim sky (luma well under 2) produces an exactly black light mask. The sun disc (luma 5 and up)
  saturates it. This is what SkipIdle relies on.

Blur (each of the three passes):
- Centre: the sun's projected screen position (the view-space sun direction times the far distance, projected). It can be outside
  the screen.
- Direction: from the pixel to that centre, with the vertical part scaled by the aspect factor (section 7 and open question 3).
- 10 taps at distances i times the step (step times RayLength), i = 0 to 9. A tap is used only if its distance is at most the distance to
  the centre AND it lies inside the screen. Taps outside the screen are skipped, not clamped.
- The colour result is the sum of the used taps divided by one more than their count (an extra black sample): the rays are slightly
  dimmer near the edges of the shaft where few taps are in range.
- Alpha (only Blur3 keeps it): the ray attenuation, otherwise 1.
- Ray attenuation: the view-space sun direction dotted with the normalised view direction of the pixel, clamped at 0 from below,
  mapped to 0.5 to 1 (0.5 plus half of it), raised to the power 2.5. So it runs from about 0.18 (sun behind the camera) to 1 (sun
  straight ahead). Times the time factor when TimeEnabled is on (0.2 at the horizon to 4 high, blend by the sun's height to the power
  4). Forced to 0 when the sun is at or below the horizon (its height not less than 1 in the shader's units).

Combine:
- Input = scene (linear light) plus rays (ray buffer 0 after Blur3). Where the rays are exactly zero and SkipIdle is on, the combine
  returns the scene as it is (without the linear round trip, which changes about half the texels by one half-float step otherwise).
- Ray colour: the sun colour from the sun direction's elevation (brightened with elevation; sunset colour boost near the horizon
  times the day time factor), or the custom colour; mixed by Saturate. Multiplied by the strength (TESR_GodRaysData.z), then by the
  Blur3 attenuation (the alpha).
- Result: linear scene times (1 + 5 times the rays) plus 0.2 times the rays (rays clamped at 0), re-encoded. Ray values are not
  clamped at 1 here.

SkipEmpty (exact):
- The light mask's maximum at 1 x 1: if it is 0 (or not above 0), every blur pixel is black, so the blurs return black at once.
- Maximum, not average, so a single bright texel is never lost. Ray values after the skip are identical to the full run.

SkipIdle (approximate, guarded):
- Guard (checked each frame): the sun is at least 20 degrees above the horizon (sun height at least 0.342), AND the sun is at least
  12 degrees outside the view frustum (the test uses the true angle to the frustum's side planes), AND the projection is valid.
- Check: every 4th frame, while the guard holds, the sky mask is drawn, reduced to 1 x 1 and tested by an occlusion query around the
  one-pixel GodRaysIdleTest draw (the query is read the next frame or later, never waited on). The answer is "empty" when the count is 0
  and the answer is not more than 8 frames old.
- Limit: 0.8 times the light-mask threshold for the same sun height. A sky maximum below the limit proves the light mask is 0.
- While the guard holds and the sky is known empty, the effect draws nothing for that frame (the input is the output, no chain
  commit). On a check frame that finds the sky empty, the sky mask and the check run but the blurs and combine are left out.
- Any change (guard fails, query not ready, or the device has no occlusion queries) makes the sky unknown and the effect draws normally.
- Not exact in one case: something else brightens the sky within the few frames between two checks (a lightning flash). Rays then start
  up to about 8 frames late.

Frame-chain and buffers:
- The combine writes through the frame chain when the target is owned by it (the chain's output is then committed); otherwise the
  result is copied back into the scene copy. Full target size and format must match the scene copy (checked; mismatch = no rays).
- The D3D9 path clears the depth-stencil binding while it draws (leaves the game's depth-stencil bound when the ReShade depth compatibility
  option asks) and restores it after.
- Scissor: the dedicated D3D9 passes turn scissor off and set their own viewports. The combine restores the caller's scissor state.
- MSAA targets: not supported (no rays).

Errors and fallbacks:
- A failed dedicated pass sets dedicatedRenderFailed (the effect uses the legacy path for the session) and restores the scene copy.
- A failed Vulkan pass sets the Vulkan failure flag for the session (logged once) and the frame is redone with D3D9 (section 7).
- Output with NaN or infinite values: the sky mask's clip step does not discard NaN, so NaN can reach the light mask and the combine. The
  SkipIdle query treats NaN as "bright" (not empty) so a NaN sky never causes a skip. No other NaN guard exists in these passes.

Diagnostics (development builds only, release builds leave them out): ProbeLog 1 reads the maxima back and logs them; 2 also compares the
combine's input and output texel by texel; 3 validates the SkipIdle query against a read-back. Any ProbeLog above 0 forces the D3D9 path.

### 7. Vulkan path

Selection (C++, effects/GodRays.cpp, the effect's render entry):
- The Vulkan route is tried first when: the effect has not failed on Vulkan this session, the dedicated path has not failed, and
  DxvkCompute::PassesOn() is true. PassesOn() = [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses (default true) AND no RenderRect active AND the
  Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is present ("Available"). Test builds with ProbeLog above 0 skip it.
- The Vulkan dedicated path (RenderDedicated with the Vulkan flag) also needs: no scissor on the device, the five pipelines made, the
  two samplers, the depth texture (CombineDepth's combined depth), the scene texture, the constants present, and the target sizes
  as the D3D9 path requires. If any fails the same frame goes to the D3D9 dedicated path, then to the legacy path.
- The NvrDxvkKeepD3D9 setting (a comma list of effect names, default empty) can keep this effect on D3D9 (test tool).
- Full-screen Vulkan passes (the combine) need Vegas Shaders DXVK extension 1.1 or later (the log says so). Without it the combine fails and
  the frame goes to D3D9.
- A failed Vulkan pass: logged as "pass GodRays failed to draw: its D3D9 technique is used from now on". The Vulkan path is then off
  for the session. Nothing of the frame has been committed at that point, so D3D9 draws the whole frame.
- The SkipIdle test (the 1 x 1 occlusion-query draw) stays on D3D9. Its reduction runs on Vulkan.

Pipelines and files (C++ names = SPIR-V file names in src\hlsl\NewVegas\Compute, built into .spv beside them):

| Pipeline name (C++) | Shader file (.frag) | .spv | Samplers | Textures in set 1 binding order |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| GodRaysSkyMask | GodRaysSkyMask.frag | GodRaysSkyMask.spv | linear | 0 scene, 1 combined depth |
| GodRaysLightMask | GodRaysLightMask.frag | GodRaysLightMask.spv | linear | 0 ray buffer 0 (sky mask) |
| GodRaysBlur | GodRaysBlur.frag | GodRaysBlur.spv | linear and point | 0 ray buffer read, 1 maximum (point), 2 combined depth |
| GodRaysCombine | GodRaysCombine.frag | GodRaysCombine.spv | linear | 0 scene, 1 ray buffer 0 (Blur3's output) |
| GodRaysMaxReduce | GodRaysMaxReduce.frag | GodRaysMaxReduce.spv | linear | 0 the step's source |

- GodRays.glsl is the shared include (no .spv of its own): the uniform block, the push block, the sampler macros, the projection and
  sun-height helpers, the smoothstep, and the standard colour helpers used by the shaders.
- Blur pass uses one shader for all three blurs; the step and the storeAttenuation come from the push block (values 1/10, 1/100,
  1/1000; storeAttenuation 0, 0, 1).
- Reduction: one shader, one 8 x 8 step per draw. The point sampler is the one for the maximum read in the blur.
- The skip, ray-size and reduction vectors are the same as the D3D9 technique's.
- UnderwaterGodrays.frag / .spv, and the Underwater effect's own pipelines (effects/Underwater.cpp), are NOT used by GodRays. The
  volumetric light, volumetric fog and volumetric smoke files are NOT used by GodRays.

Uniform block (set 2, binding 0, std140 "Constants"), 320 bytes, filled once per frame by the C++ (one uniform buffer per frame via the
extension's uniform-buffer call), in this order:
1. projection matrix (TESR_ProjectionTransform) and inverse projection matrix (TESR_InvProjectionTransform), each 4 x 4, stored as
   D3DX holds them. The GLSL side multiplies with the matrix in the order that gives the same result as the D3D9 row-vector product
   (the transpose of the stored matrix). A new Vulkan version must do the same.
2. vec4 reciprocal resolution (TESR_ReciprocalResolution)
3. vec4 sun colour (TESR_SunColor)
4. vec4 ray (TESR_GodRaysRay)
5. vec4 ray colour (TESR_GodRaysRayColor)
6. vec4 data (TESR_GodRaysData)
7. vec4 view-space sun direction (TESR_ViewSpaceLightDir)
8. vec4 sun direction (TESR_SunDirection)
9. vec4 sun position (TESR_SunPosition)
10. vec4 sun amount (TESR_SunAmount)
11. vec4 sunset colour (TESR_SunsetColor)
12. vec4 camera data (TESR_CameraData: x near, y far)
13. vec4 layout (NVR_GodRaysLayout)

Push block (48 bytes, push constants, filled per draw by the C++):
- offset 0: vec4 skip (NVR_GodRaysSkip: x SkipEmpty reduction ran, y SkipIdle on, z SkipIdle limit)
- offset 16: vec4 reduce (NVR_GodRaysReduce: xy = 1 / source size of the step)
- offset 32: float blurStep (the blur's step argument)
- offset 36: float storeAttenuation (0 or 1)
- offset 40: uint linear sampler index (LINEAR, CLAMP; the extension fills it in from the heap)
- offset 44: uint point sampler index (POINT, CLAMP); used only by the blur pipeline

Sampler binding: a sampler binding with slot 0 is the linear sampler (push offset 40) and slot 1 the point sampler (push offset 44).
Sampled-image bindings are set 1, binding = slot = the order in the table above. The uniform buffer is set 2, binding 0.

Outputs: the sky mask, light mask, blurs and reduction write each whole target through the extension's draw-to call (one colour
target each, viewport = the whole target). The combine writes the frame through the full-screen call (last pass), so it goes into
the frame chain or the render target like the D3D9 combine.

### 8. Look and cost

Correct:
- Soft shafts that fan out from the sun's screen position across bright sky, fading with distance along each ray, with a visible
  tint from the sun colour (warm near the horizon, with SunsetGlareFix on).
- Nothing in the interior, underwater, at night, or where the sky is dim while the sun is out of view (SkipIdle on, the picture is the
  same as with the effect off).
- No rays across geometry: the edge of an object in front of the sky is sharp, not lit by rays from behind it.
- Day strength near DayMultiplier 1.0; the ramps across sunrise and sunset are smooth.

Typical wrong results:
- Rays over buildings, trees or the ground (sky test failed or the depth texture read in the wrong place).
- Rays at night, indoors, or under water.
- Red haze all day (SunsetGlareFix off).
- Shafts that flicker or shimmer when moving, most with GodRaysLowRes or in thin foliage.
- A hard line where the screen edge crosses a shaft (the screen-bound tap rule).
- A visible pop when SkipIdle switches (a frame that draws rays where the picture was clean), or rays arriving late after a lightning flash.
- A darkening of the whole frame (the combine must add, not multiply), or banding in the rays.
- Rays from a sun below the horizon (the attenuation must be zero there).

Cost class: multi-pass with per-pixel loops. Half-resolution work: sky mask (one scene read, one depth read), light mask (five reads),
three blurs (up to 10 reads each), plus one full-resolution combine. Plus, with SkipEmpty, a 4-step reduction (64 reads per output texel
at the first step). Quarter resolution (GodRaysLowRes) is about 4 times cheaper. The code comments measure the whole effect at about
0.45 ms per frame at game hours 8 to 16, and about 0.04 ms on frames the SkipIdle guard skips.

### 9. Open questions

1. Four keys are read but used by no shader: RayDensity (TESR_GodRaysRay.z), RayVisibility (C++ member only), SunGlareEnabled
   (C++ member only) and LightShaftPasses (TESR_GodRaysData.x). Confirm they are dead, or say what they should do. The defaults file
   says SunGlareEnabled scales with the weather sun glare and LightShaftPasses is "Not used", but the RayDensity text is not clear.
2. The sun-height horizon factor: the code comments say the sky mask is exactly black while the sun is below the horizon, but the
   arithmetic (1 minus the sun's height clamped at zero) gives the factor 1 below the horizon. The rays are then removed only by the
   attenuation (forced to 0 at or below the horizon). Which one is intended? The two give the same rays but not the same sky-mask
   values (the glare still shows in the sky mask below the horizon).
3. The meaning of the z component of TESR_ReciprocalResolution as the blur's aspect factor (the shader divides 1 by it). The register
   code that fills it was not read in this pass, so the exact layout (height over width, or width over height) is not confirmed.
4. TESR_ViewSpaceLightDir is not always unit length (the SkipIdle comment says 0.707 at 45 degrees). The glare power (raised to 180) and
   the ray attenuation use it unnormalised. Check how it is filled before a rewrite assumes unit length.
5. Default of SkipIdle: the defaults file has true, with a comment saying "PLAYTEST ON ... release default off", and the C++ default is
   false (overwritten by the file's value). Which default ships?
6. NightMultiplier: the effect is gated on day light above 0.5, and the strength curve is at least 0.5 to the curve power there. So the
   night value only applies in the upper half of the sunrise and sunset windows, and the strength is near DayMultiplier everywhere the
   effect runs. Confirm that is intended, or whether the gate should be lower.
7. Menu: the frame call is unconditional, and the effect has no menu rule of its own. What the menu and the loading screens show was not
   traced (the frame function's menu branch was not read).
8. D3D9 texture binding for s0 to s3: the effect's shared texture slots are bound by the base effect's constant-table call from the
   registered texture list; that list for GodRays was not traced.
9. The Vegas Shaders DXVK extension version shipped now: the combine needs 1.1; the version in the current extension was not checked.
10. The legacy (packed) path: reachable only after a dedicated-path failure, and the rect-frame case draws nothing there. Its real
    frequency in use was not measured.

### 10. Rewrite status (2026-11-02, commit bcb04003)

The new shader is src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/GodRays.fx.hlsl. Techniques: DedicatedGodRays (SkyMask, LightMask, Blur1, Blur2, Blur3,
Combine), GodRaysMaxReduce (Reduce), GodRaysIdleTest (Test), and the unnamed technique (the same six passes, see below). Explicit
slots: TESR_RenderedBuffer s0, TESR_DepthBuffer s1, NVR_GodRaysBuffer s5, NVR_GodRaysMax s6.

Bug and spec items:
- Blur and combine share the same maths as the spec (10 taps, step x RayLength, tap inside the screen and within the centre distance,
  the sum over count plus one; the attenuation in Blur3 alpha; the combine adds linear light with the strength and the attenuation).
- The combine returns the stored scene where the rays are exactly zero and SkipIdle is on (no linear round trip there).
- Ray colour from the sun direction's elevation (TESR_SunDirection.z), by the sunset boost and the day factor, or the custom colour.

Not done, or changed:
- The packed (legacy) path is not reproduced: the unnamed fallback uses the dedicated maths instead, so the top-left packing is gone.
- The aspect factor of the blur direction (TESR_ReciprocalResolution.z) is not applied (the direction is taken in uv units).
- The horizon factor of the sky mask (open question 2) is not applied; the attenuation still removes the rays below the horizon.
- The light mask threshold is 2 x (elevation to the power 8) and the output keeps the averaged colour, scaled by the ramp; the spec does not say whether
  the mask is colour or scalar, so colour was kept.
- Vulkan frags (GodRaysSkyMask, GodRaysLightMask, GodRaysBlur, GodRaysCombine, GodRaysMaxReduce) are not written.
- Rect frames: not handled here (the C++ does not draw the Vulkan path in rect frames anyway).

### Idle targets (2026-11-12)
