With `[Main.Main.Performance] TargetReleaseSeconds` above 0 (default 20) the texture manager keeps 1 x 1 stand-ins in place of this effect's render targets while it is off (not made at start-up when it starts off; released that many seconds after it is switched off; made again, zero filled, before the frame draws when it is switched on). Which targets and why that is safe here: the ray buffers and the reduction chains are used only from Render, behind the switch. A new reader or writer of these targets outside the effect's own switch, or a copy of their pointers kept past a frame, breaks this: then answer false in `TargetParkable` (the header). See `core/TextureManager.h`.

## Grass

The game's grass, re-shaded: the sun's shadow on the tufts, brightness and night brightness, normal maps with the sun's
highlight, the grass wind and the player's bend, grass shadows, the placed lamps and the flashlight on the grass, and the
optional GPU strand grass. Settings: the `[Shaders.Grass.*]` sections in `defaults.toml` (each key's comment says what it does).

This README is new (2026-11-22) and so far covers only the lamps in full; the other parts are described in the code comments
of `Grass.cpp`, `GpuGrass.cpp` and the shaders below until their sections are written.

Files:
- `Grass.cpp` / `Grass.h` (GrassShaders): constants, settings, the lamp list (`UpdateLights`), the grass's own flashlight beam.
- `GpuGrass.cpp` / `GpuGrass.h`: GPU strand grass (needs the DXVK fork 1.7-grass; off by default).
- Shaders (`src/hlsl/NewVegas/Shaders/`): `GRASS23x000TMS.pso.hlsl` (the grass PS; `GRASS23x000.pso.hlsl` includes it with the
  game's hard alpha cut), `GRASS23x000/001/002/003.vso.hlsl`, `GrassShadowCaster.pso.hlsl`; GPU grass: `Compute/GpuGrass.glsl`.

### Lamps on the grass (`[Shaders.Grass.Lights]`: Enabled, MaxLights 0-8, Strength)

Grass is never in the G-buffer's lit set: the deferred lamp pass does not light it, in the forward grass or in its deferred
record (`DeferredFoliage`). So the grass PS lights itself by up to 8 lamps, in both paths (`GrassLamps`).

What `UpdateLights` hands the shader each frame (`TESR_GrassLightData` c82: x lamps live, y Strength;
`TESR_GrassLightPosition[8]` c83: camera-relative position, w reach; `TESR_GrassLightColor[8]` c91: rgb colour x dimmer):
- every slot cleared first (a frame with fewer lamps leaves none behind; GPU grass copies the slots too);
- the active point lights of the world ShadowSceneNode, taken as ManyLamps' table takes them: not switched off for the view,
  not hidden, not flagged; the deferred flashlight's game lamp left out (the grass has its own beam, `TESR_GrassSpot`);
- reach = the lamp's own `Spec.r`; colour = `Diff` x `Dimmer` (capped at 1 without HDR) x the LOD dimmer;
- nearest first (distance minus reach), at most MaxLights.

In the shader, per lamp: the game's falloff `1 - d^2/r^2` through `LampAtt` (as the objects; with
`[Shaders.PBR.LampFalloff] Exterior/Interior` on, the physical shape), the colour decoded on its own (`PBRLight` per lamp, so
overlapping lamps add in linear light), and a wrapped diffuse `saturate((N.L + 0.35) / 1.35)`. The sum times `LinScale(Strength)`
and the tuft's light scale (`f_amount.x`, 0.25-1, as the game scales every light on a tuft). NightBrightness does not touch lamps.

History: upstream 1.1.2 (commit 6e85afb) "grass lamp blowout", hand-ported 2026-11-22. Before it the lamps came from
`ShadowLightPosition` / `LightPosition` (the shadow radius `Spec.r x LightRadiusMult`, 1.5 by default, and the raw Dimmer: no LOD
dimmer, no HDR cap, lamps the game had switched off included), the sum of all lamps was decoded at once (pow of a sum: blown-out
white where two lamps met), and `abs(N.L)` let a lamp below the ground light the blades from underneath.

What correct looks like: at night by a street light the grass is lit over the same pool as the ground under it, fading out at
the same edge, no brighter where two lamps overlap than the ground there, and dark on the side away from the lamp.
Wrong: a lit disc on the grass half again as wide as the ground's, white patches where lamps overlap, grass lit by a switched-off
or culled lamp, or glowing above a lamp under the terrain.

GPU grass (`Compute/GpuGrass.glsl`, `GG_LAMPS`) still lights its strands with the old per-lamp formula (gamma-space falloff,
`abs` wrap); it gets the new lamp list (true reach, LOD dimmer). Not ported: the strands light in the stored curve, not linear light.

Leaves are not part of this effect, but the same upstream fix touched them: `Includes/Leaf.hlsl` (the leaves' deferred record
with `DeferredFoliageLamps`, `LEAF_FORWARD_PLUS`) now takes each lamp's falloff through `LampAtt` as well.

## ImageAdjust

Rewritten 2026-11-02 (wave 3, branch claude/rw3-screen, commit 4a235236). The new shaders are
src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/ImageAdjust.fx.hlsl (D3D9) and src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/ImageAdjust.frag (Vulkan). The C++ is unchanged.
The behaviour in section 6 is the same as before (as the spec describes it); the stale header is FIXED (item 3 below).


### 1. Purpose

ImageAdjust is a set of global picture controls: brightness, contrast, saturation, a strength blend, and separate tints for the darker and lighter tones. Each control has three values, for daylight outdoors, night outdoors, and indoors, and the picture moves smoothly between them as the time of day changes. At the default values the effect does nothing and is not run. Set a value and the player sees a lighter or darker, flatter or punchier, more or less saturated picture, with shadows or highlights tinted a colour the player picks.

### 2. When it runs

- **Switch:** `[_Shaders.ImageAdjust.Status] Enabled`, default **true**. Even when on, the pass is skipped when the values leave the picture unchanged (see below).
- **Frame position:** in the post chain after Cinema and before Bodycam, SkyDither and Debug (`ShaderManager.cpp`, the ImageAdjust block). It sees the output of the earlier colour effects (Coloring, LUT, Sharpening, Cinema).
- **Skip rule (`ShouldRender`):** the effect runs if any of these hold, checked in this order:
  1. Saturation is not exactly 1 (the saturation step runs regardless of Strength).
  2. Otherwise, if Strength is exactly 0, the effect does not run.
  3. Otherwise, the effect runs if brightness, contrast, or any of the six tint values is not exactly 1.
  4. Otherwise it does not run.
- **Day / night / interior blend:** the values are chosen per frame by the game state. Outdoors, each value is a linear blend between the Night value and the Main (day) value, weighted by the sky's transition curve (0 = night, 1 = day). Indoors, only the Interiors value is used. The C++ `GetTransitionValue` rule is: exterior returns `lerp(Night, Day, transitionCurve)`, interior returns `Interior`.
- **Menus and special cases:** no special rule; it runs whenever the post chain runs and the switch is on, subject to the skip rule.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (slot order)

| Slot | Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | `TESR_RenderedBuffer` | The picture arriving at ImageAdjust | Linear, clamp |

It is the only texture. No depth is read.

#### Constants

| Name | Register | Components and meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `TESR_ImageAdjustData` | not fixed (registered by name) | x = brightness (multiplier of the tone-weighted light, at least 0); y = contrast (scale around mid-grey; 1 = unchanged); z = saturation (1 = unchanged, 0 = grey, above 1 = more colour); w = strength (0 = no change, 1 = full effect; the blend between the original and the adjusted picture) |
| `TESR_DarkAdjustColor` | not fixed (registered by name) | rgb = tint multiplier for the darker tones (1 = none). Gamma-encoded values (the shader raises them to 2.2 to work in linear light). w unused. |
| `TESR_LightAdjustColor` | not fixed (registered by name) | rgb = tint multiplier for the lighter tones (1 = none). Same encoding as the dark tint. w unused. |

Units: all dimensionless. Values are colours in 0..1 display space.

The C++ computes each of the eleven values with the day / night / interior rule in section 2. Its `UpdateConstants` writes the three constants.

### 4. Settings

All read in `ImageAdjust.cpp` (`UpdateSettings`). The same set of keys exists for each of the three sets (`Main` = day, `Night`, `Interiors`). The toml gives no range for any of them, so ranges are "not stated" (section 9). Each default is 1.0.

| Key | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|
| `Brightness` | 1.0 | Overall brightness. Multiplies the light-weighted part of the picture (section 6). 1 = unchanged. |
| `Saturation` | 1.0 | Colour strength. Below 1 washes towards grey, above 1 adds colour. |
| `Contrast` | 1.0 | Contrast around mid-grey. Above 1 harder, below 1 flatter. |
| `Strength` | 1.0 | Blend of the whole adjustment. 0 = off (except saturation, see section 2). |
| `DarkColorR`, `DarkColorG`, `DarkColorB` | 1.0 | Tint of the darker tones, per channel. |
| `LightColorR`, `LightColorG`, `LightColorB` | 1.0 | Tint of the lighter tones, per channel. |

Section and key names:
- `[_Shaders.ImageAdjust.Main]`: daylight outdoors.
- `[_Shaders.ImageAdjust.Night]`: night outdoors.
- `[_Shaders.ImageAdjust.Interiors]`: indoors.
- `[_Shaders.ImageAdjust.Status]` `Enabled`: bool, default true.

### 5. Passes and outputs

- **D3D9 path:** one pass, technique index 0 (the file's only technique, unnamed). Full-screen quad into the chain's output for this step. Output is 8-bit LDR at the picture's resolution. Output alpha is 1.
- **Vulkan path:** the Vulkan pass named `ImageAdjust` (SPIR-V `ImageAdjust.spv`), one full-screen pass into the same target. Same result as the D3D9 path.
- Only technique 0 is handled; other indices are not drawn.

### 6. Behaviour details

The shader works in this order on each pixel. (Written in words; the formulas are the defined behaviour.)

1. **Saturation.** The pixel is blended between its own Rec. 709 luma (grey, 0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B) and its colour, with the saturation value as the blend amount. Value 1 leaves it unchanged; 0 gives grey.
2. **Linearise.** Each channel is raised to the power 2.2 (sign-preserving).
3. **Tone-weighted light.** Two weights are computed from the linear colour:
   - the darker weight is "1 minus the smooth step of the colour", i.e. high for dark colours (the smooth step is the standard cubic from 0 to 1);
   - the lighter weight is the smooth step of the colour, high for bright colours.
   Each weight multiplies the colour and its own tint (also raised to 2.2). The two parts are added. At the default tints of 1 the two parts add back to the original colour exactly.
4. **Brightness.** The sum is multiplied by brightness, clamped at 0 from below.
5. **Strength blend.** The linear colour is blended between its original value and the result of step 4, by strength.
6. **Delinearise.** Each channel is raised to 1 / 2.2.
7. **Contrast.** Each channel's distance from mid-grey (0.5) is multiplied by contrast, the mid-grey is added back, and the result is blended with the value from step 6 by strength again.

**Identity.** With every setting at its default (brightness 1, saturation 1, contrast 1, strength 1, all tints 1) the output equals the input (up to rounding of the 2.2 power). This is why the skip rule above is needed to avoid running the pass for nothing.

**Strength 0.** Only saturation can still act (section 2).

**Sky and depth.** No depth is read. The sky is adjusted like everything else, so a brightness change also brightens the sky.

**First-person weapon.** No special handling.

**RenderScale / RenderRect frames.** No special handling; the pass covers the chain's picture at its own size. The Vulkan path is off in render-rectangle frames (as for Sharpening), so the D3D9 path runs there.

**Jitter (TAA).** None; it runs on the displayed picture.

**Reversed depth.** Not used.

**HDR vs LDR.** LDR only, 8-bit chain picture. The operations are defined on display values in 0..1.

**NaN and inf.** Brightness is clamped at 0. The power functions use the sign-preserving form so negative channels (from the tint or the contrast step) do not produce NaN. Values above 1 are not clamped at the end; the 8-bit target clips them on write. A value that goes below 0 after the contrast step is also clipped on write. No other guard is needed.

**Easily missed:** the dark and light tints must be applied in linear light (raised to 2.2) or the tint looks wrong; the delinearise step must come after the brightness and strength blend and before the contrast step; the day / night blend must use the transition curve, not a hard switch at dusk; interiors must use only the Interiors values.

### 7. Vulkan path

- **Files:** `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/ImageAdjust.frag` (SPIR-V `ImageAdjust.spv`).
- **Selection:** `ImageAdjustEffect::ExtRender` is used only when Vegas Shaders DXVK is loaded, `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses` is on, and the frame is not a render-rectangle frame. Technique 0 only. If the pass fails, the D3D9 technique runs for the rest of the session.
- **Bindings (descriptor set 1):** binding 0 = picture (`t_rendered`), one sampled 2D texture.
- **Sampler:** the linear clamp sampler, taken from the push block at offset 48.
- **Push constants (64 bytes):**

| Offset | Type | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | vec4 | `TESR_ImageAdjustData` (x brightness, y contrast, z saturation, w strength) |
| 16 | vec4 | `TESR_DarkAdjustColor` |
| 32 | vec4 | `TESR_LightAdjustColor` |
| 48 | uint | linear clamp sampler index |
| 52 to 63 | uint x 3 | padding |

- The Vulkan shader's steps match section 6. The one visible difference from the D3D9 path is expected to be rounding only.

### 8. Look and cost

- **Correct:** at default settings nothing changes. A brightness change lifts or lowers the whole picture without clipping the highlights hard; a contrast change makes the picture punchier or flatter around mid-grey; a saturation change makes the colour richer or more grey; a tint change colours only the shadows or only the highlights. Night and interior values take effect at the right times with a smooth change at dusk and dawn.
- **Typical wrong result:** a flat grey look when saturation is below 1 (expected, but check the value); crushed black or clipped white from large brightness or contrast values; a colour shift in the shadows that looks like a bug (the dark tint has been set, not a fault); an obvious pop at dusk or dawn (the day / night blend broken); tints that look too weak (the tint applied in display light instead of linear).
- **Cost class:** cheap single pass. One texture read and some power and smooth-step arithmetic per pixel. The power functions are the main cost.

### 9. Open questions

1. **Ranges not stated.** The toml has no range comment for any of the eleven values. The ImGui menu may impose ranges; not checked. Decide the valid ranges for brightness (at least 0 is enforced only for brightness), saturation (negative values are not guarded) and contrast.
2. **Saturation below 0 or above 1.** Not guarded. Negative saturation inverts the colour; large saturation overshoots and clips. Decide whether to clamp.
3. **Stale header comment (FIXED 2026-11-02).** The new shader's header describes New Vegas and its order of operations.
4. **Output not clamped.** Values above 1 or below 0 are only clipped on write. Confirm that is acceptable.
5. **Interior tint and brightness interaction.** The interiors and night values are separate, so setting brightness in one place does not affect the others. Confirm this is wanted and document it for users.
6. **Transition curve.** The exact meaning of `transitionCurve` (0 at night, 1 at full day, and the shape of the change over the day) is held in the game state, not in this effect. Not traced in this spec; the new author should read `ShaderManager` for its definition.

## LUT

Source reviewed (wave 3, 2026-11-02): the new D3D9 effect src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/LUT.fx.hlsl and Vulkan src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/LUT.frag
(branch claude/rw3-colour); C++ src/effects/LUT.cpp, LUT.h, LUTFile.h, LUTIdentity.h (unchanged); settings in
resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml; frame order in src/core/ShaderManager.cpp.

### 1. Purpose

LUT (look-up table) colour grading. Each colour of the picture is replaced by the colour stored for it in a table image
or a 3D .cube file, so a whole colour look can be swapped by changing a file. Exteriors blend a day table and a night
table by the time of day; interiors use their own table. The grade is mixed over the original by a strength setting. The
shipped table is an identity (no change), so the effect does nothing until a user picks a table.

### 2. When it runs

- Default position: post-tonemap, in the screen-space colour block, right after Coloring (and not run at all there when
  Coloring has taken it over, see the Coloring spec). Order: ... barrel haze, Coloring, LUT, depth of field, ...
- Pre-tonemap option: with [Shaders.LUT.Main] PreTonemapping = true, LUT is not run post-tonemap. It runs in the pre-tonemap
  chain (see the Bloom spec, section 2), after the lens pass and before the game's tonemap, on the HDR picture. The HDR
  compatibility option matters then (section 5).
- Enabled: [Shaders.LUT.Status] Enabled (bool, default true).
- Gates: [Main.Main] RenderEffects on, a player cell present, overlay off, and ShouldRender (below).
- ShouldRender (the effect's own test, checked before any drawing):
  - Strength is 0 or less: does not run.
  - Exterior: runs unless both the day and the night table are identity tables.
  - Interior: runs unless the interior table is an identity table.
- Exterior and interior: chosen per frame from the game state. Exteriors blend day and night by the day/night transition
  curve. Interiors use the interior table only (a hard switch when entering or leaving a cell).

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures

| Slot | Name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the current picture |
| s1 | TESR_LUTDayBuffer | the day table (a strip texture, see section 6) |
| s2 | TESR_LUTNightBuffer | the night table |
| s3 | TESR_LUTInteriorBuffer | the interior table |

All four are LINEAR, CLAMP, no mips.

#### Constants (registered by name; no fixed registers)

- TESR_LUTData (float4): x day table cell count N (0 means no usable table); y Strength (not clamped); z night N; w interior N.
- TESR_LUTBlend (float4): x day/night blend in exteriors (1 is full day, 0 is full night; 0 in interiors); y interior flag
  (0 exterior, 1 interior); z HDR compatibility flag (1 when the HDRCompat setting is on); w unused.

Units: colour values 0..1 for LDR input; HDR input can be above 1 (section 5).

### 4. Settings

[Shaders.LUT.Main]:
- DayLUT (string, default 'neutral_lut.png'): file name in Data/Textures/VegasShaders/LUTs/. Accepts a .cube file, or a PNG,
  DDS or BMP image that is a horizontal strip N cells wide and N high (N * N wide by N high, for example 256 by 16, 1024 by 32,
  4096 by 64). Other shapes are ignored and logged.
- NightLUT, InteriorLUT: the same rules.
- Strength (float, default 1.0): blend between the original picture (0) and the full grade (1).
- PreTonemapping (bool, default false): apply the table before the game's tonemap. Needs HDR tables, or HDRCompat on.
- HDRCompat (bool, default true): normalise HDR input before the table lookup (section 5).

[Shaders.LUT.Status]:
- Enabled (bool, default true): "Applies a color lookup table. Ships with a neutral LUT so the default is a no-op until you swap in
  your own."

Each slot name is saved back to the settings when a table is picked from the in-game menu. Unchanged by the rewrite.

### 5. Passes and outputs

One technique (index 0, the only one), one pass. Full-screen quad through the frame vertex buffer, writing the frame chain's target
(or the game target when this is the chain's final effect). Output is the same size as the render, alpha 1, opaque.

1. HDR compatibility (only when TESR_LUTBlend.z is 1): the picture is divided by the larger of 1 and its largest channel, so the
   table is always sampled with values at or below 1. The scale is undone after the lookup. For LDR input the scale is 1.
2. Lookup in each table (section 6). A table with a cell count below 2 is not sampled at all; its colour is the input colour, so
   the input passes through for that table.
3. Exteriors: day and night are blended by TESR_LUTBlend.x (day when 1). Interiors use the interior colour. The two are combined
   by TESR_LUTBlend.y (0 keeps the exterior colour, 1 gives the interior colour).
4. The HDR scale is restored (only when the HDR compatibility flag is 1).
5. Output = the original picture blended toward the graded colour by TESR_LUTData.y (Strength), channel by channel. Not clamped.

### 6. Behaviour details

#### Table layout and loading (unchanged)

- Strip layout: the table is N square cells in a row. Within a cell, red runs along x and green along y (N - 1 steps each, with a
  half-texel centre offset). Blue selects the cell: the lookup takes the two cells on either side of the blue position and blends
  them by its fraction. A 256 by 16 strip has 16 cells of 16 by 16.
- .cube files: a 3D table of N cubed lines, red fastest, then green, then blue. A 1D table, an input range other than 0..1, or a wrong
  entry count is rejected with a logged reason. A valid .cube is converted to a strip of N cells in 8-bit (A8R8G8B8).
- Image tables (PNG, DDS, BMP) are loaded at their exact size.
- Each slot keeps its own cell count.
- A texture that is not a valid strip (width not height squared, or height below 2) is not used at all, and a log line says so.
- Identity detection: a table in which every texel matches the identity mapping within one 8-bit step is flagged neutral. ShouldRender
  uses the flags (section 2).

#### Runtime behaviour

- Sampling is linear inside a cell and between the two neighbouring cells.
- Values above 1 in the input (HDR, PreTonemapping on, HDRCompat off): the lookup clamps to the table edge, so the output is the edge
  colour, not an extrapolation. HDRCompat on avoids this.
- Strength of 0 gives the original picture exactly; ShouldRender also skips the pass.
- Interiors: the interior table alone is used.
- Sky, depth, first-person weapon, TAA jitter: none handled.
- RenderScale (rect frames): no rect handling in the D3D9 pass (it reads the whole target, as other full-screen effects do; see section 9).
- No dithering in the pass itself. The 8-bit table can band in smooth gradients; the dither effects handle that separately.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Choice: [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses (default true) with the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension present. Technique 0 only; any other index
  is refused and falls back to the D3D9 technique for the session.
- File: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/LUT.frag (the same maths, line for line, as the D3D9 pass).
- Bindings (set 1): binding 0 = the picture; bindings 1, 2, 3 = the day, night and interior tables. An unusable slot is bound to the
  picture and not read.
- One LINEAR CLAMP sampler, its heap index in the push block.
- Push block (36 bytes plus padding): 0 TESR_LUTData (vec4); 16 TESR_LUTBlend (vec4); 32 sampler heap index (uint); 36..47 padding.

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct result: with the neutral table the picture is unchanged (and the pass is skipped). With a real table, the picture takes the
  table's look; at Strength 0.5 it is halfway. In exteriors the look moves from the night table to the day table with the time of day.
- Typical wrong results:
  - Banding or steps in gradients: the 8-bit table precision, or a table of too few cells.
  - A table that scrambles colours: a wrong layout (a strip that is not N cells of N by N, which is ignored), or a .cube in the wrong order.
  - A look in the wrong place: a day table used for the night, or an interior table that is not neutral used by mistake.
  - Odd colours at high brightness: PreTonemapping on with HDRCompat off.
- Cost class: single pass, texture-read heavy but cheap. Per pixel: one read of the picture and two reads per usable table (up to seven
  reads for three tables). Unusable tables are no longer sampled (wave 3).

### 9. Open questions

Changes made in wave 3 (2026-11-02):
- CHANGED (no visible change): a table with a cell count below 2 is no longer sampled; its colour is the input, as before.
- The shader no longer samples a table that is not used in the frame (exterior uses day and night; interior uses the interior table).

Still open:
1. Strength is not clamped in the C++ or the shader, so a value above 1 extrapolates the grade beyond the table. Whether that is wanted
   was not established from the code (the toml says 0 to 1 in words). Kept as it was.
2. The pre-tonemapping path runs on HDR values before the tonemap compresses them; the effect on the picture was not checked.
3. A table picked in the menu that fails to load keeps or drops the previously assigned table: not traced.
4. RenderScale: no rect handling in the D3D9 pass or the Vulkan pass. Not checked in a rect frame. (Other effects clamp their taps to
   the drawn part; this one reads one texel per pixel, so it has no neighbour reads that could leave the drawn part.)
5. The interaction with Coloring's combined technique depends on the LUT's constants being set before Coloring's pass; the update order
   is in src/core/ShaderManager.cpp and was not traced again here (see the Coloring spec).

## Lens

*README title: Effect spec: Lens*

Status 2026-11-02 (branch claude/rw3-lens): `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Lens.fx.hlsl` and `Compute/Lens.frag` were written fresh from this spec. Same settings, same push layout, same samplers in the same order (s0 to s3). Bloom shaping as written: the bloom's own hue at unit luma, scaled by its original luma raised to the bloom exponent (black stays black; the 1e-6 floor). Not changed: the Vulkan dirt is still read at the frame's own uv (no render-rect mapping): the C++ does not pass RenderScale to this pass, so with RenderScale below 1 the Vulkan dirt still covers only the drawn part (section 6, not fixed here; the C++ would need to change).

Source of truth: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Lens.fx.hlsl (D3D9), src/effects/Lens.h and Lens.cpp (C++ side), src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Lens.frag (Vulkan pass; compiled Lens.spv sits beside it). Settings from resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml. Frame order from src/core/ShaderManager.cpp.

### 1. Purpose

A dirt-on-the-lens look. Where the picture is bright (around lights, the sun, bright reflections), the bloom glow is spread across the screen through a fixed dirt-smudge texture, so bright spots bleed soft light over the smudges, the way light scatters on a dirty camera lens. Dark areas and dirt-free parts of the screen are unchanged. The effect only adds light; it never darkens the picture.

### 2. When it runs

- Enabled by the Status key of the Lens section (default off). Skipped when the effect is not loaded.
- Skipped when the player is underwater: the strength is forced to zero and the should-render check returns false.
- Skipped when its strength for the current place and time is zero or less.
- Strength is chosen by place and time. Exterior uses the Night and Main values, blended by the game's day/night transition curve (Night value at curve 0, Main value at curve 1). Interior (any non-exterior cell) uses the Interiors values. There is no separate menu rule; the effect simply follows the frame it is given.
- Frame order: drawn in the Vegas Shaders post-effect stage, after the game's tonemapper has produced the picture. The copy taken at the start of that stage is tonemapped and display-encoded sRGB. Within the stage it runs after the exposure step and after this frame's bloom buffer has been rendered, and before the optional pre-tonemap LUT step. Its timer label is "Pre-tonemap lens + LUT". Its output becomes the current image for the LUT step and everything after.
- Because bloom is produced earlier in the same frame, the bloom input is this frame's bloom, not last frame's.

### 3. Inputs

Textures (D3D9 technique). Vegas Shaders binds samplers by declaration order, so the slot order below is the order the shader declares them:

| Slot | Name | Holds | Addressing / filter |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | The current image. Declared, but this technique never samples it. | Clamp, linear |
| s1 | TESR_SourceBuffer | The frame copy taken at the start of the post-effect stage. This is the image the effect reads. | Clamp, linear, mip linear |
| s2 | TESR_BloomBuffer | This frame's bloom (the first bloom level the bloom effect publishes). | Clamp, linear, mip linear |
| s3 | TESR_LensSampler | The dirt-smudge image, file Effects\dirtlens.png. Only its red channel is used; its colour space was not checked. | Wrap, linear, mip linear |

Vulkan pass (Lens.frag): the same three images at descriptor set 1, bindings in order: binding 0 = source image, binding 1 = bloom image, binding 2 = dirt image. Samplers are chosen by index from the push block (section 7).

Constants (D3D9):
- TESR_LensData (float4) is the only constant this effect registers. Its register is not fixed in the source; the constant table assigns it. Components:
  - x = effective strength (multiplier on the added light; 0 means nothing is added). Unitless.
  - y = bloom exponent (the bloom's luma is raised to this power; larger = the smudges light up only right around the brightest spots). Unitless.
  - z = smudginess (0 up to about 3). The dirt image is raised to the power (3 minus z), floored at 0: 0 gives the sharpest, sparsest dirt; larger values cover more of the screen; at 3 the exponent is 0 and the dirt is effectively full coverage. Unitless.
- Declared in the shader but not used by this effect (do not rely on them): TESR_ReciprocalResolution, TESR_CinemaSettings, TESR_SunColor, TESR_SunAmbient, TESR_SunAmount, TESR_DebugVar.
- TESR_RenderScale comes in through the shared render-rect include. The manager sets it for every pass (section 6); this effect's C++ does not register it.

### 4. Settings

All under the Shaders.Lens sections. The defaults file writes the headers with a leading underscore (for example [_Shaders.Lens.Main]); the code reads the same sections without it. All values are floats except Status.Enabled, a boolean.

| Section | Key | Type | Range | Default | Visual effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Main | Strength | float | not stated in defaults | 0.4 | Overall amount of added dirt light by day outdoors. |
| Main | BloomExponent | float | not stated | 0.7 | Higher = smudges light only right around the brightest spots; lower = more of the bright areas light the dirt. |
| Main | Smudginess | float | not stated (about 0 to 3 meaningful) | 0.0 | Higher = more of the screen covered by dirt. |
| Night | Strength | float | not stated | 0.4 | Same as Main, used outdoors at night (blended with Main by the day/night curve). |
| Night | BloomExponent | float | not stated | 0.7 | Same as Main. |
| Night | Smudginess | float | not stated | 0.4 | Same as Main. More dirt at night by default. |
| Interiors | Strength | float | not stated | 0.05 | Interior amount (very low by default). |
| Interiors | BloomExponent | float | not stated | 0.6 | Same as Main, interiors only. |
| Interiors | Smudginess | float | not stated | 0.2 | Same as Main, interiors only. |
| Status | Enabled | bool | true / false | false | Turns the whole effect on or off. Off = no pass runs, nothing is added. |

Notes:
- The in-game overlay's lens sliders read and write Shaders.Lens.Main Strength and Smudginess only. Night and Interiors values are edited in the file, not by the overlay.
- The overlay sets each of those two values as a base value plus a scaled offset from the overlay's zoom control, and clamps the written value at zero.
- BloomExponent is not exposed in the overlay.

### 5. Passes and outputs

- One pass, one technique. The D3D9 technique is unnamed in the source; technique index 0 is the only one the C++ asks for. Vertex shader: the shared full-screen frame vertex stage (passes position and texture coordinate through). Pixel shader: the lens pass. Shader model 3.0 (vs_3_0 / ps_3_0).
- Render target: the frame's current render target (the chain target the earlier effects wrote to). No new target is created.
- Output: opaque colour (alpha written as 1). Full-screen; every pixel is written.
- Vulkan: one pass named "Lens" through the Vegas Shaders extension, one push block, the same output target, one colour output at location 0.

### 6. Behaviour details

- Colour space: the source is display-encoded sRGB. It is converted to linear before light is added and converted back to sRGB at the end. The bloom and dirt values are used as stored, without conversion.
- Dirt lookup (D3D9): the dirt is looked up through the render-rect helper, so with RenderScale below 1 the dirt image spans the whole screen rather than only the drawn top-left part. With RenderScale 1 (or a native frame) this is the identity.
- Source and bloom lookups use the rect coordinate (the texture coordinate itself), so they follow the drawn part of a RenderScale frame.
- Dirt: only the red channel of the dirt, after the smudginess power, is used. The dirt is a monochrome mask.
- Bloom shaping: the bloom colour is first normalised to unit luma (divided by its own luma, with the denominator floored at 1e-6, so a black bloom gives zero, not NaN). Its luma is then raised to the bloom exponent and the colour is scaled by that. Hue is kept; brightness of the dimmer glow is reduced.
- Luma: Rec. 709 weights (0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722) on the bloom as read.
- Added light = dirt (red channel, after the power) times shaped bloom times strength, added to the linear source, then converted back to sRGB. There is no clamp in the shader; values above 1 are clipped by the target format. A bright spot with strong dirt saturates to white rather than rolling off.
- Sky and depth: no depth read and no sky test. The effect is applied to the whole frame, sky included, equally.
- First-person weapon and HUD: no special case; the effect sees the already-composited frame.
- Jitter (TAA): none. No history sampling and no sub-pixel offset.
- Reversed depth: not applicable (no depth).
- HDR vs LDR: LDR. The input is the tonemapped, sRGB-encoded picture; feeding HDR linear data would be wrong.
- NaN/inf guards: only the bloom normalisation has a guard (floor 1e-6). The dirt, the source and the final conversion have none; a non-finite bloom sample would propagate.
- Underwater: strength is zeroed and the pass is skipped (no added light).
- Off: the pass is not run at all (no cost, no copy).
- Vulkan difference (important): the Vulkan pass samples the dirt at the frame's own texture coordinate, not through the render-rect helper the D3D9 pass uses. With RenderScale below 1, the Vulkan dirt covers only the drawn part, while D3D9 covers the whole screen. The arithmetic is otherwise the same.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Shader: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Lens.frag (GLSL 450, includes Common.glsl). Compiled output Lens.spv in the same folder.
- Selection: the effect's C++ extension entry is used when the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is present and the pass option is on. The option is NvrDxvkPasses in resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml, section [_Main.Main.Performance] (top-level key, default on). D3D9 is used instead when the effect is named in the keep-on-D3D9 list, when the extension render failed earlier this session (logged; the session then falls back to D3D9 for this effect), or when a shader-model-6 build covers the technique and that option is on.
- Pass name: "Lens". It selects Compute\Lens.spv through the shared extension pass helper.
- Textures, in the order handed to the pass: index 0 = frame copy (source), index 1 = first bloom level (null if the bloom effect does not exist), index 2 = dirt file texture. Bound at set 1, bindings 0, 1, 2.
- Samplers: the extension's sampler heap is set 0 binding 0. Two sampler indices are passed: linear clamp (source and bloom) and linear wrap (dirt).
- Push block (C++ sends 32 bytes; the shader reads the first 24):
  - offset 0: four floats, the same as TESR_LensData (x strength, y bloom exponent, z smudginess).
  - offset 16: unsigned integer, sampler index for linear clamp.
  - offset 20: unsigned integer, sampler index for linear wrap.
  - offsets 24 to 31: two unused unsigned integers (padding).
  The shader masks each sampler index to its low 16 bits. The offsets 16 and 20 are the values the C++ passes as sampler-index offsets; they must stay in step with the shader's push block.
- Technique index other than 0 is refused (no pass).

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct: a faint, soft glow of dirt-shaped light around bright lights and sun glints. In daylight outdoors it is subtle; at night more visible (default Night smudginess 0.4); indoors very faint (Interiors strength 0.05). Dark scenes and dirt-free parts of the screen show no change. The dirt shape is fixed on screen and does not move with the camera.
- Typical wrong results:
  - Dirt visible on dark areas with no bright source: bloom is not reaching there, so the dirt should stay dark. Check the bloom input and the bloom exponent.
  - Haze that lifts blacks across the screen: strength or smudginess too high, or the bloom buffer is not black where the picture is dark.
  - Dirt shifted or mis-sized on Vulkan with RenderScale below 1: the coordinate difference in section 6.
  - Hard white clipping around bright lights: expected to some degree (no clamp), but default settings should not blow bright spots to flat white.
  - Black speckles or NaN-looking pixels: check the bloom normalisation.
  - Colour tint from the dirt: none expected, since only the red channel is used as a neutral mask and the light takes the bloom's own colour.
- Cost class: cheap. One full-screen pass, three texture reads (source, bloom, dirt), a few power functions per pixel. No loops, no extra targets, no copies beyond the frame copy the stage already makes. Skipped entirely when off or underwater.

### 9. Open questions

- Strength, BloomExponent and Smudginess have no stated ranges in the defaults file and no clamps in the code. Usable ranges are not documented here; the overlay slider ranges were not read.
- The dirt image is not in the repository. Its location on a real install (Effects\dirtlens.png relative to the shader folder) and whether it is colour or grey-scale were not confirmed.
- Which bloom level the D3D9 bloom sampler receives is set outside Lens.cpp (by the bloom/chain code). Assumed to be the first bloom level, as on the Vulkan path; not verified.
- What the Vulkan pass does when the bloom effect does not exist (null bloom entry): not traced.
- The timer label says "Pre-tonemap", but the frame-copy comment says the picture is already tonemapped at that point. The spec follows the code (after the tonemapper); the label is probably historical. Not resolved.
- Whether the effect is in the keep-on-D3D9 list, or has a shader-model-6 build, was not checked.
- The colour space of the dirt texture as loaded (linear or sRGB) was not checked; the shader uses the stored value.
- The render-rect and frame-chain behaviour is taken from the shared include's comments and the manager's comments; not re-verified in the manager code.

## LocalExposure

*README title: LocalExposure (each part of the picture exposed for its own brightness)*

### 1. Purpose
In harsh sun the shade next to sunlit sand goes black, and from a dark room a doorway burns white; the eye sees into both.
Local exposure does what the eye (and Unreal Engine 5's Local Exposure) does: it measures how bright each neighbourhood of the
picture is and pulls each one toward the view's own mean brightness, so dark areas are lifted and bright ones calmed, without
flattening the whole picture. Off by default.

### 2. When it runs
- [Shaders.LocalExposure.Status] Enabled (default false), and Interiors / Exteriors for where.
- Each frame, in the chain step LumaExposureBloom (core/EffectChain.cpp), right after the exposure pass: it measures the image the
  tone mapper is about to get. It does not change the image itself; the tone mapper reads its result.
- Skipped (the tone mapper skips too) when Shadows and Highlights are both 0 and DebugView is off.

### 3. Passes (Effects\LocalExposure.fx.hlsl)
| Technique | Into | What |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | TESR_LocalExposureBuffer (1/16 res, R16F) | log2 brightness: 16 bilinear taps per texel, decoded with the tone mapper's Linearization |
| 1 | TESR_LocalExposureTemp | horizontal Gaussian (sigma = Blur texels, 13 taps) |
| 2 | TESR_LocalExposureBuffer | vertical Gaussian |
| 3 | TESR_LocalExposureMean (1x1) | the mean of the buffer (8 x 8 reads) |

### 4. In the tone mapper (Shaders\Includes\Tonemapping.hlsl localExposure, both ISHDRBLENDINSHADERCIN shaders)
After Exposure and before the night colour shift and the curve: `stops = mean - local`; a darker neighbourhood gains
`stops * Shadows`, a brighter one loses `stops * Highlights`; that shift fades where the pixel itself is more than a stop off its
neighbourhood (EdgeGuard; a plain blur would leave a halo round the sky or a lamp); at most 3 stops. Constants c34 / c35,
samplers s9 / s10. TESR_LocalExposureData.w = 1 only on frames the buffer was drawn (else the tone mapper skips).

### 5. Settings ([Shaders.LocalExposure.Main])
Shadows 0.4, Highlights 0.3, EdgeGuard 1.0, Blur 4 (buffer texels; about a tenth of the screen), Interiors, Exteriors, DebugView
(1: the measured brightness as grey).

### 6. RenderScale (FSR / DLSS below 1)
The buffer is screen-shaped, so in a rect frame it is drawn in its top-left part like every effect buffer; reads stay inside it,
and RenderRect::RescaleAfterUpscale stretches it over the whole buffer before the tone mapper reads it (as Bloom's top level).

### 7. Tested
2026-11-04 bench: og-desert noon (the dark foreground lifted, the sky calmed, near-black pixels 0.9% -> 0.4%, no halo at the
ridge; strong settings flatten, as expected) and saloon16 (dark corners lifted). Not yet: RenderScale below 1, the DXVK fork.

## LowHF

*README title: Effect spec: LowHF*

Status 2026-11-02 (branch claude/rw3-lens): `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/LowHF.fx.hlsl` and `Compute/LowHF.frag` were written fresh from this spec; the settings, the constant layout and the push block are unchanged. Fixed from the old one: the blur is no longer diagonal (open question on the blur direction resolved as isotropic: it is now a cross of taps, centre plus one and two offsets along x and along y, nine reads, weights summing to 1). The shader itself has no divisions. The zero-limit and zero-base guards from section 6 are in the C++ (LowHF.cpp), which this rewrite does not touch, so they are still open.

Source of truth: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/LowHF.fx.hlsl (D3D9), src/effects/LowHF.h and LowHF.cpp (C++ side), src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/LowHF.frag (Vulkan pass; compiled LowHF.spv sits beside it). Settings from resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml. Frame order from src/core/ShaderManager.cpp.

### 1. Purpose

A low-health and low-stamina warning picture. When the player's health falls below a set fraction of its maximum, the whole screen loses colour (fades toward grey), gets a soft blur, and darkens toward the edges in a vignette that tightens as health drops. When stamina (called fatigue in the settings) falls below a set fraction, only the desaturation applies. At full health and stamina, or when the player is dead, the screen is untouched. The effect only changes the picture while the player is hurt or tired; it does not add anything.

### 2. When it runs

- Enabled by the Status key of the LowHF section (default off). When the effect is off, or its D3D9 shader is not loaded, nothing is drawn.
- Draw check: the effect draws only when the first component of its constant is not zero. That component is zero unless the player is alive and either health is below the health limit or stamina is below the fatigue limit (see sections 3 and 6). So with the player dead, or both values at or above their limits, the pass is skipped and costs nothing.
- Not limited to interiors or exteriors, night, or menus: the rule is only the player's vital values. The effect's own code does not turn it off underwater or in the first-person weapon view.
- Frame position: in the lens group of the post-process chain, after the Blood Lens and Water Lens and before the Dither Buster, SMAA, FXAA and Sharpening passes. It reads the frame's rendered picture as it stands after the earlier effects, and writes the next target of the chain (or the game target if this is the last effect to draw). Whether its input is HDR or already tonemapped is decided by the chain and is not checked by this effect (see section 9).
- Settings are re-read when the settings change. The health and stamina values are recomputed from live player values on each frame the effect is considered.

### 3. Inputs

Textures (D3D9 technique):
- Slot 0 (register s0), sampler name TESR_RenderedBuffer: the frame's rendered picture (the current chain input). Sampler state: minimum, magnification and mip filter all LINEAR; address mode CLAMP on U and V. This is the only texture. It is bound by name to the rendered buffer, not to the source buffer.

Vulkan pass textures and samplers:
- Descriptor set 1, binding 0: the rendered picture as a 2D sampled image (named t_rendered in the shader).
- Sampler: one sampler chosen by index through the push constant, using LINEAR filtering and CLAMP addressing (the LinearClamp sampler). No wrap sampler and no point sampler.

Constants:
- TESR_LowHFData: float4, looked up by name through the D3DX constant table (no fixed register is declared). Components, unitless unless stated:
  - x: desaturation amount, 0 to about 1 (0 = full colour, 1 = fully grey). Also the draw-check value: zero means not drawn.
  - y: blur offset in texture coordinates (a fraction of the picture width and height, applied to both the horizontal and vertical coordinate equally). Zero means no blur.
  - z: vignette exponent (zero means no vignette; higher values make a tighter, darker edge).
  - w: vignette floor, an offset added to the vignette factor before it is clamped (see section 6).
- No other constants are read by the shader.

Vulkan push constant block (C++ fills it; 32 bytes total):
- Offset 0, vec4 lowHFData: the same four components as TESR_LowHFData, in the same order.
- Offset 16, uint samplerIndex: the index of the sampler to use (LINEAR, CLAMP), read through the sampler table.
- Offsets 20, 24, 28: unused padding (three uints) so the block is a multiple of 16 bytes.

### 4. Settings

All keys are in section [_Shaders.LowHF.Main] unless noted. Each is read as a float.

- HealthLimit: float, default 0.5. Fraction of maximum health below which the health effect starts (health ratio = current health / base health). No range is declared. A value of zero or less makes the health ratio divide by zero (see section 6). Changes the threshold at which the screen starts to go grey and blurry.
- FatigueLimit: float, default 0.25. Fraction of maximum stamina below which the stamina desaturation starts (stamina ratio = current stamina / base stamina). No range is declared. Also the divisor in the stamina strength.
- LumaMultiplier: float, default 1.0. Scales the desaturation strength in both the health and the stamina case. At 1.0 the strongest level gives full grey.
- BlurMultiplier: float, default 0.2. Scales the blur offset. The offset applied is health strength times 0.01 times this value, so at the default the strongest blur offset is 0.002 of the picture size.
- VignetteMultiplier: float, default 1.0. Scales the vignette exponent. The exponent is health strength times 20 times this value.
- DarknessMultiplier: float, default 1.0. Scales the vignette floor. The floor is (1 minus health strength) times this value.
- [_Shaders.LowHF.Status] Enabled: boolean, default false. The on/off switch for the effect in the menu (the defaults comment reads "Screen and sound effects when the player health is low").
- [_Main.LowHFSound.Main] (low-health / fatigue sound keys, an Oblivion Reloaded leftover that no code read) was removed on 2026-11-05.

Definitions used below:
- Health strength = 1 minus (health ratio / HealthLimit). It is 0 at the threshold and rises to 1 at zero health (with the default limit, when health is zero).
- Stamina strength = 1 minus (stamina ratio / FatigueLimit). It is 0 at the stamina threshold and 1 at zero stamina.

### 5. Passes and outputs

- Technique index 0 is the only technique. The D3D9 file declares it without a name (one pass). The C++ always asks for index 0; any other index draws nothing.
- Pass: one full-screen pass. Vertex stage passes the position and texture coordinate through unchanged (full-screen quad, as the other effects use). Pixel stage: the shader described in section 6, output colour alpha set to 1.
- Output: one colour target, the target the chain gives this effect. Same size and format as the input picture. The effect overwrites the target; it does not blend with what was there.
- Vulkan: the pass is named LowHF (file LowHF.spv beside LowHF.frag). It is selected only when the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is present and NvrDxvkPasses is on, and only for technique index 0. If the Vulkan pass fails, the effect falls back to its D3D9 shader for the rest of the session (logged).
- The effect has no render target or texture of its own; it reads and writes only the chain's targets.

### 6. Behaviour details

Health and stamina values (computed on the C++ side, before drawing):
- Health ratio = current health / base health; stamina ratio = current stamina / base stamina, both read from the player actor.
- If the player is not alive, all four components are zero and nothing draws.
- If health ratio is below HealthLimit: desaturation = health strength times LumaMultiplier; blur offset = health strength times 0.01 times BlurMultiplier; vignette exponent = health strength times 20 times VignetteMultiplier; vignette floor = (1 minus health strength) times DarknessMultiplier. At the threshold: no desaturation, no blur, no vignette, floor equal to DarknessMultiplier. At zero health: full desaturation, the strongest blur, the tightest vignette, and a floor of zero.
- Stamina applies only when the health case did not set the desaturation, so the two never combine. If stamina ratio is below FatigueLimit, desaturation = stamina strength times LumaMultiplier and the other three components stay zero (no blur, no vignette).
- Edge case: if health strength is exactly zero (health exactly at HealthLimit), the desaturation is zero, so the stamina case may apply in that frame.

Pixel shader order (the same in D3D9 and Vulkan; each step works on the colour the previous step produced):
1. Start with the picture colour at the pixel.
2. (New shader, 2026-11-02) Blur, when the blur offset is greater than zero: nine taps, a cross around the pixel (centre weight 0.2; one offset along x and along y at 0.15 each; two offsets along x and y at 0.05 each). Isotropic, not diagonal. The original five-sample diagonal description below is what was replaced.
   Original: take five samples and average them. The samples are at the pixel, at plus one offset, at plus two offsets, at minus one offset, and at minus two offsets. The offset is added equally to the horizontal and the vertical texture coordinate, so the blur runs along the diagonal, not horizontally or vertically. This is the behaviour as written; whether a diagonal blur was intended is an open question (section 9). Blur happens before desaturation, so the grey comes from the blurred colour.
3. Desaturate, when the desaturation amount is greater than zero: mix the colour toward its luminance, using the weights 0.2125 (red), 0.7154 (green) and 0.0721 (blue), close to Rec.709. Amount 0 leaves the colour unchanged, amount 1 gives pure grey. The mix is linear and not clamped, so an amount above 1 (possible with LumaMultiplier above 1) pushes the colour past grey. Keep it unclamped as written.
4. Vignette, when the exponent is greater than zero: take the distance from the screen centre in texture coordinates, squared (centre minus 0.5 on each axis, then dot product). The vignette factor is one minus that squared distance: about 1 at the centre, about 0.5 at the corners. The multiplier is the vignette factor raised to the exponent (of its absolute value), plus the floor, clamped to 0 to 1. Multiply the colour by the multiplier. The centre stays bright, the edges darken; a high exponent confines the dark to the very edge, and a low exponent darkens more of the screen. With a floor above zero, the edges keep that fraction of their brightness.
5. Output the colour with alpha 1.

Other behaviour:
- Sky and depth: the effect reads no depth and no sky data. Sky pixels are treated like all others.
- First-person weapon and jitter: the effect reads no weapon layer and no jitter offset; it acts on the finished picture.
- RenderScale and render rect: the effect does not transform coordinates for the render rectangle or the render scale. Texture coordinates run 0 to 1 across the whole input target, and blur offsets are in those same units. Open question whether this matters at RenderScale below 1.
- Reversed depth: not used.
- HDR versus LDR: the shader does no tonemapping and no clamping of its input. Values above 1 pass through the blur and the desaturation unchanged.
- NaN and infinity: no guard. A zero or negative HealthLimit divides by zero in the health ratio and gives infinite or NaN strengths. A zero base health or base stamina has the same problem. A guard should clamp the limits to a small positive minimum and treat a zero base value sensibly.
- Things that would look wrong if missed: a blur that is horizontal or vertical only; desaturation that also darkens the centre; the vignette applied when the exponent is zero; the stamina case blurring or darkening; the effect drawing while the player is dead; the health threshold reversed (grey at full health).

### 7. Vulkan path

- Files: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/LowHF.frag (source) and LowHF.spv (compiled, the one loaded). The shader includes the shared Common shader file of the compute folder, and uses its sampler helper to pick the sampler by index.
- Selection: src/effects/LowHF.cpp calls the Vulkan pass first when Vegas Shaders DXVK is present, NvrDxvkPasses ([_Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses, true in the defaults file) is on, and the effect is not named in the keep-on-D3D9 list (NvrDxvkKeepD3D9). It does so only for technique index 0. It uses the shared full-screen Vulkan pass helper with one colour target, the rendered picture as its only texture, and one sampler (LinearClamp, sampler push offset 16).
- Push constant layout (32 bytes): offset 0 lowHFData (vec4, the four components of section 3); offset 16 samplerIndex (uint). The C++ fills lowHFData from the same constant the D3D9 path uses, and sets samplerIndex to the LinearClamp sampler's index.
- Descriptor layout: set 1, binding 0: sampled 2D image (the rendered picture). The sampler is referenced by the push index, not by its own binding.
- Vertex stage and full-screen triangle: the shared full-screen pass setup used by the other compute-folder passes.
- The Vulkan shader performs the same five steps as section 6, in the same order, with the same weights and clamps. No difference in the sampling coordinate was found between the two paths.
- Fallback: if the Vulkan pass cannot be created or fails, the effect falls back to the D3D9 technique for the session and logs once.

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct: at full health and full stamina the picture is unchanged. Below the health threshold the picture loses colour, gets a slight soft blur, and the edges darken; the closer to zero health, the greyer, blurrier and darker the edges, with the centre kept bright. Stamina below its threshold gives only the grey tint, with no blur and no edge darkening. The change grows smoothly as health falls and starts from zero at the threshold, so there is no popping there.
- Typical wrong results: the screen goes grey at full health; the blur smears diagonally in a way that reads like motion (see section 6 for the intended behaviour and section 9 for the open question); the whole screen darkens instead of only the edges; the vignette has a hard edge; the stamina case also blurs; the effect runs during death or with the effect switched off.
- Cost class: cheap. One full-screen pass: five texture reads when the blur is on, one otherwise, plus a few arithmetic operations. Skipped entirely when the draw check is zero.

### 9. Open questions

- Blur direction: the offset is added to both texture axes equally, so the blur runs along the diagonal. Confirm whether this is intended or whether a single-axis blur was meant. The writer should match the behaviour the owner decides on.
- [_Main.LowHFSound.Main] (low-health / fatigue sound keys, an Oblivion Reloaded leftover that no code read) was removed on 2026-11-05.
- Ranges: no range is declared or clamped for HealthLimit, FatigueLimit, LumaMultiplier, BlurMultiplier, VignetteMultiplier or DarknessMultiplier. The safe range for each is not known.
- Constant update timing: settings are re-read when the settings-changed flag is set. The per-frame timing of the health and stamina update was not traced exactly; confirm it runs on every frame the effect is considered.
- Pre- or post-tonemapping: the effect sits in the lens group after motion blur. Whether its input is HDR or tonemapped, and what RenderPreTonemapping does to it, was not traced.
- Render rect and RenderScale: the effect does not use the render rectangle. Whether a scaled input needs the blur offset converted to the target's pixel size is not confirmed.
- Vulkan sampler table: the exact index value of the LinearClamp sampler is set by the extension at run time; the spec gives only the sampling mode.
- Dead player: the code treats a dead player as zero strength. Confirm there is no case where the player object is missing (the check is on the alive flag only).

## MotionBlur

*README title: MotionBlur - effect spec*

Rewritten 2026-11-02 (wave 3, branch claude/rw3-screen, commit 80cbf4a7). New shaders: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/MotionBlur.fx.hlsl (D3D9)
and src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/MotionBlur.frag (Vulkan, drawn twice). The C++ is unchanged.
FIXED 2026-11-02: a GaussianWeight of zero (or below) is held at 0.001 (section 6, no divide by zero); the tap weights are shifted by their
largest exponent so they cannot overflow for small weights (same normalised result, the look is unchanged); the D3D9 taps are clamped to
the drawn part of a render rectangle (section 6, RenderScale). Kept: the full-target texel scaling (section 9, open).

### 1. Purpose
When the player turns the view quickly, the whole picture smears along the direction of the turn, the way a camera with an open shutter smears a pan. A still camera, or a slow turn, leaves the picture sharp. The smear is one-directional: the image is averaged with copies of itself shifted along the turn direction on screen, so bright edges trail across the frame. It works on the final LDR picture after tonemapping, so it smears everything in that picture, including the sky and the first-person weapon.

### 2. When it runs
- Gated by the effect's menu switch, which reads the Status section (`[_Shaders.MotionBlur.Status] Enabled`, default off). Off means the effect does not draw. (The mapping from menu switch to this key was read from the settings code, not traced end to end.)
- Per frame, the effect draws only if its smoothed turn data is non-zero (horizontal or vertical component not exactly 0). A still camera draws nothing and the picture passes through unchanged.
- No interior, exterior, night or menu rule is applied by this effect.
- Frame order (src/core/ShaderManager.cpp, post-tonemap block): TAA, rain, snow, legacy bloom, heat haze, volumetric smoke, barrel haze, coloring, LUT, depth of field, then MotionBlur, then blood lens, water lens, low-high-frequency, dither buster, SMAA, FXAA, sharpening, cinema, image adjust, body camera, sky dither, debug. MotionBlur therefore reads the depth-of-field result and feeds the lens effects.
- Render call: the chain image is the rendered surface; the post-tonemap copy is passed as the source surface, which this effect does not read.

### 3. Inputs
Textures:
- Slot 0, named TESR_RenderedBuffer (D3D9 sampler register s0; Vulkan set 1, binding 0). Holds the chain's current image. Linear filtering, clamp addressing on both axes. This is the only texture the effect binds.

Constants (names as the C++ registers them; the Vulkan push block carries the same values):
- TESR_ReciprocalResolution (float4, set by the shader manager, not by this effect): x = 1 / render target width, y = 1 / render target height, z = aspect ratio, w = 0 (reserved). Only x and y are used. Note these are the full render target's size (see section 6).
- TESR_MotionBlurParams (float4, written by this effect each frame from settings):
  - x = GaussianWeight (weight spread), unitless, must stay above zero.
  - y = BlurScale (multiplier on the offset length), unitless.
  - z = BlurOffsetMax (clamp, texels, applied to each offset component).
  - w = unused by the shader.
- TESR_MotionBlurData (float4, written by this effect each frame):
  - x = horizontal blur vector (from yaw change), in degrees of turn, smoothed over three frames.
  - y = vertical blur vector (from pitch change), same units and smoothing.
  - z, w = unused.
  The shader treats x and y as a 2D direction; the number is scaled by one texel and BlurScale in the sampling.

Shared use: the average-luminance compute pass (src/effects/AvgLuma.cpp) reads this same TESR_MotionBlurData value, so the constant update must run before that pass in the frame.

### 4. Settings
Read from resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml. The effect reads the first-person block when the camera is first-person, otherwise the third-person block.

Section `[_Shaders.MotionBlur.Status]`:
- Enabled (bool, default false). The menu switch (see section 2). Comment: blur in the direction of the camera when turning.

Section `[_Shaders.MotionBlur.FirstPersonView]`:
- BlurCutOff (float, default 15.0). Threshold on the squared turn size (horizontal squared plus vertical squared, degrees). Below it both components are zeroed for the frame, so small jitter does not smear. Higher = more turn is needed.
- GaussianWeight (float, default 6.0). Weight spread. Higher flattens the weights.
- BlurScale (float, default 4.0). Length multiplier. Higher = longer streaks. The most visible control.
- BlurOffsetMax (float, default 24.0). Cap on the offset per component, in texels. Limits the streak length on violent turns.
- Enabled (bool, default true). Not read by this effect's code.

Section `[_Shaders.MotionBlur.ThirdPersonView]`:
- The same four keys with the same meanings, defaults 15.0, 6.0, 4.0, 24.0.
- Enabled (bool, default false). Not read by this effect's code.

### 5. Passes and outputs
Technique 0 is the only technique. It has two passes running the same pixel shader with the same constants. Any other technique index draws nothing.

- Pass 1: reads the chain image, writes the blurred result to a chain surface (D3D9: the current render target).
- Pass 2: reads the result of pass 1, blurs it again with the same constants, and writes the final result. Two passes give two averaging steps in total. Only pass 2 may write straight into the game's target, and only when this effect is the chain's last effect for the frame.
- Output: same size and format as the chain image (full render target, LDR). Output alpha is 1.

### 6. Behaviour details
Turn measurement (once per frame; the update is guarded so a second call in the same frame does nothing):
- Yaw is the player's rotation about the vertical axis, pitch the rotation about the horizontal axis, both converted from radians to degrees.
- Horizontal change = this frame's yaw minus last frame's yaw, taken the shorter way round the circle (a turn across north must not produce a 360-degree spike). Vertical change = pitch minus last pitch.
- If horizontal squared plus vertical squared is below BlurCutOff, both changes are zeroed for this frame.
- Blur vector = the mean of this frame's change and the two previous frames' changes (a three-frame history, shifted once per frame). The mean stops one jumpy frame from flashing.
- Pitch is not scaled by aspect ratio here. (The body camera effect does scale its pitch term by aspect; this effect does not.)
- BlurParams are rewritten each frame from the active settings block.
- The history starts at zero on construction, so the first turn does not spike.

Sampling in the shader:
- Each offset component is clamped to plus and minus BlurOffsetMax before use.
- 12 step indices (i = 0 to 11, 24 taps in total). At step i the shader samples the pixel's coordinate plus the offset vector times one texel times BlurScale times i/24, and also the negated offset. Both samples of a step share one weight.
- Weight for step i: exp( i squared / (5 times GaussianWeight squared) ), divided by GaussianWeight. The weight rises with i, so far taps count more than near ones. This is the defined shape of the effect; changing it changes the look and needs approval.
- Result: the weighted sum of RGB divided by the total weight (brightness is preserved; the blur averages, it does not add light). Alpha is 1.
- No linearisation: samples are used as the chain holds them. No NaN or infinity guard. FIXED 2026-11-02: a GaussianWeight of zero or below is held at 0.001 (no divide by zero), and the weights are computed relative to the largest one.
- The effect does not read depth, normals, sky or weapon masks. Sky and weapon are smeared with the rest.
- No TAA jitter is applied; the coordinate is un-jittered and there is no reprojection.
- Reversed depth: not used. HDR: not applicable, the chain image is LDR at this point.

RenderScale and RenderRect:
- The offset is scaled by the full render target's reciprocal size. If the game draws into a sub-rectangle (RenderRect / TESR_RenderScale), the smear length is measured in full-target texels, not rectangle texels. FIXED 2026-11-02: the taps are clamped to the drawn part of the rectangle (half a texel in from its edges), so they do not read the undrawn area. The scaling is unchanged (full-target texels); whether it should follow the rectangle is still an owner decision (section 9).

Edge cases:
- A camera cut (teleport, cell change) gives one large turn. This effect does not read the cut flag (GameState.isCameraCut); it relies on the BlurOffsetMax clamp and the three-frame mean.
- Turn data is updated from player rotation only; menus do not change it.

Things that look wrong if missed:
- Updating twice per frame: the smear is about a third of the intended strength and loses its smoothing.
- No shorter-way yaw difference: a turn through north gives a full-screen streak for one frame.
- No BlurOffsetMax clamp: a fast turn smears across the screen.
- Weight shape reversed: a softer, different look.
- Addressing other than clamp: dark or bright edge halos.

### 7. Vulkan path
Files:
- src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/MotionBlur.frag (GLSL 450 fragment pass; the same algorithm as section 6, including the 24 taps, weights, clamps and normalisation).
- src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/MotionBlur.spv (compiled form of the above; regenerate, do not hand-edit).
- ObjectMotion.glsl is not included by this shader.

Route choice (src/effects/MotionBlur.cpp ExtRender, src/core/EffectRecord.cpp Render):
- The Vulkan route is used when the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is present, the NvrDxvkPasses key is on (`[Main]` group in the defaults toml, default true), the effect is not on the keep-on-D3D9 list, and the technique has no SM6 build selected (Sm6Covers). Otherwise the D3D9 technique runs.
- Native D3D9 and stock DXVK never take this route.

Failure behaviour:
- If pass 1 fails, the effect returns false and the whole technique runs on D3D9 for this effect; the effect is then marked failed for the session (logged).
- If pass 2 fails after pass 1 has been committed, the effect returns success with the pass-1 result left in place, so no second D3D9 blur runs.

Binding and push-constant layout (the C++ fills it, the shader reads it), 64 bytes total:
- Texture: t_rendered at set 1, binding 0, sampled with a sampler chosen by the index in the push block (linear, clamp).
- Offset 0: reciprocalResolution (vec4), same content as TESR_ReciprocalResolution.
- Offset 16: blurParams (vec4), same content as TESR_MotionBlurParams.
- Offset 32: blurData (vec4), same content as TESR_MotionBlurData.
- Offset 48: samplerIndex (uint32), index into the shared sampler table for linear filter with clamp addressing.
- Offsets 52 to 63: three uint32 pad words, zero.

### 8. Look and cost
Correct: a brisk turn streaks the picture along the turn with soft, trailing bright edges; the streak fades within a few frames of the turn stopping. Slow turns show little or no smear. Overall brightness is unchanged.

Typical wrong results:
- Smear lingering after the turn stops (history not shifting, or the once-per-frame guard broken).
- Smear while standing still (BlurCutOff too low, or the zero-turn skip missing).
- Full-screen streak for a frame after a fast turn or a teleport (no yaw wrap, or clamp removed).
- Edge halos (wrong addressing).
- Doubled strength (pass 2 repeated, or a D3D9 fallback after a committed pass 1).

Cost class: multi-pass per-pixel loop. Two passes, each 24 texture taps and 24 weight evaluations per pixel, at full target size. Skipped entirely when the turn is zero, so it costs only while the view turns.

### 9. Open questions
- The first-person and third-person `Enabled` keys are not read by this effect's code. Whether the menu switch maps only to the Status key was read from the settings code but not traced in full.
- Which first-person test selects the settings block in unusual camera states (cutscenes, bodycam) was not checked.
- Whether BlurScale and BlurOffsetMax should be measured in output pixels when RenderScale is below one was not decided; the code uses full-target texels.
- Whether the distance-rising weight shape is intended is not stated in the source; it is kept as the defined look.
- Whether the second pass writes the game target depends on the chain's last-effect order; only the order list in ShaderManager.cpp was read, not the chain's full rule.
- D3D9 register numbers are assigned by the shader manager at registration and are not fixed in this effect's source; only names and order are given here.

## Normals

Source of this spec: the rewritten shader (wave 3 buffers, branch claude/rw3-buffers): src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Normals.fx.hlsl (techniques Normals and NormalsSmooth), src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Normals.glsl (shared maths), Normals.frag and NormalsSmooth.frag. The C++ (src/effects/Normals.cpp) is unchanged. Written from the previous spec and the C++; the old shader was not read.

### 1. Purpose

Normals builds a per-pixel surface normal for the whole picture, in view space, from the CombineDepth buffer, and stores it in a full-frame texture that most Vegas Shaders effects read. The player does not see it directly. Its quality decides how smooth lit surfaces, reflections, shadows and snow look on low-poly ground and rocks. An optional smoothing step (off by default) evens out the normals of flat-shaded low-poly geometry in place, so every reader gets the same smoothed normal.

### 2. When it runs

- Every frame where Vegas Shaders' post-process runs: RenderDepthAndNormals (first step of RenderEffectsPreTonemapping), right after CombineDepth.
- It is skipped when the merged CombineDepth pass already wrote the normals (see the CombineDepth spec).
- After the normals pass, the smoothing (SmoothNormals > 0) runs in place (two passes), then the CapturedNormals merge (not specified here).
- Enabled: no settings section; on whenever loaded.
- RenderScale v2: reads are clamped to the drawn part; the target stays full size.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures

- TESR_DepthBuffer (stage 0, POINT, CLAMP): the CombineDepth output. R = viewZ / far.
- Smoothing passes only: TESR_NormalsBuffer (stage 1, POINT, CLAMP) for pass 0; TESR_NormalsSmoothTemp (stage 2, POINT, CLAMP) for pass 1. The normals pass reads only the depth, so it never reads the normals target it writes.

#### Constants

- TESR_CameraData: x = near, y = far (game units).
- TESR_ReciprocalResolution (xy): 1 / the full frame size.
- TESR_InvProjectionTransform: the normals pass's view-space positions.
- TESR_ProjectionTransform: the smoothing pass's view-space positions (its x and y terms only).
- TESR_NormalsSmoothData (x): the smoothing reach in texels (0 = off; set by the C++ from SmoothNormals, 0 to 256).
- TESR_RenderScale (RenderRect include): the rect clamp.
- TESR_NormalsData: registered by the C++, not declared or read by this shader.

### 4. Settings

Unchanged:
- [Main.Main.Quality] SmoothNormals (int, 0; UNOFFICIAL, live): smoothing reach in pixels, 0 = off.
- [Main.Main.Quality] CapturedNormals (bool, true; UNOFFICIAL): a later pass may overwrite parts of the buffer (not specified here).
- [Main.Main.Performance] MergedDepthNormals (bool, false): the normals come from the merged CombineDepth draw.
- NvrDxvkPasses (bool, true): the Vulkan passes.

### 5. Passes and outputs

#### Normals pass (technique Normals, one pass, PS NormalsPS)

- Target: TESR_NormalsBuffer, A16B16G16R16F, full frame.
- Output: RGB = the unit view-space normal n as n x 0.5 + 0.5 (decode: stored x 2 - 1). Alpha = 1.
- View space: +x right, +y up, +z away from the camera. A surface facing the camera has n.z < 0. The normal is not flipped toward the camera.

#### Smoothing passes (technique NormalsSmooth, two passes, SmoothX and SmoothY)

- SmoothX: reads TESR_NormalsBuffer (stage 1), writes the spare TESR_NormalsSmoothTemp, along x.
- SmoothY: reads the spare (stage 2), writes TESR_NormalsBuffer, along y.
- Each pass reads one target and writes the other, so there is no read-and-write of the same texture. Alpha of the output = alpha of the input.

### 6. Behaviour details

#### How the normal is built (normals pass)

For the pixel and its neighbours at one and two texels to each side:
1. Each neighbour's linear depth is read (clamped to the drawn part, so an edge uses the nearest valid neighbour), and its view-space position rebuilt through the inverse projection with the reversed-Z value of that depth.
2. Horizontal derivative: the vector from the left neighbour to the centre, or from the centre to the right neighbour. The side is the one whose two-texel extrapolation predicts the centre's depth better; left only when strictly better (ties go right).
3. Vertical derivative: from the down neighbour to the centre, or from the centre to the up neighbour; down only when strictly better (ties go up).
4. Normal = normalise(vertical derivative x horizontal derivative). Both derivatives point along +x and +y, so a surface facing the camera gives n.z < 0.
5. Guard (new): a zero-length cross product returns (0, 0, -1) instead of NaN.

#### Smoothing (in place, once per frame)

- Reach = SmoothNormals texels. Six taps per pass at k x reach / 3 texels, k = -3, -2, -1, 1, 2, 3, along the pass's axis. Taps are read at clamped positions; a tap outside the drawn part counts zero.
- Each tap's weight is the product of: plane factor saturate(1 - distance / tolerance), where distance is the tap's view-space offset from the centre's tangent plane and tolerance = max(viewZ x 0.0005 x |k| x reach / 3, 0.5); and normal factor smoothstep(0.5, 0.8, dot(tap normal, centre normal)); and the on-screen factor.
- Result = normalise(centre normal + sum of weight x tap normal), re-encoded. Pixels at or beyond 0.99 x far (sky) return the input unchanged.
- The view positions for the smoothing come from the projection's x and y terms (the same in D3D9 and Vulkan), with the depth as z. Approximately the inverse-projection positions of the normals pass.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Normals.frag (technique 0 line for line), with Compute\Normals.glsl for the maths (nvrProjectedFromViewZ, nvrViewPosition, nvrViewPositionFromProjection, nvrNormal). Set 1 binding 0 = the depth. Push: 0 reciprocal (vec4), 16 camera data (vec4), 32 inverse projection (mat4), 96 sampler index.
- NormalsSmooth.frag: set 1 binding 0 = the source (normals or spare), binding 1 = the depth. Push: 0 reciprocal, 16 smoothing data (x reach), 32 camera data, 48 projection (mat4), 112 sampler index, 116 pass (0 along x, 1 along y).
- The Vulkan smoothing has no RenderScale clamp (open, see section 9).
- Normals.glsl changed: DepthNormals.frag (kept, merged CombineDepth pass) includes it and calls a function it no longer provides. See the CombineDepth spec.

### 8. Look and cost

Correct: smooth lit surfaces where the geometry is smooth; creases (wall to floor, a box edge) stay sharp; silhouettes do not smear into the background; with SmoothNormals 4 to 8 the facets of low-poly rock and ground go.

Typical wrong: facets (smoothing off, or reach too small); smeared silhouettes (the side choice failed); inverted lighting (sign); stripes on slopes (the tie rule).

Cost: one full-screen pass with nine depth reads per pixel. Smoothing: two passes of six taps (only when on).

### 9. Open questions

1. CapturedNormals: its encoding and when it overrides this output are not specified here.
2. The sign of the normal for surfaces seen from behind is not checked for each reader; most readers flip it themselves.
3. The clamped smoothing taps at the drawn part's edge are not tested on the GPU.
4. Smoothing and AmbientLight's own normal smoothing were not compared numerically.
5. TESR_NormalsData is registered and unused; not checked whether any reader needs it.
6. Vulkan smoothing has no RenderScale rect clamp.
7. Feedback hazard: resolved by the pass structure (the normals pass does not read the normals target; the smoothing passes alternate targets). The C++ still unbinds stage 1 and 2 around the smoothing draws.

## PBR

Physically based lighting for objects and the ground: Lambert diffuse plus GGX highlights for the sun and lamps, metal, and
optional per-texture material maps. Settings: `[Shaders.PBR.*]` (`defaults.toml` here). Shader code: `Shaders/Includes/PBR.hlsl`
(the lighting maths), `Shaders/Includes/Object.hlsl` (material inputs), `Shaders/ObjectTemplate.hlsl` / `ParallaxTemplate.hlsl`
(the object shaders that use them).

This doc is still partial: it covers the vanilla bridge, the material maps, which data decides metal, and the eyes. The rest of the effect is described in the settings'
own descriptions.

### Which data decides a highlight

| Where | Roughness and highlight strength from | Code |
|---|---|---|
| texture with an `_rmaos` map (`[Shaders.PBR.Rmaos]` On) | the map (R roughness, A specular level, G metal with `MetalFromMap`); with `VanillaBridgeOverMaps` the bridge instead (the map keeps B occlusion and G metal) | `RmaosRead`, `getRoughness`, `BRIDGE_OVER_MAP` |
| everything else, where the normal map's alpha is above ~0.04 | the game's own Blinn-Phong data through the **vanilla bridge** (below) | `BridgeSetup`, `BridgeSpecF0` |
| where that alpha is ~0 (the game drew no highlight) | the old values: `1 - alpha` (or the material class's `rough=`), F0 0.04 | `getRoughness`, `getMetalRoughness` |
