| a material class with `authored=replace` | the class's `rough=` everywhere, as before the bridge | `VegasShaders.materials.txt`, `MaterialRules.h` |

### Vanilla bridge (`[Shaders.PBR.Main] VanillaBridge`, 2026-11-05)

Diagnosis from the PBR-Reloaded fork's author: authored shine (power armour, polished metal) lost its sun glints because every
highlight went through GGX with a generic roughness. The game's highlight is `mask x (N.H)^n x light`: the normal map's alpha is a
highlight **strength** (not a gloss map), `n` is the mesh's glossiness (`NiMaterialProperty::m_fShine`, sent as `Toggles.z`, c27; the
4+ lamp OPT shaders read it from `PSLightColor[1].w`), and the game's highlight is not normalised and has no Fresnel. Before the
bridge NVR used the mask as `1 - roughness`, dropped `n`, and fixed the strength at F0 0.04: a mask of 0.5 with n = 30 gave a
highlight peak of 0.16 x the light at roughness 0.5 where the game drew 0.5 x the light at about a fifth of that width.

The bridge (`Includes/Object.hlsl`) maps the authored lobe onto the same GGX BRDF:

- **Width**: `(N.H)^n ~ exp(-n t^2 / 2)` is the Beckmann lobe of `alpha^2 = 2 / (n + 2)` (Walter et al. 2007);
  roughness `= alpha^(1/2) = (2 / (n + 2))^(1/4)` (NVR's `GGX()` takes roughness^4 = alpha^2).
- **Peak**: GGX lit and seen head-on returns `D(1) F Vis N.L pi = F / (4 alpha^2)` of the light (Vis = 1/4 for both Smith terms);
  the game's returns `mask`. So the highlight's F0 is `4 alpha^2 mask = 8 mask / (n + 2)` (at most 1). This is the inverse of the
  Blinn-Phong normalisation `(n + 8) / (8 pi)`: the game's lobe is unnormalised, so a sharp lobe of the same peak holds less energy.
- **Why alpha^2 = 2/(n+2) and not a GGX-specific fit** (numbers: `tests/furnace/bridge_lobe_fit.py`): with the peak matched,
  GGX at that alpha has 0.77-0.91 of the Blinn-Phong half width and 1.04-1.20 x its energy for n = 2-200. An exact energy match
  needs `(1.7-1.9) / (n + 2)` and narrows the core to 0.73 x; the least-squares shape fit is `(1.9-2.6) / (n + 2)`. 2 is between.
- **Mask not decoded** under linear lighting: the game sums `(albedo + mask) x light` in gamma space. Over albedo 0.05-0.6 and mask
  0.05-1 the mask used as it is matches that on average (mean error +0.004 of full scale, mean |error| 0.06); decoding it
  (mask^2.2) loses 0.14 on average, which is exactly the "glints gone" look.
- **Only the highlight** takes the bridged F0 (`PBR.hlsl PBR_SPEC_FRESNEL`); the diffuse keeps the dielectric's `(1 - F)` share,
  so a surface's diffuse light does not change. Metal keeps the bridged strength, tinted by its colour's hue (normalised to the
  brightest channel). Fresnel still rises to 1 at grazing angles and the GGX highlight still brightens as the view grazes
  (`1 / N.V`): those are the microfacet model's, not the game's.
- **Gap fill**: the bridge's weight ramps over mask 0-0.04 (`BridgeT`): where the game drew no highlight, the old roughness and
  F0 0.04 (and a material class's `rough=`) stay, so matte surfaces are unchanged. `Roughness` (per weather) scales the bridged
  roughness as it did the old one; `Reflectivity` scales its F0 as before.

Where it applies: the object and parallax pixel shaders that draw a highlight and know `n` (`NVR_VANILLA_BRIDGE` in
`ObjectTemplate.hlsl` / `ParallaxTemplate.hlsl`): every SPECULAR variant with Toggles, the highlight-only passes, and the 4+ lamp
OPT ones, except the 6-lamp OPT shader at the driver's slot limit (SLS2035 with `SPECULAR_LAMPS6`, `NVR_SLOT_TIGHT`; the bridge
costs about 40-46 instruction slots). Not hair, not the skin shader, not terrain.

Switches: `VanillaBridge` (restart; off = the shaders exactly as before, `VANILLA_BRIDGE_ON` is not defined) and
`VanillaBridgeAmount` (live, TESR_PBRData.x, the lane the unused Metallicness setting had: 0 = the old values exactly, a lerp by 0).

`VanillaBridgeOverMaps` (live, off by default; + 2 in the same lane): the bridge also decides where an `_rmaos` map is bound. Vegas
Shaders' generated maps guess roughness from the colour and carry no glossiness: on an install with them (power armour has one),
the bridge changes nothing on those objects unless this is on.

`VanillaBridgeDielectricMax` (restart, default 0.08; 1 = no cap, the 1.0.5 bridge exactly; `VANILLA_BRIDGE_DIELECTRIC_MAX`, defined
only below 1). 2026-11-06 report (Bale, 1.0.5): cliffs very shiny. The peak match gives F0 = 8 mask / (n + 2), so a wide authored lobe
(glossiness about 10, common on world meshes) with a bright mask, or a normal map with no alpha (reads as 1), came out at F0 0.3-0.7
on rock, concrete, wood and plaster: a near-metal sheen. The non-metal share of the bridged F0 is capped (real non-metals: 0.02-0.08);
the metal share keeps the full authored strength. Unseen on our own install: there the generated `_rmaos` maps cover the rock and
the bridge stays out (VanillaBridgeOverMaps off), so the case only shows on installs without the maps.

Measured (2026-11-05, bench slot, Goodsprings 13:00, the player in T-45d power armour in third person, 1920x1080; region = the
armour; "glint px" = pixels standing more than 40 levels above their 31x31 neighbourhood mean; lists `pbrbridge-s1b/s2/s3`):

| picture | armour p99 / p99.9 luma | glint px | glint excess sum |
|---|---|---|---|
| before the bridge, as installed (maps, rules, metal on) | 116 / 133 | 2735 | 438k |
| before, `_rmaos` off / + metal off / rules off (restart) | 114 / 155, 112 / 132, 117 / 134 | 2174, 1897, 2814 | 389k, 445k, 451k |
| bridge, maps on (default: the armour's generated map decides) | 115 / 133 | 2742 | 444k |
| bridge, maps off | 144 / 187 | 3851 | 547k |
| bridge, maps on + `VanillaBridgeOverMaps` | 145 / 188 | 4037 | 566k |
| same launch, `VanillaBridgeAmount` 0 | 112-114 / 134-159 | 2221-2603 | 393-422k |
| the game's own shaders (PBR off) | 201 / 244 | 8600 | 1.19M |

So neither the maps, the material rules nor the metal look killed the glints (each moves them by noise); the generic GGX mapping
did. With the bridge the armour's highlight pixels grow by 45-73 % and the brightest 1 % by 30 luma levels; the rest of the
picture moves 0.87-1.4 levels on average (the noise between two launches of one build is 1.95). The game's own picture stays
brighter because its diffuse light is also brighter on the armour (region mean 69 vs 43-45: gamma-space lighting and no metal look,
which leaves the metal parts no diffuse light), not because of the highlight alone.

Known gaps: the FoldHighlights light-only passes (objects reached by 3+ lamps indoors) and the DeferredLamps pass have no `n`
and keep the old highlight. DeferredLamps reads the bridged roughness from the material buffer and, since upstream 1.1.3, the record's
non-metal F0 (`DLSpecF0` from `FDSpecF0Decode`, D3D9 passes only; the Vulkan lamp compute `Compute/DeferredLamps.comp` still uses 0.04).

### Reflectivity under the deferred renderer (checked 2026-11-26)

`ReflectivityExterior` / `ReflectivityInterior` reach every PBR highlight as `TESR_PBRMath.y` (c223) = Reflectivity - 1 (`PBR.cpp`
UpdateConstants): `Includes/PBR.hlsl` `ReflectivityF0` (F0 x R) and `REFLECTIVITY_F90` (saturate(R)), in `PBR`, `PBRSun`,
`PBRSunSpecular`, `PBRSpecular` and `PBR_SPEC_FRESNEL`. In DeferredRenderer 2 the deferred passes get the same value:

| Pass | Surfaces | How c223 is set | Code path |
|---|---|---|---|
| DeferredSun | land, objects, parallax, metal | the record's `SetCT` (c223 is in its constant table: the game's cached `Deferred\Sun\DeferredSun.pso`) | `LandSun` -> `PBRSun`; `getSunLightingModel` |
| DeferredLamps (lamp, land, land film passes) | objects, land | `core/DeferredLamps.h` Apply, `kGlobals` | `getPointLightLightingAtt`, `DLLampLand` -> `PBR` |
| Vulkan lamp compute | objects, land | `cb.pbrMath` (`copyConst`) | `DeferredLamps.comp` `ReflectivityF0` |

Not scaled, as in forward: skin (its own F0), the wet film (`WetSpecular`), the foliage records' carried sun.

Why the slider showed little on the ground after the upstream 1.1.1-1.1.3 merge (58b8e1c): upstream added `PBR_ROUGH_F90`
(roughness-damped grazing F90, for parity with the path tracer) to DeferredSun, DeferredLamps, FlashlightDeferred and the lamp compute.
There F90 = max(saturate(R) x (1 - roughness x (1 - metal)), F0); the forward templates (TerrainTemplate, ObjectTemplate) keep
saturate(R). The slider still scales F0 and F90 the same way in both (for R up to 1 the highlight is exactly R times), but on rough ground
the grazing sheen toward the sun, which is most of what the slider moved, is damped away, so the scaled part is small. A model of the
`PBRSun` maths (flat ground, sun 25 degrees up, eye 10 degrees up looking toward the sun, albedo 0.25), total sun light at R 0.5 against
R 1: roughness 0.4 -47 % forward / -45 % deferred; 0.8 -27 % / -14 %; 0.95 -15 % / -4 %. Not measured in game.

`[Shaders.PBR.Main] DeferredLandRoughF90` (2026-11-26, live; default false, the owner's call: "add the reflectivity to ground"). False: the
deferred land skips the damping and takes the forward land's F90, saturate(R), so the Reflectivity sliders scale the ground's shine as in
forward and as before the merge; rough ground also shines more toward a low sun at Reflectivity 1 than in 1.1.1-1.1.3. True: upstream's
damped F90, as in 1.1.1-1.1.3 (the fallback). Objects, parallax, skin and the flashlight keep the damping either way; the land's wet film
never had it. How it is sent: `TESR_LinearScales.w` (c168.w, a lane nothing else reads) = 1 for false, 0 for true (`PBR.cpp`
UpdateConstants; a register never written = the damped F90). Where it is read:

- `DeferredSun.hlsl` `LandSun` sets `DSLandF90` before `PBRSun`; the pass's `PBR_ROUGH_F90_PICK` then gives `REFLECTIVITY_F90` instead
  of `RoughReflectivityF90` for the land's calls only.
- `DeferredLamps.hlsl`'s land pass (`DL_LAND_ONLY`, not the film pass) sets `DLLandF90` before its lamp loop.
- `Compute/DeferredLamps.comp` `PBRFull(..., landF90)`: true only for land pixels, from `cb.linearScales.w`.

`Includes/PBR.hlsl`: `PBR_ROUGH_F90_PICK(damped)` defaults to `(damped)` inside the `PBR_ROUGH_F90` block, which now starts on its old
comment's line so that every forward shader's preprocessed text is unchanged (checked with fxc /P on the land, object, skin, parallax,
water, env and hair templates: byte-identical). With the key true the deferred maths is the old expression, chosen by a select.

### Material maps (`_rmaos`)

`[Shaders.PBR.Rmaos] Enabled` (restart). Where a texture has `<name>_rmaos.dds` beside it or under `textures\pbr\` at the same
path, the object shaders read it on sampler s2 (`Render.cpp` RmaosDraw binds it per draw, sets `NVR_Rmaos` c220). Layout is the
Community Shaders "True PBR" one:

| Channel | Meaning | Used as |
|---|---|---|
| R | roughness | replaces the guess from the normal map's alpha (`getRoughness`) |
| G | metal | only with `MetalFromMap` (default off: Vegas Shaders' generated maps carry a guessed metal) |
| B | ambient occlusion, white = none | darkens the ambient light (`RmaosAmbient`, times `Occlusion` and the weather's `AOScale`) |
| A | specular level | non-metal reflectance F0 = 0.04 x A (`SpecularFromMap`, 2026-11-02) |

Specular level: white (1) is the usual 0.04, so maps without a painted level (and Vegas Shaders' generated maps, which write A = 1)
look exactly as before; darker values reflect less. It is set through `PBR_DIELECTRIC_F0`, which `Object.hlsl` defines before it
includes `PBR.hlsl`; every other includer of `PBR.hlsl` (skin, terrain, water) gets the plain 0.04. Not applied in the 6-lamp
interior shader (SLS2035, `NVR_SLOT_TIGHT`), which is at the instruction count the driver refuses. `SpecularFromMap = false`
compiles it out (`RMAOS_SPEC_OFF`, `ShaderRecord.cpp`).

Edge cases: a map that has just loaded fades in over `FadeIn` seconds (the specular level too, from 0.04); `DebugView` 1-4 shows
roughness, metal, occlusion, or which objects have a map. MaterialAware reflections read the map's roughness and metal from the
scene's alpha (`core/ReflMaterial.h`).

Memory (2026-11-12, `Render.cpp` RmaosDraw `Trim` / `Drop`): the loaded maps are a working set, not kept for the session (an install
can hold 5 GB of them, and they take the game's 4 GB address space). `BudgetMB` (default 512; 0 = no limit, the old behaviour) and
`KeepSeconds` (default 30), both live. Over the budget, the maps no draw used for `KeepSeconds` are released, the longest unseen
first, at most 8 a frame, until 85 % of the budget is in use; a released map loads again through the normal queue at its next draw
(and fades in again). The keep time is counted only over frames in which maps were drawn, so a pause or a loading screen ages
nothing. If every loaded map was drawn within `KeepSeconds`, nothing is released and the log says so once. Log lines start with
`UNOFFICIAL Rmaos budget:` (the first pass of the budget, a count at most once a minute, the "needs more than the budget" line).
A release first takes the texture off the device's stages and out of the game's note of them (`NoteDeviceTexture`); the sampler is
`NVR_RmaosMap`, not a `TESR_` one, so no shader record holds the pointer. Also: maps start loading only with `On` (they used to load
with it off), the reading thread waits while 4 read files are waiting to be made into textures, and the per-texture lookups are
emptied at 4096 entries.

How to check it: an object with a hand-made map whose alpha is dark should show weaker sun and lamp highlights on its non-metal
parts than before; with Vegas Shaders' generated maps nothing changes.

### Micro Shadow (`[Shaders.PBR.MicroShadow]`; forward dropped 2026-11-27)

The bumps of a normal map shadow themselves by the `_rmaos` map's occlusion (blue): per light `saturate(|N.L| + 2 AO^2 - 1)`, with
`Strength` deepening the map and `Bounce` the least light a crevice keeps (`Includes/Object.hlsl` `MicroShadowTerm`, c194).
- Since 2026-11-27 (owner: "Yes, forward only") it is compiled only into the deferred lighting-free object and parallax records
  (`LAMPS_DEFERRED 2`, `CAPTURED_NORMALS_ON >= 4`), which record the SUN's share for DeferredSun (`FDSunVisMicro`, switched by
  `[Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Deferred] MicroShadow`). The main view under the deferred renderer is unchanged (those records are
  byte-identical to before, offline). The forward records no longer take it, for the sun or the lamps: in reflections, blended (fading)
  draws, other cameras and everything with `DeferredRenderer 0`, crevices on objects with `_rmaos` maps lose that extra darkening (a slight
  change). It only ever acted where an `_rmaos` map exists, so skin and vanilla armour lose nothing; the one visible exception is gear
  with its own `_rmaos` maps (e.g. room 04's Ghost outfit), whose crevices lose the darkening in forward draws. Bench 2026-11-27 (the
  owner's settings, v-desert-noon, 600-frame settle): the main view matches main 550d8c9 by eye; SLS2024 loads (dry, as on main with
  these settings) and SLS2036 now fits with the water layer. The deferred lamp passes and the flashlight never had it (no `_rmaos` read there; their `MICRO_SHADOW` expands to nothing):
  lamps to be done in the deferred path later. ShaderFallback's object order is Wetness alone now (dropping Micro Shadow cannot shorten a
  forward record any more). The settings stay: the deferred sun reads all three.

### Which data decides metal (mirror-like surfaces)

| Mode | A pixel's metal share | Code |
|---|---|---|
| default (`[Shaders.PBR.EnvMaps] FromGameData` false) | an `_rmaos` map's G with `MetalFromMap`; else a material class's; else the guess from grey colour and the normal map's alpha (bare alpha: `GreyMetal` on the first-person weapon, `WorldGreyMetal` on the world, default 0 since 2026-11-16 so bare rock and concrete are not metal), times `[Shaders.PBR.Metal]` World / FirstPerson | `getMetalStrength`, `getDerivedMetallic` |
| `FromGameData` true (restart; then live) | the game's env-map data: Environment_Mapping flag + its `_m` mask (texture slot 5) = env-map scale x mask; flag, no mask = scale x (`FromGameDataNoMask`: 1 the whole surface, 0 the normal map's alpha, the game's own rule); no flag = 0, whatever the colour; an `_rmaos` map's G over all of it; times World / FirstPerson. Material classes' metal is not used | `NVR_EnvData` (c94), `Render.cpp` RmaosDraw |

Facts the design rests on (decomp of 1.4.0.525, 2026-11-09): the flag is BSShaderProperty flags word 0 bit 7; the strength is the
property's float at +0x30 (`fEnvMapScale`, the env pass's c27.z); the env cube is texture slot 4 and the mask slot 5; the game's env
pass (0x248-0x24B, `EnvTemplate.hlsl`) reads the mask on s3 when slot 5 holds a texture, else the normal map's alpha.
The mask is bound on s2, the `_rmaos` stage (a draw with a map never needs it), and carried in `RmaosValue.g` (no temp register of its
own). Constant c94: ps_3_0 has only c0-c223, and every other register is taken in some object-shader variant (register-table audit
of every ObjectTemplate pixel shader); c94 is otherwise only the land shaders' (`TESR_TerrainParallaxIso`), so it is written before
every object draw, never cached. Limits: the 4+ lamp SPECULAR interior lamp-shadow records (SLS2034-2036 with `INTERIOR_SHADOWS`)
are at the 32-temp limit and never read the mask (`ENV_NO_MASK`: the no-mask rule there); the 6-lamp OPT shader SLS2035 has the game
data INSTEAD of the guess while compiled in (it is at the slot count a driver refused), so its objects have no metal while the live
switch is off. Eyes (their own variant) and windows (Window_Environment_Mapping, glass) are never made metal. Not done: the SSR
MaterialAware buffer and the RT materials still know only `_rmaos` maps; the env pass's `WithMetal` is per pass, not per pixel.

What correct looks like (`[Shaders.PBR.Metal] DebugView 1`): with `FromGameData` on, orange only on env-mapped meshes (guns, chrome
fittings, polished stone with a mask), following the mask's pattern; grey concrete and cloth no longer orange.

### Eyes (`[Shaders.PBR.Main] GameShaderEyes`, `EyeShaderOld`)

The game draws an eyeball (BSShaderProperty flag 17, Eye_Environment_Mapping) with its ordinary lit passes, specular forced off
(so the metal guess, compiled only into SPECULAR variants, never reaches it), then its own eye env pass (0x24B: SLS2052.vso +
SLS2059.pso, additive ONE/ONE, the fixed eyereflection.dds cube; a skinned eye takes the generic 0x249 env pass instead). In game the
lit pair is SLS2022.vso + SLS2031.pso (logs 2026-10-28). Under LinearScene (default on) the old exemption (the game's own shaders) was
off, so the eyes took the mod's object shader: sun shadow from the head and lids, the normal map's bumps, lit as a small ball.
`GameShaderEyes` on with `EyeShaderOld` false (default, live): every lit pass of an eye draws with an eye record of its pixel shader
(`ObjectTemplate.hlsl` `NVR_EYE`, made on first use by `Render.cpp` GameEyes::RecordFor, cached under `Shaders\...\Eyes\`): the ambient
as for any object plus the sun's and the lamps' light as a wrapped diffuse (wrap 0.5) on the eyeball's own surface (tangent-space
(0,0,1): no normal map, no UV assumption), no shadows, no metal, output through `LinearSceneOut` (no frame conversion). The game's
eye env pass is kept as it is. `EyeShaderOld` true: as before (the game's shaders with LinearScene off, the mod's ordinary shader
with it on). `GameShaderEyes` false: the mod's ordinary shader. The log line `EYE PROBE` names each distinct eye draw (pass, pair,
NVR or game handle, blend state, textures) once.
Trap met while building it: a pixel record's `SetCT` empties all 16 texture stages (it expects to run at a bind, before the game binds
the pass's textures). The eye record's `SetCT` runs inside the draw, so eye records have `ClearSamplers` off and the restore after the
draw uploads constants only; otherwise the second eye drawn (both share `eyeblue.dds`, so the game does not bind it again) is black.
Bench pictures and the full write-up: `docs/next/EYES-ENVFLAG-2026-11-09.md` (not in this repo).

### Upstream 1.1.5 merge (2026-11-27)
- Glow maps: `[Shaders.PBR.Main] DecoupleGlowMaps` (true) and `GlowScale` (1.0, 0-4, live) keep self-lit maps (weapon sights, displays) out of
  scene lighting. `GlowScale` rides on `TESR_SharpSky.w` (c136) in the forward object shaders, because `TESR_LinearScales.w` (c168.w) is our
  `DeferredLandRoughF90` (`PBR.cpp UpdateConstants` writes it; `Includes/Object.hlsl` `GlowDecoupleMask` / `GlowScaleFactor` read it). The
  deferred passes are untouched.
- Clearcoat and pearlescence: `[Shaders.PBR.Metal] Clearcoat` and `Pearlescence` (both 0.0, 0-1). Clearcoat is a glossy lacquer (F0 0.04,
  roughness 0.06) over metal; pearlescence a viewing-angle hue shift. The deferred sun and lamp passes read both live (`DS_Sheen` z / w, the
  lamps' `PBRFull` clearcoat / pearl parameters). The forward object shaders compile the code only when the key is above 0 at startup
  (`METAL_CLEARCOAT_ON` / `METAL_PEARL_ON`, set by `BuildDefines`, mirrored in `template_defines.py`): turning one on from 0 needs a restart
  (the menu says so), after that it is live. Why: with both always compiled the 2-lamp object records ran out of room (SLS2023 wet -> X4505,
  `docs/DESIGN.md "Upstream 1.1.5 merge"`). Not run: a forward compile or a game run after the gate.
- Metal defaults moved to upstream's (steps 104-108; `GreyMetal` 0.45 and the others): a saved value at the old default moves, a tuned one stays.
- Land reflectivity: `DeferredLandRoughF90` stays ours (see "Reflectivity under the deferred renderer").
- Look: with both keys at 0 nothing changes from before. Wrong: a lacquer glint on metal at 0, or forward records dropping to dry after
  turning either on (the define makes the records bigger; the dry fallback catches it).

## Particles

*README title: Particles - lit and soft particles*

UNOFFICIAL. Written 2026-11-09 (roadmap track 3, steps 1 and 2); the code is older (lit particles 2026-10, soft particles 2026-11-04).

### 1. Purpose

The game draws smoke, dust, blood and debris particle systems unlit: they keep full brightness in shade, at night and indoors. This
collection replaces their shaders so they take the scene's light, and makes every particle and unlit effect mesh fade where it meets
the scene behind it (soft particles) instead of cutting a hard line.

### 2. When it runs

- `[Shaders.Particles.Status] Enabled` (default false). Read at startup for the soft-particle parts (the depth copy texture,
  `core/ParticleSize.h`'s vertex hook); the lighting follows the switch live (`ShaderCollection::SwitchShader`).
- `Strength` above 0 for the lighting; `SoftScale` / `MeshSoftScale` above 0 for the fade.
- With "Vanilla Plus Particles" loaded (`VanillaPlusParticles.dll`), `ParticleSize` is not installed: particle systems get no fade
  (meshes still do, and the lighting works).

### 3. How it works

Shaders (all game-shader replacements, loaded at runtime from `Shaders\VegasShaders\Shaders\`; see SHADERS.txt):
- `NOLIGHT016.vso` / `NOLIGHT017.vso` (particle systems, plain and texture-sheet): the game's vs_2_0 shaders ported, plus the
  camera-relative world position (with the shadow sentinel), the vertex light (`ParticleVertexLight`: ambient + the 24 tracked lamps,
  unshadowed, radius falloff) and the view depth + particle size for the fade.
- `NOLIGHT006.vso` (falloff cards): the same outputs, vertexLight.w = 2.
- `NOLIGHTTEXVC.pso`: texture x vertex colour, the game's fog modes, then `ParticleLight`: vertex light + the sun through the forward sun
  shadow (`GetSunShadow`) x `SunShare` x the sun phase (below). Lit only behind the particle vertex shaders (the sentinel), and falloff
  cards only when normally blended: additive draws (muzzle and impact flashes, sparks, glows: the game marks them with the
  fade-to-black fog mode) stay exactly as the game draws them. `SetShadersHook` gives draws with the game's own vs_2_0 vertex shaders the
  game's pixel shader back (D3D9 cannot pair ps_3_0 with vs_2_0).
- `GDECAL.vso` / `GDECALS.vso` / `GDECAL.pso` (blood decals on characters): the same light, without the sun phase.
- `Includes/SoftParticle.hlsl`: alpha x saturate((scene depth - particle depth) / (size x SoftScale)), the scene depth from a copy of
  the world's depth taken at the first alpha-blended NoLighting draw of the world scene (`ParticleShaders::ForDraw`).

#### Sun phase (2026-11-09, `SunPhase`)

A thin particle scatters the sun toward the camera more when the sun is behind it. `ParticleSunPhase` multiplies the sun term by the
Henyey-Greenstein phase on the angle between the sun's light and the view ray, divided by its isotropic value 1/(4 pi):

    P(cos) = (1 - g^2) / (1 + g^2 - 2 g cos)^1.5,   cos = dot(toward the sun, camera -> particle)

Its average over all view directions is exactly 1, so the average brightness stays the flat `SunShare`'s. g > 0: smoke and dust glow
looking toward the sun (peak (1+g)/(1-g)^2: 2.65 at g 0.3, 10 at 0.6) and read darker from the sun's side (minimum (1-g)/(1+g)^2:
0.41 at g 0.3). g = 0 is skipped by a branch: exactly the old shader path. The direction is `ShaderConst.SunDir` (the sun disc by
day, the game's directional light, i.e. the moon, at night). Only in `NOLIGHTTEXVC.pso` (`PARTICLE_SUN_PHASE`); the blood decals
compile byte-identical to before.

### 4. Settings ([Shaders.Particles.Main])

| key | default | what |
|---|---|---|
| Strength | 1.0 | 0 = the game's unlit look, 1 = fully lit |
| SunShare | 0.6 | share of the sun a particle catches outdoors |
| SunPhase | 0.3 | Henyey-Greenstein g of the sun on particles, [-0.9, 0.9]; 0 = the old flat share |
| SoftScale | 0.25 | particle systems: fade over their size x this; 0 = off |
| MeshSoftScale | 0.25 | unlit effect meshes: fade over their bound radius x this; 0 = off |

### 5. Constants

| name | register (ps / vs) | components |
|---|---|---|
| TESR_ParticleData | c168 / c200 | x Strength, y 1, z SunShare, w 1 outdoors |
| TESR_ParticleAmbient | - / c201 | rgb ambient (the sky's outdoors, the cell's indoors) |
| TESR_ParticlePhase | c193 (NOLIGHTTEXVC only) | xyz toward the sun (world), w SunPhase |
| TESR_SunColor | c192 (ps) | the weather's sun colour (c170 until 2026-11-09: overwritten per pass, see section 9) |
| TESR_ParticleSoft | c175 | x soft scale for this draw (0 off), yz 1 / resolution |
| NVR_SoftSize | vs c41, set per draw | x mesh size, y 1 when alpha-blended |

### 6. Vulkan path

None of its own: the game shaders run through DXVK as any D3D9 shader. RT / PT do not trace particles.

### 7. What right and wrong look like

Right: smoke and dust darker in shade and at night, warm near fires and lamps, brighter and glowing when looking toward a low sun
(SunPhase > 0), dimmer with the sun behind the camera; no hard line where smoke meets the ground; flashes, sparks, glows and flames
exactly as with the switch off.
Wrong: particles black or pure white (a missing constant), additive flashes dimmed (the pairing guard broke), a halo or speckled outline
of object edges through smoke with game MSAA on (the depth copy), smoke vanishing near the ground over a long distance (SoftScale too
high for big particles).

### 8. Measured

See docs/next/PARTICLES-BENCH-2026-11-09.md (not in this repo) (pictures off / on / phase, MSAA 0 and 4, frame totals).

### 9. Known problems (bench 2026-11-09)

- Fixed: the sun never reached particles or blood decals. `TESR_SunColor` sat in pixel c170, which `HudRaindrops::SetDrawConstants`
  writes on every geometry pass (zeros when dry) after the shader's constants are uploaded, so the sun term was always black. Moved to
  c192 (the phase to c193: BarrelHeat writes c171-c173 per draw). Pick pinned pixel registers for these shaders outside every per-draw
  write: c168-c175, c180-c191, c194, c197-c198, c204, c206-c222 are written per draw by some hook.
- Open (why it is still off by default): normally blended glow meshes take the lit path like smoke. The Strip's night light dome goes
  dark, and its steam turns purple. The mesh soft fade (MeshSoftScale x bound radius) cuts big glow meshes where geometry stands behind them,
  and dims window glow cards that sit flush on walls. Both are gone with Strength 0 + MeshSoftScale 0.

## PipboyFit

Code: `src/core/PipboyFit.h` (no C++ file in this folder), shader `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Shaders/Includes/PipboyFit.hlsl`, defaults
`resource/defaults/PipboyFit.toml` (`[_Shaders.PipboyFit.Main]`). Written 2026-11-25 from the room-02 memory note
`pipboy-sleeve-clipping-research` (2026-11-03 to 2026-11-05 work), which is archived in `F:\Claude Brain\archive\2026-11-25.zip\vs\memory\`.
Where this file and the code disagree, the code wins: the note's values were checked against `resource/defaults/PipboyFit.toml` on 2026-11-25.

### 1. Purpose
After GPU skinning, pulls the left-forearm vertices of armor sleeves in under the Pip-Boy casing, using a soft clamp inside an ellipse
in the frame of the "Bip01 L ForeTwist" bone, so sleeves no longer poke through the casing.

- Who gets it: first person, plus the player only in third person (`ThirdPerson`), never NPCs. Bare skin is never fitted
  (`SkinTemplate` has no fit code; owner's decision 2026-11-03: a bare arm, Improved Vanilla Male Body, got a ~0.9 unit dent at x 3-4).
- Upstream has had the fit since 1.0.2; only the `PowerArmor` toggle is ours (fork branch `pipboy-fit`: d7af59b fit, bc75ba2 PowerArmor).

### 2. Inputs, registers, passes
- Compiled into ObjectTemplate and EnvTemplate under `#ifdef SKIN` only; compile define `PIPBOY_FIT_ON` (restart to change).
- Vertex-shader registers c182-c187, written per skinned partition draw from `ClothWind::DrawPartition` (the partition hook is installed
  when Cloth or PipboyFit is on). c182: bone slots, w = wrist scale; c183-c185: shader space to capsule frame; c186: on, strength, knee,
  falloff; c187: start, end, radius y, radius z. Conflicts checked in 2026-11: PS c182 (PointShadowForward) is a separate register file,
  and GDECALS VS c180-c187 is not skinned.
- Finding its draws: `ForGeometry` from `RenderPassSkinnedHook` (skin has "Bip01 L ForeTwist", name not containing "pipboy"); in third
  person only for the player (`OnPlayer` walks m_parent to Player->GetNode()). Only vertices weighted to L ForeTwist / Forearm / Hand move.
- Bone matching goes through `src/core/SkinBones.h` (by pointer; an earlier `_stricmp` through a bad bone slot crashed with a VirtualLegs
  mesh from PlayerPhysics / NV-SMP-Physics: see `docs/DESIGN.md "SMP compatibility"`). Tester workaround for old builds: `Exclude += virtuallegs`.

### 3. Settings (`[_Shaders.PipboyFit.Main]`, all live except Enabled; defaults as in the repo)
Shape: Strength 1, Knee 0.7, Falloff 2, Start 4.5, End 17, CenterY 0.1, CenterZ 0.4, RadiusY 2.6, RadiusZ 2.6, WristScale 0.6, Axis 0.
Who: ThirdPerson true, PowerArmor true (false skips the fit while the player wears a power-armor torso), Exclude "pipboy".
The owner tunes the values live in the menu; old saved values can hide changed defaults (RadiusY/Z 3.0 stayed after the default changed).

### 4. Measurements and engine facts
**Proven by measurement (2026-11-03, a Sonnet agent):**
- ForeTwist +X runs along the forearm; the wrist is at x 18.1.
- The casing is `meshes\pipboy3000\pipboyarm.nif`: shape at x 4.4-17.1, inner radius about 3.3, centre y 0.1 z 0.4; its inner narrows to
  about 1.6-2.6 over the last 4 units at the wrist end (why WristScale tapers the radius over End-4..End).
- The glove1st NIFs hold only the hand. Vanilla sleeves poke out to about 1.0-1.7.
- The third-person skeleton + `pipboyarmnpc.nif` measure the same, so one set of values serves both views.
**Engine facts (FNV 1.4.0.525):** Pip-Boy = biped slot 6; the pipboyon / pipboyoff child-name switch is in BipedAnim::AttachSkinnedObject
(0x4AD4A0); `foo1st.nif` is used automatically in first person (0x4AF950); power-armor torso test =
MiddleHighProcess::IsWearingPowerArmorTorso (0x8D8290) on Player->process.

### 5. What correct / wrong looks like, debugging
- Correct: sleeves end on the casing's inside, no cloth visible outside the Pip-Boy, no step at the elbow or wrist end.
- If it misbehaves, suspect the shader-space derivation (palette c44 against the computed bone) or the Axis. The 2026-11-03 log build
  printed "PipboyFit: space check" (want about 0; it read 0.0001-0.0007 in first and third person, scale 1.000). One 1800-frame window
  had 288k first-person fits (~160 a frame, likely many lamp passes or the Pip-Boy menu), otherwise about 2 a frame.

### 6. Open
- **Open:** an in-game visual check of the tighter values and of power armor (not done as far as the note says).
- **Open:** cloth wind on SMP-simulated garments needs an extra-data reader for "SMP Bones" (docs/DESIGN.md "SMP compatibility").
- Balebandro's fork fixed a crash in `PipboyFit::ForPartition` after a load; ours is the by-pointer bone match above.

## PointShadows

*README title: Effect spec: PointShadows*

REWRITTEN 2026-11-02 (branch claude/rw3-lights): the shaders are new, written from this spec (Effects/PointShadows.fx.hlsl; Compute/PointShadows.frag, PointShadowsCommon.glsl, PointShadowsContact.frag, PointShadowsUpsample.frag). The C++ (effects/PointShadows.cpp, ShaderManager.cpp) and the settings are unchanged. Fixed bugs are marked FIXED where they are listed.

### 1. Purpose

This pass builds one grey mask for the whole screen that says how much of the nearby point lights (lamps) and the one spot light reach each visible surface. A lamp is shadowed where a lamp shadow map says something stands between the lamp and the surface, and that lamp then gives nothing there. The mask goes into a screen-sized buffer that the later shadow effects read to darken what no lamp reaches. The player sees lamp shadows: a bookcase or a character blocks a lamp and the wall behind it goes dark, indoors always and outdoors at night when point-light shadows are enabled. The pass also writes an optional small contact-shadow multiplier (dark lines where a prop meets the floor or a wall meets a table) indoors.

### 2. When it runs

Frame placement (src\core\ShaderManager.cpp, the pre-tonemap chain, function RenderEffectsPreTonemapping):
- It runs after the depth and normals are built (depth combine, normal reconstruction and smoothing, captured normals, object motion, lamp visibility ranking, the "scene in" capture, the sun depth-fit measurement, the ray tracing core point, capsule shadows and back depth). It runs before the lamp contact pass, the sun contact shadows, and the shadow apply and later effects that read the buffer.
- The whole chain is skipped when the main render toggle (RenderEffects) is off, when the player has no parent cell, or when an overlay (terminal, lockpicking) is on while the menu background is ready.

Gates, in order:
1. Indoors (not exterior): runs when the interior shadow effect is enabled ([Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Status] Enabled, default true). Exception: when the per-object forward lamp shadows drew this frame ("forward interior") and [Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward] KeepDarkening is off, the pass is skipped and the buffer is cleared once to "no shadow" instead.
2. Outdoors: runs when the exterior shadow effect is enabled ([Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Status] Enabled, default true). The pass itself does not check [Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] UsePointShadowsDay / UsePointShadowsNight (defaults false). Those set the per-frame switch that enables cube-map reads (see section 3, TESR_ShadowFade.z). With that switch off, the pass still runs with no cube reads. Whether that is intended is an open question (section 9).
3. Day or night outdoors: the switch used is UsePointShadowsDay when the game's day flag is above 0.5, otherwise UsePointShadowsNight.
4. Indoor pass with forward lamp shadows on: the cube-map reads are switched off for the pass (the switch is forced to 0 for that frame and restored after), so the mask is only the lamps' reach (the room darkening). The per-object shadows already carry the shadows.
5. Within the pass the buffer is cleared to 0 (black) before the merged technique draws. Then, in order: the outdoor batches (ExteriorLightPoints above 12, section 5), the second-pass effect PointShadows2 (only when the merged technique is missing and [Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main] LightPoints is above 6; a different effect, not specified here), the outdoor traced-lamp mask, and then the indoor lamp contact pass (technique 2) when ContactShadows is above 0.
6. Half-resolution mask (section 5, techniques 3 and 4): only indoors, only with forward lamp shadows, and only with [Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward] MaskHalfRes on (default off), and only when both techniques exist in the loaded effect file.
7. Scissor option (section 6): the merged pass may be drawn only inside a rectangle on screen. Default on.

Cost gating in the Vulkan route: see section 7 (the Vulkan route is not used in a rect frame, see section 6).

### 3. Inputs

#### 3.1 Textures and samplers (D3D9 pixel shader)

Samplers are bound by their declared register numbers (s0 to s12) and one name-bound slot (stage 13). Filter and address modes are the effect file's own states.

| Name | Register | Holds | Filter / address |
|---|---|---|---|
| TESR_DepthBuffer | s0 | Vegas Shaders linear depth: stored value multiplied by the far plane distance (TESR_CameraData y) gives view distance in game units | linear, clamp |
| TESR_NormalsBuffer | s1 | per-pixel normal, encoded 0..1 (x2-1 gives a view-space normal, then multiplied by TESR_ViewTransform to get world space) | point, clamp |
| TESR_ShadowCubeMapBuffer0 .. TESR_ShadowCubeMapBuffer10 | s2 .. s12 (slot k is register s(k+2)) | the lamp's shadow cube map of lamp slot k: the nearest caster's distance from the lamp, divided by the lamp's radius, per direction. A texel of 0 or 1 means nothing was drawn there (no test) | linear, clamp, cube |
| NVR_LampMaskHalf | s13 (bound by name, not declaration order) | the half-resolution mask in R and the full-resolution depth in G (format G16R16F, half size rounded up) | point, clamp in the effect file (see section 9) |

Slot 11 (the 12th shadow lamp, index 11 in TESR_ShadowLightPosition) has no cube map: it is lit without a shadow lookup. The cube map for lamp slot 11 is never sampled.

#### 3.2 Constants

No constant has a fixed register in this effect file; the compiler assigns them. The table gives each constant's meaning. "Units" are game units; the settings file says about 70 units make one metre.

| Name | Size | Meaning per component | Units / notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TESR_ShadowLightPosition | 12 vec4 | xyz world position of the lamp in shadow slot k (slots 0..10 have cube maps, slot 11 does not). w = that lamp's radius (w 0 = no lamp in the slot) | game units; the radius is the reach the cube map covers |
| TESR_LightPosition | 12 vec4 | xyz world position of tracked lamps (no shadow slot). w = radius; w 0 ends the list | game units |
| TESR_LightColor | 24 vec4 | entries 0..11 are the colour of shadow slots 0..11; entries 12..23 are the colour of the tracked lamps (lamp i uses entry i+12). rgb = colour, w = dimmer | the lamp's light weight = Rec.709 luma of rgb times w |
| TESR_ExtraLights | 16 vec4 | xyz position of an extra lamp (lamps past the shadow and tracked sets, nearest first). w packs radius and weight: radius = floor(w / 8), weight = w - 8 x radius. w 0 ends the list | weight is luma x dimmer, clamped 0..7.99 when packed. Merged technique only |
| TESR_ShadowFade | 1 vec4 | x: sun fade (not used here). y: interior shadow flag (not used here). z: cube-map switch (1 = read cube maps; 0 = no cube reads, every lamp counts unshadowed). w: point-light draw distance (not used here) | z is 1 indoors (0 for the forward-interior pass), and outdoors it is UsePointShadowsDay or UsePointShadowsNight |
| TESR_PointShadowNear | 1 vec4 | x: NearFade (game units, 0 = off). y: EdgeFade (fraction of a lamp's radius, 0 = off). z: reach-mode value. w: MaskSkipFar switch (1 = skip lamps past their radius; exact either way) | z > 0: reach mode, z = 1 / LightRadiusMult (never below 1 for the multiplier). z < 0: forward mode 2 (attenuation without its angle term). z = 0: normal attenuation with the surface angle |
| TESR_SpotLightPosition | 1 vec4 | xyz world position of the spot light; w = its radius (0 = no spot) | game units |
| TESR_SpotLightDirection | 1 vec4 | xyz direction the spot points; w = full cone angle in degrees (half this angle is full strength) | degrees |
| TESR_SpotLightColor | 1 vec4 | rgb colour; w = dimmer | spot weight = luma(rgb) x w |
| TESR_LampContact | 1 vec4 | x: contact strength (0..1, 0 = off). y: ray length toward the lamp. z: thickness (a surface thinner than this in front does not block). w: normal offset for the start of the ray (0.5) | y, z, w in game units |
| TESR_LampContactFade | 1 vec4 | x: fade-in start, distance from the camera (25). y: 1 / fade-in range (1/20). z: fade-out end distance (the ContactDistance setting). w: 1 / fade-out range (1 / (0.3 x ContactDistance)) | game units |
| TESR_LampContactBias | 1 vec4 | x: depth bias (0.3). y: bias per unit of depth (0.002). z: normal offset per unit of depth (0.001). w: smallest share of the total lamp light that the traced lamp must give (0.2) | units as stated |
| TESR_LampContactNoise | 1 vec4 | x, y: factors that turn a pixel's uv into the noise position (0.06711056 x screen width, 0.00583715 x screen height). z: 52.9829189. w: 1 = noise on (darkening blurred), 0 = even steps | |
| TESR_ReciprocalResolution | 1 vec4 | xy = 1 / full-resolution width and height | |
| TESR_CameraPosition | 1 vec4 | xyz world position of the camera | game units |
| TESR_CameraData | 1 vec4 | y = far plane distance (scale for the stored depth); x = near plane | game units |
| TESR_ViewTransform | 4x4 | the view matrix as stored (HLSL row-vector convention); columns 0..2 give the camera's right, up and forward axes | |
| TESR_ProjectionTransform | 4x4 | the projection matrix as stored | |

Values fed by the engine per frame (src\core\ShaderManager.cpp and src\effects\ShadowsExterior.cpp):
- Lamps are chosen nearest first. A lamp keeps the shadow slot it had last frame when possible. Slot 11 keeps the farthest shadow caster, and is lit without a shadow lookup. Tracked lamps (TESR_LightPosition) are the lamps beyond the shadow slots. Extra lamps (TESR_ExtraLights) come from the forward-shadow slots past 24 and the tracked lamps they push off (indoors, with forward shadows).
- In the outdoor batch passes, the shadow slots of one batch are swapped in for each pass, then restored (section 5).

### 4. Settings

The effect has no settings section of its own. Every value below is read by the engine and fed into the constants in section 3. The effect file itself has no switches. "Read by" names where the value is consumed; the source is the defaults file resource\VegasShaders.defaults.toml and the reader in src\effects\ShadowsExterior.cpp unless stated.

#### 4.1 [Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main]

| Key | Type | Range (clamp in code) | Default | Visual effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NearFade | float | 0 to 128 (floored at 0) | 0.0 | Blockers closer than this (game units) to a lamp cast less shadow from it, none at the lamp itself. 0 = as before |
| EdgeFade | float | 0 to 0.9 | 0.0 | Each lamp's lit disc, and the shadows in it, fades to zero over this fraction of its radius, instead of ending on a hard circle. 0 = as before |
| MaskSkipFar | bool | on/off | true | Skips lamps past their radius (same picture, cheaper). Off gives the same picture |
| LampReachMask | bool | on/off | false | Reach mode: a lamp counts in full out to its game radius and fades over the rest of its reach (the game's own lamp light does the falloff). Changes the picture |
| ForwardReachMask | int | 0 to 2 (clamped) | 2 | Indoors with lamps: 2 = attenuation without the surface angle (no facets on characters, room as bright as before); 1 = reach mode (LampReachMask's rule); 0 = old surface-angle attenuation (facets). LampReachMask on overrides it |
| ContactShadows | float | 0 to 1 (clamped) | 0.0 | Strength of the indoor lamp contact shadows (technique 2). 0 = off, and the contact pass is not run |
| ContactLength | float | 4 to 200 (clamped) | 40.0 | How far toward the lamp the contact ray looks, game units |
| ContactThickness | float | 1 to 100 (clamped) | 12.0 | Thickness of a surface taken to block a contact ray, game units |
| ContactDistance | float | 200 to 20000 (clamped) | 3000.0 | Distance from the camera where the contact shadows fade out (fade-out ends here, starts at 70% of it) |
| LightRadiusMult | float | at least 1 | 1.5 | Multiplier that sets the reach of the lamp shadows; the reach mode uses its reciprocal |
| LightPoints | int | (see toml) | 24 | Number of lamps with cube-map shadows indoors (up to 64 with forward shadows) and the slot count the pass sees; outdoors at most 12 |
| ExteriorLightPoints | int | 0 to 44 | 12 | Outdoors with point shadows on: how many lamps cast shadows (above 12 adds batches of 11, section 5) |
| ShadowCubeMapSize | int | (see toml) | 512 | Resolution of one face of each lamp's cube map (the map this pass reads) |
| Enabled | bool | on/off | (no key in the defaults file) | Read by the exterior-constant code as Settings.Interiors.Enabled. See open questions |

#### 4.2 [Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward]

| Key | Type | Default | Visual effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| Enabled | bool | true | Per-object lamp shadows indoors (needs restart). When on and drawn, the pass is the reach-only mask (section 2) |
| KeepDarkening | bool | true | Keeps the darkening of what no tracked lamp reaches. Off: the pass is skipped indoors (section 2, gate 1) and the unlit corners are brighter |
| DarkeningBlur | bool | false | Blurs the darkening as the old pass did. Also turns on the noise for the contact shadows (section 6) |
| MaskHalfRes | bool | false | Draws the reach mask at half resolution and upsamples it (techniques 3 and 4) |

#### 4.3 [Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] and status switches

| Key | Type | Default | Visual effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| UsePointShadowsDay | bool | false | Outdoors in daylight: lamp cube-map shadows on (sets TESR_ShadowFade z). Off: no cube reads; the mask is unshadowed lamp light |
| UsePointShadowsNight | bool | false | The same, at night |
| [Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Status] Enabled | bool | true | Whether the exterior shadow effect runs (pass gate outdoors) |
| [Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Status] Enabled | bool | true | Whether the interior shadow effect runs (pass gate indoors) |

#### 4.4 [Main.Main.Performance]

| Key | Type | Default | Visual effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| LampMaskScissor | bool | true | Draws the merged pass only inside the screen rectangle its lamps can reach; outside it stays 0. Same picture. Does not apply when extra lamps exist, or with unusual camera matrices (then the whole pass is drawn) |
| LampMaskScissorCheck | int | 0 | Development-only diagnostic. Leave 0. Does not change the picture |
| NvrDxvkPasses | bool | toml true (the code's own default is false) | Runs this effect's passes as Vulkan passes when the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is present (section 7) |
| NvrDxvkVariants | bool | true | Uses the compile-time mode variant files where they exist (section 7) |
| NvrDxvkKeepD3D9 | string | "" | Comma list of effect names that keep their D3D9 passes. Naming PointShadows here forces the D3D9 passes |
| NvrDxvkTestSuffix | string | "" | Test only: tries a variant file with this suffix added to the mode name first (section 7) |
| NvrSm6Shaders | bool | false | Runs technique 0 from its SM6 build when present (section 7) |

### 5. Passes and outputs

The effect file has five techniques. The engine asks for them by index and by name. Each full-screen draw uses the same full-screen vertex pass (except technique 3, which offsets the uv by half a full-resolution texel) with pixel shader profile ps_3_0.

| Index | Name | Blend | Target | Clear first | What it writes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | MergedPointShadows | none | the point-shadow buffer (TESR_PointShadowBuffer: G16R16, full resolution; the engine's ShadowPassSurface) | yes, to black (0) | the mask: the sum of every lamp's share of light reaching this pixel, saturated to 0..1, written to R, G and B (alpha 1). Covers the cube-mapped lamps 0..10, the unshadowed lamp 11, the tracked lamps, the spot light and the extra lamps |
| 1 | AddShadowedLamps | additive: source One, destination One | the same buffer | no | the outdoor batch: the cube-mapped lamps 0..10 only (the batch's swapped-in lamps), added onto what was there. Extra, tracked and spot lamps are not added here |
| 2 | LampContact | multiply: destination = destination x source | the same buffer | no | the contact multiplier: 1 where no contact shadow, below 1 where a lamp is blocked by a nearby surface |
| 3 | LampMaskHalf | none | NVR_LampMaskHalf (G16R16F, half size rounded up) | yes, to black | R: the mask (the same sum as technique 0, read at the top-left full-resolution texel of each half-resolution pixel). G: the full-resolution depth at that texel |
| 4 | LampMaskUpsample | none | the point-shadow buffer | no | the mask upsampled from the half-resolution buffer, depth-aware (section 6), written to R, G and B |

Which technique the engine picks:
- Normal outdoor or indoor pass: technique 0 (MergedPointShadows) if the effect file has it; if not, the second-pass effect runs instead (not specified here).
- Outdoor batches (ExteriorLightPoints above 12): technique 1, once per batch of 11 lamps after technique 0.
- Indoor contact pass: technique 2, after the mask, when ContactShadows above 0 (indoors only).
- Half-resolution path: technique 3 into NVR_LampMaskHalf (cleared), then technique 4 into the buffer (not cleared, it overwrites every pixel). Before technique 4 the stage 13 texture is bound to NVR_LampMaskHalf.
- Scissor path: the buffer is cleared to 0 over the whole target with the scissor test off, then technique 0 draws only inside the rectangle. If no lamp reaches the screen the draw is skipped and only the clear is done.

### 6. Behaviour details

Lamp arithmetic, per lamp, per pixel (world position from the depth and the view, normal from the normals buffer):
- Distance d = distance from the pixel to the lamp, divided by the lamp's radius. A lamp gives nothing when d is 1 or more (not less than 1), or when its weight is 0, or when its radius is 0 (the division gives infinity or NaN, which the test also rejects).
- Attenuation (standard radius falloff, as in the published "point light attenuation" construction): with s = clamp(d squared, 0, 1), atten = (1 - s) squared / (1 + 5 s), clamped to 0..1.
- Angle term (normal mode, reach-mode value z = 0): diffuse = mix(1, dot(unit direction to the lamp, normal), smoothstep(0, 0.2, d)). The mix gives the light a little bleed at very short distance. Under forward mode 2 (z < 0) the normal is set to the direction to the lamp, so the angle term is 1 and only the attenuation remains. Reach mode (z > 0) replaces the whole attenuation with 1 - smoothstep(z, 1, d).
- Shadow test: the cube map is read in the direction to the lamp with the x and y components negated (z kept). The stored value is compared with d plus 0.018 x d: lit when stored + bias x d is greater than d. The test is only used when the stored value is strictly between 0 and 1; otherwise the lamp counts as lit. When the lamp's near fade is set, the shadow result is blended toward 1 by smoothstep(0, NearFade / radius, stored): blockers within NearFade of the lamp cast a weaker shadow, none right at the lamp.
- Cube switch: when TESR_ShadowFade z is 0 the test returns lit for every lamp.
- Lamp amount = clamp(lamp factor, 0, 1) x lamp weight, then clamped to 0..1. The edge window multiplies it: when EdgeFade > 0, the amount is multiplied by 1 - smoothstep(1 - EdgeFade, 1, d), which takes the outer EdgeFade of each lamp's radius to zero. Without EdgeFade, the window is 1.
- The sum of all lamps is saturated to 0..1 at the end.

Spot light (merged technique only, not in batches): radius from TESR_SpotLightPosition w; attenuation as above; cone = (clamp(dot(spot direction, direction from the surface to the spot), 0, 1) with the cone limits cos(full angle) to cos(half angle)), inverse-lerped to 0..1 and then squared. Diffuse term = max(dot(direction to the spot, normal), 0). Weight = luma(colour) x dimmer. Spot light is never shadowed.

Extra lamps (merged technique only): the loop stops early once the running sum is 1 or more, or at the first empty entry. Each extra lamp's radius and weight are the packed values from TESR_ExtraLights w.

Cube-map casters (C++, `ShadowManager::RenderShadowCubeMap`): since upstream 1.1.2 a caster whose world scale is non-finite or
above 1000, or whose world bound is non-finite or wider than 1e6 units, is skipped (`IsExplodedShadowCaster`: weapon animation
mods counter-scale a hidden child by 1e9; see SunShadows README section 9, fixed item 10).

Sky and far pixels: the pass has no sky test. Sky or far depth lies beyond every lamp's radius, so it gets 0 from lamps. Anything the consumers do about sky happens in those effects.

First-person weapon: the mask pass has no weapon test. The contact pass fades in only past 25 game units from the camera, so the weapon and hands get no contact shadow.

Contact pass (technique 2):
- Fade: 0 nearer than 25 units from the camera, rising to 1 over the next 20 units, falling from 1 at 70% of ContactDistance to 0 at ContactDistance. Multiplied together.
- Lamp selection: the brightest shadowed lamp among slots 0..10, by (1 - s2) squared times the lamp's light (s2 = squared distance over squared radius, clamped), and only when that lamp gives more than 20% (TESR_LampContactBias w) of the total of all the slots' light. Lamps with a 0 radius are excluded.
- Facing: when the direction to the lamp makes less than 0.1 with the surface normal, the lamp is not blocked (the surface faces away).
- March: up to 6 steps along the direction toward the lamp, over min(ContactLength, half the distance to the lamp). Each step starts at the surface offset along the normal (TESR_LampContact w plus a per-unit-depth share). A step lies on a blocker when its depth is behind the stored depth by more than the bias (a constant plus a share of its depth) and less than the thickness. A blocker found at step fraction t contributes (1 - t), keeping the largest. The march ends at the first step that leaves the screen or lies in front of the camera.
- Jitter: with DarkeningBlur off, steps sit at the middle of each sixth (no grain). With it on, the step start is offset by interleaved gradient noise (the factors in TESR_LampContactNoise).
- Output: 1 minus strength x fade x (blocked light share), so the multiply darkens only where the traced lamp is blocked near the pixel.

Half-resolution mask upsampling (technique 4): each full-resolution pixel reads the four nearest half-resolution texels (point samples at their centres). Each has bilinear weight, divided by 1 + 16 x (gap squared), where gap = |sample depth - pixel depth| / (1 + 0.01 x pixel depth). The output is the weighted average. Not exact at silhouettes and creases.

Scissor rectangle (merged, plain path): the rectangle is the screen box where the lamps' spheres project (the camera's right, up and forward axes come from the view matrix; the projection must be a plain perspective, or the whole pass is drawn). It is skipped entirely when the list of lamps is empty. With extra lamps present, the whole pass is drawn. In a rect frame (RenderScale), the rectangle is scaled by the rect's scale factors and clamped to the rect.

Rect frames (RenderRect, TESR_RenderScale): the Vulkan route is not used in a rect frame. The contact pass maps its screen positions into the rect before it reads depth, so the rect's depth is read. The D3D9 pass itself is unaware of the rect.

Jitter (TAA): the pass uses the uv it is given and does not remove any jitter itself. Whether the uv it gets is jittered is not checked here (section 9).

Reversed depth: the depth is the linear Vegas Shaders depth (not a reversed buffer). The pass does not handle reversed depth.

HDR vs LDR: the scene is HDR and this pass does not touch scene colour. The buffer is G16R16 (unsigned normalised, 0 to 1), so the mask is clamped to 0..1 on write, and the additive batches are clamped too. The contact multiply can only darken.

NaN and infinity guards: the lamp-radius division gives infinity for a zero radius; the test "not less than 1" rejects it. The final sum is saturated. The contact pass divides by the distance to the lamp without a guard (section 9).

Things that look wrong if missed:
- A lamp's shadow filling the room when you stand next to it (NearFade 0 leaves it sharp and total at the lamp).
- A hard circle at the edge of a bright lamp's reach (EdgeFade 0).
- Facets on characters (forward mode 0 or the surface-angle term left on).
- The room darkened twice (reach mode and the game's own falloff both applied).
- Contact shadows on the first-person weapon (the 25-unit fade must stay).
- The buffer not cleared before the merged technique (a stale mask shows through).
- Outdoor batch slots not swapped back (the lamp list is restored after the batches).

### 7. Vulkan path

#### 7.1 Files

| File | Status | Role |
|---|---|---|
| src\hlsl\NewVegas\Compute\PointShadows.frag | source | Modes 0, 1 and 2 of MergedPointShadows, AddShadowedLamps and LampMaskHalf. The mode comes from the push block (general shader), or from a compile-time define (variant) |
| src\hlsl\NewVegas\Compute\PointShadowsCommon.glsl | source | Shared constants block and the shadow, attenuation, spot, and depth and normal helpers (the same maths as the effect file) |
| src\hlsl\NewVegas\Compute\PointShadowsContact.frag | source | Technique 2 (LampContact) |
| src\hlsl\NewVegas\Compute\PointShadowsUpsample.frag | source | Technique 4 (LampMaskUpsample) |
| Compute\PointShadows.spv | binary, built from PointShadows.frag | general shader (mode from push block) |
| Compute\PointShadows-m0.spv, -m1.spv, -m2.spv | binary, built from PointShadows.frag with the mode defined | mode variants |
| Compute\PointShadows-m0x.spv, -m0y.spv, -m0z.spv, -m0e.spv | binary, built from PointShadows.frag with test defines | test variants listed in Compute\variants.txt (no cube reads; no shadowed lamps; no lamps; empty). Not for play |
| Compute\PointShadowsContact.spv | binary, from PointShadowsContact.frag | technique 2 |
| Compute\PointShadowsUpsample.spv | binary, from PointShadowsUpsample.frag | technique 4 |
| Compute\Sm6\PointShadows.MergedPointShadows.0.811c9dc5.spv (with .txt and the index .txt) | SM6 build of technique 0 | used only with NvrSm6Shaders (default off). Its source is the effect file's technique 0 |

PointShadows2.frag and PointShadows2.spv belong to the separate PointShadows2 effect and are not part of this spec. No variant files exist for the contact and upsample passes.

Wave 3 (2026-11-02): PointShadows.spv, the four -m variants and the five test variants, PointShadowsContact.spv and PointShadowsUpsample.spv were rebuilt from the new sources with tools/nvr-dxvk/compile-compute.sh (spirv-val passes). The Sm6 build of technique 0 is NOT rebuilt: it is from the old technique 0 and must not be used until it is rebuilt from the new .fx (open; NvrSm6Shaders is off by default).

#### 7.2 How the C++ chooses D3D9 or Vulkan

Checked in the order the engine does it (src\core\EffectRecord.cpp, the effect's Render path; src\core\DxvkCompute.cpp; src\core\Sm6Pass.cpp):
1. The Vulkan draw is tried first when the passes are on: NvrDxvkPasses on, not a rect frame, and the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension available. Those are the conditions of DxvkCompute's passes-on check.
2. The effect is skipped from Vulkan when NvrDxvkKeepD3D9 names it ("PointShadows").
3. The Vulkan draw is skipped for a technique whose passes all have SM6 builds while NvrSm6Shaders is on (technique 0 only has one).
4. The effect's Vulkan draw returns false (so the D3D9 technique runs for that draw) when: technique index above 4; the extension or the linear and point samplers are missing; the scissor test is on (the Vulkan passes ignore the scissor); a texture is missing; a required constant is missing.
5. If a pass fails to build or draw, the effect falls back to its D3D9 technique for the rest of the session (logged).

#### 7.3 Variant choice

For each Vulkan draw the file name is chosen in this order (EffectRecord's variant lookup):
1. When NvrDxvkTestSuffix is not empty: name with the suffix, e.g. PointShadows-m0 plus the suffix, if that file exists.
2. When NvrDxvkVariants is on and the draw has a mode suffix: the mode variant (PointShadows-m0, -m1 or -m2), if the file exists.
3. Otherwise the general file (PointShadows.spv, PointShadowsContact.spv or PointShadowsUpsample.spv).

The merged, batch and half-mask draws use mode 0, 1 and 2 respectively. The contact and upsample draws look for a mode-0 variant file, which does not exist, so they use the general file.

#### 7.4 Bindings and push block (what the engine fills)

Texture bindings, set 1, binding number equal to the list position (the cube maps are the first entries):
- Merged, batch and half-mask draws: binding 0 to 10 = the cube maps for lamp slots 0 to 10 (cube view). Binding 11 = depth (2D). Binding 12 = normals (2D).
- Contact draw: binding 0 = depth, binding 1 = normals.
- Upsample draw: binding 0 = NVR_LampMaskHalf (its half-resolution mask), binding 1 = depth.

Constants: set 2, binding 0, one uniform block with std140 layout, all vec4 unless stated. Byte offsets, in order:

| Offset (bytes) | Name (as in section 3) | Count |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | TESR_ShadowLightPosition | 12 |
| 192 | TESR_LightPosition | 12 |
| 384 | TESR_LightColor | 24 |
| 768 | TESR_ExtraLights | 16 |
| 1024 | TESR_ShadowFade | 1 |
| 1040 | TESR_PointShadowNear | 1 |
| 1056 | TESR_SpotLightPosition | 1 |
| 1072 | TESR_SpotLightDirection | 1 |
| 1088 | TESR_SpotLightColor | 1 |
| 1104 | TESR_LampContact | 1 |
| 1120 | TESR_LampContactFade | 1 |
| 1136 | TESR_LampContactBias | 1 |
| 1152 | TESR_LampContactNoise | 1 |
| 1168 | TESR_ReciprocalResolution | 1 |
| 1184 | TESR_CameraPosition | 1 |
| 1200 | TESR_CameraData | 1 |
| 1216 | TESR_ViewTransform | mat4 (64 bytes) |
| 1280 | TESR_ProjectionTransform | mat4 (64 bytes) |
| 1344 | per-shadow-lamp derived values | 12 |
| 1536 | per-tracked-lamp derived values | 12 |
| 1728 | per-extra-lamp derived values | 16 |
| 1984 | near-fade derived values | 1 |
| 2000 | spot cone derived values | 1 |

Total block size 2016 bytes. The engine fills the derived values once per draw from the constants above:
- Per shadow lamp k (offset 1344): x = 1 / radius; y = weight (luma of TESR_LightColor entry k times its w); z = radius / NearFade (0 when NearFade is 0).
- Per tracked lamp i (offset 1536): x = 1 / radius; y = weight (luma of entry i+12 times its w).
- Per extra lamp e (offset 1728): x = 1 / radius, where radius = floor(packed w / 8); y = weight = packed w minus 8 x radius.
- Near-fade block (offset 1984): x = 1 / EdgeFade (0 when EdgeFade is 0); y = 1 / (1 - reach value z); z = 1 / spot radius; w = luma of spot colour times its w.
- Spot block (offset 2000): x = cos(full cone angle); y = 1 / (cos(half angle) - cos(full angle)).

Push constants (16 bytes, read by the shader as four unsigned ints):
- Byte 0: heap index of the linear clamp sampler (all draws use it for depth; merged, batch and half-mask draws also for the cube maps).
- Byte 4: heap index of the point clamp sampler (normals; contact draw). For the upsample draw this slot holds a point WRAP sampler (for the half-resolution mask).
- Byte 8: mode: 0 = merged (also used by contact and upsample), 1 = batch, 2 = half-resolution mask.
- Byte 12: padding.

Render target formats: point-shadow buffer G16R16; half-resolution mask G16R16F. Clear to black before the merged, half-mask and scissored draws, as the D3D9 pass does.

Blend: technique 1 = additive, technique 2 = multiply (destination x source), others none.

Sampler states (the effect file's): depth linear clamp; normals point clamp; cube maps linear clamp; NVR_LampMaskHalf point (see section 9 for the address mode).

Vulkan route must draw the same picture as the D3D9 technique. Known test variants exist only to measure cost.

### 8. Look and cost

Correct look:
- Indoors: a lamp's light is cut where props and walls block it, giving soft-edged shadows that follow the lamp's position. Rooms keep their mood (KeepDarkening) and the far part of a lit room is not darkened twice.
- Outdoors at night with point shadows on: street lamps and campfires cast shadows where things stand between them and the surface; open country by day gets no mask at all (scissor skips it).
- Characters keep their smooth shading, with no facets (forward mode 2 or the surface-angle term off).
- No hard circle at the edge of a bright lamp's reach.
- Lamp contact (when ContactShadows is above 0): small dark lines where a prop meets the floor or a wall meets a table, faded out with distance, none on the weapon.

Typical wrong results:
- A shadow that fills the room when standing at a lamp (NearFade 0).
- A hard disc edge around a bright lamp (EdgeFade 0).
- Triangle-shaped facets on skin or clothes near a lamp (surface angle on).
- Dark gaps where no lamp is blocked (a stale buffer, or an unset clear).
- Grain on the contact lines (noise with DarkeningBlur on, unfiltered).
- A contact shadow on the weapon (fade start changed).

Cost class: multi-pass, per-pixel loop. The merged technique reads up to 11 cube maps and loops over up to 12 tracked lamps and 16 extra lamps, with branches on each. The batch technique adds about 0.3 ms at 1440p per batch of 11 lamps (from the settings file's note). The contact pass is a second per-pixel pass with a 6-step march. Measured figures in the code notes: LampMaskScissor saves about 0.15 to 0.37 ms at 1440p; the derived per-draw values in the Vulkan route were measured at 0.3 to 0.5 ms cheaper than per-pixel divisions on a GTX 1070 in the Vulkan merged pass.

### 9. Open questions

1. Sampler address mode of NVR_LampMaskHalf: the effect file declares CLAMP on both axes, but the C++ comment and the Vulkan upsample shader (point WRAP) say WRAP. Still open: wave 3 keeps CLAMP (the .fx is the reference). The difference is only at the screen edge.
2. [Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main] Enabled is read (as Settings.Interiors.Enabled) but has no key in resource\VegasShaders.defaults.toml. Its effect on this pass was not traced.
3. Outdoors, the pass runs whenever the exterior shadow effect is enabled, even with UsePointShadowsDay and Night both off (cube reads then off). It is not clear whether the mask then gives unshadowed lamp light outdoors on purpose, and whether that costs anything with nothing to show.
4. Disabled effect: when the effect's own Enabled flag is off, its Render returns before the clear. The buffer then keeps the previous frame's contents, so the mask may not be cleared. Not checked in a run.
5. FIXED (wave 3): contact pass: the distance to the lamp is clamped to 1e-6 before dividing (no NaN at a zero distance). The lamp selection also skips zero-radius lamps before any division.
6. Meaning of the buffer's value for consumers: this spec describes it as the sum of lamp light reaching the pixel, 0 to 1. Consumers (the exterior and interior shadow effects, ambient light, snow, blood flow and others) darken by it; their exact use is outside this spec.
7. TAA jitter: whether the uv this pass receives is jittered before it runs was not checked.
8. Vulkan matrix order: the shared Vulkan helper says its matrix products equal the effect file's row-vector products. That was read, not run.
9. The contact and upsample draws look for a mode-0 variant file that does not exist. Harmless, but it is a wasted lookup. Still open (a C++ detail, not changed in wave 3).
10. FIXED (wave 3): the Vulkan binaries are rebuilt from the current sources (spirv-val passes). The Sm6 build is still old (see section 7.1).
13. Wave 3 readings (the spec was not exact here; the shader decides): (a) the contact blocker's contribution is the largest (1 - t) times the traced lamp's share of all slots' light, times fade and strength; (b) the spot cone uses the direction from the spot to the surface against the spot direction, clamped at 0; (c) tracked and extra lamps use the same factor as the normal lamps (reach, forward mode 2 or normal, as the value z says); (d) the near fade blends the shadow toward 1 near the lamp: result = lerp(1, shadow, smoothstep(0, NearFade / radius, stored)), the reading that gives "none right at the lamp".
14. Wave 3 choice: the contact pass is computed in view space (the depth and normals are view space). The Vulkan passes use the same matrix convention: D3D mul(v, M) is M * v in GLSL, and D3D mul(M, v) is v * M.
11. The LightRadiusMult reach: the settings file says the lamp shadows cover LightRadiusMult times the game radius, and the reach mode fades over the rest of that. Where the multiplication happens (the radius sent in TESR_ShadowLightPosition or TESR_LightPosition) was not traced.
12. Engine default for NvrDxvkPasses is false in code but true in the defaults file; the defaults file wins in normal play. Not a problem for a new writer, but worth knowing.

## PointShadows2

*README title: PointShadows2 - effect spec*

REWRITTEN 2026-11-02 (branch claude/rw3-lights): the shaders are new, written from this spec (Effects/PointShadows2.fx.hlsl; Compute/PointShadows2.frag). The C++ and the settings are unchanged. Still open: the D3D9 read-after-write (section 6 and section 9 item 9).

files in Compute (PointShadows2.frag, PointShadows2.spv, PointShadowsCommon.glsl for comparison), resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml,
core/ShaderManager.cpp (frame order), core/EffectRecord.cpp (dispatch), core/DxvkCompute.cpp/.h (Vulkan switch).

---

### 1. Purpose

PointShadows2 darkens or lights the scene for point lamps that PointShadows does not handle. It is a screen-space pass over the
point-shadow buffer: for every pixel it reconstructs the world position from depth and the world normal from the normals buffer, then
adds, to the value already in the buffer, the light that lamps 6 to 11 deliver to that pixel (each shadowed lamp is tested against its
cube shadow map; lamp 11 is lit without a shadow test). The player sees lamps past the first six cast their light and their shadows
on walls, floors and actors, indoors and outdoors wherever the point-shadow pass runs. It is a second pass in the same buffer, not a
composite over the picture.

### 2. When it runs

- Only inside the point-shadow block of the pre-tonemapping stage (ShaderManager, RenderEffectsPreTonemapping), after depth and normals
  exist and after the ray-tracing, capsule and back-depth steps. The order inside the block is: optional lamp mask, the main point-shadow
  pass (PointShadows), the outdoor batches (if ExteriorLightPoints is above 12), then PointShadows2, then the outdoor ray-traced lamp
  mask, then the restore of the fade constant. The indoor lamp-contact pass comes after the whole block.
- The block runs when the buffer is needed: outdoors when the exterior shadows effect is enabled, indoors when the interior shadows
  effect is enabled, except the case where indoor forward point shadows are drawing the lamps themselves and keep no darkening (then the
  buffer is cleared and this effect does not run).
- Gate, exact: it is called only when the loaded PointShadows shader does NOT have the technique named MergedPointShadows AND the
  interior setting LightPoints is greater than 6. The shipped PointShadows.fx has MergedPointShadows, so with the shipped shader this
  effect does not run at all. It runs for a custom or older PointShadows shader that lacks that technique (its lamps 6 to 11 then take
  the original second pass, which is this effect).
- Outdoors it also runs when LightPoints is above 6 (the same setting is read outdoors).
- Before the draw, the fade constant TESR_ShadowFade.z decides the lamp shadows: it is 0 when point shadows are off for the current
  time of day (exteriors: UsePointShadowsDay / UsePointShadowsNight), 1 indoors, and forced to 0 for the indoor forward-shadow case.
  With z at 0 every cube lookup returns "lit" (see section 6).
- Enabled state: the effect has no section of its own in the defaults file. Its on/off comes from the shader menu's status record for
  the name PointShadows2 (see section 4 and section 9).
- Render target must be the shadow pass surface, technique index 0, and the clear flag must be off. Any other call draws nothing.
- Frame chain: it is not a chain step. It writes the shadow pass surface directly and does not run through the post-process chain.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (D3D9 sampler registers, declared order)

| Slot | Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_DepthBuffer | linear depth, value = view depth / far distance (one channel) | linear, clamp |
| s1 | TESR_NormalsBuffer | view-space normal encoded 0..1 (xyz) | point (no filter), clamp |
| s2 | TESR_PointShadowBuffer | the point-shadow buffer, format G16R16, full screen; the pass's own target | linear, clamp |
| s3 | TESR_ShadowCubeMapBuffer6 | shadow cube map of shadow lamp slot 6 | linear, clamp, cube |
| s4 | TESR_ShadowCubeMapBuffer7 | slot 7 cube map | linear, clamp, cube |
| s5 | TESR_ShadowCubeMapBuffer8 | slot 8 cube map | linear, clamp, cube |
| s6 | TESR_ShadowCubeMapBuffer9 | slot 9 cube map | linear, clamp, cube |
| s7 | TESR_ShadowCubeMapBuffer10 | slot 10 cube map | linear, clamp, cube |

Slot 8 (cube map 11) is reserved and unbound: lamp slot 11 is never shadow-tested. Samplers are bound by declaration order.
An absent texture makes the pass skip (in the Vulkan path, the D3D9 pass runs instead).

#### Constants (D3D9 registers are not fixed by Vegas Shaders; the compiler assigns them and the engine finds them by name)

| Name | Type | Meaning and units |
|---|---|---|
| TESR_CameraPosition | float4 | xyz: camera world position, world units. w unused |
| TESR_CameraData | float4 | y: far distance (world units). Used to turn the depth texel into view distance. x (near) and z, w not used here |
| TESR_ViewTransform | float4x4 | world-to-view matrix as the engine uploads it. Used for the view-space to world direction and for the normal rotation |
| TESR_ProjectionTransform | float4x4 | projection matrix. Only its [0][0] and [1][1] entries are used (horizontal and vertical focal scale) |
| TESR_ShadowFade | float4 | x: sun-shadow fade (not read here). y: not read here. z: point lamp shadows on (1) or off (0). w: reach of point shadows (not read here) |
| TESR_ShadowLightPosition[12] | float4 x 12 | per lamp slot: xyz world position of the lamp, w light radius. w = 0 means the slot is empty |
| TESR_LightColor[24] | float4 x 24 | entries 0 to 11 are the shadow lamps' tracked colours (not read here). Entries 6 to 10 are read for the shadow lamps: rgb colour, w dimmer (multiplier). Entry 11 is not read (its lamp has no colour weight) |

Only the entries listed above are read. Empty slots are zero (position w 0, colour 0), so they add nothing.

#### Vulkan-side inputs
The same values, packed in one uniform block (section 7). Textures at set 1 bindings 0 to 7 in the same order as above, with the
shadow buffer as a copy (section 6).

### 4. Settings

PointShadows2 has NO settings section of its own. Its on/off is the shader menu status for the name PointShadows2 (no defaults entry
exists for it, so the menu treats it as a status switch; the saved value is not in the defaults file). The keys below decide whether
and where it runs.

| Section | Key | Type | Range | Default (defaults file) | Effect on this pass |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| [_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main] | LightPoints | int | 0 to 64 (clamped to the engine maximum of 64) | 24 | Must be above 6 for this pass to run. Caps how many interior lamps are shadowed; the pass only covers the lamps in the shadow slots it reads |
| [_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main] | ExteriorLightPoints | int | 0 to 44 | 12 | Outdoors, lamps beyond 12 are drawn in batches; this pass is unaffected except through the same gate |
| [_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main] | ShadowCubeMapSize | int | 64 to 2048 | 512 | Resolution of each cube map this pass samples. Not read by the pass itself |
| [_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] | UsePointShadowsDay | bool | 0 or 1 | false | Outdoors in daytime: when 0, the fade constant z is 0 and every lamp counts as unshadowed |
| [_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] | UsePointShadowsNight | bool | 0 or 1 | false | Outdoors at night, same effect as the daytime key |
| [Main.Main.Performance] | NvrDxvkPasses | bool | 0 or 1 | true | Selects the Vulkan pass (section 7) when the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is present |
| [Main.Main.Performance] | NvrDxvkKeepD3D9 | string list | names separated by commas | empty | If this effect's name is listed, the Vulkan pass is skipped and the D3D9 pass is used (a test tool) |

Keys this pass does NOT read, though they sit next to it: NearFade and EdgeFade (of [_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main]), DrawDistance
(the interior reach), and the ForwardReachMask, LampReach and edge-window settings that PointShadows uses. Those belong to PointShadows
and do not change this pass.

Visual meaning of the settings for this pass: LightPoints and the UsePointShadows keys decide whether lamps 6 to 11 get shadows at all;
with them off the pass still lights its lamps unshadowed or does not run.

### 5. Passes and outputs

- One unnamed technique with one pass (technique index 0). The C++ requests technique 0 only; any other index draws nothing.
- Vertex stage: the engine's full-screen frame vertex (position and texture coordinate, pass-through).
- Pixel stage: the shadow lamp accumulation described in section 6. Output is opaque (no blending, the target's old value is replaced
  by the new one). Output colour is the value v in all channels: (v, v, v, 1). Because the target is G16R16, the red and green
  channels receive v; the consumers of the buffer read red.
- Render target: TESR_PointShadowBuffer's surface (the shadow pass surface of ShadowsExteriors). Format G16R16, the size of the frame
  (rect size when RenderScale is on), one level.
- Clear: none. The pass reads what the earlier point-shadow passes wrote and adds to it. It must never clear.
- Result range: clamped to 0..1.

### 6. Behaviour details

#### Per-pixel result
For each pixel the result is: the buffer's previous red value, plus the lamp light of each of lamps 6 to 11 at the pixel, clamped to 0..1.
The buffer's previous value is that pixel's own texel only; no neighbour is read from the buffer.

#### Reconstructing the position and normal
- The depth texel times far distance gives the view distance along the view ray at the pixel. The view ray direction is built from the
  projection's horizontal and vertical focal scales and the view matrix's axes, as the engine's depth helper does. The world position is the
  camera position plus ray times distance.
- Depth coordinates are rect coordinates (RenderScale): the position and normal lookups use them as they come. The ray construction maps them to
  screen coordinates through the rect helper; the Vulkan copy does not, which is why the Vulkan pass is off in rect frames.
- Normal: the normals texel is decoded from 0..1 to -1..1, given w = 1, and rotated by the view matrix (the normal's view-to-world step
