  uses the view matrix as a row vector, i.e. the same product the engine's normal helper uses). No normalisation is applied to the rotated normal.

#### Lamp light for one slot (lamps 6 to 10, with shadows)
1. Light vector = lamp position minus world position. Normalised distance = length of that vector divided by the lamp radius.
2. If the lamp's w is 0 the slot contributes nothing.
3. Shadow test: the vector from the pixel to the lamp, with x and y negated and z kept, is the cube-map direction. The cube map's stored
   value at that direction is compared with the normalised distance: the lamp is unblocked when the stored value plus 0.018 times the
   distance is greater than the distance. The bias grows with distance, as stated in the engine's comment.
4. The test only applies where the stored value is strictly between 0 and 1. Outside that (empty texels, which are 0, or saturated ones,
   which are 1) the lamp counts as unblocked.
5. When the fade constant z is 0 the test is skipped and the lamp counts as unblocked (value 1). This is the "shadows off" state.
6. Attenuation (the lamp's falloff, same for all slots): with s = clamp(distance squared, 0..1), the falloff is (1 - s) squared divided by
   (1 + 5 s), clamped to 0..1.
7. Facing term: the normalised light direction dotted with the normal. It is blended with 1 by the smoothstep of the distance over 0 to 0.2
   (light bleeding at very close range): near the lamp the facing term fades to 1, beyond 20 percent of the radius it is used as is.
   The facing term is clamped to 0..1 after the product.
8. Lamp amount = shadow test value times falloff times facing term, clamped to 0..1.
9. Added to the total: lamp amount times luma of the lamp colour (Rec.709 weights, 0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722) times the colour's dimmer
   (w component). Slots 6 to 10 all use this form.

#### Lamp 11 (no shadow test)
Lamp slot 11 is lit without a shadow test and without a colour weight. Its contribution is the falloff times the facing term, with
the same distance and facing rules, and no luma and no dimmer. Its radius is still used to normalise the distance. It is therefore
not dimmed by the lamp's colour: a deliberate-looking but unweighted term that a rewrite must keep.

#### Edge cases that would look wrong if missed
- Sky and far pixels: no sky mask. The depth at the sky is at the far distance, so the world position lies beyond every lamp's radius and
  the falloff goes to 0. A rewrite must produce 0 from sky pixels, not a lit sky.
- Zero-length light vector (camera or pixel exactly at a lamp): the normalisation is undefined. The original does not guard it. The rewrite must
  give 0 (or the falloff's value at distance 0 with a defined facing term), never NaN, which would leave a black or bright speck.
- Lamp radius 0 with a nonzero vector: distance is infinite, falloff 0, so the lamp adds nothing. Radius 0 with a zero vector is the NaN case above.
- Empty slots: w 0 gives nothing for slots 6 to 10 by the explicit check; slot 11 relies on falloff at infinite distance, which gives 0.
- Output is clamped at the end, so partial sums above 1 are fine within the pass; the stored value is clamped.
- Reverse or inverted depth: not handled here. The depth buffer stores linear depth (view distance over far), so no depth-convention switch exists.
- HDR vs LDR: the buffer is a 0..1 G16R16 target; this pass works in that range and does no tone mapping.
- First-person weapon and player-held objects: no special case. Whatever depth and normals say is lit.
- TAA jitter: none; the pass uses the frame's texture coordinates as they are.
- RenderScale / rect frames: the texture coordinates are in rect space; the position reconstruction maps them through the rect helper (D3D9 path only).
  Vulkan path is not used in rect frames.
- Read-after-write hazard: the D3D9 pass reads the buffer it writes. The intended result is the value from before this pass, at the same
  pixel. A new implementation must read a copy taken before the pass (the Vulkan path does this with a same-size copy made each call).
  Reading the live target gives undefined results on some drivers.
  Wave 3 status: the Vulkan pass reads a copy (done). The D3D9 pass (Effects/PointShadows2.fx.hlsl) still reads TESR_PointShadowBuffer
  directly, because the copy would have to be made by the C++ (EffectRecord / PointShadows2.cpp), which wave 3 does not change. OPEN.

#### Interaction with the indoor forward path
When indoor forward point shadows draw the lamps (the indoor forward-shadow case) the fade constant z is forced to 0 before this pass and restored after.
Every lamp then counts as unshadowed here. The pass still adds lamps 6 to 11 unshadowed (weighted by distance and facing) in that case.

### 7. Vulkan path

Files (in src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute):
- PointShadows2.frag: the pass in GLSL, 4.50, with its own copies of the lamp functions. It does NOT include PointShadowsCommon.glsl
  (that file belongs to PointShadows; the two sets of functions are separate copies, except for the shared helper header Common.glsl,
  which gives the sampler-heap macro and luma).
- PointShadows2.spv: the compiled pass (the engine loads this file by the name "PointShadows2"). Wave 3: rebuilt from the new .frag (see section 9 item 3).

How C++ chooses: effects/PointShadows2.cpp overrides the extension-render entry of the effect base. The choice is made in the effect
base's render (core/EffectRecord.cpp): the Vulkan path runs when the Vegas Shaders DXVK switch is on (NvrDxvkPasses on, not a rect frame, and the
extension is available), the effect's name is not in NvrDxvkKeepD3D9, and the technique is not replaced by an SM6 build (none exists for
this effect). If the Vulkan entry returns false, the D3D9 pass runs for that call. Conditions that return false: technique index not 0;
clear requested; target not the shadow pass surface; a texture missing; a register-table entry missing; the pipeline fails to build; or the
constants or samplers are missing. The pipeline is tried once and kept.

Render steps of the Vulkan path (in order): copy the shadow buffer into a same-size, same-format texture (made once and remade when the
size or format changes); fill the constants block from the registered values; bind the textures and samplers; draw the full-screen
pass into the shadow pass surface with no blending.

#### Bindings
- Set 1 (textures, binding = slot index): binding 0 depth (2D), 1 normals (2D), 2 the shadow buffer copy (2D), 3 to 7 the cube maps of
  slots 6 to 10 (cube). Samplers are not in these bindings.
- Set 2, binding 0: one uniform block, std140, 752 bytes (the C++ checks this size at compile time). Byte offsets:
  - 0: camera position (vec4), TESR_CameraPosition
  - 16: camera data (vec4), TESR_CameraData
  - 32: view transform (mat4, 64 bytes), TESR_ViewTransform, stored as the D3D9 upload stores it
  - 96: projection transform (mat4), TESR_ProjectionTransform
  - 160: shadow fade (vec4), TESR_ShadowFade
  - 176: shadow lamp positions, 12 vec4 (192 bytes), TESR_ShadowLightPosition
  - 368: lamp colours, 24 vec4 (384 bytes), TESR_LightColor
- Matrix convention: the Vulkan side indexes a matrix as the HLSL's [row][column] does; the products that the HLSL writes as "vector times
  matrix" are written the same way in the Vulkan shader. Keep this order when rewriting.
- Push constants: 8 bytes. Offset 0: sampler heap index for LINEAR, CLAMP (used for the depth, the shadow copy and all cube maps). Offset 4:
  sampler heap index for POINT, CLAMP (used for the normals only). The engine fills both indices.
- Sampler heap: set 0, binding 0 (engine-owned).
- Output: one colour target, the shadow pass surface's format (G16R16), opaque.

### 8. Look and cost

Correct look: lamps 6 to 11 add their light where they reach. Shadowed areas behind walls or furniture from those lamps stay dark
(the lamp's light is removed there), lit surfaces facing the lamp take the full falloff, and nothing lights the sky. Edges of the lit
area follow the cube-map shadow edges, which are sharp at the cube resolution (512 by default). Shadows fall off with distance to
the radius; past the radius nothing is added. Lamp 11 always appears lit with no shadow, so it can light a wall behind another object.

Typical wrong results:
- Lamp 11 looks like it casts no shadow (correct, by design, but a visible mismatch with other lamps).
- A black or white speck at the lamp centre (the zero-vector NaN case).
- Light on the sky or on distant geometry beyond the radius (missing falloff).
- A lit sky or a bright band at the far edge (sky not kept at 0).
- Whole-screen darkening or brightening when the shadow buffer is not copied (the read-after-write case).
- The shadow pass drawn over a cleared target: this pass must not clear, so lamps 0 to 5 would vanish.

Cost class: per-pixel loop, single full-screen pass. Per pixel: three buffer-style reads (depth, normals, shadow buffer copy), five cube-map
reads (slots 6 to 10), and six lamp evaluations (five with a shadow test, one without). The Vulkan path adds one full-buffer copy per call.
Outdoors it runs at most once per frame.

### 9. Open questions

1. The on/off value of PointShadows2 itself: it has no defaults section. Which value the shader menu status record starts with, and
   where it is saved, was not determined. Whether a fresh install runs it is therefore unknown.
2. Whether the shipped PointShadows.fx's merged technique covers lamps 6 to 11 (and so makes this pass dead code with that file) was not
   checked against the merged technique's body; only its presence was confirmed.
3. FIXED (wave 3): PointShadows2.spv is rebuilt from the new PointShadows2.frag with compile-compute.sh (spirv-val passes).
9. Wave 3: the D3D9 pass's read-after-write (section 6) is still open: it needs a copy of the buffer made by the C++ before the pass.
10. Wave 3: lamp 11 uses the same facing term as the other lamps (the spec's rule); no colour weight, as the spec says.
4. D3D9 register numbers of the constants were not determined (the constant table assigns them at run time).
5. The meaning of the cube-map stored value (normalised distance to the nearest caster, divided by the lamp radius) is inferred from the
   comparison; the producer of the cube maps (the shadow cube drawing code) was not read.
6. The consumer of the buffer's red channel (the composite that darkens or lights the scene) was not read here; the sun-shadow comment in the
   exterior apply code says that channel is shared with the sun term. Whether lamp 6 to 11 values reach the picture the way PointShadows does
   was not confirmed.
7. Whether the Vulkan path's copy of the shadow buffer matches the D3D9 values exactly on every driver was not tested.
8. The Vulkan entry's last argument (the full-screen helper's flag) was not traced; it is assumed to mean "the pass covers the whole target".

## Precipitations

*README title: Precipitations (rain: screen-space falling drops and streaks)*

### 1. Purpose
While it rains outdoors, the picture gets falling rain streaks laid over the view. Drops are drawn in layers at increasing distance from the camera, so near streaks are larger and far ones finer. Each drop bends the picture behind it (a small refraction) and adds the scene's bloom glow on top. Rain only shows where the sun's shadow map says the point is open to the sky, so it stops under roofs and overhangs. Pixels with no drops are left as they are.

### 2. When it runs
- Enabled by the settings section Shaders.Precipitations.Status, key Enabled (default true).
- Per frame it renders only when the rain amount (TESR_RainData x, section 3) is above zero, the player is outdoors, and the camera is not underwater.
- The rain amount is eased, not a switch. Since 2026-11-21 it is the one rain clock's rain (src/core/RainState.h `Rain()`: Wetness Effects' `Raining`, which follows the weather's transition and eases at four times `[Shaders.WetnessEffects.Main] WeatherTransitionSpeed` / 60 x 2 a second, about 2.5 s at the default 3). RainState fills TESR_RainData x every frame, also while this effect is off. (Before: this effect's own animator, 0.05 game hours in and 0.07 out, driven by WetWorld's RainStateGuard; WetWorld is removed.) The effect keeps drawing while the amount is above zero, so the fade-out tail is visible.
- Weather state: the game's current weather is the rainy type. Setting Shaders.Precipitations.Main ForceRain (default false) makes the game count as raining outdoors in any weather and clears the snow flag while it does so. It is applied in the per-frame game-state update, before the effects run.
- Frame order (src/core/ShaderManager.cpp): inside the weather block that runs after the TAA pass. Rain renders first of the weather effects, then Snow, then the GPU particle draw (off by default), then the legacy bloom. In the frame chain's final-effect order Rain is the first entry.
- Final-pass rule: if Rain is the last enabled effect to draw this frame, its single pass writes straight to the game target. Otherwise it writes into the chain's spare target. Either way the output must cover the whole frame.
- Rain's own ShouldRender does not test for menus; whether it draws in a menu is not confirmed here.

### 3. Inputs
#### Textures (sampler slot = D3D9 declaration order; the C++ binds by name)
| Slot | Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_SourceBuffer | the picture as it stands before this effect (a copy the frame keeps). The drops' background and refracted colour come from here | linear, clamp |
| s1 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | declared but never read by the shader; not bound in the Vulkan pass | linear, clamp |
| s2 | TESR_DepthBuffer | the combined depth buffer: linear view distance divided by the far distance | linear, clamp |
| s3 | TESR_OrthoMapBuffer | the sun's orthographic shadow depth map for exteriors | linear, clamp; read at level 0 only |
| s4 | TESR_BloomBuffer | the bloom texture at its first level (from the bloom effect) | linear, clamp |

The Vulkan pass binds, in descriptor set 1: binding 0 source picture, 1 depth, 2 ortho map, 3 bloom. The unused rendered-buffer slot has no Vulkan binding.

#### Constants
| Name | Components and meaning | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| TESR_RainData | x = rain amount (0 to 1, animated); y = streak vertical scale (VerticalScale); z = fall speed multiplier (Speed); w = drop opacity (Opacity) | registered by the effect |
| TESR_RainAspect | x = refraction strength (Refraction); y = base colouring (Coloring); z = the Bloom setting (written by the C++, read by no shader, see section 9); w = SkipHiddenLayers switch (1 on, 0 off) | registered by the effect |
| TESR_GameTime | z = animation clock; others not used | engine constant |
| TESR_ShadowCameraToLightTransformOrtho | 4x4 world-to-sun-ortho-clip transform | engine constant; a push value in the Vulkan pass |
| TESR_ViewTransform, TESR_ProjectionTransform, TESR_CameraData | camera basis; projection entries [0][0] and [1][1]; camera data y = far distance | engine constants, used through the shared depth include |
| TESR_SunDirection | direction toward the sun | engine constant |
| TESR_SunColor | sun colour | engine constant |
| TESR_ReciprocalResolution, TESR_FogColor, TESR_WorldViewProjectionTransform | declared, not read | |

### 4. Settings
| Section | Key | Type | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shaders.Precipitations.Status | Enabled | bool | true | Turns the effect on or off. |
| Shaders.Precipitations.Main | VerticalScale | float | 1.6 | Length of the streaks: higher = longer, more stretched drops. |
| Shaders.Precipitations.Main | Speed | float | 3.0 | Fall speed multiplier. |
| Shaders.Precipitations.Main | Opacity | float | 1.0 | How strongly the drop colour replaces the picture (scales the drop mix). |
| Shaders.Precipitations.Main | Refraction | float | 1.0 | How far the drops shift the picture behind them. |
| Shaders.Precipitations.Main | Coloring | float | 1.1 | Base brightness added to the refracted colour inside each drop (multiplied by 0.02). |
| Shaders.Precipitations.Main | Bloom | float | 0.5 | Meant to weight the bloom glow in the drops. No shader reads it (section 9); the bloom always enters at full weight. |
| Shaders.Precipitations.Main | SkipHiddenLayers | bool | true | Skips layers that lie behind the pixel (they add nothing). Same picture, less work. |
| Shaders.Precipitations.Main | ForceRain | bool | false | Not a look setting: makes the game count as raining outdoors (section 2). |

### 5. Passes and outputs
- One unnamed technique with one pass: a full-screen vertex pass and a pixel pass. The C++ asks for technique index 0; any other index draws nothing.
- Output: one colour per pixel at full frame size, in the frame target's format. Nothing is pre-cleared, so every pixel must be written. Pixels with no drops return the source picture after the linearise and delinearise round trip.
- The Vulkan pass (section 7) writes to the same target.

### 6. Behaviour details
- Colour space: source colours are converted from display (sRGB) to linear with the standard sRGB curve, combined there, and converted back with the inverse curve. The refracted source colour is converted the same way. The bloom sample is added as read, without conversion (the shader does not convert it).
- Ray and layers: the world ray through the pixel comes from the camera basis and projection. The effect runs 14 layers (indices 1 to 14; index 0 is never drawn). Layer i is at 100 x i game units along the ray. A layer contributes only where the pixel's surface is further away than that distance; closer geometry hides it.
- Cylinder mapping: each layer's drop grid lies on a cylinder around the camera. The horizontal coordinate is the ray's azimuth, mapped into 0 to 1 with a half-turn offset. The vertical coordinate is the height over horizontal distance times 0.004 times VerticalScale.
- Drop grid: each layer is a grid of cells, each with a pseudo-random brightness and a streak-shaped drop that is elongated along the vertical. Each layer is offset horizontally by a term that depends on the layer and on the grid position, and vertically by Speed times the animation clock. A cell's brightness fades with the layer index (divided by 1 plus 0.2 times the index).
- Drop size: the size edge grows with the layer index and with the rain amount (the smaller of the layer index and 4 times the amount, scaled by 0.05, plus 0.005). The edge must never be zero, because the smooth step breaks at zero.
- Shadow test: each layer's drop is multiplied by the sun ortho-map test at the layer's point along the ray. The point counts as visible only if the map's stored depth is not closer than the point (bias 0.0001) and the point lies inside the map. Points outside the map do not show rain. This is the reverse of Snow, which counts outside points as open; keep the rain rule as it is (section 9).
- Division guard: a cell's brightness divides by a fractional product that can be zero. The divisor has a floor of 1e-4. Keep it.
- Refraction: the total drop amount shifts the sampling position diagonally (right by the total times Refraction, and up on screen by the same amount). The shifted position samples the source picture and the bloom texture.
- Drop colour: a tint that moves from mid-grey (0.5) toward twice the linearised sun colour, weighted by the square of the saturated dot product between the ray direction and the sun direction. Drops look brighter toward the sun.
- Final mix: the result is the linear mix between the original colour and (refracted source + bloom + tint times Coloring times 0.02), weighted by the total drop amount times Opacity, then converted back to display space.
- SkipHiddenLayers: a layer is evaluated only when the pixel's depth is greater than the layer's distance, or when the switch is off. Both settings give the same picture; the switch only changes cost.
- Render-rectangle frames: the shared depth include maps coordinates into the drawn part of the target before the ray and depth are worked out. The Vulkan pass is off in those frames (section 7).
- Reversed depth: the depth buffer holds linear view distance over far distance, so reversed depth does not arise.
- Jitter (TAA): the shader has no jitter term; it uses the picture as the frame has it.
- HDR vs LDR: input is the display-referred frame after tonemapping; output is the same kind of value.
- NaN/inf guards: the division floor above, and the explicit level-0 read of the ortho map (so the read is safe inside the layer branch). Both must stay.
- Things that look wrong if missed: a grey film, or no streaks (amount or ShouldRender wrong); rain under roofs (shadow test missing); the picture shifting by blocks (refraction scaled wrong); black or white speckles (division floor missing); rain indoors (interior gate missing); a grey tint instead of a sun-coloured one (sun colour missing).

### 7. Vulkan path
- Shader: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Precipitations.frag, a line-for-line port of the D3D9 pixel pass; the header records the matrix conventions and that the smooth step is written out. Compiled form: Precipitations.spv in the same folder. The pass is requested under the name "Precipitations".
- Selection (src/core/EffectRecord.cpp, EffectRecord::Render): the same rule as Snow. The Vulkan pass runs only with NvrDxvkPasses on, not in a render-rectangle frame, with the Vulkan extension available, when "Precipitations" is not in NvrDxvkKeepD3D9, and when no SM6 build covers the technique. Otherwise the D3D9 pass runs.
- C++ side: src/effects/Rain.cpp (class RainEffect, whose effect name is "Precipitations"), ExtRender. It returns false (D3D9 runs) if the technique index is not 0, if the shadow effect is missing, or if any of the textures is missing. The bloom texture is required: with no bloom texture the Vulkan pass does not run.
- Textures: descriptor set 1, binding 0 source picture, 1 combined depth, 2 ortho map, 3 bloom (first level). The sampler is the linear clamp sampler at binding 4; its index is read from the push block at offset 208.
- Push block (224 bytes; offsets in bytes):
  - 0: shadow-to-light matrix (four columns).
  - 64: view transform (four columns, as the engine stores the view matrix).
  - 128: view scale: x projection [0][0], y projection [1][1], z far distance (camera data y), w game clock z.
  - 144: rain data (TESR_RainData as registered).
  - 160: rain aspect (TESR_RainAspect as registered; its z component is sent but not read).
  - 176: sun direction.
  - 192: sun colour.
  - 208: sampler index (32-bit unsigned), then 12 bytes of padding.
- The Vulkan pass runs the same 14-layer loop, the same shadow test, the same colour mix, and the same guards: the division floor, the explicit level-0 ortho read, and the SkipHiddenLayers skip. The tint's saturated dot product is the same operation as the D3D9 shading term.
- The Vulkan pass does not run in render-rectangle frames, so it has no rectangle mapping.

### 8. Look and cost
- A correct result: thin, slightly bent streaks falling over open ground and sky, longer when VerticalScale is high, with a faint glow on them. Rain disappears under roofs and in shadows. Drops are brighter toward the sun. Only the drops change the picture.
- Typical wrong results: a grey wash (opacity or tint broken); streaks indoors or under roofs (gate or shadow test broken); the picture shifting by blocks (refraction broken); black or white sparkles (division floor missing); no refraction at all (refraction term missing); bloom too strong or absent (section 9).
- Cost class: per-pixel loop. One full-screen pass per frame, 14 layers, each with at most one ortho map lookup (skipped when SkipHiddenLayers skips the layer), plus three or four texture samples after the loop.

### 9. Open questions
- The Bloom setting ([Shaders.Precipitations.Main] Bloom, default 0.5) is written into the rain's aspect constant (its z component) but no shader reads that component. The drops' bloom is always at full weight, so the setting does nothing in either path. Decide whether the new version should honour it (the current picture is the reference for a like-for-like result) or retire the setting.
- The value the combined depth buffer holds for sky pixels is not confirmed here. The effect treats sky as further than every layer; confirm against the depth-combine pass.
- Whether Rain ever draws in a menu is not confirmed: ShouldRender has no menu test. The frame-level gating in ShaderManager was not traced in full.
- The shadow-test sense differs between Rain (points outside the ortho map show no rain) and Snow (points outside count as open). Both are the current behaviour; it was not checked whether the difference is intended.

### 10. Status of the wave 3 rewrite (2026-11-02, branch claude/rw3-weather)
- New D3D9 effect: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Precipitations.fx.hlsl. One technique, the same sampler order (source, rendered, depth, ortho, bloom), the same constant names. Compiles (fx_2_0, all four defines).
- Settings are read as before. SkipHiddenLayers now only saves work: the picture is the same with it on or off (the layers behind the pixel contribute nothing).
- Chosen here, not in the spec: the refraction scale (0.004 of the total times Refraction in screen units), the drop cell grid (64 by 32 per layer), the drop hash and its 0.55 presence threshold, the streak shape, and the drop size as the spec's formula. The spec's division guard does not apply, because this shader has no division by a fractional product.
- Vulkan path (Compute/Precipitations.frag, .spv): NOT written yet. Without it the C++ falls back to D3D9.

## Puddles

*README title: Puddles (rain puddles, wet ground)*

2026-11-21 (owner: "remove WetWorld", "make the default mine"): Puddles and Wetness Effects are the rain wetness and ON by default, with the owner's look (the table in section 4). WetWorld, the old screen-space wet pass, is removed. The water level below keeps its own game-hour clock (FillHours / DryHours): it is the standing water left after the ground's film has dried. The film itself, the Rain setting groups, skin, heat haze, the drops and the water's rings follow Wetness Effects' clock (`src/core/RainState.h`). ForceRain fills the puddles now and ForceSnow stops their rain (before, ForceRain wetted the ground but never filled a puddle).

2026-11-18 (owner's request): the wall streaks (rain running down steep faces) are removed: their code in `Shaders/Includes/Wetness.hlsl` (PuddleStreaks), the settings Streaks, StreakWidth and StreakSpeed (saved values are skipped quietly, `SettingManager.cpp` Retired) and DebugView 4. The result is what Streaks = 0 gave before. The notes below about streaks are history.

### 1. Purpose
Puddles that behave like a modern engine's: they sit where water would gather (dips in the noise field, the land's crevices, flat open ground), grow while it rains and shrink back from their edges after it stops, at a pace set in game hours. Inside a puddle the surface is a flat, near-mirror water layer (IOR 1.33, F0 0.02) that hides the material's bumps, darkens what is under it with depth and ripples with the rain. Around each puddle a ring of soaked ground. On walls and other steep faces open to the rain, water runs down in streaks. Puddles reflect the sky (forward shaders), the scene on screen (Wetness Effects' reflections) and, with the Vulkan reflection passes, ray-traced reflections.

It is an upgrade of Wetness Effects (`src/effects/WetnessEffects`, `Shaders/Includes/Wetness.hlsl`): the same water layer, a new puddle model. It needs `[Shaders.WetnessEffects.Status] Enabled`.

### 2. Design: what the mainstream engines do, and what this effect takes
| Source | Technique | Here |
|---|---|---|
| Lagarde, "Water drop" 2a/2b/3a/3b (Remember Me, DONTNOD) | Two wet states: a porous surface darkens (albedo raised to a power by porosity) and gets glossier; standing water is a separate layer that hides the normal map and gives F0 0.02. Puddles fill the height map's cracks first, then the puddle areas, by a water level compared with the height. Ripples: a ring texture animated by time per drop. Streaks: a scrolling mask on vertical surfaces. | All four. The water level becomes a threshold on a world-space field (noise + the land's height), so puddles fill from the deepest points and dry from their edges. Wet ring = the porous state, puddle = the water layer. Ripples are procedural rings (no texture slot free in the object shaders). Streaks are procedural lanes. |
| UE5 (Landscape / material wetness, Substrate water slab) | Height-blended puddle masks; a water layer with IOR 1.33 over the base; puddle normal flattened toward the vertex normal; ripples as a normal layer. | Height-blend on the land (`HeightAwareness`), IOR 1.33 Fresnel (Wetness.hlsl's F0 0.02 film), normal flattening (`Flatten`). |
| Frostbite (Battlefield, Need for Speed) | Wet roads by roughness and albedo, not a texture swap; screen-space reflections on the wet road; stretched lamp highlights. | Roughness/albedo response; SSR via Wetness Effects' reflections. Stretched highlights: not done (section 9). |
| Far Cry 5 | Top-down depth map of the world for rain occlusion; wetness accumulated over time. | The ortho map (s12 objects, s13 land) was already there (Wetness.hlsl `WetnessOcclusion`); the accumulation is the CPU driver below. |
| RDR2, The Last of Us Part II, Cyberpunk 2077 | Puddle reflections from SSR, falling back to probes; Cyberpunk adds RT reflections on wet roads. | Forward sky reflection, then Wetness Effects' SSR, then the Vulkan reflection pass (SSR + RT): the puddle mask is in `Compute/Reflections.glsl`, so RT reflections see the puddles at mirror roughness. |
| Decima (Horizon) | Procedural rain ripples on puddles, more and faster with heavier rain. | Wetness.hlsl's raindrop rings (`WetRainDrops`), on the land and (Quality 3) on objects, at full strength only inside puddles. |
| Unity HDRP | Wetness as a coat layer: a smooth clear coat over the base, the base darkened by porosity. | The same split: Wetness.hlsl's film is the coat. |

Two things go to the Vulkan path, both because of where they live, not because ps_3_0 ran out:
- The reflection pass (`Compute/Reflections.glsl`) is Vulkan only (DXVK fork). Its puddle mask runs there; the D3D9 reflection effect is unchanged.
- The ray-traced reflections take the puddles' roughness from that mask.

The object, parallax and land pixel shaders keep ps_3_0: the new constants fit in four free registers (section 3) and the code fits the instruction limits (checked offline, section 8).

### 3. Inputs
#### Constants (pixel shaders; set by name from `PuddlesShaders`)
| Register | Name | Components |
|---|---|---|
| c174 | TESR_PuddleData | x water level 0-1 (CPU, section 6), y Coverage, z 1 / Size (game units), w Quality (0 = off: Wetness Effects' old puddles) |
| c175 | TESR_PuddleLook | x puddle roughness, y depth darkening, z edge softness (field units), w ripple strength in puddles |
| c165 | TESR_PuddleShape | x slope start (world up of the face), y slope full, z cavity (land height) strength, w wet-ring width |
| c166 | TESR_PuddleStreak | w debug view; x (2026-11-26) Mirror, read only by `Effects/Reflections.fx.hlsl` (y z were the wall streaks', removed 2026-11-18: 0) |

c165-c166 are the sun cross-fade's (Shadow.hlsl, SUN_CROSSFADE), which never compiles with Wetness Effects. c174-c175: free in all object pixel shaders (VEGAS_SHADERS.md section 3) and in the land's (c172-c173 lamp RT, c176-c179 blending and macro). Also read by `Effects/WetnessReflections.fx.hlsl`.

Plus Wetness Effects' own: TESR_WetnessData (c146: wetness, puddle wetness, raining, time), the ortho map, the camera.

#### Vulkan (Reflections.glsl, uniform block `cb`)
Two vec4 appended after `renderScale`: `puddle` (= TESR_PuddleData) and `puddle2` (x slope start, y slope full, z puddle roughness, w edge softness). Set by `ReflectionsEffect` from `TheShaderManager->Shaders.Puddles`.

### 4. Settings
`[Shaders.Puddles.Status] Enabled` (default true since 2026-11-21; restart: it picks `WETNESS_EFFECTS=2` for the surface shaders). Everything in `[Shaders.Puddles.Main]` and `.Debug` is live. Defaults marked * changed on 2026-11-21 to the owner's settings (settings step 91 moves a saved old default once).

| Key | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Quality | 2 | 0 off (Wetness Effects' puddles), 1 low (mask, water layer, flattening), 2 medium (+ land height cavities, wet ring, land ripples), 3 high (+ ripples on objects, a third noise octave) |
| Coverage | 0.262* | Share of flat open ground under water at a full water level |
| Size | 250* | Puddle pattern size, game units |
| FillHours | 0.5 | Game hours of steady rain to fill the puddles |
| DryHours | 3.0 | Game hours of daylight to dry them (night and overcast dry at NightDrying of that speed) |
| NightDrying | 0.35 | Drying speed at night, share of the day's |
| Roughness | 0.02 | Water surface roughness: how sharp the puddles' mirror is (0.1 from 2026-11-21, 0.02 since 2026-11-26, settings step 102) |
| Darkening | 1.0* | How much deeper water darkens what is under it |
| EdgeSoftness | 0.108* | Width of the shore, field units |
| SlopeStart / SlopeFull | 0.92 / 0.97 | World up of a face where puddles start / reach full |
| Cavity | 1.993* | How much the ground's relief places the water (land: height maps; objects: the colour texture) |
| WetRing | 0.12 | Width of the soaked ring round each puddle, field units |
| Flatten | 1.0 | How much the water hides the material's normal map |
| Ripples | 2.0* | Rain ripple strength inside puddles |
| Mirror | 1.0 | (2026-11-26) Mirror puddles: how strongly standing water reflects, at least 0.8 x Mirror at any angle, its blur this Roughness (not WetRoughness); 0 = the plain water Fresnel as before. Reflection passes only (`docs/EFFECTS.md "Reflections"`, "Mirror puddles") |
| MapMode | true | Restart: the large-scale terms from the puddle map on the NVR DXVK extension (section 6b) |
| ObjectPuddles | true* | Standing water and soaked rims on objects too (roads, slabs, rocks); never characters or the first-person model |
| ReflectionPuddles | true | The Vulkan reflection pass (SSR + RT) mirrors the puddles too |
| `.Debug` LevelOverride | -1 | Force the water level 0-1 (-1 off), for testing in dry weather |
| `.Debug` DebugView | 0 | 1 mask (red puddle, green wet ring), 2 ripples (normal xy), 3 levels (red rain wetness, green water level, blue raining), 4 nothing (was the wall streaks), 5 the puddle map's terms (map mode) |

### 5. Passes and outputs
No pass of its own. The puddle model runs inside `GetWetness` (Includes/Wetness.hlsl) in the object, parallax and land pixel shaders, and in the Vulkan reflection passes' `Reflectivity`. The effect's C++ (`Puddles.cpp`) only works out the water level and fills the constants.

### 6. Behaviour
#### Water level (CPU, `PuddlesShaders::UpdateConstants`)
- Rain, as the engine decides it (IDA `Sky::GetIsRaining` 0x59E950): the current weather is rainy (`TESWeather::IsRainy`: data byte 11 flag 4) and the weather percent is past its begin-precipitation byte (6) / 255, or the last weather is rainy and the percent is below its end-precipitation byte (7) / 255. Here as an amount: it ramps from the begin threshold to full, and from full down to the end threshold.
- Game time from `GameDaysPassed * 24 + GameHour` (so waiting and sleeping count; paused menus do not). Steps over 48 hours are taken as a load and ignored.
- Rising: level += rain x hours / FillHours. Falling (no rain): level -= hours / DryHours x lerp(NightDrying, 1, daylight x (1 - cloudiness)). Indoors the level is held (no weather there).
- [Shaders.Precipitations.Main] ForceRain counts as full rain, [Shaders.Snow.Main] ForceSnow as none (2026-11-21, `RainState::ForcedRain` / `ForcedSnow`).
- `.Debug` LevelOverride wins.

#### The puddle field (Wetness.hlsl `PuddleField`)
- f = value noise of the world xy at Size (two octaves; three at Quality 3), stretched to 0-1.
- + Cavity x (0.5 - height) (2026-11-12: x1 of a RELATIVE height, was x0.5 of an absolute one), so hollows, cracks and ruts fill first and bumps poke out as islands (Lagarde's flood level). The height: the land's height maps; a land layer without one gives its colour's brightness detail instead (0.6 x (luminance - its last mip's)); objects (no height maps in FNV) the colour texture's detail the same way (`ObjectPuddleHeight`, a mip chosen by distance). The material sorts itself: a flat texture (asphalt, concrete) barely moves the field, so the large noise shapes big irregular puddles; a bumpy one (gravel, mud, rock) gets many small pools and islands. x1.5 broke big puddles on gravel into islands everywhere in the bench, so x1. Limit: brightness is not height (a dark stain fills like a dip).
  2026-11-11: while there is water, each layer's height counts against its own average (its last mip, TerrainTemplate.hlsl), so the
  level a height map sits at overall no longer adds or removes water by texture, and a layer without a height map adds nothing (flat).
  Cost: one cached 1x1-mip read per layer, only while wet. (Seams at the land's quad edges seen by the user at Size 64 come from the
  per-pixel position rebuild of each quad draw, magnified by a small Size; not fixed: keep Size at about 250 or more.)
- The level's threshold t = 1 - level x Coverage. Puddle where f > t, softened over EdgeSoftness. As the level falls, t rises and the shallow edges go first: puddles dry from the edge in.
- x the slope mask on the face's own (geometric) up, x the ortho map's open-sky test, x a distance fade.
- Three zones (2026-11-12, the user's reference pictures): damp ground (the rain: darkening rain^2 x 0.7, roughness lerp(1, 0.22, rain), the normal map KEPT: wet mud glints on its bumps); the soaked rim (f between t - w and t, w = WetRing x (0.35..1.65) by a second noise so the band wanders; squared; darkening 0.85, roughness 0.12, normal kept); standing water (below). The shore softness wanders too (EdgeSoftness x 0.5..1.5).
- Floating bits (land, Quality 2+, standing > 0.3, within 1500 units): one hashed cell per ~6 units, about 1 in 80 holds a speck (dull green) or a leaf (brown), 0.6-1.8 units, laid over the albedo; the water there is not flat (`w.debris`, TerrainTemplate).

#### The water layer
- Inside: darkening = 1 + Darkening x depth (the porous darkening of Wetness.hlsl taken further: water absorbs), gloss 1, roughness lerp to Roughness, the film normal = the face normal (no bumps) plus ripples. Murk (2026-11-12): `w.murk` = depth x standing tints what the water reflects toward brown-grey (`PuddleMurkTint`, land sky reflection and lamp highlights). The templates also flatten their own tangent-space normal by `wet.flatten`, so the sun, lamps and ambient see a flat surface too.
- Ripples: Wetness.hlsl's `WetRainDrops` rings (land: Quality 2, objects: Quality 3), at Ripples strength inside puddles.
- (Removed 2026-11-18) Streaks (Quality 2+): faces steeper than about 55 degrees, open to the sky, while it rains: lanes StreakWidth wide, one in three dry, each with drops running down at its own speed (the bright head at the bottom, the tail fading above it). A streak adds to the wall's own rain wetness (darkening and gloss), hides the material's bumps, and its surface bulges (rounded across the lane, the drop's tail sloping up) so it catches the sky as moving glints; a flat film on a wall reflects almost nothing. A wall under an overhang stays dry (user 2026-11-11): the 40-unit look-out of the cover test is kept.

#### Lamps on wet ground (2026-11-12, the land)
- Each lamp adds its highlight on the water film (`WetSpecularStretched`: GGX with the half vector's part along the view, projected on the surface, shrunk to 0.3 at full gloss), so the highlight runs long toward the eye: the wet-road streak. x the lamp's RT shadow, x murk.
- The land's outdoor RT lamp-shadow record (TERRAIN_LAMP_RT) has no room for it (32 temps: 20 records no longer fit): `PUDDLES_LEAN` there raises the lamps' own gloss by the wetness instead (PBR: roughness = 1 - gloss; the game's look: power 200), and leaves out debris and the brightness height (height maps only). The objects' outdoor lamp record has its own lean path (no rim noise, no texture height).

#### Under the deferred renderer: DeferredWetness (2026-11-19, test only, off)
- `[Main.Main.Quality] DeferredWetness` (restart; needs DeferredRenderer 2): the near land's deferred records (TerrainTemplate, LAMPS_DEFERRED 2) are made with `DEFERRED_WETNESS`: no water layer of their own, the layers' height left in COLOR2.r. One pass after the G-buffer (`core/DeferredWetness.h`, `Shaders/DeferredWetness.hlsl`) runs the same `GetWetness` (same defines: map mode, ripples, height) and writes the darker albedo, the flattened normal, the wet ambient + the film's sky reflection into the frame, and a film target (gloss, roughness, film normal, murk). DeferredSun adds the film's sun highlight with its own shadow; (2026-11-20) each lamp's streak on the land's film is drawn by DeferredLamps' land film pass (`DL_LAND_FILM_ONLY`) with that lamp's shadow; the screen pass below takes the film from that target for the SSR (and for the streaks only where the lamp pass could not draw them). (2026-11-20, step 2) objects and parallax too (their deferred records drop the layer, the pass draws it); the far land stays dry; objects' lamp streaks stay in the screen pass.

#### Reflections
- Forward: the film's sky reflection (Wetness.hlsl `WetSkyReflection`, Fresnel F0 0.02).
- `[Shaders.WetnessEffects.Reflections]` (D3D9 SSR): recomputes `GetWetness` from the depth, so it sees the same puddles (noise part; no height map there). (2026-11-19: on the near land with DeferredWetness it reads that pass's film target instead, so it reflects exactly the water that pass drew.)
- `[Shaders.Reflections]` Vulkan passes: `ReflectivityOwn` adds the puddle share to the rain wetness on floors and pulls the roughness to the puddle's. With RT reflections on, the rays come from there.
- 2026-11-12 the surfaces' own mask: the land and object templates add the standing water they drew to the material buffer's alpha (`TESR_MaterialBuffer` a = 1 + standing, `PuddleStandingOut`; needs `[Main.Main.Quality] MaterialBuffer`), and `PuddleStanding` (Reflections.glsl) reads it where a template wrote (r > 0.5), so SSR/RT mirror exactly the drawn water (height pooling, cover, debris). Elsewhere (grass, LOD, no buffer) the old noise rebuild. Reflections.cpp binds the buffer for this even with MaterialBufferRoughness off: material2.x bit 1 = roughness override, bit 2 = puddle mask.

### 6b. Puddle map (map mode, WETNESS_EFFECTS 3; 2026-11-12)
Why: the in-shader puddles cost the ps_3_0 surface shaders their temps (the rain-cover test: 16 ortho taps + a 3D Perlin wander;
the field: 2-3 value-noise octaves; the rim wander). That forced lean paths (the land and object outdoor lamp-shadow records) and a
dry lamp record, and 7-layer land had no sampler for the cover map.
- **Mode:** `[Shaders.Puddles.Main] MapMode` (default true, restart) with the NVR DXVK extension: ShaderRecord sends WETNESS_EFFECTS 3
  (`PuddleMap::ModeAtStart`, logged once). Without the extension, or MapMode off: WETNESS_EFFECTS 2, the in-shader path as before.
- **The map** (`PuddleMap.h`, `Compute/PuddleMap.comp`): TESR_PuddleMap, rgba16f 2048 x 1024, two 1024^2 cascades side by side
  (near 4 units a texel = +-2048 round the camera, far 20 units = +-10240), each snapped to its texel grid; built on the extension right
  after the ortho map's draw (ShadowManager), only when a cascade should move (the camera left the middle quarter of its window), the
  camera moved 64 units up or down, Size or Quality changed, or every 8th ortho draw. Per texel: r the field's large-scale part (the
  same value-noise octaves), g the top surface's height (the ortho depth through the inverse ortho transform, one refinement for its
  0.05 tilt, averaged over a 3 x 3 tent of ortho texels at a position jittered by world noise: the old tent + wander), b the rim wander.
- **The surfaces** read it ONCE a pixel (`PuddleMapRead`: the near cascade inside it, else the far one, no blend), at the cover's
  look-out point (steep faces 40 units out): the cover = saturate((its height - the top's) / 16 + 0.5); the field = r + Cavity x relief
  (the per-pixel height maps / colour detail); the wander = b. Everything else stays per pixel (zones, shore, rim, ripples, streaks,
  debris, murk, darker water, lamp highlights). Constants: TESR_PuddleMapNear / Far on the old ortho matrix's registers (c153-c154; land
  lamp record c11-c12). Sampler: the old cover map's (objects s12, land s13; 7-layer land s15, see below).
- **7-layer land:** the GAME binds its seventh normal map to s13 on every land draw whatever the shader declares (measured: the map's
  alpha read 1 there), so 7-layer land takes the map on s15 (free without terrain blending, outside the lamp-shadow record). With
  blending on, or in the 7-layer lamp-shadow record, no stage is left: PUDDLES_NOMAP, the in-shader path WITHOUT the cover (as before
  the map). Not done: a vertex-shader fetch of the map (spare interpolators exist: vcol.w, tanO/binO/nrmO/eyeO.w; vertex samplers s0, s2,
  s3) or the no-limits SPIR-V surface shaders.
- **Lamp records:** the land and object outdoor lamp-shadow records now fit the full puddles (no PUDDLES_LEAN in map mode); the land's
  lamp loop takes the lamp highlight from the lamps' own specular with the wet gloss (no temps left there for a second lobe per lamp,
  even `WetLampFilm`); elsewhere the stretched `WetLampFilm` (land: every lamp; objects: lamps 2-3 of the full pass, OBJ_LAMP2/3).
- **Fallbacks left:** SLS2027 with [PBR] VanillaBridge + MicroShadow: its main record is past the D3DX9 compiler's limit in map mode
  (a register-allocation quirk: it fails even with the map read replaced by a constant) and loads through the lean retry
  (`PUDDLES_LEAN` + `PUDDLES_NOMAP`: the in-shader lean path for that record only); its outdoor lamp-shadow record stays dry (as since
  2026-11-09). Fix: the no-limits SPIR-V route (NvrDxvkReplacements).
- **Dry last fallback (2026-11-27):** with Wetness Effects + Puddles + Terrain Blending the forward main records of SLS2023/24/26/27.pso
  fit neither the full nor the lean puddles (X4505 at both flags, with and without the clouds' lookup), so their forward draws
  (reflections, other cameras, blended draws, DeferredRenderer 0) used the game's own shader. A record that fails both is now made once
  more without the water layer (`WETNESS_OFF`, `Cache\Dry\`; `ShaderManager.cpp` after the PuddlesLean retry, Object and Parallax templates
  only): this mod's shader, dry. Under the deferred renderer the main-view pixels keep their water from DeferredWetness (the lighting-free
  records). Offline: the water layer is about 750 of the 2,550 ps_3_0 slots of these four.
  Bench 2026-11-27: with the bench toml all four load dry; with the owner's live wet toml six failed on main (also SLS2034 / SLS2036, the
  many-lamp records) and five now load dry; SLS2024 (skinned) still does not fit dry and stays on the game's shader. Main-view pictures
  (v-desert-noon, the owner's puddle1 save, 900-frame settle) match main 2480e33 by eye.
- **Checking a record the way the game compiles it:** `tools\landc\landcf.exe` (D3DX9 + the PREFER_FLOW_CONTROL retry); the offline
  template tool's newer compiler fits shaders the game's does not. A record that fails in game leaves `<cache>\<name>.failed.hlsl` (the
  exact source) for it.
- **Debug:** `.Debug` DebugView 5 (map mode): red the map's field, green this point against the map's top surface (0.5 = on it), blue
  the map holds data. The log has the first build's transform and one readback of the texels round the camera.

### 7. Vulkan path
- `Compute/Reflections.glsl`: `PuddleShare(uv, depth, worldNormal)`, the same field without the land's height term (the post pass has no height map), the slope mask and the ortho map's open test (`OpenAbove`). Used in `ReflectivityOwn` (floor guess) and `MaterialReflectivity`.
- Rebuilt: every `Reflections*.frag` that includes Reflections.glsl (glslang 16.6.0).
- No new images: the RT shared-image count is unchanged.

### 8. Look and cost
- Correct: in rain, flat open ground grows irregular puddles from the low spots of the pattern and the land's crevices; puddles are flat mirrors with ripples, darker inside, with a soaked ring; none under roofs or on slopes; after the rain the puddles shrink from their edges over DryHours.
- Wrong: puddles on walls or slopes (slope mask); puddles under cover (ortho map); the whole ground under water (Coverage or the level too high); a hard staircase edge (EdgeSoftness); puddles that pop in or out (level not eased).
- Cost: inside the surface shaders, only where wet (branches on the level and the wetness). Measured numbers: section 10.

### 9. Not done / open
- Stretched lamp highlights: done on the land (2026-11-12, above). Objects (roads and slabs that are objects) keep the round GGX of their lamp passes: their lamp loops are spread over many template paths and passes; not done.
- (Fixed 2026-11-09) The outdoor RT lamp-shadow records had no water layer at all, so ground and objects near any light (towns, campfires) stayed dry. They have it now: objects with the puddles' two on c160/c161, the land (WETNESS_ALT_REGS) on c2, c4-c17, c98-c99. The first try (c192+ / c188-c189) sat on registers the DLL writes raw per draw (material rules c197-c199, lamp binding c174-c189) and drew nothing. A record that doesn't fit with the water layer is rebuilt with WETNESS_OFF and keeps its lamp shadows (SLS2027 in game).
- Wall streaks show on open steep faces (NVRAutoTest2's sign post, the puddle2 metal building) and not on the puddle2 shack walls: confirmed 2026-11-11 with DebugView 4, their eave plus the cover lookup's spread (about +-37 units) reaches past the 40-unit look-out (open at 120). Kept on purpose (user: walls under an overhang stay dry). Slanted rain under short eaves (a dry band under the eave, wet below) is not modelled.
- `[Shaders.WetnessEffects.Debug] ShowCover` blue ("no map") on the land = 7-layer land quads (2026-11-12, the puddle4 canopy): s13, the cover map's sampler, holds the seventh layer's normal map there, so that land has no cover test and gets puddles under roofs (the gas-station canopy). 6-layer land is fine (puddle2: green open, red under the car). Fix options: a vertex-shader lookup of the cover map passed down (coarse: one value per land vertex, ~128 units), dropping the 7th layer's normal map for the cover map, or the Vulkan side (a wetness pre-pass writing the cover into the material buffer). Not done.
- Object surfaces have no height map in the shader, so their puddles are noise-shaped only.
- Splashes (WetWorld had them; WetWorld is removed since 2026-11-21) are not drawn anywhere.

### 10. Bench (2026-11-08, slim slot C, RTX 4070 Ti SUPER, full RT, rain = Zone weather 0B300012)
- Runs: `nvr-bench/runs/20261108-220055-capture-NVRAutoTest1` (list `lists\puddles-ab.txt`: dry, rain fill at timescale 240, Quality 2/0/3/1, debug views 1-3, drying at timescale 1200) and `20261108-220936-capture-NVRAutoTest2` (`lists\puddles-perf.txt`).
- Log: 0 `REGISTER CLASH`; 2 compile failures, both SKIN2010/2011 in the exterior-lamp records (Skin.hlsl temp limit; the skin template does not include Wetness.hlsl: not this effect's).
- Look: puddles fill the flat open ground from the low spots of the field, are flat sky- and scene-reflecting water (the sign is mirrored by the SSR/RT pass), soaked rings show in the mask, the sign's post gets streaks; after the rain they shrink from the edges and are gone after about 3 game hours. At Coverage 0.35 full puddles flooded most flat ground: default lowered to 0.25.
- Cost: not measurable here. Three slot games shared the GPU, the world scene swung 2.2-7.3 ms in blocks unrelated to the setting; neighbouring Quality 3 / 0 blocks: world scene median 2.7 / 2.8 ms. Retime on a quiet machine.

#### 2026-11-09 (slim slot C, the user's own saves puddle1-4: a road near a wreck, a town of shacks, Freeside, a gas-station canopy)
- Lists: `lists/puddles-spot.txt` (dry, rain fill, off, Quality 3, mask, ripples, night, drying), `lists/puddles-lampcheck.txt` (mask with the outdoor lamp records on / off, ShowCover). Rain: Honest Hearts `NVDLC02ZVRainWeather` (02010C78 in the 12-plugin slot); clear `NVWastelandClear` 000FFC88. `setgs fVanityModeAutoDelay 99999` (the idle third-person camera moved the view in long runs).
- Found and fixed: ground and objects near lights got no water layer (above); the lamp-record registers' first try overwritten by raw per-draw writes; SLS2027's lamp record no longer fits (rebuilt dry, lamp shadows kept).
- Final runs (20261109-103940 lampcheck, -104815, -104953, -110013, -110223): 0 REGISTER CLASH, 0 crashes, 0 lamp-shadow records lost; the lampcheck mask is the same with the lamp records on and off. puddle2 ran out of the plugin's 600 s at 5 of 12 pictures (about 5 fps with other slots on the GPU).
- The user's own tuning (toml, 2026-11-08 23:14): Coverage 0.664, WetRing 0.5, Cavity 2, Darkening 2, Ripples 2, StreakSpeed 8; the bench pictures use those.

#### 2026-11-11 (slim slot C, puddle2): streaks
- "Streaks not working": the streak mask was 0 on the shack walls. DebugView 4 (new) showed them steep and with lanes but covered; ExteriorForward off changed nothing; a 120-unit look-out made them open. Kept at 40 (overhangs stay dry).
- Where streaks ran they hardly showed: max() against the wall's rain wetness (MinRainWetness 0.65: darkening 0.42, film 0.65) hid most of them, and the drop's bright head sat at the top of its tail. Now additive, bulging (glints) and head-down.
- The user's StreakSpeed 8 (drops at 2-13 m/s, flicker) set to 1.5 with the user's OK.

### 2026-11-12 Who gets standing water
Only the land, by default (user): `ObjectPuddles = false` sends object draws TESR_PuddleData with level 0 (Puddles::SetDrawConstants, per pass from Render.cpp SetShadersHook; ShaderRecordPixel::IsLand = TerrainTemplate). Skinned meshes (actors) and the first-person model are always dry. Objects keep the rain dampness and wall streaks.

### 2026-11-20 With DeferredWetness on (deferred wetness step 3, test only)
- Puddles has no switch of its own: it follows Wetness Effects' (the menu's "Rain wetness"; `SettingManager` RainWetnessOneSwitch /
  ApplyRainWetnessSwitch keep `[Shaders.Puddles.Status] Enabled` equal to it).
- `ReflectionPuddles` is hidden: the Vulkan reflection passes take the standing water and its roughness from DeferredWetness's film in frames
  that have one (`docs/EFFECTS.md "Reflections"`, last section), not from the field / mask in section 3.
- Wetness Effects' own puddle settings are hidden while Puddles is on with Quality above 0 (2026-11-20, owner's request;
  `ImGuiManager.cpp` RainWetnessDeadKey): MaxPuddleWetness, PuddleRadius, PuddleMaxAngle, HeightAwareness, CreviceFill. Every record then
  takes GetWetness's Puddles branch (`Includes/Wetness.hlsl` PUDDLES_ON), which replaces the CS noise puddle they shaped. PuddleMinWetness
  stays (it weighs the ripples there).

## Reflections

Screen-space reflections on the ground (wet streets, dry sheen, interior floors, materials that reflect), with ray-traced reflections
on top where the RT core runs. Half-resolution trace, history and blur into the effect's own buffers, then a full-resolution composite
in the HDR chain (after Specular, before the fog). Started 2026-11-07 (this README did not exist before; the RT path's long history is
in docs/next/FORK-RT-REFLECTIONS.md (not in this repo)).

### Leaning, narrower mirror images: the normal maps' DXT offset (2026-11-27, refl-projection-fix)

- Seen: owner's save tilted01 (the ARK Computer Room, a room 07 port), level camera facing a red door on a glossy floor: the door's
  reflection leaned and was 10-15 % narrower than the door at the far end (its right edge ~12 px in at 1080p, more with Roughness 0.02).
- Not the projection: the trace, the composite and every reconstruction use the same projection (ViewPosition / ToScreen / toWorld agree);
  with the floor's true normal a level mirror keeps every reflected point's screen x exactly, whatever the depth. Proven on the bench
  (DIAG run 20261127-113331-slotA, a throwaway composite that classed the normals): the depth's own plane and the smooth normal were level
  (|x|, |z| under 0.02 in view space); only the normal-mapped shading normal leaned (0.02-0.08 in view x and z, a few degrees).
- Cause, proven: the floor's normal map (sm22_111_A_..._n.dds, DXT5) stores 95 % of its red texels as 123, the 5-bit red's nearest value
  to a flat 128: x = -0.035, a ~2 degree tilt of the whole floor along the map's u axis. A mirror doubles it and the reflection carries it out
  to the door. A/B (run 20261127-113547-slotA): UnifiedReflections and DeferredBumpedNormals both off (the smooth normal) gave an exact
  mirror. (The earlier run that switched them one at a time kept the shading normals on in both shots: each one alone brings them.)
- Fix: `ViewNormal` (Compute/Reflections.glsl, and the D3D9 effect's REFL_GBUFFER_ON one) leaves out a bend from the smooth normal up to
  BumpCodecFloor 0.045 (~2.6 degrees) and keeps it whole from BumpCodecFull 0.09 (~5 degrees), smoothstep between, on top of BumpKeep's
  distance fade. The DXT error (red up to ~0.035, green ~0.02) is under the floor; tile edges, dents and cobbles stay in the ray. All Vulkan
  passes (trace, Hi-Z trace, temporal, blur, composite, RT RayGen / Resolve) read ViewNormal, so RT follows. Every surface with a DXT normal
  map and the shading normals in use, inside or out, had the offset; it shows on smooth near-mirror floors.
- Bench after (runs 20261127-114138-slotA before, -114243-slotA after; crops `F:\NVR-bench\shots\tilt-fix-crops.png`): the reflection's edges
  line up with the door's at the owner's settings, with Mirror 1 and with Roughness 0.02; the octagon window sits straight under the door's.
  Outdoors (puddle1, owner-b-wet-r01.toml, puddles held full, level, Mirror 1; runs 20261127-114409-slotA / -114900-slotA): the truck's
  reflection in the standing water sat under the truck before and after (DeferredWetness flattens the shading normal there); only the rain
  differs between the two pictures.
- Left as it was: the D3D9 effect without REFL_GBUFFER_ON (DeferredBumpedNormals with the D3D9 fallback) has no smooth normal bound to
  compare with, so it still follows the offset. Gentle real slopes under ~3 degrees in a normal map no longer bend a mirror.

### Puddles as mirrors (2026-11-26)

- Cause, proven on the bench (owner's puddle1 save, the owner's settings, puddles held full; runs 20261126-163231-slotD and
  -163637-slotA): SpecularOcclusion (default 1.0) dims a reflection by the surface's own lit brightness against what it reflects. A puddle
  is dark because the water darkens the ground (Puddles Darkening, 1.0 since the upstream 1.1.1 merge), so under a bright sky the
  occlusion removed most of the sky: dark holes instead of mirrors. With SpecularOcclusion 0 the puddles mirror the sky; Puddles
  Roughness 0.1 against 0.41 and ReplaceForward made little visible difference there.
- Fix: `SurfaceWetShare` (Reflectivity: the reflection's share that is water on the surface, the wet film and puddles, not bumpy damp
  ground) is kept out of SpecularOcclusion, in the Vulkan composite and the D3D9 effect. Dry floors keep it.

### Mirror puddles: how close FNV gets to a flooded-street mirror (2026-11-26)

Owner: "see if puddles can be made to be this reflective", with a modded GTA V street after rain as the reference: the flooded road a
near-perfect, bright mirror of palms, traffic light, cars, sky and player, slightly darkened, barely rippled. Branch `mirror-puddles`.

**What held our puddles back** (the owner's path: DXVK fork, DeferredRenderer 2, DeferredWetness film, UnifiedReflections; RT reflections
`Enabled` but the RT master switches `RTExteriors` / `RTInteriors` off, so screen-space reflections + the cube + the sky):
1. *Blur floor* (proven from the code): `Reflections.glsl` FilmWetness held every wet pixel at `WetRoughness` or rougher
   (`clamp(f.g, WetRoughness, 0.3)`), so standing water reflected at the owner's WetRoughness 0.099 although their Puddles Roughness is
   0.022. Under 0.03 a pixel is a mirror pixel (exact ray, no spread, no temporal history: ReflectionsRayGen / ReflectionsTrace
   `mirrorPixel`, RtMirrorJitter); at 0.1 the rays spread and the blur / denoiser smoothed them. The D3D9 technique never had the floor.
2. *Strength* (the main one): water's Fresnel. F0 is 0.02 (IOR 1.33; the composite uses 0.04 for wet ground); at the 10-30 degrees a
   standing player looks at the street the split-sum Fresnel is about 3-15 %, so the darkened ground showed through and the puddle read
   as a faint sheen. The reference is far past physical water (its puddles are mirrors at the player's feet too): an art choice.
3. *The sky where nothing was hit* (proven, bench): the gradient a missed ray took used the weather's raw sky colours, without
   `[Shaders.Sky.Main]`'s look (the owner's SaturationDay 1.6, blue tint): the puddle's sky came out pale grey, brighter than the drawn
   sky (run 20261126-194117-slotA: reflected sRGB ~(194, 200, 210) against the drawn sky's (137, 176, 211)).
4. *Blocks in the mirrored sky overhead* (proven, bench): the sky panorama was 128 x 128 for the upper hemisphere (1.4 degrees a texel);
   a puddle seen from above magnifies it into hard blocks of cloud.
5. Already fixed before: SpecularOcclusion darkening water (the section above).
6. *Resolution*: the trace / RT rays run at half resolution and the composite upsamples (depth-aware bilinear); MirrorJitter + the
   denoiser's short mirror history add detail on a still camera (RT only). A full-resolution mirror trace was not built (below).

**Built** (Vulkan passes first, the deferred path's film; the D3D9 technique gets the same rules so DeferredRenderer 0 and native D3D9
behave alike; the forward templates are unchanged):
- `[Shaders.Puddles.Main] Mirror` (0-1, default 1.0, live; `cb.film2.w`, D3D9 `TESR_PuddleStreak.x`): the *strength*. The composite gives
  standing water at least 0.8 x Mirror of its reflection (`MirrorPuddleShare`; `share = max(Fresnel share, floor)` x the trace's coverage
  x the amount); grazing angles keep the higher Fresnel; the ground under the water is dimmed by the same share. With Mirror above 0,
  standing water's reflection takes the Puddles `Roughness` itself, not WetRoughness's floor. 0 = exactly as before.
- `[Shaders.Puddles.Main] Roughness` stays the *sharpness* slider (it is not overridden); its default 0.1 -> 0.02 (settings step 102,
  moves a saved 0.1 only; the owner's 0.022 stays).
- Which pixels: the film's standing-water share, read back from its roughness (`FilmStandingShare`: smoothstep(0.85, 0.98) of the share
  the damp-cube split reads), so the soaked rim (roughness 0.12, share ~0.8) and damp ground keep their old look; the first build's
  linear share made rims and shores into bright mirrors.
- Ripples: standing water keeps its G-buffer normal in `BumpKeep` (DeferredWetness flattened the material's bump there, so what is left
  is the rain's ripple rings: a still mirror in dry weather, rippled while it rains); damp ground still leans to the smooth normal.
  (Ripples not judged by eye on the bench pictures.)
- The sky: the reflection passes' `cb.skyColor` / `horizonColor` are Sky's look-applied copies (`TESR_FogSkyColor` / `FogHorizonColor`,
  made for the fog by Sky.cpp UpdateFogSky); the D3D9 SkyAlong reads them too. `[Shaders.Reflections.RT] SkyPanorama` on by default
  (settings step 102, false -> true; it also serves the screen-space trace, not only RT), 512 x 512 (2 x 4 MB), and a panorama texel not
  seen lately takes the sky last seen lower on the same bearing (4 filtered taps) before the gradient. A near-mirror screen-trace ray
  (roughness < 0.15) that points into open sky (nothing above it in the ortho map at 64-4096 units: `OpenSkyAlong`) takes the panorama
  before the cube, as Resolve's RT misses already did.
- Coverage for a "flooded street": the existing `[Shaders.Puddles.Main] Coverage` (default 0.262; the owner runs 0.54). No new key.

**What FNV cannot match, and why**
- Off screen without RT: screen-space reflections only show what is on screen; a puddle seen from above mirrors what is over the player
  (a wall, a sign, the tops of trees), which is never on screen. The sky panorama and the cube fill it with the sky and a blurred, coarse
  copy of what was seen. RT reflections (NVR DXVK fork + RT helper, `RTExteriors` on) do show it (bench run 20261126-195646-slotD: the
  whole "OPEN 24 HOURS" sign, string lights and porch in the puddle); native D3D9 has no RT.
- A planar reflection (what the game's water does: the scene drawn a second time, mirrored at the water height) would also show off-screen
  things without RT, for puddles at about one height near the player. Not built: one more scene draw (the game's water reflection pass
  costs that much), puddles at other heights would be wrong, and the deferred G-buffer has no second view. Estimated 2-3 working sessions.
- The player in the mirror: the first-person model is not in the world, so a puddle cannot show the player as the reference does (third
  person: the actor is drawn and RT has it).
- Content: GTA V's dense, high-detail city; FNV's sparse low-poly scenes make a plainer mirror. Reflected clouds look as the sky's own
  clouds do (pixel-stepped in the bench slot).
- Full-resolution mirrors: the trace, RT ray list, Resolve and denoiser at full resolution on standing water (about 4x the rays there):
  2-3 working sessions; not built.

**Bench** (slot A/D/E, owner's live settings with the puddles held full: `F:\NVR-bench\tomls\owner-live-1126c-wet.toml` (no rain),
`-rain.toml` (RainOverride 0.6), `-wet-rt.toml` (RTExteriors on); lists `tour-mirror-day/-night/-rain/-perf`; contact sheet
`F:\VS-scratch\mirror-puddles-sheet.png`):
- Before (main 04d9515 = test36): runs 20261126-192241-slotA (puddle1 noon), -193124-slotA (Strip night), -193655-slotE (Goodsprings rain).
- After (final build): -201835-slotA, -201947-slotA, -202058-slotA; RT on: -195646-slotD. All VERDICT PASS, 0 shader errors in the logs.
- Look: the saloon puddle mirrors the porch, post, bikes and the neon sign sharply with blue sky; the open puddle the sky, clouds, bush
  and rocks; the Strip's puddles the robots and lights; in rain the sky and clouds. Damp ground and rims as before.
- Frame cost (run -202217-slotA, alone on the bench, RTX 4070 Ti SUPER, the owner's settings, ~23 fps): Mirror 1 / 0 / 1 / 0, 1500 frames
  each: 42.736 / 42.797 / 42.748 / 42.697 ms a frame: no measurable cost (noise about +-0.05 ms). The 512 sky panorama (on in all four)
  was not timed on its own (one 512 x 512 pass a frame; believed well under 0.1 ms).
- Settings step 102 proven on the bench: the log line `old defaults saved by an earlier build moved ...: Shaders.Reflections.RT.SkyPanorama
  false -> true` with the owner's saved file (version 101).

### Frame (RenderBuffers)

1. The surroundings cube, the light grid's constants, the sky panorama.
2. **Trace** (technique 1, `Compute/ReflectionsTrace.frag` / the .fx) into Trace (Temporal on) or Temp. With RT
   (`[Shaders.Reflections.RT] Enabled`, the NVR DXVK fork, the RT core's helper): **RayGen** (`ReflectionsRayGen.frag`: the march,
   then Dir / Ray / March / MarchHit) -> the helper (`Compute/Rt/ReflectionsRT*.comp`) -> **Resolve** (`ReflectionsRTResolve.frag`:
   the hits shaded from the screen, the cube or the estimate; Spread = x the hit's distance share, y the hit distance).
   (2026-11-07) The hit normal the estimate is shaded with (sun N.L, lamps, grid / cube) is bent by the hit's own normal map in the
   textured variants (`ReflectionsRTTex*`) when the core's `[Main.Main.RayTracing] RealNormalMaps` is on (default, with RealTextures):
   bricks and panels off screen keep their bumps in mirrors. The shadow rays still leave from the flat triangle normal.
3. Smoothing, one of:
   - **the RT reflection denoiser** (`[Shaders.Reflections.RT] Denoiser`, default on with RT; core/RtReflDenoise.h,
     docs/next/RT-REFLECTION-DENOISER.md (not in this repo)): 4 fork passes on frames the RT trace ran (native frames, not under DLSS RR), result copied
     into Buffer. Core 0 ours (ReBLUR-style: hit-point reprojection, long history, lobe-sized blur), core 1 AMD FidelityFX
     Reflections (ported, A/B). With it on, RayGen's glossy rays use decorrelated noise (the old pair sampled one line of each lobe).
   - **the old chain**: Temporal (technique 3: surface reprojection, 3 x 3 clamp, `Temporal` 0.85) -> Blur X / Y (2 / 4: depth-aware
     Gaussian, `BlurRadius` x roughness, narrower at RT contact). Always used without RT, in RenderScale rect frames, under DLSS RR
     (raw: no blur), and with `Denoiser false`.
4. The composite (technique 0) reads Buffer (water shaders read it next frame with RT Water).

### Settings
`[Shaders.Reflections.Main]` (the look and the SSR trace), `[Shaders.Reflections.RT]` (the RT trace, its fallbacks, and the denoiser:
`Denoiser`, `DenoiserCore`, `DenoiserHistory`, `DenoiserMirrorHistory`, `DenoiserBlur`, `DenoiserAntiLag`, `DenoiserClip`,
`DenoiserDebugView`; see defaults.toml for each).
- (2026-11-20, owner's request) `HiZ`, `HiZSafeCells`, `HiZCheck` and `HiZInteriorsTest` have no menu row (`ImGuiManager.cpp`
  InternalHidden; shown with ShowInternal): the Hi-Z walk measured slower, the others are its test keys. The file still sets them.
- (2026-11-07 perf-history, docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) "perf-history") `DenoiserHistory` and `DenoiserMirrorHistory` are frames AT 60
  FPS: with `[Main.Main.RayTracing] DenoiseTimeBased` (default on) the denoiser keeps the same seconds at the game's frame rate (32 -> ~11
  frames at 20 fps, at most 64 above 120 fps). At 60 fps nothing changes.
- (2026-11-07) The RT rays' reach is FarRadius when the core holds the far ring: FarObjectLOD, FarLandLOD, or PTRealtime (which turns
  FarLandLOD on by itself, docs/next/RT-DETAIL-GAPS.md (not in this repo) 7).
- (2026-11-07) Decals (`[Main.Main.RayTracing] Decals`, with AlphaTested: the ReflectionsRTAlpha* variants): the reflection rays see
  posters, cracks and blood (lifted 0.5 unit off their surface); the sun / key-lamp shadow ray at a hit passes them
  (ReflectionsRTBody.glsl RtProceedShadow). RT host scene `decals`.
- (2026-11-09, roadmap track 1 step 2) `[Shaders.Reflections.Main] TemporalJitterFix` (on): the old chain's Temporal pass reads the
  history where the change of TAA / FSR / DLSS's jitter since last frame puts it (as AmbientLight's). Done in C++ only:
  `TemporalBackbone::ShiftReprojection` shifts `TESR_ReflectionsReproject` (the matrix the .fx and `Compute/ReflectionsTemporal.frag` both
  read), `lastJitter` is kept with `lastView`. The RT denoiser's own reprojection is not shifted (not measured). Off: as before. Without
  TAA's Jitter the shift is 0. Wrong would look like the reflection drifting by a pixel each frame on a still camera with TAA on.

### One helper round trip a frame: RideMainExchange, FollowRenderScale (2026-11-11; both default off, NOT yet run in a game)
Cloud bench 029 (L4, 720p): RT reflections' helper pass costs 0.01-0.08 ms, but the effect made its own second exchange with the helper
every frame ("Reflections RT (exchange)" 0.7-1.1 ms, beside the main "RT exchange: helper round trip" 1.1-2.6 ms).
- **Why a second exchange**: the helper's inputs are RayGen's images (Rt.Dir, Rt.Ray), and RayGen ran in the Reflections step of the
  chain, after RtCore::Frame, because its screen march reads the lit picture. With `RaysOnly` (default) the march is skipped; the rest
  of RayGen reads the depth, normals, first-person depth / weapon mask, glass mask, TESR_MaterialBuffer, the ortho map and MaterialAware's
  material buffer: all there before RtCore::Frame, except the material buffer, which CaptureMaterial fills at the chain's start.
- **`[Shaders.Reflections.RT] RideMainExchange`**: `RtRideBeforeFrame` (EffectChain PreStep_RtCore, right before `RtCore::Frame`) runs
  CaptureMaterial early (TESR_RenderedBuffer pointed at the game target's own texture for that pass; the chain's MaterialCapture step
  then returns at once), draws RayGen (`RtRayGen`, RtTrace's first half) and arms a second core user, "RT reflections (main exchange)"
  (Point 0, no Setup, added last so it never takes a pass from another effect). `RtCore::Frame` records it with the others; `RtTrace`
  sees the frame rode and goes straight to Resolve. Same frame's data: no latency. Falls back to the old exchange (per frame) when:
  RaysOnly off / an RT DebugView / SplitTest, the path tracer replacing the scene, the scene target not a single-level texture with
  MaterialAware on, the main exchange's 8 passes full, the main exchange not run. Log: `Reflections: RideMainExchange: RayGen is drawn
  before the frame's main RT exchange ...` or `... is on but not used now: <why>` (a line per change); with the F10 profile a count
  line every 600 armed frames. Correct: the same picture as off, the "Reflections RT (exchange)" profiler line gone (no samples),
  the helper log's "runs" per pass equal for ReflectionsRT* and the other passes. Wrong would look like: reflections on surfaces that
  should not reflect / missing on mirrors (the early material capture read something else), or reflections lagging a frame.
- **`FollowRenderScale`**: the trace, RayGen, Resolve, Temporal, the blurs and the composite have kept to the RenderScale rect since
  2026-11-03 (the bench's own log: "RT traced in the RenderScale rect: 428x240 of 640x360"); what did not shrink is the round trip.
  The key only makes the five image copies (Dir, Ray in; Hit, Shade, Bounce out) take the rect too. Log: `Reflections:
  FollowRenderScale: the RT images are copied ... in the RenderScale rect only`. Still not in rect frames: the RT reflection denoiser
  (its passes have no rect rules: reprojection, taps and history would need rectToScreen / screenToRect and a rect clamp in all seven
  RtReflDenoise*.frag), MirrorJitter, weapon rays.

### MaterialAware: what owns the scene's alpha (2026-11-07, perf-mirrorfix)
The per-pixel material (MaterialAware, core/ReflMaterial.h) lives in the world target's alpha: 2 + 0.25 x metal + 0.24 x roughness,
written by the first pass of an object with an `_rmaos` map; [2, 3) reads as a material (CaptureMaterial, technique 7), anything else
as none. The game draws the world without alpha writes, so before this fix every OTHER draw left the alpha it found: whatever stood
in front of a mirror panel (a person's shirt, a fence post, hair, the first-person gun) kept the panel's material and turned into a
mirror (tester, VSRTRoom mirror wall: a chrome shirt with a noisy reflection painted on her back; a fence post showing the room).
Now (core/ReflMaterialRule.h, applied round every object pass: the two pass hooks, BSBatchRenderer::RenderPassImmediately, the grass
and depth-first replays, and the first-person scene):
- material pass (opaque, untested, a map, world scene): its material, as before;
- every other pass whose colour is not blended by its alpha property: alpha writes on, its own alpha (the game's 0..1: no material);
  the lamp / highlight passes the device blends keep the alpha under them (the scene's separate alpha blend ZERO / ONE);
- a blended first pass of a skinned geometry (hair): the material under it x (1 - its alpha) (separate alpha INVSRCALPHA for that
  draw): solid hair clears it, its see-through texels keep it;
- blended static draws (glass, decals, particles), depth-only passes: alpha left alone (a mirror seen through a window still reflects).
Log (every 1800 frames): `MaterialAware reflections: per frame N other passes wrote their own alpha (no material), M blended skinned
passes covered it`. Correct: `DebugView 7` (the material buffer) shows the mirror panels only, with every person / post / gun in front
of them black (no material); wrong: a person's outline filled with the mirror's colour in DebugView 7.
(2026-11-07 perf-mirrorfix2, test 10: her head and hair still took the mirror) the same rule also runs at DRAW time from the device's
state (Render.cpp MaterialDraw round the skinned partition and still-object draws: the sorted blended list, which holds actors with a
blend property and hair, never came through the pass hooks; ReflMaterialRule::DecideDraw). The log lists each distinct kind of draw
once a session (`MaterialAware reflections draw (session list N)`): a `KEEPS the alpha under it` line for an opaque or skinned draw is
the one to look at if something still shows the mirror. RT-PATH-TRACING.md section 68.
Known gap: the path tracer's G-buffer (CapturedNormals MaterialBuffer / AlbedoBuffer) keeps the same leak for draws that write no
G-buffer (the game's own shaders, hair, blended draws): docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) section 67.

### What correct / wrong looks like (the denoiser)
- At rest, a glossy floor's reflection settles within a second to a clean blur that is sharp where an object stands on the floor.
- Turning or walking: the reflection stays attached to the reflected object (no trail dragged along the floor); after a door opens or
  a light changes the reflection catches up within a few frames (anti-lag).
- Wrong: a reflection that smears along the floor when you move (the hit-point reprojection failing: `DenoiserDebugView 5` shows
  where it is used, green), a stuck ghost after a change (anti-lag), blotches that crawl (history too short: `DenoiserDebugView 3`).
