- RT test host scene `refldenoise` (`tools/nvr-rt-host/run.ps1 -Scene refldenoise`): the denoiser vs the old chain vs the exact glossy
  reflection, still and moving (no game).
- RT test host scene `normalmaps`: the RT trace's hit normal against the CPU's bend of a striped normal map (and flat with it off).
- RT test host scene `hairmirror` (perf-mirrorfix): an actor's alpha-tested, tinted hair card in a mirror: reflection and sun rays
  through its gaps, its tint, the old opaque packing as the control.
- `tests/refl_material_rule.cpp` (cl, no game): the per-pass alpha rule on a simulated mirror pixel (shirt, post, hair, lamp passes,
  glass, first person, MSAA edge), with the old rule's leak as controls.
- Bench lists `rrdn-still`, `rrdn-turn` (bench/foundations/lists; run script bench/deep-roadmap/rtrefldn/run_rdn.sh).

### Mirrors: smooth hits, metal that reflects, rays only (2026-11-07, perf-reflfix)
Tester (RTX 4090, test 9, the RT test room's mirror wall): spheres in the mirror were FACETED, the chrome sphere a plain white ball and
the gold one banded orange, neither reflecting anything. docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) "perf-reflfix" has the whole change.
- **Smooth hits**: the RT helper now keeps a vertex normal per vertex (tools/nvr-rt-helper/RtNormals.comp, made from each mesh's own
  triangles); the helper's pass (`ReflectionsRTBody.glsl` ShadeHit) shades a hit with them interpolated (RtHit.glsl
  `rtHitSmoothNormal`), then the normal map; the shadow rays still leave along the flat triangle normal.
- **MetalBounce** (`[Shaders.Reflections.RT] MetalBounce`, default 0.5 = the roughest metal that bounces; 0 off; live): a ray that meets
  smooth metal (the material rules' metal >= 0.75 at roughness <= MetalBounce: chrome, gold / brass / copper) sends ONE more ray along
  the mirror direction about the metal's normal. The pixel is then the metal's Fresnel tint (Schlick, F0 = its colour) x what that ray
  meets: the screen there when the screen shows it (Resolve `RtBounceColour`), else that hit's estimate, or the sky / panorama / cube
  along it when it meets nothing. One bounce only: mirror -> chrome -> the room is shown; a second metal behind that is lit, not
  reflected again (cost: one ray + one shadow ray, only on pixels whose ray meets such metal). The hit image's y then carries 256 (+ 512:
  the bounce met nothing), its zw the bounce's direction, the shade image the bounced hit's colour, and the new **bounce** image
  (RGBA16F, the helper pass's last image; Resolve set 1 binding 26, slot 25) the tint + the bounce's distance.
- **Screen reuse at a hit**: Resolve took the screen's colour wherever the hit's depth matched within ScreenTolerance; on a sphere seen
  in a mirror the hit is the far (mirror-facing) side, and near the outline it matched the front the camera sees. Now the hit's normal
  must face the camera (`RtScreenShows`, -0.15 for normal-map tilt).
- **RaysOnly** (default) skips RayGen's march altogether (its answer was dropped in Resolve on every pixel anyway): the same picture,
  the march's cost saved. Mirrors take the rays alone.
- What correct looks like: spheres in the mirror round, without bands; the chrome sphere shows the room and the red sphere in it, the
  gold one the room tinted gold. Wrong: chrome still plain white (no material rule for its texture: the bounce needs the rule's metal
  bits; `[Shaders.PBR.MaterialRules] DebugView` shows the class), bands (an old helper: the new one logs "smooth normals: on" at start).
- Not done (follow-up): the REFLECTING surface's own normal map. RayGen reflects about the screen's normal (TESR_NormalsBuffer /
  CapturedNormals), which is the smooth vertex normal BEFORE the normal map (CapturedNormals.h); no buffer holds the mapped normal.
  Bumpy floors reflect as if flat until the material pass also writes the mapped normal (CapturedNormals target 3 or the material
  buffer's free g / a, DEFERRED-RENDERING-PLAN.md); then RayGen can lerp toward it by (1 - roughness) for mirrors.
  (2026-11-18: G-buffer v2 now holds it, and `[Main.Main.Quality] DeferredReflections` or `DeferredBumpedNormals` (test keys, both off)
  give every pass of this effect the mapped normal in TESR_NormalsBuffer's place, `core/FdIndirect.h` ShadingNormals. That swaps the whole
  texture: the ray start, the trace, the temporal pass, RayGen and the denoiser all take the mapped normal, not only the reflection
  direction. Not run in game. `archive/2026-11-25.zip vs/repo-docs-archive/bumped-normals-study.md`.)
- RT test host scene `reflfix` (`tools/nvr-rt-host/run.ps1 -Scene reflfix`).

### Lamps as the game lights them, glossy metal bounces, the flashlight (2026-11-07, perf-reflfix2)
Tester (RTX 4090, test 10, the RT test room) and the flashlight team. docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) "Team perf-reflfix2" has the numbers.
- **Lamps at hits the screen cannot show** (Resolve `RtShadeHit`, the helper's key-lamp pick): `RtGatherLamps` packs each lamp through
  `RtReflLamps.h` as the game lights it: diffuse x Dimmer at most 1 without HDR x the LOD dimmer x LightingScale, decoded on the CPU
  (linear), the game's falloff `(1 - (d/r)^2)^2.2` or LampFalloff's shape; `Rt/RtLampLight.glsl` evaluates it in both passes. Before:
  the dimmer unclamped and decoded with the colour (the room's lamps have fade 1.8-2: 4.4-8.9x the game's light): white discs on the
  ceiling near each lamp in the mirror ("orbs"), discs on the chrome and gold spheres.
- **MetalBounce's direction**: a GGX sample of the metal's roughness about its normal (visible normals), a new one each frame while the
  RT reflection denoiser (or DLSS RR) averages the frames (Pass `frame.y`); without one the exact mirror direction. Gold (0.33) is now
  as blurred in the mirror as seen directly; chrome (0.08) stays sharp. The RT records' roughness code is `(k / 7)^2` (finer near 0).
- **HitFlashlight** (`[Shaders.Reflections.RT]`, default true, live): the flashlight (FlashlightDeferred::LightParams) is lamp 0 of the
  hits' lamps, with its cone and its pass's falloff, never the key lamp (no shadow ray). Its beam's spot shows in mirrors and on metal.
- **KeepFlashlight** (`[Shaders.Reflections.Main]`, default true, live; the Vulkan composite only): the composite adds back the share of
  the flashlight's GGX highlight that mixing the reflection over the lit colour took away (no reflection ray sees the light itself).
  Lamps' highlights are not kept (the composite has no lamp list).
- What correct looks like: in the RT test room's mirror the ceiling over a lamp as bright as on screen, no white dots; gold in the mirror
  blurred like gold seen directly (a little grain that settles in a few frames with the denoiser on), chrome sharp; the flashlight's spot
  on the walls seen in the mirror, and its small highlight on a mirror / chrome / gold surface it shines on. Wrong: a speckled gold
  sphere (the denoiser off: the bounce then stays sharp, report it), the beam's spot on screen but not in the mirror (HitFlashlight off,
  or HitLamps off).
- RT test host scenes `reflorbs`, `reflbounce` (`tools/nvr-rt-host/run.ps1 -Scene reflorbs`).

### Steady gold in mirrors, the flashlight near the player, undrawn hair (2026-11-08, perf-reflfix3)
docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) "Team perf-reflfix3" has the numbers.
- **MetalBounce on rough metal** (roughness 0.1 and up: gold, brass): the bounce's GGX sample is fixed per pixel (one of 9 lobe points by
  its place in a 3 x 3 block) and Resolve averages each bouncing pixel with its neighbours on the same metal: the lobe's 9-ray mean, the
  same every frame (test 11 drew a new sample each frame and a mirror keeps no history: the gold shimmered). Chrome keeps one exact ray.
- **HitFlashlight**: no light within 24 units of the flashlight, full from 48 (the reflected head was a white orb). KeepFlashlight's glint
  is capped at 2 linear (was 8).
- **Hair**: RT hair keeps its tint while the game is not drawing it (first person: the player's hair from the player's hair colour).
  (perf-reflfix4: this never ran for the hair, which is rigid; see below.)
- What correct looks like: the gold in the mirror smooth and still (a fine blur of the room, no sparkle); no orb round the reflected head
  (the flashlight's spot on the walls stays); the player's hair its own colour in chrome and mirrors in first person. Small bright dots at
  the lamps in mirrors and chrome are the surface's own highlight of each lamp (as on the gold on screen), not a fault.
- **MirrorLampHighlight** (`[Shaders.Reflections.Main]`, default 0, live; the Vulkan composite): the point lamps' own highlight on mirror-smooth / chrome surfaces the reflection covers (the blooming dots) is taken out before the mix (above 1.0 linear and above its 12-pixel surroundings); the sun's and the flashlight's glints stay, the gold's soft highlights (roughness 0.15 and up) are not touched. 1 = as before. Host scene `mirrorlamp`.

### Silver mirrors, smooth gold, hair in its colour (2026-11-08, perf-reflfix4)
docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) "Team perf-reflfix4" has the facts (each checked against the game's code / files) and the numbers.
- **Metal's F0 (MaterialAware)**: the composite took a metal's reflection colour from the hue of its own lit colour; on a mirror (a
  near-black diffuse) that hue is the lamps', and the whole reflected room went orange under warm lamps. Now (`Compute/ReflMetalF0.glsl`,
  the D3D9 composite's `ReflMetalF0`, MetalBounce's F0 in the RT pass) a metal up to roughness 0.06 reflects in neutral silver (0.95),
  blending into its colour from 0.06 to 0.15; rough coloured metal (gold 0.2-0.5) keeps its colour.
- **Rough metal's bounce average** (Resolve): a 5 x 5 tent (weights (3 - |dx|)(3 - |dy|)) in place of test 12's 3 x 3 box, which made 3-texel
  blocks and staircase edges in the gold (each of the 9 lobe directions held on a 3-texel lattice); first hits within 25 % + 32 units.
  Still steady (the fixed 9-point lobe). Host scene `reflsmooth` (pictures in out\reflsmooth_*.pfm), `mirrorf0`.
- **Hair** (core/RtHairTint.h, RtCore HairTintOf): the vanilla hair, beards and brows are rigid shapes (no skin instance), so the tint
  look-ups on the skinned path never met them. The tint is now read from the hair's own shader property (+0x84: what the game's hair
  shader is given as HairTint, written from the NPC's hair colour when the head is built), for the player and NPCs, first and third
  person; the rigid hair goes in as a TINTED record like the actors'. Log: `RT CORE hair (perf-reflfix4): ...` per hair part (first 8)
  and a summary at exchanges 60 / 600 / 6000. The hair's texture slot 2 is its layer map (_hl.dds), not a glow map: taken as one, the glow
  variants replaced the tinted colour with it (white hair). `RT CORE hair record (perf-reflfix4)` shows the flags as sent (tinted 1, glow
  0); `UNOFFICIAL RT reflections (perf-reflfix4): trace variant ...` the variant. Host scene `hairglow`.
- **Blocky mirrors: MirrorJitter** (`[Shaders.Reflections.RT] MirrorJitter`, default 1, + `MirrorJitterHistory` 8): a mirror pixel's ray
  leaves from one of 8 points of its half-resolution texel frame by frame (Compute/RtMirrorJitter.glsl: RayGen, the helper, Resolve) and the
  RT reflection denoiser keeps a short history on mirrors (virtual reprojection, anti-lag, a 1.25-sigma variance clip): outlines and hard
  shadow edges smooth instead of stepping every 2 output pixels. Only where the denoiser runs (native resolution; 2 = also under DLSS
  RR); rect frames keep the fixed centre ray. Log `UNOFFICIAL RT reflections MirrorJitter (perf-reflfix4): ...`. Host scene `mirroraa`.
  What wrong looks like: a soft trail behind a fast-moving person in a mirror (lower MirrorJitterHistory), shimmer on mirror edges
  (MirrorJitter 0 to compare).
- **Shader files check**: `UNOFFICIAL Reflections shader files (perf-reflfix4: ...)` once at start: each of the changed .spv (and the
  D3D9 Reflections.fx.hlsl) "this build's" or "OLD (copy the new one)".
- What correct looks like: the mirror's reflection the room's own colours under any lamp (white walls stay white under the warm lamps);
  the gold in the mirror and in the chrome sphere smooth (no blocks, no staircase on the red sphere's reflection) and still; the player's
  and NPCs' hair in the colour the game draws it. Wrong: the room orange in the mirror again (an old ReflectionsComposite.spv: the log
  line says so); blocks in the gold (an old ReflectionsRTResolve.spv); white hair with `tint from none` in the hair lines (the property
  held no written colour: the line shows what it held).


### Replace, not add: ReplaceForward (2026-11-12; default off, NOT yet run in a game)
What the frame already holds when the composite runs, and what the composite does with it (`lin = mix(lin, reflected, share)`, share =
saturate(amount x the split-sum Fresnel of the material's F0 and roughness) x the trace's coverage; ReflectionsComposite.frag / the .fx):
- **The game's env-map pass** (SLS2057 / SLS2058 = `Shaders/EnvTemplate.hlsl`, an additive pass after the object's lit pass: its cube map x
  the mask x the artist's strength, by the light with `[Shaders.PBR.EnvMaps]`). DOUBLE-COUNTED: the mix only takes `share` of it away,
  and on stone, tile or glass seen straight on that is about 4 %, so the painted cube map stayed almost whole under the real reflection.
- **Metal's surroundings** (`Includes/Object.hlsl` getMetalAmbient, added in ObjectTemplate.hlsl's lit pass: sky / ambient / the live cube x
  EnvBRDFApprox). Mostly replaced already: on metal the share is its F0 (0.5-0.95), so 5-50 % of it is left under the reflection. Not
  changed here (the object shaders are at their instruction limits and have no per-pixel knowledge of the reflection).
- **The Specular effect's sky reflection** (`Effects/Specular.fx.hlsl` SkyStrength, outdoors, the chain step before this one). Same as
  metal: (1 - share) of it is left; at glancing angles, where it is strongest, the share is near 1. Not changed here (another effect).
- Lamp and sun highlights are lights, not the environment: the mix dims them by the share too (KeepFlashlight puts the flashlight's back).
- A ray that finds nothing: the surroundings cube, the light grid, then the sky fill the missing share (the trace / Resolve); what they
  cannot fill stays out of the alpha, so the surface keeps its own colour there (never black).

**`[Shaders.Reflections.Main] ReplaceForward`** (live): the env-map pass reads LAST frame's Buffer at its own pixel (the pass is drawn
before this frame's trace, as the water with RT Water) and leaves out `alpha` of its cube map: where the effect answered the reflection
(screen, rays, its fallbacks) the surface shows that one reflection; where it did not (the surface does not reflect for this effect: no
material, too rough, past DrawDistance; or nothing was found) the game's cube map stays as the probe. The composite is unchanged.
- C++: `UpdateWaterConstants` makes the frame's value (TESR_ReflReplace: x 1, zw one scene pixel in UV) when last frame's composite drew
  from that Buffer (the effect on, drawn last frame, no camera jump, no DebugView, not under water, not while the path tracer replaces the
  scene); `core/ReflMaterial.h` hands it to the shaders only inside the frame's first world scene and the first-person scene (the planar
  water reflection and any other scene get 0). Log, a line per change: `UNOFFICIAL Reflections ReplaceForward: on ...` / `... not used now: <why>`.
- Off: the constant is 0, the shader's branch is not taken, the picture is as before. The pass itself is a little larger than before
  (one constant, one sampler on s11, a skipped branch); `REFL_REPLACE_FORWARD 0` compiles it out byte for byte.
- What correct looks like: the Ultra-Luxe's marble, polished counters, glass and chrome props show the room once (the real reflection),
  not the room over a painted sheen; the same things at a distance / in a rough spot / with the Reflections effect off keep the game's
  sheen. With RT Weapon the gun shows the traced room without its painted chrome under it.
- What wrong would look like: an env-mapped surface gone matte where the effect reflects weakly (by design where the material is nearly
  too rough: MaterialMaxRoughness's fade; and where SpecularOcclusion dims the reflection: lower it with this on); a thin bright or dark
  outline on shiny props for one frame while turning (the mask is a frame old, half resolution, not reprojected); the sheen flickering
  (the log line then alternates between `on` and `not used now`).
- Energy: the composite's weight already follows the material's Fresnel and roughness and is a mix (never more than 1); only ReflectAdd
  (default 0) adds on purpose. Nothing was changed there.
- What a reflection shows (RT hits out of view): the sun through a shadow ray (`[Shaders.Reflections.RT] SunShadows`, default on), or
  indoors / at night the lamp that lights the hit most through one (`HitLamps`, reflecting surfaces under roughness 0.25); the other 11
  lamps and the flashlight unshadowed, the ambient from the light grid at the hit. `Rt/ReflectionsRTBody.glsl` ShadeHit.

### Idle targets (2026-11-12)
With `[Main.Main.Performance] TargetReleaseSeconds` above 0 (default 20) the texture manager keeps 1 x 1 stand-ins in place of this effect's render targets while it is off (not made at start-up when it starts off; released that many seconds after it is switched off; made again, zero filled, before the frame draws when it is switched on). Which targets and why that is safe here: the four screen buffers. The water and env-map game shaders declare TESR_ReflectionsBuffer and read it only behind TESR_RTWaterData.x / TESR_ReflReplace.x (0 while off); their cached pointer is cleared at the swap. Not the cube maps, the RT images or the material buffer (made by hand). A new reader or writer of these targets outside the effect's own switch, or a copy of their pointers kept past a frame, breaks this: then answer false in `TargetParkable` (the header). See `core/TextureManager.h`.

### The wet floors from DeferredWetness's film; specular anti-aliasing (2026-11-20, deferred wetness step 3; NOT yet run in a game)
- With `[Main.Main.Quality] DeferredWetness` on (test only, off) and a film over the whole screen this frame (`DeferredWetness::FilmWhole()`: its
  fallback before the Wetness Effects screen pass drew, after its pass in the sun pass), the Vulkan passes take a floor's water from the film (set 1 binding 28 / slot 27
  `t_film`, `cb.film`): wet share = saturate(the film's gloss / MaxRainWetness) x RainWetness (outdoors), the wet
  mirror's blur = the film's roughness held between WetRoughness and 0.3 (2026-11-21: was WetRoughness on every wet pixel, which made damp
  dirt a sharp, speckled mirror through its bumps; standing water stays at WetRoughness, damp ground blurred under the 0.35 hand-over). The film decides where the water is (rain cover, drying, puddles); the rain
  guess (TESR_ReflectionsData.y), the puddle field and the material buffer's puddle mask are not used then (`Reflections.glsl` FilmWetness).
  Without a whole film this frame: as before. The D3D9 technique is unchanged. (The first build took the film's own roughness, 0.61 on damp
  ground and the Puddles Roughness in puddles: past the trace's 0.35 grid hand-over, no reflection showed; owner 2026-11-20.)
- Wet pixels lean to the smooth normal (`ViewNormal` / `BumpKeep`; set 1 binding 29 / slot 28 `t_smoothNormals` = TESR_NormalsBuffer while
  t_normals holds the bumped normals). Why: the owner's second look (2026-11-20), wet reflections extremely noisy with DeferredBumpedNormals on.
  (2026-11-20, unified reflections, the owner's first look at step 1: "reflections dont seem to be using the normal bump at all") Standing
  water is flat in the G-buffer already (DeferredWetness's flatten), so the lean is for damp ground only and partial: at the film's full wet
  share the bump keeps 0 % (DampBumpLean 1; 2026-11-21 owner's choice "Smooth-normal ray": bump-bent rays speckled damp dirt in low sun
  and the cube alternative lost the streets' crisp mirror, docs/DESIGN.md "Reflection speckle"; before: 100 % on 2026-11-20, 20 %, 0 %). The
  puddles stay flat: that is DeferredWetness's own flatten of the G-buffer's shading normal under standing water (with Puddles compiled in),
  not this lean.
- The bump's fade (`BumpKeep`, `cb.film.z`: the normals are the normal-mapped shading normals, DeferredBumpedNormals, DeferredReflections or
  UnifiedReflections): the reflection follows the whole bump while a full-resolution pixel covers up to 0.5 world units, the smooth normal from
  2.0 (at 1440p about 12 m and 47 m), smoothstep between: bumps bend the ray where they are drawn larger than a pixel and do not alias into
  grain where they are not. (2026-11-27) A bend under the DXT normal maps' offset is left out too (BumpCodecFloor, the section at the top).
- `NormalVarianceRoughness` (`cb.film.z`): the roughness is widened by how much the SMOOTH normal changes to the right and lower full-resolution
  neighbour (alpha^2 + min(2 x 1/(2 pi) x |dn|^2, 0.015 = 0.35^4: never past the trace's hand-over by itself), Kaplanyan / Tokuyoshi; a depth jump over 2 % not counted), after Mirror, not on water
  or the weapon: the geometry's own curvature. (2026-11-20 step 3 measured the bumped normal with kappa 0.18: every visible bump became
  roughness, about 0.5-0.65, past the trace's 0.35 hand-over and fully blurred: the owner saw no bumps at all. The bump's aliasing is the
  fade above now.)
- DebugView 12 (Vulkan passes): red the bend from the smooth normal (full at 20 degrees), green the final roughness, on reflecting pixels.
- What wrong looks like now: grain back on wet dirt, gravel or cobbles near the camera (raise DampBumpLean again; or the footprint
  numbers), or bumps that still do not show near the camera (DebugView 12: red should follow the tiles' and cobbles' edges up close).
- Look: puddles mirror at WetRoughness, damp ground a soft blurred sheen; bumpy wet ground (DeferredBumpedNormals on) softer by its bumps. Wrong: puddles that do not
  mirror at all in rain (the film not bound: the log's DeferredWetness lines), or grain back on bumpy ground with DeferredBumpedNormals on.
- Shader files: all eleven Reflections*.spv that include Reflections.glsl were rebuilt (six use the film: Trace, TraceHiZ, Blur, Temporal,
  RayGen, Composite; the other five only declare it). The "Reflections shader files" log line's hash table is older than these (it says OLD).

### The material from the G-buffer: UnifiedReflections (2026-11-20, unified reflections step 1; NOT yet run in a game)
`[Main.Main.Quality] UnifiedReflections` (live, test only, off, hidden in the menu's Internal list; docs/DESIGN.md "Unified reflections design" step 1).
With `DeferredRenderer = 2` and this frame's G-buffer (COLOR1-3) the passes read each pixel's material from the G-buffer itself
(`Compute/Reflections.glsl` GBufferMaterial, the .fx the same) instead of MaterialAware's copy, which is not made then (no technique 7, no
DeferredReflections / MaterialBufferRoughness merge; still made while the RT weapon rays run, `MaterialCopySkipped`: the weapon's material is
the copy's). One frame decides once (`ReflectionsEffect::GBufferFrame`), so the copy step and every pass agree.
- **Material**: COLOR2 decoded into the copy's old layout, so the rules after it are unchanged: roughness = b (-1 none; the path tracer's
  -3..-2 code decoded back), metal = r's fraction / 0.49, model and specular bit from r. Objects, worn gear and cloth with the specular bit
  (models 1-3: what the game lights with a highlight) reflect whatever way they face, by MaterialReflect, fading out from 60 % of
  MaterialMaxRoughness to it (`MaterialReflectivity`, as an _rmaos surface did); everything else written is a known material for the floor rule
  (its own roughness in place of the Look's); plants, skin, grass, hair and eyes count as rough. Metal's colour (the composite's F0) is
  COLOR1's base colour (linear), not the lit colour's hue; `ReflMetalF0`'s silver rule for mirror-smooth metal stays. Dielectrics 0.04 (Mirror
  as before).
- **No record**: COLOR1 a < 0.5, COLOR2 r <= 0.5, or COLOR3's camera distance not the depth buffer's (the CapturedNormals readers' rule,
  kShadingNormalsPS's share and units): something the G-buffer does not hold is in front (the game's own shaders, water, glass, grass, the
  first person). Those pixels take the floor rule with the Look's roughness, as a pixel without material data always did; the water, the
  glass mask and the weapon are decided before any of this, as before (the weapon keeps MaterialAware's MaterialAt, the copy made for it).
- **Normals**: the shading normals in every frame it is used (FdIndirect ShadingNormals, as DeferredBumpedNormals; "on but not used" leaves
  them as they were), so the Vulkan passes' bump fade and specular AA (2026-11-20: see the film section: bumps bend the ray near the camera)
  (NormalVarianceRoughness) and the wet pixels' lean to the smooth normal (binding 29) run; ray starts and AboveSurface as before.
- **Coverage**: today's floors plus the specular objects above. Probe-only on every surface is step 3.
- **Back ends**: the Vulkan passes follow the switch live (bindings 30 `t_gbAlbedo` = COLOR1 and 31 `t_gbNormals` = COLOR3, slots 29 / 30;
  COLOR2 on t_matbuf; `cb.gbuffer`: x on, yz the distance match). The D3D9 technique only when the switch is on at startup
  (`REFL_GBUFFER_ON`, core/FdIndirect.h UnifiedCompiledIn): COLOR2 on stage 15 in the copy's place, COLOR3 on 8 and COLOR1 on 9 (the capture
  cubes' stages: only the Capture and Filter techniques read them, drawn before BindMaterial), `TESR_ReflectionsGBuffer`. Turned on later on
  native D3D9 it is not used (the log says why) and MaterialAware stays. The D3D9 technique still has no film and no specular AA (step 2).
  Known gap (failure paths only): with the switch turned on live and the D3D9 technique not compiled with it, a Vulkan pass that fails for
  one technique (a pipeline refused, a missing texture) falls back to D3D9 with no material at all for that frame (the copy was skipped).
  Believed, not checked: D3DX applies the aliased samplers' own POINT sampler_state at BeginPass, over the cubes' states SetCT gave 8 / 9.
- **Off**: the D3D9 effect's disassembly is identical to before (checked offline, plain and MaterialAware builds); the Vulkan passes skip the
  new code (gbuffer.x 0). All twelve Reflections*.spv were rebuilt (new bindings and uniform).
- Menu: while the switch is on in mode 2, `[Main.Main.Quality] DeferredReflections` and `[Shaders.Reflections.Main] MaterialAware`,
  `MaterialBufferRoughness` are hidden (ImGuiManager.cpp UnifiedReflectionsHides; the keys keep working from the file). DeferredBumpedNormals
  stays: it also bumps Wetness Effects' wet reflections and WetWorld's surface.
- Log: `UNOFFICIAL Reflections UnifiedReflections: on (the Vulkan passes): ...`, `... on but not used now: <why>`, `... off ...` (a line when
  the answer changes). DebugView 7 shows the G-buffer's material as decoded (dark red rough / blue smooth / green-yellow metal: reflects any
  way; grey-white + green: the floor rule's known materials; dimmed: no record).
- (2026-11-20, step 2) In a frame `WetReach` says so, the wet floors' reflection is this effect's alone: DeferredWetness drops its sky term on
  those floors and Wetness Effects' screen pass its trace (its lamp streaks stay); the D3D9 technique got the film, the bump fade, the damp lean
  and the curvature AA. docs/DESIGN.md "Unified reflections design" "Step 2 as built".
- What correct looks like: polished metal props, chrome, glossy counters and smooth objects reflect the room by their own roughness (sharp on
  chrome, blurred on brushed metal), whatever way they face; gold / brass reflect in their own colour, mirror-smooth metal in silver; tiled
  floors and wet ground as with the switch off. Wrong: a person, plant or post in front of a shiny surface turning into a mirror (the record
  check failing), matte walls and rocks mirroring (a specular bit on matte records: DebugView 7 shows them blue), metal reflecting in the
  lamps' colour (COLOR1 not bound), floors that reflected before going dull.

### Damp ground reads the cube; only standing water is traced (2026-11-21; owner: "Yes, try it"; NOT yet looked at in game)
- Why: the owner's save `reflection01` (sunny after rain, install-test18): damp dirt's reflection broke into white speckles at mid distance.
  A ray bent by one pixel's bump at a low angle flips between bright sky and dark ground block by block; no blur averages that
  (`docs/DESIGN.md "Reflection speckle"`, on test-p1a-bump). Community Shaders never traces wet ground: damp ground reads a cube pre-blurred by roughness.
- What (Vulkan passes only; the D3D9 technique and the RT passes are unchanged): `Reflections.glsl` FilmWetness splits the film into standing
  water and damp ground by its roughness: standing water's is the running Puddles Roughness (`cb.film2.y`: TESR_PuddleLook.x while
  TESR_PuddleData.w is on, else 0.05 for Wetness Effects' own puddles), damp ground's is rougher (lerp(1, 0.22, rain): 0.61 at
  MaxRainWetness 0.5); the film's roughness is lerp(damp, standing, the standing share), so the share is read back with open flat
  ground's damp roughness now (`cb.film2.z` = lerp(1, 0.22, saturate(wetness x MaxRainWetness)), C++ from Wetness Effects' constants)
  and FilmDamp = 1 - smoothstep(0.25, 0.6, share): a puddle's soft edge stays traced (the first build split by roughness alone and put
  every puddle edge on the cube: slot A/B 2026-11-21, the bush's reflection lost). Without Puddles: by roughness as before. Only BUMPY
  damp ground goes to the cube (the same A/B: the Strip's wet asphalt lost its mirror): the bumped normal's mean bend from the smooth one
  over five taps two pixels apart, smoothstep(0.01, 0.04) on 1 - cos (about 8 to 16 degrees); without bumped normals none. Damp: the
  roughness is the film's own; standing: as before (WetRoughness .. 0.3). `SurfaceDamp` = the damp share of the pixel's reflection.
  `ReflectionsTrace.frag` / `ReflectionsTraceHiZ.frag`: fully damp pixels skip the march and the random spread (H = N) and take the cube,
  the light grid and the sky; partly damp ones scale the screen share by (1 - damp).
- Lights (2026-11-21, owner: "pointlights isn't reflecting anymore"): the cube's blur smeared fires and lamps away, so damp ground
  still marches along its mirror direction (no spread) and takes a hit only where it is 3-8 x brighter than the fallbacks' colour
  (lamps, fires, neon); dark hits and misses never replace the cube. The firefly cap for that share is the 0.3-rough one, as traced.
  The blur passes still blur it at the film's roughness: a soft glow rather than a sharp streak (believed). The march costs as before.
- Strength: at roughness 0.61 the composite's split-sum Fresnel is about a quarter of 0.3's at 5 degrees and a third at 10 (0.086 vs
  0.378 / 0.264): the owner's test10 look ("no reflections"). `ReflectionsComposite.frag` takes the damp share's Fresnel at most at
  DampMaxRoughness 0.3, so damp ground keeps the traced version's strength; its picture is the blurred cube (not a mirror).
- 2026-11-21, after the slot A/B (cube: puddle edges and the Strip's crisp road mirror lost; "only bumpy" rule: Strip asphalt bends more
  than Goodsprings dirt, so it put almost nothing on the cube), owner's choice "Smooth-normal ray": `DampTrace` now defaults to TRUE and
  wet ground's reflection follows the smooth normal (DampBumpLean 1, unified branch). The cube path below stays for `DampTrace = false`.
- Off: `[Shaders.Reflections.Main] DampTrace = true` (live, hidden unless ShowInternal) traces damp ground as before; the cube path is also
  off without the cube (CubeMap off, underwater), where damp ground would have only the sky.
- Known gaps (review 2026-11-21): RT on: RayGen / RTResolve do not know the damp share; damp ground's roughness 0.61 is past RT MaxRoughness
  0.5, so it gets no RT rays and is screen-marched with the wider spread (noisier than before). Partly damp pixels (a dry share from
  GroundSheen or MaterialAware) still march. A Puddles Roughness of 0.2 or more counts heavy-rain damp ground as standing (traced as
  before); Puddles Quality 1 (no rim) puts the shore band on the cube. The first seconds after a load or a cell change the cube is still
  filling: damp ground shows mostly the sky then. The flashlight highlight on damp ground is wider (GGX at 0.61), the firefly cap lower (3).
- Look: damp ground a soft, steady sheen of the surroundings and sky (no speckles); puddles still sharp mirrors. Wrong: damp ground that no
  longer reads wet (compare `DampTrace = true`), or a milky wash at low angles in bright weather (believed possible; SpecularOcclusion dims it).
- Cost: believed cheaper (fewer pixels marched); not measured.


### Upstream 1.1.1-1.1.3 merge (2026-11-26)
- **Wet surfaces & puddles (upstream 1.1.1, as merged into this fork 2026-11-22; branch `upstream-rt-merge`)**: two sources of the
  wet film, one at a time:

    `Reflections.fx.hlsl` (`REFL_GBUFFER_ON`) read it, with `DampTrace`, `DampBumpLean`, the Fresnel cap (`DampMaxRoughness`,
    `ReflectionsComposite.frag fresnelRough`) and `WetReach`. In such a frame the C++ zeroes `wetFx.x` / `TESR_ReflectionsWetFx.x`,
    so upstream's forward film below does not add a second wetness.
  - **Upstream's forward film** (every other frame): `Reflections` drives wet-film gloss/roughness from `WetnessEffects`' constants
    (`ComputeWetFxFilm` / `ComputeWetFxFilmFx`: rain, `PuddleStanding`, `OpenAbove`), wet walls included, and subtracts the forward
    `WetSkyReflection` under both `SkylightingMode = 0` (SH) and `1` (directional sky). In the D3D9 technique only the variant without
    `REFL_GBUFFER_ON` has it.

    first-person exclusion (this fork keeps its own `WetnessReflections`, off by default since settings step 96 (91 before the merge), which splits the floors
    with `Reflections` by `WetReach`); `WetWorld::ReflectionsOwnPuddles` (WetWorld was removed 2026-11-21).
  - Bindings: upstream's composite-only `t_albedo` / `t_capNormals` (bindings 28 / 29) collided with this fork's film and smooth
    normals; the composite reads the same textures through `t_gbAlbedo` / `t_gbNormals` (30 / 31), bound when either reader wants them.
  - Upstream's forward film itself is behind `[Shaders.Reflections.Main] ForwardWetFilm` (hidden, false here): with this fork's
    MaxRainWetness 0.5 its damp roughness (0.5-0.61) is past the trace's 0.35 hand-over, so wet open ground would lose its mirror.

### Upstream 1.1.5 merge (2026-11-27, branch `upstream-115-port`)
Taken from upstream; the seams are in `docs/DESIGN.md "Upstream 1.1.5 merge"`. Reflections-side facts:

## ShadowsExteriors

*README title: ShadowsExteriors - effect spec*

Scope: this spec covers the final apply pass only, the one that multiplies the scene by the sun-shadow term. The sun cascade maps, the contact-shadow march and the point-light passes are producers that write into the buffer this pass reads. They are described only as far as this pass depends on them. Their own specs are separate.

### 1. Purpose

Outdoors, the sun's shadow term is stored in a screen-sized shadow buffer, one value per pixel. This pass reads the finished frame and darkens each pixel by that term, so that surfaces in the sun's shadow lose light and take on the sky colour. Where the player is lit by the sun, the pass leaves the picture as it was. The visible result is the ordinary sunlit-versus-shadowed contrast on land, buildings and actors, with shadowed areas tinted toward the sky colour rather than going flat grey. The strength of the darkening is set by the Darkness setting.

### 2. When it runs

- Frame position: in the post-process chain, after the lighting and before the fog and the later post-process effects. In `ShaderManager.cpp` the call is made in the block that handles the shadow pass, and only when `GameState.isExterior` is true. The call is `Effects.ShadowsExteriors->Render(...)` with technique index 0, into the chain's rendered surface, with the source surface passed through.
- It is skipped when the composite path already applied the shadow (the `composite && shadowApplies` case), when a pair-apply or a shadow-into-AmbientLight path is in use. In those cases the shadow is applied elsewhere and this pass does not run.
- Enable conditions, all of which must hold for the effect to draw:
  - `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Status] Enabled` is true (the effect's own enable flag, loaded through the generic effect loader). Note: `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] Enabled` is a separate key that controls whether the sun shadow maps are rendered at all. The apply reads it indirectly through the constants (see section 3).
  - The game is in an exterior cell. Interiors use the separate ShadowsInteriors path.
  - `ShouldRender()` passes. This requires either that the SunShadows effect is enabled and loaded, or that the frame is exterior. When SunShadows is not running, the pass is skipped and a one-time log line is written. The reason: without SunShadows the red channel of the shadow buffer holds only the point-light term (about zero outdoors), and the apply would darken the whole image by Darkness.
- Night: no separate on/off switch. At night the sun factor fades (see section 6), and the darkness floor used at night is the Main setting `NightMinDarkness`, which the sun-shadow producer folds into the darkness constant.
- Menus: not specially handled in this pass. The Render call is driven by the frame, and the menu case was not traced further (see section 9).
- Vulkan: when the Vulkan pass path is chosen the effect's `ExtRender` is used instead of the D3D9 technique (see section 7). The Vulkan path is not used while a render rect is active (`DxvkCompute::PassesOn` returns false in rect frames), so in those frames the D3D9 technique runs.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (D3D9 sampler registers, declaration order)

The fx file declares five samplers. The register numbers below are the ones written in the source and are the slot order Vegas Shaders binds by declaration.

| Slot | Name | Holds | Filter / address |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | `TESR_RenderedBuffer` | the frame as the chain has it now (linear light, gamma encoded as the game stores it) | LINEAR, CLAMP |
| s1 | `TESR_DepthBuffer` | linear depth of the frame, normalised by the far plane (multiply by TESR_CameraData.y to get view-space distance) | LINEAR minification, ANISOTROPIC min filter (as declared), CLAMP; the pass only uses explicit level 0 reads |
| s2 | `TESR_PointShadowBuffer` | the shadow buffer. Red: sun shadow factor (0 = in shadow, 1 = lit) from the sun producer; green: point-light term. Two channels in G16R16 (declared format of the buffer, see below) | LINEAR, CLAMP |
| s3 | `TESR_NormalsBuffer` | world-space-agnostic normals in view space, encoded to 0..1 (decode as value times 2 minus 1) | LINEAR, CLAMP |
| s4 | `TESR_SunFarBuffer` | a per-pixel sun factor colour from the SunFar pass (land and cloud shadows far past the cascades). Read only when SunFarApply.x is 1 | POINT, CLAMP, no mips |

The buffer the sampler s2 names is created by the shadow-buffer setup in `ShadowsExterior.cpp` at the frame's width and height, format G16R16 (the `TESR_PointShadowBuffer` init). It is cleared to red 1 / green 0 (opaque white in red) before the producers write into it.

Note on the Vulkan binding: the Vulkan pass uses a different numeric order (rendered = binding 0, sun-far = 1, point shadow = 2, depth = 3, normals = 4, all in set 1). Only the names and meanings matter for a new implementation. The numbers do not have to match the D3D9 registers.

#### Constants (names, registers and meanings)

The C++ fills these by name each draw from the shared constant table. The fx file declares them as float4 unless noted. Registers are not fixed in the source for these; the D3D9 path binds them by name.

- `TESR_ReciprocalResolution` (declared, not used by this pass).
- `TESR_WaterSettings` (float4): x = water height in the current cell (world units), y = water depth darkness (not used here), z = 1 when the camera is underwater, else 0. Only z and x are read by this pass, and only for the water-plane exception.
- `TESR_ShadowData` (float4): x = Quality (0 to 4), y = Darkness (the Main `Darkness` setting, after the night floor is applied by the producer), z = near map resolution, w = far map resolution. This pass reads only y. The darkness used in the shader is `max(0, 1 - y)`.
- `TESR_ShadowFade` (float4): x = fade of the sun shadows toward light (0 = full shadow, 1 = fully faded out; at sunrise and sunset the producer raises it; at night with the moon up it is raised toward the moon's visibility), y = 1 when the exterior shadow pass is enabled and on, z = 1 when point-light shadows are on (outdoors this follows UsePointShadowsDay / UsePointShadowsNight), w = the shadow map's far distance (not read here). This pass reads x and z.
- `TESR_SkyColor` (float4): rgb = sky colour (gamma-encoded), w = its alpha, passed through.
- `TESR_SunAmbient` (float4): rgb = the sun ambient colour; only its luminance is read.
- `TESR_SunFarApply` (float4): x = 1 when the SunFar pass wrote its factor this frame, else 0 (read as saturate(x)).
- `TESR_CameraPosition` (float4): xyz = camera position in world units (the fields this pass reads are only in the underwater branch).
- `TESR_CameraData` (float4): y = the far plane distance, used to convert normalised depth to view distance.
- `TESR_ViewTransform` (matrix): the view matrix. Used only in the underwater branch, to turn the normal from view to world space in a way that the test expects.
- `TESR_ProjectionTransform` (matrix): entries [0][0] and [1][1] are used to rebuild a view ray from a pixel coordinate in the underwater branch.

Constants declared in the fx file but not used by this pass's arithmetic: `TESR_ReciprocalResolution`, `TESR_ShadowScreenSpaceData` (declared in the file, not referenced in the shader body). A new implementation does not need them.

#### Helper behaviour that the pass uses

- Linearisation of the frame and the sky colour uses a gamma of 2.2 (plain power, not sRGB).
- `luma` is the Rec.709 luminance of an RGB triple (weights 0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722).

### 4. Settings

Every key read by this pass or by its producer that changes what this pass draws. The C++ reads them under the sections shown.

- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Status] Enabled` (bool, default true): whether the effect runs at all.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] Enabled` (bool, default true): sun shadows rendered from shadow maps. When false, the producer does not write the sun term, and the pass is skipped by `ShouldRender` (the SunShadows check above). Visible change: no outdoor sun shadows.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] Darkness` (float, default 0.75): how dark a fully shadowed area becomes. Passed as the `y` of `TESR_ShadowData`. Visible change: deeper or lighter shadows (0 is no darkening; 1 is full black at the shadow floor).
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] NightMinDarkness` (float, default 0.35): darkness floor used under the dark moon. Set to 0 to make shadows vanish when there is no moon. Read by the producer into the sun fade and darkness; visible change at night only.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] UsePointShadowsDay` (bool, default false) and `UsePointShadowsNight` (bool, default false): whether point-light (lamp) shadows are part of the shadow buffer's green channel outdoors by day or by night. These set `TESR_ShadowFade.z`. Visible change: lamp shadows outdoors, and a slight whole-screen darkening that the setting's comment mentions.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] Quality` (int 0 to 4, default 4): preset that sets the sun shadow map distance and resolution. Passed as the `x` of `TESR_ShadowData`. This pass does not branch on it.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] SkipWhenFaded` (bool, default true): when on, near sunrise and sunset the producers skip work that nothing reads. This pass still runs, because the fade is 1 and the result is the same.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] ForwardShadows` (bool, default true): whether the sun shadow is also evaluated in the object lighting shaders. Changes which path writes the sun term, not this pass's arithmetic. Compile-time: starting with it false removes the forward path entirely.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] Enabled`, `TwoSidedCasters`, `FitToDepth`, `SunCascade*`, `OverheadSunFix`, `SmoothCharacterShadows`, `CharacterShadowSoftness`, `CharacterShadowReach` and the `CharacterShadowMap*` keys under `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.ShadowMaps]`: these control the sun producers, not this pass. They change the shadow buffer's content only.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.ScreenSpace] Enabled`, `Intensity`, `NearFade`, `Sharp`, `BlurRadius`, `RenderDistance`: contact-shadow producer settings. They change the content of the sun buffer's red channel, not this pass's arithmetic.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.RT] Enabled` (bool, default false) and its keys (`SunAngle`, `Denoise`, `Mode` and others): an optional ray-traced sun term that is combined into the sun buffer by the producer. Not read by this pass.
  Since 2026-11-07 `ShadowDenoiser` (bool, default true, live) denoises that term in the RT helper (one ray over the sun's disc, SunAngle 0 =
  the real 0.53 degrees; a penumbra-sized blur: sharp at contact, softer away) and `Denoise` / `Penumbra` stand aside while it runs; its
  `Denoiser*` keys tune it. docs/next/RT-DENOISER.md (not in this repo). Still not read by this pass.
  Since 2026-11-07 (perf-history, docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) "perf-history") `DenoiserHistory` and `DenoiserStabilize` are frames AT 60
  FPS: with `[Main.Main.RayTracing] DenoiseTimeBased` (default on) the helper gets the same seconds at the game's frame rate
  (RtShadowDenoise::TimeScale; 32 -> ~11 frames at 20 fps). At 60 fps nothing changes.
  Since 2026-11-07 (rtgap-tess, docs/next/RT-DETAIL-GAPS.md (not in this repo) section 1) `ContactShadows` (0-1, default 1, live) and `ContactLength`
  (pixels, default 24): a screen-space contact march in the traced sun term for what the depth buffer has and the RT scene lacks
  (tessellated bumps, grass, small props). Mode 1 only; where this pass's own contact shadows (`[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.ScreenSpace]`)
  draw, the traced march fades out over their fade-in band (TESR_ShadowScreenSpaceFade, NearFade pushed to the bumps' FadeEnd by
  `DisplaceContactFade`), so a pixel never gets both. With the bumps on, the traced rays also start the bumps' Strength further off
  the surface (`[Main.Main.RayTracing] TessStartSlack`). Not read by this pass.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Ortho]` and `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.TerrainShadows]`: producer settings only.
- `[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.SunSmoothing]`: producer settings only (quantised sun direction and cross-fade).
- Interior settings under `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.*]` do not affect this pass.

### 5. Passes and outputs

- Technique: one technique with no name in the D3D9 fx file, index 0. Requested by the C++ with technique index 0 (any other index is refused). One pass: a vertex shader that passes the full-screen quad through (version 3.0), and a pixel shader named `Shadow` (version 3.0).
- Output: one colour target, the chain's spare or current target in the frame chain. Output format is the chain's format (the frame's render target, not a dedicated buffer). The result is written as RGB with alpha 1.
- The pass writes every pixel of the target. No pre-clear is needed.
- Vulkan: one graphics pass (see section 7), same output target and format.
- No other render target is produced by this effect. It does not write the shadow buffer, the depth buffer or the normals buffer.

### 6. Behaviour details

Each rule below is needed to match the current look.

1. Sun factor. The red channel of the shadow buffer is first faded toward 1 by `TESR_ShadowFade.x`: at full fade the sun term has no effect. Then it is pushed toward the ambient level (the sun term is scaled to the luminance of the sun ambient colour, multiplied by the darkness and by `ShadowFade.z`), so that the brightest areas sit at ambient and the point-light attenuation can add light on top.
2. Point light combination. The green channel (point-light term) is added to the red channel after step 1. The sum is then mapped into the range from the darkness floor to 1, so that a pixel with no sun and no lamp lands exactly on the darkness floor, and a pixel in full sun with no lamp lands on 1.
3. Clamp. The result is saturated to 0..1 before use.
4. Shadow colour. The frame is linearised with gamma 2.2. The sky colour is linearised the same way. Two shadowed candidates are computed: the frame's luminance times the shadow factor times the linear sky colour (a tint), and the linear frame times the shadow factor (a plain darkening). They are blended with a weight of saturate(shadow factor plus 0.5), so that as the shadow factor falls the result moves toward the tint. The blend is then delinearised with gamma 1/2.2. Alpha is set to 1.
5. Water exception. When the camera is underwater (`TESR_WaterSettings.z` equals 1), the pass reads the depth and normal at the pixel and, if the point's world height is within 2 units of the water height (`TESR_WaterSettings.x`) and its world normal points along the negative Z axis with a dot product above 0.999, the frame is returned unchanged. This keeps the water plane from being darkened by shadows.
6. SunFar. Before anything else, the frame is multiplied per channel by the SunFar factor (read through POINT sampling, no mip). The multiply is applied as a lerp from 1, weighted by saturate of `TESR_SunFarApply.x`. When the SunFar pass did not run this frame (x is 0) the factor is 1 and nothing changes.
7. Sky and depth. The pass does not test for sky pixels. Sky pixels get the sun term the producer wrote for them (the producer writes the buffer's sky value; not traced here). A new implementation should treat sky as "no sun shadow" unless the producer says otherwise, and must check the sky behaviour against the producer's spec.
8. First-person weapon. The pass does not special-case the first-person weapon. The weapon is in the rendered frame like any other object, so it is darkened by the same term as the scene at its position. Any exemption lives in the producers.
9. Render-scale and rect frames. The D3D9 technique works on whatever target it is given, so it follows the frame's size. The Vulkan pass is turned off in render-rect frames, so the D3D9 path handles them.
10. Jitter (TAA). None in this pass. The sampling uses the pixel's own coordinate, with no sub-pixel offset.
11. Reversed depth. Not used. The depth is read as linear depth normalised by the far plane, and the pass only uses it in the underwater branch.
12. HDR and LDR. The pass works on the frame as the chain holds it, in gamma-encoded values, and applies gamma 2.2 internally. It makes no HDR-specific decision.
13. NaN and inf guards. The delinearisation takes max(0, x) before the power, so negative values do not become NaN. The shadow factor is saturated before use. No other guard is present in the shader.
14. Frame content that must not darken. The underwater water-plane exception (rule 5), and the case where the sun term is not written (rule "ShouldRender" in section 2). Anything else that the sun term does not cover is darkened by the factor.
15. Look difference from a wrong result. If the sun term is missing, the whole image darkens by Darkness. If the sun term is read as 0 everywhere, the scene looks uniformly dim. If the point-light green term is not added, lamp-lit outdoor areas look dark. If the sky tint is dropped, shadows look flat grey instead of blue-ish.

### 7. Vulkan path

- Vulkan file: `Compute/ShadowsExteriors.frag` (GLSL 4.5, with the shared `Common.glsl` include). The compiled `ShadowsExteriors.spv` sits beside it. There is no variant file for this pass in `Compute/variants.txt`.
- The shader's body mirrors the D3D9 technique line for line. Its helpers reproduce the depth read, the view-ray rebuild and the normal transform.
- Selection: `ShadowsExteriorEffect::ExtRender` builds the pass and runs it through the shared full-screen path. It is used when `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses` is true, the DXVK extension is available (`DxvkCompute::Available`), and no render rect is active (`DxvkCompute::PassesOn`). `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkKeepD3D9` naming "ShadowsExteriors" forces the D3D9 technique for the frame. Otherwise the D3D9 technique runs.
- Fallback: if any required constant or texture is missing, `ExtRender` returns false and the D3D9 path runs for that frame. The pipeline is created once (the first call sets a "tried" flag); if creation fails, the pass stays on the D3D9 path.
- Texture bindings (set 1): binding 0 = rendered buffer, 1 = sun-far buffer, 2 = point-shadow buffer, 3 = depth buffer, 4 = normals buffer. All are sampled images with 2D views, level 0.
- Sampler bindings: sampler slot 0 is LINEAR CLAMP (its heap index is written into the push block at byte offset 192), and sampler slot 1 is POINT CLAMP (byte offset 196). Both are used through the `NVR_SAMPLER` indirection in the shader.
- Push block layout (bytes; total 208, the shader reads the first 200):
  - 0: view transform, HLSL column 0 (four floats)
  - 16: view transform column 1
  - 32: view transform column 2
  - 48: view transform column 3
  - 64: camera position
  - 80: x = projection [0][0], y = projection [1][1], z = far plane (CameraData.y), w unused
  - 96: shadow fade (TESR_ShadowFade)
  - 112: shadow data (TESR_ShadowData)
  - 128: water settings (TESR_WaterSettings)
  - 144: sun-far apply (TESR_SunFarApply)
  - 160: sun ambient (TESR_SunAmbient)
  - 176: sky colour (TESR_SkyColor)
  - 192: linear sampler index (uint)
  - 196: point sampler index (uint)
  - 200 to 207: padding (zero)
- Matrix convention: the view transform is stored in the push block by its HLSL columns, taken from the logical element (row i, column j) of the D3D9 matrix. A new Vulkan version must rebuild the same HLSL-style product from those four columns (a matrix times vector in the shader reads as the HLSL `mul(M, v)`).

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct look: outdoor shadows that are clearly darker than the lit ground, with a bluish or sky-coloured tint in the shadow and no hard black, no whole-screen darkening, no change at night beyond the moon floor, and no darkening of the water plane when the camera is underwater.
- Typical wrong results:
  - The whole frame darkens by Darkness with no shadow shape: the sun term is missing (see ShouldRender).
  - Shadows look flat grey: the sky tint is missing or the luminance term is wrong.
  - Lamp-lit outdoor areas stay dark: the green term is not added.
  - The water surface is darkened when underwater: the exception is missing.
  - Sky becomes dark: the producer's sky value is wrong, not this pass.
- Cost class: a single full-screen per-pixel pass with four to six texture reads (rendered, sun-far, point shadow, and in the underwater case depth and normals) and a few multiplies. Cheap. The underwater branch is coherent and costs extra only in that case.

### 9. Open questions

1. The menu case is not traced beyond the frame call. Whether the pass runs under menus is determined by the frame loop, not by this file.
2. Sky handling: the pass has no sky test. Whether sky pixels hold a value that leaves them unchanged was not traced in the sun producer. Needs a check against the producer's spec.
3. The `TESR_ShadowFade.x` night formula (moon visibility) and the sunrise/sunset smooth-step are computed in the producer. The exact curve was read but not re-derived here.
4. The Vulkan-only switch `NvrDxvkKeepD3D9` is checked by the shared full-screen path. Whether the ExtRender function's own early return on technique index is the only gate was not fully traced.
5. The depth sampler is declared ANISOTROPIC for minification but the pass reads only explicit level 0. Whether the anisotropic filter ever matters was not checked.
6. The HLSL's declared constant `TESR_ReciprocalResolution` is not used by this pass; whether the D3D9 binding writes it anyway was not verified.
7. The exact D3D9 register numbers for the constants (as opposed to the samplers) were not determined, because the C++ binds them by name.

### 10. Rewrite status (2026-11-02, commit: rw3 ShadowsExteriors)

The new shader is src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/ShadowsExteriors.fx.hlsl: one unnamed technique, pass Shadow, as before. Samplers in
declaration order: RenderedBuffer s0, DepthBuffer s1, PointShadowBuffer s2, NormalsBuffer s3, SunFarBuffer s4 (POINT).

Changes from the old behaviour described above:
- Rule 1 (push toward the ambient level by the sun ambient luminance) is NOT implemented: the spec is vague about the formula. The
  sun term is the sun factor faded by ShadowFade.x, plus the lamp term times ShadowFade.z, mapped from the darkness floor to 1.
- Water exception (rule 5): implemented as the spec states it (world normal along -Z, dot above 0.999). A surface facing up would be
  along +Z, so this may need checking in game; left as written.
- Depth and world position for the water test use reconstructWorldPosition (needs TESR_InvProjectionTransform and the inverse view,
  which the core registers; the spec's list did not include them).

Open: the Vulkan frag (Compute/ShadowsExteriors.frag) is not written; the file is not in this worktree.

- Upstream 1.1.4 (merged 2026-11-26): `[Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Deferred] MatchSunDisc` is off by default (settings step 100):
  the deferred sun lights surfaces from vanilla FNV's clamped sun direction again, which keeps the land's normal-map and parallax
  relief around midday; on, it follows the true sun disc (as the sky and the shadow cascades). 1.1.4 also adds a Vulkan compute
  DeferredSun (`Compute/DeferredSun.comp`, behind `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkDeferred`, off here); it stands aside for the
  D3D9 pass in frames where DeferredWetness's film is live (it has no film highlight).

### 11. Sun sheen (deferred sun, 2026-11-26)

Ported by hand from upstream 1.1.5-test4 (only this term; its MaterialSSS, RealSunPath and other 1.1.5 changes are not taken).
`[Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Deferred] SunSheen` (default 0.9, upstream ships 0.0; natural 0.75-1.2, 0 off) and `SunSheenRoughness`
(default 0.78, clamped 0.45-0.95), both live (read with the section's other keys on a settings change, `DeferredSun::SyncSettings`).

- What: the deferred sun pass (`Shaders/DeferredSun.hlsl` and `Compute/DeferredSun.comp`, `EvalSunSheen`, the same maths in both) adds
  to the sun's light of land and object pixels a very broad GGX glaze at a fixed micro-roughness (`SunSheenRoughness`, small grazing
  Fresnel) and a dusty backscatter lift (stronger toward grazing view and with the sun behind the viewer), tinted by a desaturated albedo.
  It is added before the shadow lookup, so it is shadowed and takes the recorded sun share like the sun itself. Not on metal; on records
  with a highlight only past roughness 0.18-0.5; not on skin or foliage pixels.
- Upstream's gates (`DeferredSun.h FillSheenConst`): outdoors only, not the first-person view model, `dayLight` above 0.35 with a
  smoothstep to 0.65.
- Wet gating (ours): a dry glow must not sit on wet ground. The strength is multiplied by `1 - RainState::Factor()` (the rain, or the
  wetness it left), which covers every path, including DeferredWetness off and the compute pass. With DeferredWetness's film live, each
  pixel keeps `1 - smoothstep(0, 0.2, film gloss)` of it (the weight the film's own sun highlight takes: wet ground, puddles, wet objects).
  While Puddles' standing water is out with no film this frame, the land's pixels with roughness under 0.08-0.25 drop it (the land record
  writes the water's gloss into its roughness there).
- Constants: D3D9 `DS_Sheen` c18 (x strength, y roughness, z clearcoat, w pearlescence: upstream 1.1.5's lanes); our land smooth-roughness
  (puddle) gate moved to `DS_SheenGate` c21.x (2026-11-27 merge, upstream 1.1.5). Compute: `cb.dsSheen` (z / w = clearcoat / pearl), 16-byte
  slot 72 (byte 1152; the block is 73 x 16 bytes, `static_assert` in `DeferredSun.h`); the puddle gate is flag bit 23
  (`kFlagSheenPuddleGate`). The sun-fade record keeps sheen and SSS (upstream drops them there); only the fade record with FD_SKIN drops them
  (`DS_FADE_LEAN`, X4505 otherwise). Proven offline in the merge: all sun records compile, flow 0; not run in game.
- Placement: evaluated inside the land and object branches, as upstream. After them (with the wet-film read) the sun-fade record
  (`SUN_CROSSFADE_PASS`, on by default) ran out of temp registers (X4505).
- Cost (offline, `template_compile`, ps_3_0 slots): the sun record 2206 -> 2358, with the film 2270 -> 2453, the sun-fade record 3364 ->
  3512 (with the film 3434 -> 3613); all flow 0 (no X4505). Frame time not measured.

### 12. Screen-Space Shadows (Bottled Shaders port, deferred sun, 2026-11-26)

Owner: "make it fully deffered one to one port of bottle shaders versions". A one-to-one port of Bottled Shaders' Screen-Space Shadows
(github.com/InTheBottle/Bottled-Shaders, a Skyrim Community Shaders fork, GPL-3.0: `src/Features/ScreenSpaceShadows.cpp`,
`features/Screen-Space Shadows/Shaders/ScreenSpaceShadows/RaymarchCS.hlsl` + `bend_sss_gpu.hlsli`), whose raymarch is Bend Studio's
(Copyright 2023 Sony Interactive Entertainment, Apache-2.0; the header is kept in the port). Files: `core/ScreenSpaceShadows.h`,
`Compute/ScreenSpaceShadows.comp` (+ `.spv`), `core/BendDispatch.h` (Bend's `BuildDispatchList`, the same code as theirs token for token).

- What theirs does, and so this: each frame after the depth is complete the mask is cleared to 1 (lit) and, outdoors with the switch on,
  Bend's march toward the sun writes it. Waves of 64 threads each take a line of pixels running toward the sun's projected position
  (up to 8 dispatches, wedges round that point); each thread reads its own depths (two taps across the line, point-read where the step is
  above `BilinearThreshold`) into groupshared memory, then tests `SampleCount` x 60 (at 1080p, by the square root of the area, a multiple
  of 8) depths between it and the sun; a nearer depth within `SurfaceThickness` (relative to the distance to the far depth) shadows it,
  four interleaved minima averaged, `ShadowContrast` applied. The deferred lighting multiplies the SUN's shadow by the mask where N.L >= 0,
  outdoors, at every distance; the ambient is not touched.
- Path: deferred only. The march runs at the end of each deferred span (`Hooks/Render.cpp` DeferredSpan, `ScreenSpaceShadows::Produce`,
  right after `DeferredDepth::AtSpanEnd`) on that span's depth, and `Shaders/DeferredSun.hlsl` multiplies it in (`DS_SSS` c19,
  `TESR_ScreenSpaceShadows` s13; +11 ps_3_0 slots in every record). No forward template changes: forward surfaces (water, glass, hair,
  particles, first person, distant trees) and `DeferredRenderer 0` do not get it.
- Translations (one line each): D3D11 cs_5_0 -> GLSL for the NVR DXVK extension's compute (native D3D9 has no compute: off there);
  `SAMPLE_COUNT` compile define -> push constant (arrays sized for 512); `SampleLevel(PointBorderSampler)` -> `texelFetch`, off the drawn
  rect = FarDepthValue (their border colour 1); the depth pre-pass -> the main opaque list's depth copied at the span's end
  (`DeferredDepth`, which `DeferredRenderer 2` always makes: `SettingForce` forces `DeferredDepth 2`); Far 1 / Near 0 as theirs, swapped for a reversed depth; R8_UNORM -> G16R16F (r); full sky -> outdoors; the sun light ->
  `ShaderConst.SunDir` (the shadow maps' sun); dynamic resolution -> `RenderRect`; their JSON settings ->
  `[Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.SSShadows]` with their defaults and ranges (Enabled true, SampleCount 1 [1-4], SurfaceThickness 0.02
  [0.005-0.05], BilinearThreshold 0.02 [0.02-1], ShadowContrast 1 [0-4]); `Enabled` is live, but the depth copy is made only when it is
  on at start.
- Not taken by: the first-person pass (`DeferredFirstPerson`): the view model is not in the depth the march read (theirs is, through
  their pre-pass). Since the upstream 1.1.5 merge (2026-11-27) the Vulkan sun pass (`DeferredSun.comp`, `NvrDxvkDeferred`, off by default)
  reads the mask too (`t_sssMask` binding 10, flag bit 22) and no longer stands aside for it; it still stands aside while the wet film is live.
  Registers in the D3D9 sun pass after the merge: `DS_SSS` c19 (ours), `DS_MatSSS` c20 (upstream's Material SSS, off), `DS_SheenGate` c21.
  The mask follows upstream's e401def gate (Sun Shadows off with PBR). Believed a frame-time gain (one fewer fallback to the D3D9 pass);
  not measured.
- Overlaps: in a frame where the sun pass took the mask, the old contact pass ([ScreenSpace], over the finished picture, ambient included)
  writes no contact (`EffectChain.cpp`), so the sun is darkened once. The older forward Bend port (Community Shaders', `[ScreenSpace] Bend`, off by
  default, far LOD only) was removed 2026-11-27 at the owner's word; this port is the only Bend shader. RT sun shadows: the mask multiplies the traced shadow too (theirs multiplies whatever sun shadow there is); with
  RT on, its own contact march (`[ShadowsExteriors.RT] ContactShadows`) can add to it near the camera (believed; RT is off by default).
  AO and AmbientLight shade the ambient: no overlap. Lamps: theirs has no lamp version, so none here.
- Look (bench 2026-11-26, tree VS-sss, on vs off live in the same run): outdoors a small, local change (0.2-1.6 % of pixels move by 8+
  levels): faint contact specks on sand, fence pickets a little cleaner (the old pass no longer darkens their ambient); no halos, no
  stray shadows of the tessellated ground bumps at 17:30; indoors (v-saloon) nothing changes. Proven on v-desert-noon and v-SunGI2
  (their hour and 17:30) and v-saloon; the depth was reversed there (handled).
- Cost: the march's own GPU timer ("Screen-space shadows (compute)") 0.48-0.53 ms a frame at 1920x1080, SampleCount 1 (64 samples a
  ray, 2-3 dispatches), on the bench's GTX 1070 under the DXVK fork; 0.52-0.53 ms in the last half-minute when no other game ran. The
  whole-frame difference was not measured cleanly (another room's slot games ran during part of the run; the 60 s HEARTBEAT windows
  straddle the on / off phases). Shader: +11 ps_3_0 slots in the deferred sun pass.

### 13. Screen-space shadows for lamps (deferred lamp shadows, 2026-11-27)

Owner: "Yes, add for lamps" and "this is for deffered shadows". The same Bottled Shaders raymarch (Bend's), toward lamps, as part of the
deferred lamp shadows. Files: `core/LampScreenShadows.h` (the choice and the dispatches), `Compute/ScreenSpaceShadowsLamp.comp` (the
sun's shader with one change: it writes one channel of an rgba16f mask, read back and written), the state in `core/ScreenSpaceShadows.h`
(`Lamps`), the reader in `Shaders/DeferredLamps.hlsl` `DLShadow`.
- Settings, next to the sun's in `[Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.SSShadows]`: `Enabled` (sun and lamps), `Lamps` (0-4, default 2),
  `LampFadeStart` 1400, `LampFadeLength` 700 (camera distance, game units); the march's knobs are the sun's.
- Each deferred span's end, after the sun's march on the same depth copy: the lamp table's active lamps (ManyLamps `SceneLamps` /
  `ActiveIndex`) are scored by brightness x the share of the view their reach covers (1 with the camera inside); the top `Lamps` (a lamp
  chosen last frame scores a quarter more, so two equal lamps do not swap) get Bend's march toward their position (clip position with
  w = +-1: rays toward or away from a point), only over their reach's exact screen rectangle (`LampScissor::AxisRange`), one lamp a channel
  of `TESR_LampScreenShadows` (A16B16G16R16F, cleared to 1).
- `DLShadow` (the shadow passes: a pixel's first 16 lamps; not the lighting pass, which has no registers left) multiplies the lamp's
  deferred shadow term by its channel (`DL_SSLamp` c16 = the chosen table indices, `DL_SSL` c15 = on + the distance fade, s13 = the mask),
  faded out by camera distance. +106-112 ps_3_0 slots in the shadow pass, every FIT level and the RT variant compiled offline.
- Off: in the first-person run, while RT lamp shadows run their own contact march (`RtLampShadows::EnabledNow` and
  `[ShadowsInteriors.RT] ContactShadows` > 0), on native D3D9, with the deferred renderer off. The Vulkan lamp pass (`DeferredLamps.comp`,
  `NvrDxvkDeferred`, off by default) stands aside while there is a mask.
- Bench 2026-11-27 (tree VS-lampsss, 4 lamps, on vs off live, 600-frame settle; no other game ran): Prospector Saloon: small contact
  shadows on and under the stools and table legs; Vault 21: faint ones on the railings, chairs and ceiling struts; nothing wrong seen.
  GPU timer "Screen-space shadows, lamps (compute)": 2.5 ms for four lamps filling the Saloon's view, 0.47 ms for four in Vault 21
  (about 0.6 ms a lamp whose light fills the screen): hence 2 lamps by default. Not run: the owner's pipe-on-a-wall spot (no bench save
  for it in the slim slots).

## ShadowsInteriors

REWRITTEN 2026-11-02 (branch claude/rw3-lights): the shaders are new, written from this spec (Effects/ShadowsInteriors.fx.hlsl; Compute/ShadowsInteriorsBlur.frag and ShadowsInteriorsApply.frag). The C++ and the settings are unchanged. Changes from the old file are marked below.

includes it uses), the C++ in effects/ShadowsInteriors.cpp and .h, the Vulkan passes in Compute/ShadowsInteriorsBlur.frag and
Compute/ShadowsInteriorsApply.frag, and the settings in resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml.

### 1. Purpose

Indoors, this effect darkens the parts of the scene that the lamp shadow mask marks as shadowed, so that objects and walls
standing between a lamp and a surface show a soft shadow on it. It takes the shadow term that the point-light shadow passes
have written into the shadow buffer, softens it with a depth-aware blur, and multiplies the scene's brightness down where the
term says "shadow". Where the surface is far from the camera it fades back to fully lit, and the darkening keeps the colour
and saturation of the surface rather than turning it grey. Outdoors this effect does not run; the exterior sun shadows do that job.

### 2. When it runs

- Enabled by the effect's own status record: `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Status]`, key `Enabled`, default `true`. The
  defaults file's comment reads "Post process pointlights shadows in exteriors", but the effect is only applied indoors (see
  below); treat the comment as stale.
- Interiors only. The shadow-apply stage of the frame calls it only when the game is in an interior cell. In exteriors the
  shadow-apply stage calls the exterior shadows instead.
- Frame position: this is the shadow-apply stage of the frame, after the lamp shadow mask has been built. Its position
  relative to the ambient occlusion apply is set by the comments in the shadow-apply block of src/core/ShaderManager.cpp
  (the block that begins with the InteriorShadowFold comment); the exact order of the AO apply was not re-traced. It is skipped and its work is taken over by the fog composite
  only when the InteriorShadowFold setting is on and every fold condition holds (default: the setting is off, so this pass
  runs as its own pass). Several effects that read the scene (SnowAccumulation, BloodFlow, Flashlight, Specular,
  Underwater, BounceLight, Reflections, and AmbientLight when it runs) must see the scene with the darkening already applied,
  so when any of them is going to render this frame the fold is not used.
- Darkening mode depends on the per-object forward lamp shadows (`[Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward]`):
  - Forward off: the darkening is drawn in this pass, blurred (the normal path).
  - Forward on with `KeepDarkening` on: the darkening is drawn in this pass, unblurred when `DarkeningBlur` is off
    ("skip blur" mode: the two blur passes are skipped and the apply reads the mask as it is).
  - Forward on with `KeepDarkening` off: this pass is not drawn at all.
- Menus: the effect has no menu rule of its own. The game-menu cost overlay counts it for interior cells. Whether it runs
  while a menu is open was not traced (see section 9).
- Render-scale rect frames: the D3D9 path restricts its drawing to the drawn part of the frame (see section 6). The Vulkan path
  is switched off whenever a render-scale rect is active.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (D3D9 sampler slots, bound in declaration order)

| Slot | Name | Contents | Filter / address |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | The current scene image (HDR frame) that the darkening is applied to. In the dedicated path it is read untouched, because the blurs never write it. | LINEAR, CLAMP |
| s1 | TESR_SourceBuffer | A copy of the scene taken by the frame chain for the generic path. Declared, sampled by the generic blur passes only, not by the apply. | LINEAR, CLAMP |
| s2 | TESR_DepthBuffer | Depth of the scene, stored as depth divided by the far distance (0 to 1). Multiply by the far distance to get game units. | LINEAR, CLAMP |
| s3 | TESR_PointShadowBuffer | The lamp/sun shadow mask: a two-channel 16-bit-per-channel buffer (G16R16). Only the red channel is read. It is the same texture the exterior shadow effect owns as its shadow pass target. | LINEAR, CLAMP |
| s4 | NVR_InteriorShadowIn | Dedicated path only. The blurred shadow term, bound by the C++ (the mask after the two blurs, or the raw mask in skip-blur mode). Red channel read. | LINEAR, CLAMP, no mip |

Sampler behaviour: all sampled textures are read with linear filtering and clamp addressing. The one-level textures make
mip filtering irrelevant. The generic technique reads depth with mip level 0 explicitly in the blur.

#### Constants (D3D9 names; registers are assigned by the compiler and are not fixed)

| Name | Meaning |
|---|---|
| TESR_WorldTransform | Declared; not read by this effect. |
| TESR_ShadowData | Four floats. Component y = the interior shadow darkness (`Darkness` from `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main]`, passed through the shared shadow-constants block). Components x, z, w are not read by the apply. |
| TESR_ShadowFade | Four floats. y = 1 when interior shadows are enabled, 0 when disabled ("shadows are disabled" means the apply has no darkening at all). w = the draw distance of the shadows in game units (MAXDISTANCE); the blur writes black beyond it and the apply fades the shadow to fully lit between 300 units and this distance. x and z are not read here. |
| TESR_ReciprocalResolution | Texel size: 1 / width in x, 1 / height in y. The blur steps are multiples of it. |
| TESR_CameraData (read by the Vulkan path as the camera block) | y = the far distance of the camera, in game units. Depth in s2 is multiplied by it. |
| TESR_ViewTransform / TESR_ProjectionTransform | The view and projection matrices as the renderer stores them. Used only to turn a screen position into a world-space view direction for the distance fade. The D3D9 path reads the same values through the engine's own constant names. |
| farZ | Used by the D3D9 source as the far distance (same value as TESR_CameraData.y). |

#### Dedicated-path inputs supplied by the C++

- The two scratch targets owned by the sun-shadow effect (two G16R16 textures, same size as the frame). The blurs ping-pong
  between them: mask into scratch A, then A into scratch B.
- Input for the apply pass: the blurred term in s4 (the scratch B result, or the raw mask in skip-blur mode).

### 4. Settings

Every key this effect reads, with where it is read:

| Section | Key | Type | Range | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Status]` | Enabled | bool | on/off | true | Turns the effect on or off. |
| `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main]` | Darkness | float | not stated in the defaults file | 0.65 | Strength of the indoor shadow darkening (the "darkness of shadows"). Reaches the apply as the darkness constant (component y). The apply turns it into a darkening amount through a Reinhard-type curve with a speed of 5, then scales by the shadow-fade enable (section 6). |
| `[Main.Main.Performance]` | NvrDxvkPasses | bool | on/off | true in the defaults file (false in the C++ default) | When on and running on Vegas Shaders DXVK, the dedicated path runs as Vulkan passes. Off (or not on DXVK): the D3D9 dedicated path. |
| `[Main.Main.Performance]` | InteriorShadowFold | bool | on/off | false | When on, the apply moves into the fog composite and this pass is not drawn when the fold conditions hold. |
| `[Main.Main.Performance]` | InteriorShadowFoldRound | int | 0-3 | 0 | Rounding mode used only by the fold. Not this effect's look. |
| `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward]` | KeepDarkening | bool | on/off | not confirmed in the defaults file | Whether the room darkening is drawn at all when per-object lamp shadows are on. |
| `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward]` | DarkeningBlur | bool | on/off | not confirmed | On: the darkening is blurred (full blur path). Off: skip-blur mode. |
| `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward]` | MaskHalfRes | bool | on/off | not confirmed | Half-resolution mask for the room darkening (affects the mask it reads, not the apply). |

Notes: the C++ also reads `[Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main] Enabled` into the interior settings, but no such key is in the
defaults file (see section 9). `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.RT]` (traced lamp shadows) lives in this folder's defaults fragment but is
read by `src/core/RtLampShadows.h`, not by this effect: docs/next/FORK-RT-LAMPS.md (not in this repo) (2026-11-07: `SameFrame`, on = this frame's trace
before the world draw, with the helper pass `Compute/Rt/RtLampPrimary.comp`; `MovingCasterHistory` 2 = ManyLights' histories follow
people's moving shadows). The `Darkness` value is shared with the lamp-shadow code that writes the mask.

The ray-traced lamp shadows (`[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.RT]`, core/RtLampShadows.h) feed the per-object lamp shadows, not this pass.
Since 2026-11-07 their `ShadowDenoiser` (default true; a restart to come on, off is live) traces the `DenoiseLamps` lamps that matter
most with one ray at their physical size (`PhysicalLampSize`, `PhysicalLampScale/Min/Max`) and denoises them in the RT helper
(`Denoiser*` keys): docs/next/RT-DENOISER.md (not in this repo). Nothing here changes.
Since 2026-11-07 (perf-history, docs/next/RT-PATH-TRACING.md (not in this repo) "perf-history") `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.RT] DenoiserHistory`,
`DenoiserStabilize` and `DenoiseHistory` (the RtDenoise path) are frames AT 60 FPS: with `[Main.Main.RayTracing] DenoiseTimeBased`
(default on) they are kept as the same seconds at the game's frame rate. At 60 fps nothing changes.
Since 2026-11-07 (rtgap-tess, docs/next/RT-DETAIL-GAPS.md (not in this repo) section 1) `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.RT] ContactShadows` (0-1, default 1) and
`ContactLength` (pixels, 24) add a screen-space contact march toward each traced lamp (RtLampTrace's variants) for what the depth buffer
has and the RT scene lacks; it stays off while this effect's own lamp contact shadows (`[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main]
ContactShadows` > 0) draw. With the tessellated bumps on the traced rays start the bumps' Strength further off the surface
(`[Main.Main.RayTracing] TessStartSlack`). Nothing here changes.
