Since 2026-11-11 `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.RT] Enabled` switched off while the game runs stops the trace at once (it was read only at
start; `RtLampShadows::EnabledNow`), and on again resumes it in a session that started with it on. `FollowRenderScale` (default false):
with `SameFrame` and RenderScale below 1 the trace before the world, its camera rays and its denoiser run at the world's render size
(`TAAEffect::WorldDrawnSmall`), not the screen's; the readers find the result through the same scaled reprojection the after-the-world
trace of a rect frame always used. Not run in the game yet. Nothing here changes.

### 5. Passes and outputs

#### Generic technique (default name: the unnamed technique, index 0, three passes), D3D9

1. Horizontal depth-aware blur of the shadow term (s3) into a full-resolution HDR-format target. Output: the blurred term
   in red. Offset direction: (1, 0) in texels.
2. Vertical depth-aware blur of the output of pass 1 (s0 is bound to it), into the same kind of target. Offset direction: (0, 1).
3. Apply: reads the scene from TESR_SourceBuffer (s1: the copy the chain makes at the start of the effect), the blurred term from
   TESR_RenderedBuffer (s0: the output of pass 2, which the chain binds to s0 for this pass), and depth (s2). Writes the darkened
   scene to the frame. Output: opaque colour, alpha 1. (Wave 3: the old text had the scene in s0 and no read of s1, which cannot
   hold for a copy-free chain; the scene now comes from s1. Same result.)

The frame chain supplies the full-resolution copy of the scene and clears each target before its pass. Blurred pixels beyond
the draw distance are discarded by the blur, which leaves the target black there (see section 6).

#### Dedicated technique `DedicatedInteriorShadows`, three passes, D3D9

1. Horizontal blur: reads s3 (the raw mask) and depth (s2). Writes scratch A (G16R16). Depth passes through the
   same blur rules as in the generic path.
2. Vertical blur: reads s4 (bound to scratch A) and depth. Writes scratch B (G16R16).
3. Apply: reads the scene (s0, untouched), the blurred term (s4, bound to scratch B), and depth. Writes the frame.

Before each blur pass the C++ clears its target to opaque black. In skip-blur mode the first two passes are skipped and the
apply runs alone, reading the raw mask bound to s4. The apply pass must be the last pass in both modes.

Every pass writes its full target except where the blur deliberately leaves black (beyond the draw distance). The apply
writes every pixel of the frame.

#### Vulkan passes (only the dedicated path has a Vulkan version)

The generic technique has no Vulkan version; it always runs as D3D9.

- Blur pass, file Compute/ShadowsInteriorsBlur.frag: two runs (horizontal, then vertical). Each reads the mask and depth and
  writes one scratch target. Textures: set 1, binding 0 = mask (2D, linear, clamp), binding 1 = depth (2D, linear, clamp).
  Sampler: set-1 sampler slot 0 (linear, clamp), heap index taken from the push block.
- Apply pass, file Compute/ShadowsInteriorsApply.frag: one run, writes the frame through the full-screen path.
  Textures: set 1, binding 0 = scene, binding 1 = blurred mask, binding 2 = depth. Sampler as above.

Output formats: both blur targets are G16R16 scratch targets owned by the sun-shadow effect; the apply writes the frame's
HDR target.

### 6. Behaviour details

- Depth scale: depth samples are read as stored and multiplied by the far distance to get game units. The blur's depth
  threshold scales with the centre depth: the threshold is 3500 game units times (centre depth / far distance), so it grows
  with distance. A tap contributes only if its depth differs from the centre by less than the threshold.
- Blur kernel: 12 taps, symmetric about the centre, sampled at offsets of -6..-1 and +1..+6 texels in the blur direction
  (no tap at zero offset, the centre is added separately). Tap weights, in order from the first tap to the twelfth:
  0.057424882, 0.058107773, 0.061460144, 0.071020611, 0.088092873, 0.106530916, 0.106530916, 0.088092873, 0.071020611,
  0.061460144, 0.058107773, 0.057424882. The centre sample has weight 0.114725602. The result is the weighted average of
  the accepted taps divided by the sum of the accepted weights (the centre is always accepted).
- Blur early exits, which must match the look (they do not change the result):
  - If the centre depth is beyond the draw distance (MAXDISTANCE), the blur returns black with alpha 1. This is what
    the older passes left in their cleared targets.
  - If every tap equals the centre (the largest difference in red or green is below 0.0001), the blur returns the centre
    value without reading depth again. The result is the same as the full blur would give.
- Blur edges with render-scale rects: the D3D9 blur clamps every tap to the drawn part of the frame when a render-scale rect
  is active, so the widest taps at the right and bottom edges stay inside the drawn part. The Vulkan blur does not clamp.
  It is safe only because the Vulkan path is switched off while a rect is active.
- Apply, step by step (the look must match this):
  1. Convert the scene colour from gamma 2.2 to linear.
  2. Compute the world-space view direction for the pixel, and the uniform distance = depth (game units) times the
     length of that direction.
  3. Clamp the shadow sample to 0..1. Fade it toward fully lit with distance: the fade runs from 0 at 300 game units to 1 at
     the draw distance (MAXDISTANCE), linearly and clamped.
  4. Darkness amount: let d = Darkness constant. The curve is 5d / (1 + 5d). Then darkness = clamp(1 - curve * ShadowFade.y, 0, 1).
     With shadows disabled (ShadowFade.y = 0), darkness = 1 and the result is unchanged.
  5. Shadow = clamp(lerp(darkness, 1, faded sample), 0, 1). 0 is full shadow, 1 is full light.
  6. shadowPower = 1 - Shadow.
  7. Colour: Shadow times the colour raised to the power (1 - shadowPower * 0.2), clamped to 0..1. This boosts the gamma of
     the darker parts.
  8. Saturation: mix the colour with its luma by the factor (1 + shadowPower * 0.3). Darker parts keep some colour. (Wave 3 reading: the mix is lerp(luma, colour, 1 + shadowPower * 0.3), which raises saturation in the shadow. The other order would give a factor above 1 that over-desaturates and can go negative, which is the grey look the spec warns about.)
  9. Add back any part of the original colour above 1 (max(original - 1, 0)), so highlights above 1 are not lost.
  10. Convert back to gamma 2.2 encoding. Output alpha 1.
- Sky: depth for sky pixels is at or beyond the far distance, so the blur writes black there and the apply finds no shadow
  beyond the draw distance. The fade is to fully lit, so the sky is not darkened. (This was inferred, not checked, see section 9.)
- First-person weapon and view model: not handled by this effect; the view model is drawn before the frame reaches it.
  Not checked in the source.
- Jitter (TAA): the effect does not apply sub-pixel jitter to its own taps. It samples at the pixel centre of the frame
  it is given.
- Reversed depth: the depth buffer is not reversed here; depth 0 is the camera and 1 is the far distance.
- HDR vs LDR: the scene the apply reads is HDR (linear-ish, values above 1 possible). The apply keeps values above 1
  (step 9), so highlights are not clipped by it.
- NaN and inf guards: none in the apply or blur. Depth is clamped by the fade and by the early exits only.
- Things that look wrong if missed:
  - Dropping the step-9 add-back makes bright lamp highlights go dim where they overlap a shadow.
  - Dropping the saturation step makes shadows grey and flat.
  - Not clearing the blur targets to black before the blur passes leaves stale colour beyond the draw distance.
  - Not using the far-distance fade (300 to MAXDISTANCE) makes shadows linger over the whole room.
  - The skip-blur mode must not run the blur passes (it would blur a mask that was supposed to be sharp).

### 7. Vulkan path

Selection (in the C++):

- The dedicated path is used when its technique and textures are available and the blur and apply targets match the frame
  size and the render target.
- The Vulkan path is tried first only when the Vulkan passes are on: `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses` is on, the
  game runs on Vegas Shaders DXVK (the extension is available), and no render-scale rect is active. In that case the C++ runs the
  two blur passes into the two scratch targets and the apply into the frame.
- If any Vulkan pass cannot be created or fails before the apply is committed, the effect falls back to the D3D9 dedicated
  path for that frame. If it fails in the middle of the D3D9 path, that path falls back to the generic technique. A failure
  of the Vulkan path sets its failed flag, so the session stays on D3D9.
- The generic technique (three passes through the frame chain) has no Vulkan version and always runs as D3D9.
- Wave 3 (2026-11-02): ShadowsInteriorsBlur.frag and ShadowsInteriorsApply.frag are written from the D3D9 dedicated passes with the push offsets above, and both compile with tools/nvr-dxvk/compile-compute.sh (spirv-val passes). Their .spv files are new builds. Not run in the game.

Blur pass push block (Compute/ShadowsInteriorsBlur.frag):

| Offset | Name | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | cameraData | vec4 | y = far distance |
| 16 | reciprocalResolution | vec4 | texel size (x, y) |
| 32 | offsetMask | vec2 | (1, 0) for the horizontal pass, (0, 1) for the vertical pass |
| 40 | maxDistance | float | draw distance (MAXDISTANCE) |
| 44 | samplerIndex | uint | heap index of the LINEAR, CLAMP sampler |

Apply pass push block (Compute/ShadowsInteriorsApply.frag):

| Offset | Name | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | cameraData | vec4 | y = far distance |
| 16 | shadowData | vec4 | y = darkness |
| 32 | shadowFade | vec4 | y = enable (0 or 1), w = draw distance |
| 48 | viewTransform | mat4 | view matrix, stored as the renderer stores it |
| 112 | projectionTransform | mat4 | projection matrix, stored as the renderer stores it |
| 176 | samplerIndex | uint | heap index of the LINEAR, CLAMP sampler |

The C++ push structs also have padding fields after the apply's sampler index. The offsets above are the ones the shader
reads; the padding is not read. The C++ records the sampler's heap index into the blur push at offset 44 and into the apply
push at offset 176, so the sampler is filled in by the extension at those offsets.

Descriptor binding: textures are bound in set 1, binding i, in the order listed in section 5. The sampler is set-1 slot 0
(linear, clamp). Views are 2D.

### 8. Look and cost

- Correct look: indoors, objects and walls that block a lamp leave a soft, darker, slightly desaturated shadow on the surfaces
  behind them. Shadows fade out over the room toward the draw distance. Surfaces right at the camera are darkened fully by
  the shadow term, not made grey. Highlights from lamps are not dimmed by the shadow. No halo or dark edge appears where the
  blur crosses depth discontinuities (the depth threshold keeps the blur from smearing across a wall edge).
- Typical wrong results:
  - Dark halos around objects where the blur averages across a depth edge (the depth threshold is too large or missing).
  - Grey, washed-out shadows (saturation step missing or wrong sign).
  - Shadows that never fade (the 300 to draw-distance fade missing).
  - A visible hard line where the draw distance cuts off (the black beyond the draw distance is not faded by the apply).
  - Shadows darker when the render-scale rect is active (taps not clamped on the D3D9 path).
- Cost class: multi-pass, per-pixel loop. Generic path: two 12-tap blurs (each tap reads the mask and, for the depth
  test, depth) plus the apply, all at full frame resolution. Dedicated path: the same work on 16-bit scratch targets (less
  bandwidth), no copy of the frame. Skip-blur mode: the apply alone, one pass.

### 9. Open questions

- `[Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Main] Enabled`: read by the shadow-exterior code into the interior settings, but the defaults
  file has no such key. Whether it has any effect on this pass was not established.
- Draw-distance and fade values: the value of TESR_ShadowFade.w and TESR_ShadowFade.y indoors depends on the shared shadow
  constants code. The interior branch sets y from the interior enable; w was taken as the exterior MAXDISTANCE value and was
  not confirmed for interiors.
- Darkness range: the defaults file gives 0.65 with no range; the menu may clamp it (not checked).
- Defaults not confirmed in the defaults file: KeepDarkening, DarkeningBlur, MaskHalfRes in `[_Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward]`.
  Which of skip-blur and blurred mode is the shipped default was not established.
- Menus: whether the effect runs while a menu is open, and how its apply interacts with menu-drawn scenes, was not traced.
- Sky handling: the claim that sky pixels hold a depth at or beyond the far distance was inferred from the blur's early
  exit, not checked against the sky pass.
- Blur sampling filter on the depth read: the D3D9 depth reads use mip level 0 explicitly; the filter the shader used for the
  shadow mask in the generic path was not confirmed beyond the sampler declarations.
- Non-forward indoor branch: when forward lamp shadows are off, the pass runs as the normal blurred path; the frame branch
  that handles it (the separate pass and the interior branch of the apply stage) was read but not every call site
  (src/core/ShaderManager.cpp around the interior apply and the shadow-apply branch) was traced to its end.
- Vulkan blur clamping: the Vulkan blur does not clamp taps to a render-scale rect. Safe only while the Vulkan path is gated
  off during rects (as the C++ does now); a change to that gate would need the clamp added.
- Generic path binding: RESOLVED in wave 3 by the shader's design. The chain binds the current image to s0 for each pass
  (EffectRecord::RebindSlotTextures), and the scene copy into s1 happens once at the start of the effect (EffectRecord::Render,
  usesSourceBuffer). So pass 2 reads the blurred term from s0 and the scene from s1.

## Sharpening

UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-04 (mrfury97 fork, branch amd-cas): the shader is now **AMD FidelityFX CAS** (Contrast Adaptive Sharpening), ported from
`ffx_cas.h` (MIT, Copyright (c) 2017-2019 Advanced Micro Devices, Inc.; the notice is in both shader files). The non-scaling filter is used,
with `CAS_BETTER_DIAGONALS` and AMD's default one weight for all channels (green's). The mod's depth falloff is kept. Shaders:
src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Sharpening.fx.hlsl (D3D9) and src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Sharpening.frag (Vulkan); keep the two equal.
The C++ is unchanged. The old shader (a weighted 3x3 average, pushed away by a luma-weighted contrast) is gone.

2026-11-18 (owner decision, branch vs-1.1.0-plus-fork): upstream 1.1.0 had ported this CAS behind a switch, `[Shaders.Sharpening.Main] CAS`
(default false: the old filter, Strength 0.75). That switch is folded in: CAS is the only filter again and the `CAS` key is gone (w of
`TESR_SharpeningData` is always 0). Settings step 90 (`Configuration::UpdateOldDefaults`) moves a saved Strength of 0.75 (the old default,
outdoors and indoors) to 0.5 once, unless the saved `CAS` was true (that player already saw CAS at their Strength), keeps any other saved
Strength (it reads as a CAS sharpness, clamped to 0..1), and removes a saved `CAS` key so it is not reported as unknown. `Enabled` stays off
by default. A Strength of 0 is CAS's lightest setting, not "off" (it was no sharpening with the old filter): use `Enabled`. The menu's
"Sharpening distance" (Offset) is the fade-out distance, not a width.

### 1. Purpose

Sharpening makes near surfaces look crisper. CAS adapts per pixel: it measures how much headroom the pixel has before its 3x3 neighbourhood
would clip, and sharpens low-contrast detail (textures, foliage, fabric) more than edges that are already strong. Strong edges get little
or no extra contrast, so CAS does not draw halos or ringing, and the result never leaves the neighbourhood's range. The effect fades out
with distance: nearby surfaces get the full CAS, surfaces past a set distance get none, so the sky and far land are left alone.

### 2. When it runs

- **Switch:** `[_Shaders.Sharpening.Status] Enabled`, default `false`. Off: the effect does not run.
- **Frame position:** in the post chain (`EffectChain.cpp`): ... SMAA, FXAA, **Sharpening**, Cinema, ImageAdjust, Bodycam, SkyDither,
  Debug. It sees the anti-aliased picture and feeds everything after it. It can be the final writer to the game target.
- **Interior and exterior:** the same pass; the values come from `Main` outdoors and `Interiors` indoors (`GameState.isExterior`), re-chosen
  every frame.
- **Night, menus:** no special rule.
- **Single technique:** index 0 only.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (slot order; Vegas Shaders binds samplers by declaration order)

| Slot | Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | `TESR_RenderedBuffer` | The current post-chain picture (alpha carried through) | **Point**, clamp (CAS reads exact pixels; the UVs are texel centres) |
| s1 | `TESR_DepthBuffer` | CombineDepth's combined depth: linear depth / far plane | Linear, clamp |

#### Constants

| Name | Components |
|---|---|
| `TESR_SharpeningData` | x = Strength (CAS sharpness, saturated to 0..1); y = Clamp (unused); z = Offset (falloff distance / 1000); w = 0 |
| `TESR_ReciprocalResolution` | xy = 1 / picture size in pixels (the tap step) |
| `TESR_CameraData` | y = far plane distance (through the depth helper) |

### 4. Settings

| Section | Key | Type | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[_Shaders.Sharpening.Main]` | `Strength` | float [0-1] | 0.5 | Outdoors CAS sharpness: the cross neighbours' peak weight is -1 / lerp(8, 5, Strength). 0 = CAS's lightest (still sharpens; turn the effect off with Enabled), 1 = the strongest. Was 0.75 with the old shader. |
| `[_Shaders.Sharpening.Main]` | `Offset` | float [0-50] | 2.0 | Outdoors fade distance in thousands of units (2.0 = 2000 units). 0 or less = no sharpening. |
| `[_Shaders.Sharpening.Main]` | `Clamp` | float | 0.1 | Unused (kept so older tomls stay valid). CAS cannot overshoot. |
| `[_Shaders.Sharpening.Interiors]` | `Strength`, `Offset`, `Clamp` | | 0.5, 2.0, 0.1 | The same, indoors. |
| `[_Shaders.Sharpening.Status]` | `Enabled` | bool | false | The whole effect on or off. |

### 5. Passes and outputs

- **D3D9:** one pass, technique index 0, full-screen quad into the chain's output (8-bit LDR, same size). Alpha = input alpha.
- **Vulkan:** the pass `Sharpening` (Sharpening.spv), same result. Writes only the colour target; never the depth.

### 6. Behaviour details

**Depth falloff.** maxDepth = Offset x 1000; falloff = saturate((maxDepth - distance) / maxDepth)^4: 1 at the camera, 0 at maxDepth and
beyond (the sky is at the far end, so it gets 0). The CAS result is blended with the original by this factor. maxDepth <= 0 returns the
original picture (no divide by zero). Pixels with falloff 0 skip the 8 neighbour reads.

**CAS (per pixel).** Taps a..i of the 3x3 neighbourhood (e = the pixel; b, d, f, h the cross):
1. Soft minimum and maximum per channel: mn = min(cross) + min(all 9), mx = max(cross) + max(all 9) (both 0..2; CAS_BETTER_DIAGONALS).
2. amp = sqrt(saturate(min(mn, 2 - mx) / mx)) on the green channel (mx guarded by 1e-5): the headroom to black or white over the max.
3. w = amp x peak, with peak = -1 / lerp(8, 5, saturate(Strength)) (so w is in -0.2..0).
4. out = saturate(((b + d + f + h) x w + e) / (1 + 4w)) per channel. 1 + 4w is at least 0.2, never 0.

The picture is 8-bit gamma-encoded; CAS runs on it as is (AMD's filter works in perceptual space as well as linear; it is not converted).

**First-person weapon:** near, so the full CAS, as before. **RenderScale rect frames:** the Vulkan pass is off while a rect is active
(`DxvkCompute::PassesOn`), so the D3D9 pass runs on the full-size picture after the upscale. **TAA jitter:** none reaches this pass (it
runs after TAA). **NaN:** none possible (guards above).

**Would look wrong if missed:** halos on strong edges (CAS weight or soft min/max wrong); a sharpened sky or horizon (falloff not reaching 0);
colour fringes (per-channel weights instead of green's); a blurred picture (the sign of peak lost).

### 7. Vulkan path

- **Files:** `Compute/Sharpening.frag` -> `Sharpening.spv` (glslang, Vulkan 1.1).
- **Selection:** `SharpeningEffect::ExtRender`, when Vegas Shaders DXVK is loaded, `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses` is on and the frame is not
  a rect frame; otherwise D3D9. A failed build or draw falls back to D3D9 for the session.
- **Bindings (set 1):** 0 = the rendered picture, 1 = the combined depth. One sampler (linear clamp, from the push block); the colour
  taps land on texel centres, so they read exact pixels.
- **Push constants (64 bytes):** 0 `TESR_SharpeningData`, 16 `TESR_ReciprocalResolution`, 32 `TESR_CameraData`, 48 sampler index, 52-63 padding.

### 8. Look and cost

- **Correct:** near textures and foliage crisper, flat areas and strong edges almost unchanged, no bright or dark outlines; far land and
  the sky unchanged; Enabled false gives exactly the picture before.
- **Cost:** one pass, 9 colour reads + 1 depth read and a few ALU ops per near pixel; 1 colour + 1 depth read past the falloff distance.

### 9. Open questions

1. `Clamp` is unused; it could be removed from the settings once older tomls no longer matter.
2. CAS on the gamma-encoded picture vs linear: AMD notes either works; a linear option could be tried if the look is too strong in darks.
3. RenderScale below 1: the pass runs after the upscale on the full-size picture, but the depth falloff reads the combined depth at the
   same uv; whether that depth is still in the rect's coordinates then was not checked (the old shader read it the same way).

## Snow

*README title: Snow (falling snow, screen-space volume)*

### 1. Purpose
While it snows outdoors, the picture gets drifting snowflakes laid over the whole view. Flakes are drawn in layers at increasing distance from the camera, so near snow is large and sparse and far snow is fine and dense. A flake only shows where the sun's shadow map says the point is open to the sky, so snow falls through open air and stops at roofs and under overhangs. Each lit flake lightens the scene toward a sun-coloured tint. The rest of the picture is left alone.

### 2. When it runs
- Enabled by the settings section Shaders.Snow.Status, key Enabled (default false). The effect is off out of the box.
- Per frame it renders only when all of these hold: the snow amount (TESR_SnowData x, section 3) is above zero, the player is outdoors (the cell is not an interior), and the camera is not underwater.
- The snow amount is animated, not a switch. When the game's weather becomes snow, the amount rises to 1 over 0.5 game hours. When snow stops it falls back to 0 over 0.2 game hours. The effect keeps drawing while the amount is above zero, so the fade-out tail is visible.
- (Until 2026-11-21, on the frame snowfall started, Snow told WetWorld to fade out its puddles over 0.3 game hours. WetWorld is removed; the rain's wetness dries by the one rain clock, src/core/RainState.h, and ForceSnow stops that clock's rain.)
- While the amount is above zero, Snow sets the frame's request for the sun's orthographic shadow map (the map Snow reads), so that map is computed on those frames.
- Weather state: the game's current weather is the snow type. Setting Shaders.Snow.Main ForceSnow (default false) makes the game count as snowing in any weather and clears the rain flag while it does so. It is applied in the per-frame game-state update, before the effects run.
- Frame order (src/core/ShaderManager.cpp): inside the weather block that runs after the TAA pass. Rain renders first, then Snow, then the GPU particle draw (off by default), then the legacy bloom. Snow therefore sees Rain's result. In the frame chain's final-effect order Snow is listed right after Rain.
- Snow's own ShouldRender does not test for menus; whether it ever draws in a menu is not confirmed here (see section 9).
- Final-pass rule: if Snow is the last enabled effect to draw this frame, its single pass writes straight to the game target. Otherwise it writes into the chain's spare target. Either way the output must cover the whole frame.

### 3. Inputs
#### Textures (sampler slot = D3D9 declaration order; the C++ binds them in this order)
| Slot | Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the scene colour as the frame has it at this point (the rendered copy, not depth) | linear, clamp |
| s1 | TESR_DepthBuffer | the combined depth buffer: linear view distance divided by the far distance, single channel 32-bit float | linear, clamp |
| s2 | TESR_OrthoMapBuffer | the sun's orthographic shadow depth map for exteriors, single channel | linear, clamp |

The Vulkan pass binds the same three in descriptor set 1: binding 0 scene, 1 depth, 2 ortho map.

#### Constants
| Name | Register / binding | Components and meaning | Units and notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| TESR_ShadowCameraToLightTransformOrtho | 4x4 matrix (registers 0 to 3) | maps world space into the sun's orthographic shadow clip space | world units in, clip out |
| TESR_ViewTransform | engine constant | camera basis; its first three columns build the world ray | |
| TESR_ProjectionTransform | engine constant | entries [0][0] and [1][1] (horizontal and vertical focal scale) | |
| TESR_CameraData | engine constant | y = far distance | game units |
| TESR_RenderScale | engine constant, via the shared depth include | render-rectangle scale (see section 6) | |
| TESR_GameTime | engine constant | z = animation clock; x, y, w not used by Snow | engine clock units |
| TESR_SnowData | registered by Snow | x = snow amount (0 to 1, animated); y not used; z = fall speed (from Speed); w not used | |
| TESR_SunColor | engine constant | sun colour, RGBA | display-referred |
| TESR_SunAmbient | engine constant | sun ambient colour, RGBA | display-referred |
| TESR_WorldViewProjectionTransform | declared | not read by the shader | |

### 4. Settings
| Section | Key | Type | Range / default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shaders.Snow.Status | Enabled | bool | default false | Turns the whole effect on or off. |
| Shaders.Snow.Main | Speed | float | default 1.4; no range in the defaults file | Multiplies how fast the flake layers move down the screen. Higher = faster falling snow. Read live into TESR_SnowData z. |
| Shaders.Snow.Main | ForceSnow | bool | default false | Not a look setting: makes the game treat the weather as snowing outdoors (section 2). |
| Shaders.Snow.Main | Flakes | float | default 0.5 | Dead. The shader does not read it, and the settings menu lists it as not used. Do not give it an effect. |

The snow amount is not a setting. It is driven only by weather and the animator.

### 5. Passes and outputs
- One unnamed technique with one pass: a full-screen vertex pass (position and texture coordinate passthrough) and a pixel pass. The C++ asks for technique index 0; any other index draws nothing.
- Output: one colour per pixel at full frame size, in the frame target's format. Nothing is pre-cleared, so every pixel must be written.
- Pixels with no snow return the input picture, after the linearise and delinearise round trip (identity on values in 0 to 1, up to rounding).
- The Vulkan pass (section 7) writes its colour to the same target.

### 6. Behaviour details
- Colour space: the input is display (gamma-encoded) colour. It is converted to linear with a power of 2.2 (sign-preserving, so negative values do not give NaN), mixed in linear space, and converted back with a power of 1/2.2. Keep the same space, or the snow looks too bright or too dark.
- Ray and layers: the world ray through the pixel comes from the camera basis and projection. The effect runs 7 layers (indices 1 to 7; index 0 is never drawn). Layer i is at 50 x i game units along the ray. A layer only contributes to a pixel whose surface is further away than that distance, so closer geometry hides the layer.
- Sky: a sky pixel must count as further than every layer, so the sky shows snow. The value the depth buffer holds for sky is not confirmed here (section 9).
- Cylinder mapping: the flake grid of each layer lies on a cylinder around the camera, not on the screen. The horizontal coordinate is the ray's azimuth (negated, divided by pi, then mapped into 0 to 1). The vertical coordinate is the height over horizontal distance times 0.1 (the snow's own scale, not the rain's).
- Flake grid: each layer is a grid of cells, each with a pseudo-random brightness (a fixed 3 by 3 mixing constant) and a round drop shape. Each layer is moved horizontally by a sway that depends on the layer and on the vertical position, and vertically by Speed times the animation clock. The sway is a sine of half the clock, with unit amplitude, offset per layer (period in clock units about 4 pi). Different layers therefore drift differently.
- Flake size: the size edge grows with the layer index and with the snow amount (the smaller of the layer index and 4 times the amount, scaled by 0.05, plus 0.005). Far layers have larger flakes, and a small amount gives smaller flakes. The edge must never be zero, because the smooth step breaks at zero.
- Shadow test: for each layer, the sample point along the ray is looked up in the sun's ortho map. The flake counts if the map's stored depth is not closer than the point (bias 0.0001 in map depth), or if the point lies outside the map (outside counts as open). Points under roofs or in a sun shadow get no snow.
- Total: the flake values of all layers are summed, then clamped to 0 to 1. The shadow test multiplies each layer's flake.
- Snow tint: the snow colour is the component-wise maximum of the linearised sun ambient colour and the linearised sun colour, with alpha maximised the same way. The picture is lerped toward this colour by the total. It is a light, sun-coloured tint, not a grey overlay.
- Division guard: the cell's brightness divides by a fractional product that can be zero on some cells. The current shader has no guard there (the rain shader does, with a floor of 1e-4). A new version must guard it. A zero gives infinite or NaN values, shown as black or bright speckles.
- Modulo: the old code uses HLSL fmod meaning (x minus y times the truncated quotient), which keeps the sign of x. A floor-based modulo gives different results for negative coordinates, and the sway can make them negative. Keep the HLSL behaviour.
- Smooth step with reversed edges (the first edge is larger than the second): the result is the standard smooth step of the clamped ratio computed with the reversed edges. GLSL leaves this case undefined, so the ratio and cubic must be written out.
- Render-rectangle frames: when TESR_RenderScale is below 1 in x or y, the texture coordinate is mapped into the drawn part of the target before the ray and depth are worked out (the shared depth include does this). The Vulkan pass is off in those frames (section 7).
- Reversed depth: the depth buffer holds linear view distance over far distance, so reversed depth does not arise. Do not rebuild a projected depth.
- First-person weapon: the shader has no special handling for the weapon; it is treated like any other pixel in the depth buffer.
- Jitter (TAA): the shader has no jitter term; it uses the view as the frame has it.
- HDR vs LDR: input is the display-referred frame after tonemapping; output is the same kind of value. No HDR values are expected.
- NaN/inf guards: the division guard above is the only one needed inside the layer loop. An ortho coordinate outside the map is treated as open sky.
- Things that look wrong if missed: flakes under roofs (shadow test missing); the whole screen snowing at amount zero (ShouldRender gate missing); snow in interiors (interior gate missing); a jump in intensity at the start of snowfall (the animator must ramp); a grey tint (sun colours missing); frozen flakes (animation clock not used).

### 7. Vulkan path
- Shader: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/Snow.frag, a line-for-line port of the D3D9 pixel pass; the header comment records the matrix conventions. Compiled form: Snow.spv in the same folder. The pass is requested under the name "Snow".
- Selection (src/core/EffectRecord.cpp, EffectRecord::Render): the Vulkan pass runs only when all of these hold:
  - the setting [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses is on (src/core/SettingManager.cpp);
  - the frame is not a render-rectangle frame (DxvkCompute::PassesOn);
  - the Vulkan extension is available;
  - the name "Snow" is not in the NvrDxvkKeepD3D9 list (DxvkCompute::KeptOnD3D9);
  - no SM6 build covers this technique (not the case for Snow).
  Otherwise the D3D9 pass runs as in section 5.
- C++ side: src/effects/Snow.cpp, ExtRender. It returns false (so D3D9 runs) if the technique index is not 0, if any of the seven engine constants it reads is missing from the registered constant table, or if the depth or ortho texture is missing.
- Textures: descriptor set 1, binding 0 scene, 1 combined depth, 2 ortho map. The sampler is the linear clamp sampler at binding 3; its index is read from the push block at offset 192.
- Push block (208 bytes; offsets in bytes):
  - 0: the shadow-to-light matrix, four columns (copied from the registered TESR_ShadowCameraToLightTransformOrtho).
  - 64, 80, 96: the view matrix's first three columns, in the order the shader's ray reconstruction uses. The C++ builds them from the registered view transform. The fourth column is not sent.
  - 112: game time (copied from TESR_GameTime).
  - 128: snow data (registered TESR_SnowData: x amount, z speed).
  - 144: sun colour.
  - 160: sun ambient colour.
  - 176: x = projection [0][0], y = projection [1][1], z = far distance (camera data y), w = 0.
  - 192: sampler index (32-bit unsigned), then 12 bytes of padding.
- The Vulkan pass runs the same algorithm: 7 layers, the same shadow test, the same written-out smooth step, the same HLSL-style modulo, and the same colour round trip. Like the D3D9 pass it has no division guard; add one in a new version.
- The Vulkan pass does not run in render-rectangle frames, so it has no rectangle mapping.

### 8. Look and cost
- A correct result: fine, slow-looking flakes falling over open ground and sky; near flakes big and sparse, far flakes small and dense; no snow under roofs or in shadows; a light, sun-coloured tint, never a grey film. Layers drift at slightly different sways and do not all move in step.
- Typical wrong results: a grey or white wash over the screen (tint or shadow test broken); flakes under roofs (shadow test broken); a pattern that does not move (animation clock wrong); black or sparkling speckles (division guard missing); snow indoors (gate broken); flakes that pop on and off with the weather instead of fading (animator not used).
- Cost class: per-pixel loop. One full-screen pass per frame, 7 layers, each with one ortho map lookup and a few arithmetic steps on the cell. The pass is not skipped by pixel content; only the amount and the gates skip the draw.

### 9. Open questions
- The value the combined depth buffer holds for sky pixels is not confirmed in this read. The effect relies on sky counting as further than every layer; confirm against the depth-combine pass.
- Speed has no range in the defaults file, and the shader applies no clamp.
- Whether Snow ever draws in a menu is not confirmed: ShouldRender has no menu test. The frame-level gating in ShaderManager was not traced in full.
- The engine's exact unit for TESR_GameTime z (seconds or game time) comes from the engine constants code, which was not read in this pass.

### 10. Status of the wave 3 rewrite (2026-11-02, branch claude/rw3-weather)
- New D3D9 effect: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Snow.fx.hlsl. One technique, the same sampler order (rendered, depth, ortho), the same constants. Compiles (fx_2_0, all four defines).
- Fixed: the division by the fractional product (section 6) is now guarded: the divisor is at least 1, so no zero can reach it.
- Chosen here, not in the spec: the flake cell grid (40 by 24 per layer), the flake hash and its 0.5 presence threshold, the round flake shape (smooth step to half its edge), the sway amplitude (0.6 cells), and the fall rate per layer (1 + 0.15 x index).
- The snow-start call that faded WetWorld's puddles is gone with WetWorld (2026-11-21).
- Vulkan path (Compute/Snow.frag, .spv): NOT written yet. Without it the C++ falls back to D3D9.

### 11. World-space snow (2026-11-09, branch claude/snow-worldspace-73a9a9), contributed by macintroll
- Switch: Shaders.Snow.Main WorldSpace (default false). False = everything above, unchanged (technique 0; its compiled pixel shader is byte-identical to before). True = techniques 1 and 2 of the same Snow.fx.hlsl (the file's second part). Snow.cpp `Technique()` picks; EffectChain passes it to Render. Live.
- Technique 1 `SnowWorldSpace`: one pass, `SnowWorld`. The view ray walks a grid of 64-unit cells that scrolls down with time (70 units x Speed a second) and sideways with the volumetric clouds' wind offset (TESR_CloudsWind), up to 40 cells and WorldDistance units. Each occupied cell holds one billboard from a 4x4 flake sheet, facing the camera, with its own size, spin and wobble. A flake shows only where the ortho map says the cell is open to the sky, fades near the camera (12 to 52 units), at the far end (last fifth) and just in front of surfaces. Colour: a blue-white tint, brighter by day, tinted a quarter by the sun + ambient colour.
- Technique 2 `SnowWorldSpaceMarks`: pass `SnowSurfaceHits` first, then the same `SnowWorld`. The first pass draws short-lived flake marks (4 s on, 2 s off, per 12-unit world cell) on surfaces that face up (normal z 0.70 to 0.92), are open to the sky and lie within 1000 units; not on the first-person model, not below the water level. Used when WorldSurfaceMarks is on and the Normals effect is enabled; else technique 1.
- Extra samplers (declaration order = stage): s3 TESR_SnowFlakeBuffer = Textures\Precipitations\fx_weather_snow01.dds (linear, clamp, mips), s4 TESR_NormalsBuffer (point), s5 TESR_DepthBufferViewModel (point). They are bound for technique 0 too (it does not read them). A missing flake sheet = no flakes and no marks, nothing else breaks.
- Extra constants: TESR_SnowWorldData (x flake radius, y density, z max distance, w opacity), TESR_SnowWorldWind (x cloud wind strength, y grid repeat in cells = max(1, floor(8192 x strength + 0.5)), z plant wind strength), both registered by Snow; TESR_CloudsWind (VolumetricClouds), TESR_PlantLeafWind / 2 / 3 (PlantWind), TESR_WaterSettings, TESR_CameraPosition.
- Settings (all Shaders.Snow.Main, live): WorldFlakeSize 1.8 [0.5-3], WorldDensity 1 [0-1], WorldDistance 1200 [200-1300], WorldOpacity 0.8 [0-1], WorldSurfaceMarks true, WorldCloudWind 0.7 [0-2], WorldPlantWind 0 [0-3]. The other look values stay compile-time constants in the shader (cell size, fall speed, fades, tints, the marks' size / lifetime / density, the debug views).
- Limits kept from the shader: 40 cells cover (40 - 3) x 64 / 1.733 = 1366 units, hence WorldDistance's top of 1300. A flake's radius is capped at 2.88 units (4.5 % of the cell) so a billboard never crosses its cell.
- Vulkan path: none. `ExtRender` returns false for any technique but 0, so with the DXVK fork WorldSpace runs as D3D9 passes. A twin would need two fragment shaders, six textures and more constants than the 240-byte push block holds (a uniform buffer).
- The flake sheet was made by the contributor for this project (confirmed 2026-11-09): it may ship, with credit.
- Bench results and cost: docs/next/SNOW-WORLDSPACE-2026-11-09.md (not in this repo).
- Cost cut added here (2026-11-09): a cell is skipped as soon as the ray is further from the flake's resting place than the wobble plus the radius can reach (the reach test in SnowWorld's loop). Same picture; 2.2 ms -> 1.2 ms at 1080p on a GTX 1070.

## SnowAccumulation

*README title: SnowAccumulation (snow cover on the ground and surfaces during snowfall)*

### 1. Purpose
While it snows outdoors, snow builds up on the ground, on the tops of rocks, walls and cars, and stays for a while after the flakes stop, then melts over game hours. It is laid over the existing picture as a white layer with its own lighting: sun, sky ambient, sun-coloured specular and rim, a faint sparkle, and light from nearby lamps. Snow appears only where the surface faces upward, where the sun's shadow map says the sky reaches it, and away from water. Actors (skin and skinned clothes) get no snow. The snow is broken up by noise, a snow normal map and a blue-noise sparkle, so it is not a flat sheet. Two looks exist: Look 0 (the original) and Look 1 (a tester's rewrite with a broader, softer cover, footprints, and impacts on the first-person weapon). An optional Persistent mode keeps the snow spot by spot in a map round the player, instead of one amount for the whole world.

### 2. When it runs
- Enabled by [Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Status] Enabled (default false).
- Per frame it renders when the snow amount is above zero, or when the persistent cover map still holds snow (its own check), and the player is outdoors and not underwater.
- The snow amount is animated in game hours. It rises from 0 to 1 over the setting Increase (default 0.3 game hours) when the game's weather becomes snow. It falls back to 0 over Decrease (default 12 game hours) when snow stops. Both are read when the effect updates its constants.
- While the amount is above zero, the frame is asked for the sun's orthographic shadow map. The map is also requested while the persistent cover map holds snow.
- Weather state: the game's snow weather flag. [Shaders.Snow.Main] ForceSnow (default false) also makes the game count as snowing, so this effect follows it too.
- Frame order (src/core/ShaderManager.cpp): this effect runs earlier than the weather effects. After the cover map's own update (if Persistent is on for wet or snow) and before the ambient-occlusion pass and the composite. It is not part of the weather block (Rain, Snow, particles) and is not part of the final-effect chain order.
- The effect's own gate does not test for menus; whether it draws in a menu is not confirmed here (section 9).
- Sun and light values are read from the frame's constants as the D3D9 constant table holds them that frame.
- Render-rectangle frames: the coverage pass covers only the drawn part of the target (RenderScale below 1). See section 6.

### 3. Inputs
#### Textures (sampler slot = the register number in the D3D9 shader; the declaration order is the same)
| Slot | Name | Holds | Filtering / addressing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the current image of the chain: the coverage map while the passes run, the blurred coverage during the snow pass | linear, clamp |
| s1 | TESR_SourceBuffer | the frame before this effect (the scene) | linear, clamp |
| s2 | TESR_DepthBuffer | combined linear depth (view distance over far distance) | linear, clamp |
| s3 | TESR_OrthoMapBuffer | the sun's orthographic shadow depth map | linear, clamp |
| s4 | TESR_NormalsBuffer | the world normals of the frame (encoded) | linear, clamp (the D3D9 sampler: the Vulkan pass samples it linearly) |
| s5 | TESR_PointShadowBuffer | point and sun shadow mask: red = sun shadow (1 lit), green = point-light shadow | linear, clamp |
| s6 | TESR_SnowNormSampler | snow normal map, file Precipitations\snow_NRM.dds | linear mips, wrap |
| s7 | TESR_BlueNoiseSampler | blue-noise texture, file Effects\bluenoise256.dds (used by Look 0 sparkle) | linear mips, wrap |
| s8 | TESR_DepthBufferViewModel | depth of the first-person weapon and hands (their own depth) | linear, clamp |
| s9 | TESR_CoverMapBuffer | the persistent cover map (wet, puddle, snow, trodden snow per spot); used only in Persistent mode | wrap, no mips |
| s10 | TESR_SnowNoiseSampler | snow noise texture, file Effects\Snownoise256.dds (used by Look 1) | linear mips, wrap |
| s11 | TESR_CapturedNormalsBuffer | captured smooth normals (see section 6); point sampled | point, clamp |

The Vulkan pass (section 7) binds the Look 0 textures in set 1 at binding 0 (rendered), 1 (source), 2 (depth), 3 (ortho), 4 (normals), 5 (point shadow), 6 (snow normal), 7 (blue noise), 8 (captured normals).

#### Constants (names as the D3D9 effect declares them)
| Name | Components and meaning | Units / notes |
|---|---|---|
| TESR_SnowAccumulationParams | x: ortho sampling radius for coverage (the setting BlurNormDropThreshhold, default 120); y: blur radius multiplier (BlurRadiusMultiplier, default 2.0); z: sun power (SunPower, default 1.0, scales every sun-driven term); w: snow amount 0 to 1 (animated) | x in game units; registered by the effect |
| TESR_SnowAccumulationColor | rgb: snow colour (SnowColorR/G/B, default 0.9, 0.9, 0.8); w: normal smoothing radius in game units (NormalSmoothing, clamped to 0..256, default 32) | registered by the effect |
| TESR_SnowAccumulationExtra | x: 1 when captured normals are compiled in and on, else 0; y: captured-normals debug view (0, 1, 2); z, w: this frame's jitter offset in screen units (zero except with FSR / DLSS low-res effects and FollowJitter on) | registered by the effect; set per frame |
| TESR_SnowData | x: active snowfall, 0 (not snowing) to 1 (full snow), as the Snow effect's animator gives it | from the Snow effect; yz unused by this effect |
| TESR_GameTime | z: the animation clock (used for the impact animation on the first-person weapon) | engine |
| TESR_ShadowCameraToLightTransformOrtho | 4x4, world to sun ortho clip | engine |
| TESR_ShadowData | y: shadow darkness (1 minus y is the darkness of shadowed snow in Look 1) | engine |
| TESR_ShadowFade | x: fade of the sun's shadows toward fully lit when the sun is low; y: with TESR_ShadowScreenSpaceData x, the shadows-on test (Look 0: when both are zero the shadow mask is ignored) | engine |
| TESR_ShadowScreenSpaceData | x: with TESR_ShadowFade y, the shadows-on test (see above) | engine |
| TESR_OrthoData | x: the reach of the ortho shadow map in game units (beyond it no coverage is computed) | engine (set by the exterior shadows effect) |
| TESR_WaterSettings | x: water level height | engine |
| TESR_SunDirection, TESR_SunColor, TESR_SunAmbient | sun direction (toward the sun), sun colour, sun ambient colour | engine |
| TESR_ViewTransform, TESR_InvViewTransform, TESR_ProjectionTransform, TESR_InvProjectionTransform | camera matrices | engine |
| TESR_CameraData | x near, y far distance | engine |
| TESR_CameraPosition | camera world position | engine |
| TESR_ReciprocalResolution, TESR_RenderScale | texel size and the drawn part of the target | engine |
| TESR_DepthConstants | z: the reversed-depth flag (0 normal, non-zero reversed); drives the first-person depth test | engine |
| TESR_ShadowLightPosition[12] | shadow-casting point lights: xyz position, w radius (0 = empty slot) | engine |
| TESR_LightPosition[12] | other point lights: xyz position, w radius (0 = empty) | engine |
| TESR_LightColor[24] | light colours: the first 12 match the shadow-casting lights, the next 12 the others; w is an intensity (rgb * a) | engine |
| TESR_CapturedNormalsData | x 1 when this frame's captured normals exist; y distance tolerance share; z distance tolerance in units; w least cosine for the agreement test | engine (captured normals) |
| TESR_CoverMapFeet[24], TESR_CoverMapFeetData | footprints: 24 print records (centre relative to camera, side and heading) and a bounding circle with the print half-length | from the cover map (Persistent) |
| TESR_CoverMapData, TESR_CoverMapWindow, TESR_CoverMapGlobal, TESR_CoverMapTrail | the cover map's placement, window, open-ground values (wet, puddle, snow), and trail parameters (Persistent) | from the cover map |
| TESR_WorldViewProjectionTransform | declared, not read | |

### 4. Settings
| Section | Key | Type | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Status | Enabled | bool | false | Turns the effect on or off. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | Increase | float (game hours) | 0.3 | How long the snow takes to build up to full cover after snow starts. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | Decrease | float (game hours) | 12.0 | How long the cover takes to melt after snow stops. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | BlurNormDropThreshhold | float (game units) | 120 | Distance around each point over which the sun's shadow map is averaged for coverage. Larger = softer, wider cover edges. Read into the ortho sampling radius. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | BlurRadiusMultiplier | float | 2.0 | Scales the screen-space blur of the coverage map. Larger = softer edges. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | SunPower | float | 1.0 | Multiplies all sun-driven lighting on the snow (0 = flat ambient only). |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | SnowColorR, SnowColorG, SnowColorB | float | 0.9, 0.9, 0.8 | Colour of the snow (the blue is lower, so it reads as warm white). |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | NormalSmoothing | float (game units, 0 to 256) | 32 | Radius over which the ground's slope is averaged, so tessellated (bumpy) ground does not make snow come and go triangle by triangle. 0 = per triangle. 70 units = 1 metre. Live. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | CapturedNormals | bool | false | Snow takes each surface's own smooth normal, written while the surface draws, instead of the normal rebuilt from depth. Needs a restart (the lit shaders are built with it). |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | CapturedNormalsDebugView | int | 0 (0 to 2) | Diagnostic (needs CapturedNormals): 1 shows the normal the snow takes, 2 how much it differs from the depth normal. 0 = the real picture. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | Look | int | 1 (0 or 1) | 0 = the original look; 1 = the rewritten look (broader, softer cover; delayed build-up; footprints; impacts on the first-person weapon). Look 1 always runs as D3D9. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | FollowJitter | bool | true | With FSR / DLSS low-resolution effects, places snow along each jittered pixel's own ray so it does not shimmer. Off = as before (for comparison). |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | Persistent | bool | false | Snow is kept spot by spot in the cover map (built up where it reaches, gathers in hollows, melts faster in sun than in shade, footprints tread into it). Off = one amount for the whole world. Read by the cover map (CoverMap.cpp, section Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main). |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | PersistentShade | float | 0.35 (0.02 to 1) | Melt rate in shade (and at night) relative to full sun. Used by the cover map. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | PersistentDrifts | float | 0.5 (0 to 1) | How much more snow gathers in hollows and against walls, and how much less on ridges and edges. Used by the cover map. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | PersistentFootprints | float | 0.7 (0 to 1) | How hard steps tread the snow (compacted, greyer, flatter prints). Used by the cover map. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | PersistentFootprintShape | int | 0 (0 or 1) | 0 = one soft oval per print, 1 = a boot print (sole and heel). Used by the cover map. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | PersistentFootprintSize | float | 10 (2 to 200) | Half the length of one print, in game units. Used by the cover map. |
| Shaders.Snow.Main | ForceSnow | bool | false | Not this effect's setting: makes the game count as snowing (section 2). |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | PersistentMapSize | float | 8192 (1024 to 65536) | Width of the map round the player, game units. Used by the cover map. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | PersistentMapResolution | int | 1024 (128 to 4096) | Texels across the map. Used by the cover map. |
| Shaders.SnowAccumulation.Main | PersistentDebugView | int | 0 (0 to 2) | The cover map's test view (1 top-down inset, 2 painted over the world). |


The persistent map's size, resolution and debug view were in the wet-world section ([Shaders.WetWorld.Main]) until 2026-11-21; WetWorld is removed, the keys moved here and settings step 91 carries a saved value over. The cover map's wet half (WetWorld's Persistent) went with it: this effect is the map's only reader.

### 5. Passes and outputs
Each technique below is requested by index. The C++ chooses it (section 7).

| Technique (index) | Passes | Used when |
|---|---|---|
| unnamed (0) | coverage, upscale, blur across, blur down, snow | Look 0, Persistent off |
| PersistentTechnique (1) | the same five, with the persistent snow pass | Look 0, Persistent on |
| LookTechnique (2) | coverage, upscale, blur across (depth tolerance 8500), blur down (depth tolerance 8500), Look 1 snow pass | Look 1, Persistent off |
| LookPersistentTechnique (3) | the same as 2 with the persistent Look 1 snow pass | Look 1, Persistent on |

Pass outputs:
- Coverage pass: writes a value in all three colour channels (the same grey) into the chain's image. The value is 1 where snow may lie (open to the sky, or outside the reach), 0 where the ground is in shade or under cover. It is computed for one pixel in every four in each direction (see section 6): the rest of the target is set to 1.
- Upscale pass (the scale factor is 4): samples the coverage at a quarter of each pixel's position, so the coverage fills the whole target. Pixels beyond the drawn part of a render-rectangle frame are discarded.
- Blur passes (horizontal, then vertical): a depth-aware 12-tap blur of the coverage. A tap counts only where its depth is close to the centre's: the depth tolerance is 3500 game units for techniques 0 and 1 and 8500 for techniques 2 and 3, scaled by the centre's distance as a fraction of the far distance. The blur radius is the radius multiplier times the base offsets. Pixels beyond the ortho reach (TESR_OrthoData x, all techniques) are not blurred: their input is passed through.
- Snow pass: writes the final colour at full frame size, linearised then converted back to display space, with alpha 1. It may write directly to the game target. Pixels with no snow return the scene unchanged.
- All passes must write every pixel of their target (nothing is pre-cleared).

### 6. Behaviour details
#### Coverage (where snow may lie)
- Sky and distant pixels: any pixel beyond the ortho reach (TESR_OrthoData x) gets coverage 1 (no coverage check). Look 1 also stops at 3500 game units and fades to fully open between 2500 and 3500.
- Points are placed in the world from the pixel's depth along its own ray (with the FollowJitter offset when set). The point's sun-ortho visibility is averaged over 13 taps (Look 0) or 9 taps (Look 1) spread round it at the radius from Look 0's ortho radius setting. Each tap counts 1 when the point is open to the sun (outside the map counts as open) and 0 when a roof or other occluder is above it.
- The average is sharpened by a smooth step from 0.1 to 0.9 (removes outliers), and faded toward open (1) with distance from 60 percent of the reach to the reach.
- Coverage is computed only at one pixel in four in each direction (the pixel stands for a 4 by 4 block): the upscale fills in between.
- Under a render rectangle (RenderScale below 1 in x or y) the coverage is computed over the drawn part only; beyond it the value is 1.

#### Snow surface (Look 0 and the non-persistent snow pass)
- Snow requires the surface to face upward enough: the surface's normal (from depth, or the captured normal, then optionally smoothed) must be above a threshold, and the snow is multiplied by a vertical gate that is 0 below a cosine of about 0.5 and 1 above about 0.8 (on the surface's upward component), and by the coverage. Undersides and walls get none.
- Water: an upward-facing surface whose height lies within a band around the water level (the band is the pixel's depth over far distance times 200 game units, each side) is skipped. Look 1 uses 150 units for this band. Look 1 also skips any surface whose depth-rebuilt normal points clearly downward (its up component at or below about -0.10).
- Actors: pixels the captured normals mark as actors (skin, skinned armour and clothes) keep the scene colour, so snow does not appear on characters standing near a snowy rock.
- Normal smoothing (NormalSmoothing, radius in game units): the normal is averaged with 12 taps on a disc of that world radius around the pixel. Each tap's weight falls to zero as its world position moves away from this pixel's (zero at twice the radius), ramps up as its normal comes within about 60 degrees of this pixel's normal, and is zero off screen (off-screen taps are clamped and do not count). Look 1 uses a fixed 48-unit radius and 8 taps with the same kind of weights.
- Captured normals: where the surface wrote its smooth vertex normal and the distance matches the depth buffer (within the distance tolerance), that normal replaces the depth-rebuilt normal. Where it disagrees by more than about 60 degrees with the rebuilt normal, the rebuilt one is kept. Surfaces the game's own shaders draw have no captured normal. The captured-normal read uses the texel the pixel lies in (point sampling).
- Light on the snow (Look 0): the snow colour is linearised. Ambient is the snow colour times the sun ambient colour raised to the power 2.2 (sign-preserving). Diffuse is the snow colour times the sun-normal dot product, sun colour, a diffuse factor of 0.56 times SunPower, and the sun shadow. Specular is a Blinn-style half-vector term with exponent 20, multiplied by a Fresnel term (power 5 of one minus the eye dot, clamped), and scaled by the specular factor 1.0 times SunPower and the shadow. Fresnel adds a rim of sun colour (factor 0.65 times SunPower). Sparkle is a very sharp term (exponent 1000) from the blue-noise texture, weighted 0.2 and by the shadow.
- Point lights: 12 shadow-casting and 12 other lights. Each adds its colour (times its intensity) times an attenuation of (1 minus s) over (1 plus s), where s is the squared ratio of distance to radius, times a diffuse-plus-specular term (exponent 20), at a power of 0.5 (Look 0). Empty slots (radius 0) add nothing.
- Coverage pattern (Look 0): the coverage noise is three world-anchored noise octaves with feature sizes of 100, 600 and 200 game units, multiplied together and raised to the power 2 minus 2 times the snow amount, then pushed through a smooth step from 0.2 to 0.4. The final mask is coverage times the min of the vertical gate and the ortho value.
- Composite (Look 0): the scene is lerped toward the snow colour by the mask.
- Snow normal map: the snow's own normal map is sampled in world XY at 200 game units per repeat (wrapping), expanded from its 0 to 1 range, and blended into the surface normal (xy added, z multiplied).

#### Look 1 (Look = 1)
- Build-up is delayed. Visible cover begins only when the active snowfall (TESR_SnowData x) is essentially full (smooth step from 0.94 to 0.995). The accumulation amount is remapped with a smooth step from 0.25 to 0.82, so visible coverage starts later and rises. Once the amount is high (0.72 to 0.88), the persistence gate keeps snow visible even after the snowfall stops, so accumulated snow lingers and melts separately.
- Shape: the lighting normal is pulled toward an upward-biased snow normal (roundness 1). A broad detail layer samples the same snow normal map at 700 game units per repeat (strength 0.12, rotated), the slope gate is softened (width 0.26), lifted by 0.35 toward up, and given world noise of 0.12. Downward faces keep a gentle underside guard.
- Coverage (Look 1) is two noise octaves (scales 100 and 200 game units) raised to the power 2.2 minus 2 times the delayed accumulation; a smooth step from 0.05 to 0.6; multiplied by a slow world breakup (value noise at 0.003 per unit, between 0.8 and 1.0).
- Eligibility: the slope value (up-facing amount) is pushed by a macro patch noise and mid noise, then gated by a smooth step around a threshold between 0.92 (no accumulation) and 0.40 (full accumulation).
- Edges: the mask is broken up at its edges (a band 0.12 wide), where a keep-probability and fine noise remove snow in patches, so the edge is ragged, not a clean line.
- Shoreline: within 5 game units of the water level the snow is removed with a noise threshold (the keep probability follows the distance) and what survives is semi-transparent (opacity 0.05 to 0.15, noisy). The shoreline's fine noise fades out with distance (1000 to 1800 game units).
- Shadows: the sun shadow factor is lerped between the darkness (1 minus ShadowData y) and 1 by the shadow mask. The snow's own contribution is darkened by that factor, not the whole pixel, so shadows are not darkened twice.
- Sparkle (Look 1): a very sharp term from the snow-noise texture, with a distance fade (800 to 1600 units).
- Light: the same sun terms as Look 0 (diffuse 0.56, specular 1.0, Fresnel 0.65, all times SunPower) and the same point-light sum (power 0.5 per light), but the point-light attenuation is computed without early exits (equivalent result).
- Footprints (Persistent only, Look 1): the cover map's trodden value darkens and flattens the snow inside prints (inner shadow up to 0.32, outer glow 0.12); the trodden snow is greyer and loses some of its surface detail; in the middle of a hard-trodden trail the ground shows through (removal 0.9).
- First-person impacts (Look 1): for the camera's own weapon and hands (the first-person test below), upward surfaces within 120 game units get impact flecks: small cells that spawn, spread and fade on a timer (about 0.35 of a cycle each), lighten the picture toward a near-white colour by up to 0.45, and are gated by the active snowfall (smooth step from 0 to 0.15). They do not use the snow surface.
- First-person test: a pixel is first-person when the view-model depth is closer than the normal depth test (normal depth: view-model depth below 0.9; reversed depth: view-model depth above 0.01). It uses the reversed-depth flag from TESR_DepthConstants z.

#### Debug and diagnostics (both looks)
- Captured-normals debug views (section 4): 1 shows the normal the snow uses, 2 the difference from the depth normal; both replace the picture.

#### Cross-cutting behaviour
- Render-rectangle frames: the coverage and blur passes use the drawn part of the target (RenderScale). Texture coordinates are mapped into the drawn part before the depth is read; reads are clamped to the drawn part.
- Jitter (FSR / DLSS low-resolution frames, FollowJitter on): the world position of each pixel is taken along the jittered pixel's own ray, using the jitter offset (TESR_SnowAccumulationExtra z, w, in screen units) scaled by the render scale. With FollowJitter off the snow is placed on the unjittered ray (the old look).
- Reversed depth: the combined depth buffer is linear and does not change with reversed depth. The first-person test (Look 1) and the view-model depth do depend on the reversed-depth flag (section 3, TESR_DepthConstants z).
- HDR vs LDR: all inputs and the output are display-referred (the scene after tonemapping). Linear conversion uses the standard sRGB curve for the scene and the snow colour, and a power of 2.2 for the sun ambient in Look 0 (sign-preserving). The output is converted back.
- NaN / inf guards: the normal smoothing and the captured normal decode normalise with a fallback; power terms use saturated or sign-preserving inputs; the sun half-vector normalises. A shadow-map lookup outside the map counts as open (see section 9).
- Things that look wrong if missed: snow on walls or undersides (vertical gate); snow under roofs (ortho test); snow on actors (actor test); snow inside water (water band); triangle-by-triangle snow on bumpy ground (smoothing or captured normals); snow not melting (Decrease); a grey film (tint or sun colour); snow shimmering with FSR (jitter offset); hard unbroken edges (edge breakup); double-darkened shadows (shadow factor applied to the whole pixel).

### 7. Vulkan path
- Shader: src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/SnowAccumulation.frag (400 lines). It ports the Look 0, non-persistent passes line for line (coverage, upscale, depth-aware blur, snow pass), with the same constants block, plus the captured-normal and smoothing helpers. It has no Look 1 and no persistent path. The compiled form is SnowAccumulation.spv in the same folder. Pass name requested: "SnowAccumulation".
- Selection (src/core/EffectRecord.cpp, EffectRecord::Render, and this effect's ExtRender): the Vulkan path runs only for technique index 0 (Look 0, Persistent off), and only when:
  - [Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses is on;
  - the frame is not a render-rectangle frame;
  - the Vulkan extension is available and the effect's name is not in NvrDxvkKeepD3D9;
  - ClearRenderTarget is false and the source buffer is the frame's source surface;
  - the depth, ortho, normals, point shadow, snow normal and blue noise textures are all present (the captured-normal texture falls back to the normals texture when no captured texture exists).
  Look 1 (techniques 2 and 3) and Persistent (technique 1 and 3) always run as D3D9 passes.
- The C++ side is src/effects/SnowAccumulation.cpp, ExtRender. It builds the constants block by copying the registered constants (as stored), and the textures by name. Failure before the first pass (missing texture, pipeline, constant or block) returns false and D3D9 runs. After the first pass has drawn, a later failure keeps what is on the target.
- Four modes in the Vulkan shader, selected by the push block's mode field, drawn as five passes: mode 0 coverage (the frame's source is copied into the chain's spare image first, as the D3D9 chain does), mode 1 upscale, mode 2 blur (run twice; the offset direction is given by the push block's offset field: (1, 0) across, then (0, 1) down), mode 3 snow (may write the game target directly).
- Textures: set 1, bindings 0 to 8 as listed in section 3 (rendered, source, depth, ortho, normals, point shadow, snow normal, blue noise, captured). Before each pass the rendered texture is rebound (the chain's current image).
- Samplers: linear clamp (index in the push block at offset 8) and linear wrap with mips (offset 12). The sampler indices come from the extension.
- Uniform block: set 2, binding 0, std140, fields in this order: shadowToLight (mat4), projection, invProjection, view, invView (mat4 each), cameraData, cameraPosition, reciprocalResolution, sunDirection, sunColor, sunAmbient, waterSettings, shadowFade, shadowScreenSpaceData, orthoData, snowParams (= TESR_SnowAccumulationParams), snowAccColor (= TESR_SnowAccumulationColor), shadowLightPosition[12], lightPosition[12], lightColor[24], capturedData (= TESR_CapturedNormalsData), snowExtra (= TESR_SnowAccumulationExtra, with x set to 0 when the captured-normal texture is missing). Matrices are stored as the D3D9 matrix (the shader's matrix reads are written to match).
- Push block (24 bytes): offset 0 offsetMask (vec2: the blur direction), 8 sampler (clamp) index, 12 sampler (wrap) index, 16 mode, 20 padding.
- Differences from D3D9 that the Vulkan pass keeps on purpose: the blur clips become direct returns of the input; the captured-normal read is a texel fetch (point), matching the D3D9 point sampler; the smooth step with reversed edges is written out (GLSL leaves it undefined).

### 8. Look and cost
- Correct look (Look 0): a thin, even white cover on flat ground and on the tops of rocks, walls and cars left clear; cover thinner on slopes and under roofs; no snow on water, on actors, or under cover; edges broken by noise, not a clean line; the sun gives a warm highlight on the top surfaces; shadows darken the snow with the ground.
- Correct look (Look 1): cover builds slowly at first and lingers after the snow stops; footprints show as greyed prints with a shadowed inside; a soft, rounded look on bumpy ground; impacts flicker on the first-person weapon while it snows.
- Typical wrong results: snow on walls or on undersides (vertical gate broken); a hard line where coverage ends (blur or edge breakup broken); snow appearing triangle by triangle on tessellated ground (smoothing or captured normals off or broken); snow inside water (water band broken); snow on the player's own body or on actors (actor test broken); snow that keeps shimmering under FSR (jitter offset missing); snow darker than the ground in the sun (sun-driven terms wrong); shadows darkened twice (shadow factor applied to the whole pixel); no melt (Decrease not applied).
- Cost class: multi-pass. Coverage (13 or 9 ortho samples at one pixel in four, so cheap), upscale, two 12-tap depth-aware blur passes at full resolution (skipped beyond reach), and a per-pixel snow pass. The snow pass is the expensive one: Look 0 has 3 noise octaves and 24 point-light evaluations; Look 1 has the same 24-light loop and a second noise set, and the Look 1 pass is much heavier to compile (the authors note minutes of compile time when the loop is unrolled badly). Normal smoothing adds 12 (Look 0) or 8 (Look 1) normal reads per pixel where it is on.

### 9. Open questions
- Sky: the depth value sky pixels hold in the combined depth buffer is not confirmed here. Coverage treats them as beyond reach (coverage 1), but the snow pass's treatment of sky normals (which are not real surfaces) is not confirmed.
- Whether the snow pass draws in menus is not confirmed: the effect's own gate has no menu test.
- Outside the ortho map: both looks count a point outside the shadow map as open (coverage 1), so snow may lie there. Confirm that is intended at the edge of the shadow map.
- The reach of the ortho map (TESR_OrthoData x) is set by the exterior shadows effect and was not traced here.
- The persistent cover map and its footprints are implemented by the cover map code (CoverMap.cpp / CoverMap.h, CoverMap.fx.hlsl), which is not one of the effects in this wave; this spec describes only the inputs the snow reads from it.
- TESR_ShadowScreenSpaceData x is treated as a shadows-enabled flag from its use; its exact meaning in the shadows effect was not read.
- The Look 1 point-light attenuation is written without early exits; the spec treats the result as identical to the Look 0 form.

## SunGlare

*README title: Effect: SunGlare*

UNOFFICIAL, 2026-11-13 (modern lighting). Source of truth: `SunGlare.h/.cpp`, `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/SunGlare.fx.hlsl`. No Vulkan twin yet (the D3D9 technique draws under DXVK too).

### 1. Purpose

The sun's lens glare: what a camera lens (or the eye, through its lashes) makes of a small, very bright source. Round the sun's place on screen it adds
- a tight **halo**,
- a **starburst**: thin lines through the sun, each a pair of points, of differing length,
- a long horizontal **streak**.

It only adds light, and only as much as the sun's disc itself shows. Behind a tree, a wall, the first-person weapon or thick cloud there is no glare.

### 2. When it runs

- `[Shaders.SunGlare.Status] Enabled` (default false: a look change).
- Outdoors, above water, by day (`dayLight > 0.5`, as the god rays), the sun in front of the camera (`TESR_ViewSpaceLightDir.z > 0.05`), Strength above 0.
- Frame order: the HDR chain before the tone mapper, right after the god rays (`EffectChain.cpp` step 37b, `PreStep_SunGlare`). The tone mapper rolls its core off to white.

### 3. Passes

1. **Cover** (technique `SunGlareCover`, `SunGlareEffect::RunCover`): one draw into a 1 x 1 `A16B16G16R16F` target, `TESR_SunGlareCover`.
   - 16 taps on a spiral over a patch of radius SunSize round the sun's centre: the share that is sky in the depth buffer (`> 0.9`), and their mean brightness.
   - 8 taps on a ring at 7 x SunSize: the sky's brightness there. Patch over ring is the cloud contrast, through `smoothstep(CloudContrastLow, CloudContrastHigh)`. With no open sky on the ring (the sun in a gap between leaves) the patch is compared with a plain 0.25.
   - A sun off the screen cannot be measured: the result fades to 0 over the last 4 % past the edge.
   - The pass blends its value over what the texel held (SrcAlpha / InvSrcAlpha, alpha = 1 / EaseFrames), so the glare eases in and out. After a frame without the pass the texel is cleared first.
2. **Picture** (unnamed technique, through `EffectRecord::Render`): one full-screen pass. A pixel the glare cannot reach (halo + longest spike + streak under 0.004) returns the stored picture before anything else. Else it reads the cover texel and adds the glare in linear light.

Positions are in screen heights from the sun's projected position, so the shape is the same at any resolution and aspect.

### 4. Inputs

| Slot | Name | Holds |
|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | the current HDR picture |
| s1 | TESR_DepthBuffer | linear depth (CombineDepth) |
| s2 | TESR_SunGlareCover | the eased cover texel (x the cover used, y sky share, z cloud contrast) |

Constants (all `SunGlareEffect::UpdateConstants`): `TESR_SunGlareData` (x strength x day x horizon fade x SunsetStrength, y halo size, z halo strength, w debug view), `TESR_SunGlareSpikes` (lines, length, width, strength), `TESR_SunGlareStreak` (length, width, strength, chromatic), `TESR_SunGlareSun` (patch radius, cloud ramp low / high, 1 / EaseFrames), `TESR_SunGlareTurn` (cos / sin of the first line's angle and of the step between lines). Shared: `TESR_ViewSpaceLightDir`, `TESR_SunColor`, `TESR_SunsetColor`, `TESR_SunDirection`, `TESR_SunAmount`, `TESR_ReciprocalResolution`.

### 5. Settings (`[Shaders.SunGlare.Main]`, all live)

Strength, HaloSize, HaloStrength, Spikes, SpikeLength, SpikeWidth, SpikeStrength, SpikeRotation, StreakLength, StreakWidth, StreakStrength, Chromatic, SunSize, CloudContrastLow, CloudContrastHigh, EaseFrames, SunsetStrength, DebugView. See `defaults.toml` for ranges.

Works best with the game's own glare sprite turned down (`[Shaders.Sky.Main] GlareStrength` about 0.2): at 1.0 that sprite is a white area a quarter of the screen wide and the starburst drowns in it.

### 6. Edge cases

- **Sun behind moving leaves, or a branch crossing it during a pan:** the cover changes from frame to frame; EaseFrames spreads it. At 1 the whole glare switches on and off (flicker sweep: 19 spikes up to 24 % in 2,400 frames; at 8: 8 spikes of 5-6 %, the bench's noise level is 4-5 %).
- **TAA / DLSS jitter** moves the depth edge by a fraction of a pixel each frame: same remedy.
- **Thick cloud over the sun:** the patch is no brighter than the ring, no glare. Thin cloud: partial.
- **RenderScale:** positions go through `rectToScreen` / `screenToRect`.
- **Night, interiors, underwater:** not drawn. The moon has no glare.
- The 1 x 1 target is the pass's own render target and sampler s2 of the picture pass: `RunCover` clears stage 2 before it draws.

### 7. Cost

0.06 ms with the sun in view at 1176 x 664 (RTX 4070 Ti SUPER), 0 when the sun is behind the camera (ShouldRender).

### 8. Looks right / wrong

- Right: a compact white core, 16 fine points of unequal length, a thin streak left and right, all gone the moment the sun passes behind a trunk.
- Wrong: glare over a tree or the weapon that covers the sun (the cover pass did not run: check the `SunGlareCover` technique loaded); a glare that blinks every frame (EaseFrames 1); a square-edged halo (HaloSize too large against the early-out threshold).
- DebugView 1 tints the picture green (sun counted visible) to red (covered); 2 shows the glare alone.

## SunShadows

*README title: SunShadows: effect spec (describes the wave-3 shader of 2026-11-02, branch claude/rw3-fog-sun)*

Files: `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/SunShadows.fx.hlsl` (D3D9), `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/SunShadows.glsl` with `SunShadowsMarch.frag`,
`SunShadowsBlur.frag`, `SunShadowsApply.frag` (+ `SunShadowsApply-c1.spv`), `SunShadowsSharp.frag`, `SunShadowsSharpFinish.frag` (Vulkan).
C++: `src/effects/SunShadows/SunShadows.cpp/.h` (passes, ping-pong, Vulkan), `src/effects/ShadowsExteriors/ShadowsExterior.cpp/.h` (constants, settings),
`src/core/ShadowManager.cpp` (cascade atlas and character map), `src/core/ShaderManager.cpp` (technique choice), `src/core/RtSunShadows.cpp`.
Reference for the cascade rules: `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Shaders/Includes/Shadow.hlsl` (`GetSunShadow`, `CharacterMapShadow`), not edited.
Settings sections in the toml carry a leading underscore (`[_Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.ScreenSpace]`); this spec drops it as the C++ does.

### 1. Purpose

SunShadows writes the outdoor sun's shadow term into the two-channel buffer `TESR_PointShadowBuffer` (G16R16, 0..1).
- Red: sun visibility, 1 = lit. It always holds the screen-space contact shadows (a short ray toward the sun from every pixel: dark
  where something stands right in front of the surface, under feet, props, along walls). In the deferred techniques it also holds
  the sun cascade maps and the player's character shadow map, combined so they agree with what the object shaders compute in the
  forward path (`Shadow.hlsl`).
- Green: the lamps' term written by the PointShadows passes before this effect. Carried through, blurred with red and raised to
  Intensity like red (as `ShadowsExteriorEffect::FadedContactUnread` describes the passes).
The exterior apply (`ShadowsExteriors.fx.hlsl`) later fades red by `TESR_ShadowFade.x` (dawn, dusk, moon) and darkens the frame by
Darkness. This effect applies neither.

### 2. When it runs

- Exterior frames only, inside the shadow block of `ShaderManager.cpp` (around line 2130), when `Effects.ShadowsExteriors->Enabled`
  holds, after the lamp passes. `SunShadowsEffect` has no `ShouldRender` override; `Enabled` comes from the effect loader (no
  `SunShadows` section in the defaults: on whenever the file loads).
- Skipped while `ShadowsExteriorEffect::FadedContactUnread()` is true: `[Main] SkipWhenFaded` on, the sun fade exactly 1, no lamp
  shadows outdoors, and none of blood flow, wet world, underwater or the debug view drawing. The buffer then keeps the lamp result.
- Technique (ShaderManager): 2 when `ForwardShadows` and `[ScreenSpace] Sharp` are on (and the file has `ForwardContactSharp` at
  index 2); else 1 when `ForwardShadows` is on, the file has `ForwardContactShadows` at index 1 and RT sun shadows are not replacing
  the cascades (`RtSunShadows::Replacing()`); else 0. `SunShadowsEffect::Render` then swaps 0 -> 3 and 1 -> 4 when
  `[ScreenSpace] FastPaths` is on and the file has `ContactFast` / `ForwardContactShadowsFast` at those indices.
- While SunFaded with ForwardShadows on, the C++ hands the passes `TESR_ShadowForwardData.x = 0` for the draw, so technique 0's
  apply leaves the cascades to the (faded) forward value and does not look them up.
- After the passes, `RtSunShadows::Apply` multiplies the traced sun visibility into red (nothing when RT is off).

### 3. Inputs

#### 3.1 Samplers (EffectRecord binds stage N to the N-th declared sampler and parses `register ( sN )`: order = register)

| Slot | Name | States | Holds / use |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | `TESR_DepthBuffer` | LINEAR, CLAMP, no mip | View depth / far (R). Sky = 1. Read at texel centres (march samples are snapped). |
| s1 | `TESR_ShadowAtlas` | LINEAR, CLAMP, no mip | 2 x 2 cascade atlas, plain light-space depth (PCF). Near top-left, Middle top-right, Far bottom-left, Lod bottom-right. Taps at texel centres. `[ShadowMaps] Anisotropy` may set ANISOTROPIC here (SetCT; harmless at LOD 0). |
