| s2 | `TESR_NormalsBuffer` | LINEAR, CLAMP, no mip | VIEW-space normal encoded 0..1 (Normals.fx). Decoded, normalised; facing the camera if empty. World normal = view normal through the transposed view rotation. |
| s3 | `TESR_PointShadowBuffer` | LINEAR, CLAMP, no mip | The pass's source: the buffer for pass 1, then the previous scratch target (the C++ rebinds stage 3 per pass). |
| s4 | `TESR_NoiseSampler` | LINEAR, WRAP, LINEAR mips | `Effects\bluenoise256.dds` (ResourceName annotation; also what `ExtFileTexture` hands the Vulkan pass). One noise texel per pixel. |
| s5 | `TESR_CharShadowMap` | POINT, CLAMP, no mip | The character sun map (R32F depth, cleared to 1). Declared always (so s6 stays stage 6); read only with `CHARACTER_SHADOW_MAP` and `TESR_CharShadowData.w > 0`. |
| s6 | `TESR_BackDepthBuffer` | POINT, CLAMP, no mip | Nearest back faces (BackDepth pass). Read only when `TESR_BackDepthData.x > 0.5` (BackDepth.hlsl). |

#### 3.2 Constants (bound by name)

- `TESR_ProjectionTransform`: `_11, _22, _31, _32` rebuild view positions from linear depth and project them back (independent of
  REVERSED_DEPTH). `TESR_ViewTransform`: view -> world direction of normals. `TESR_InvViewTransform`: view -> camera-relative world
  position (w = 1, as `GetShadowWorldPos`). `TESR_InvProjectionTransform`, `TESR_DepthConstants`, `TESR_CameraPosition`: declared by
  Depth.hlsl, unused.
- `TESR_CameraData`: x near (ray points nearer are ignored, sharp ray end clamp), y far (depth scale, sky test, ray lengths).
- `TESR_ReciprocalResolution.xy`: one texel; blur stride, noise scale, texel snap, finish taps, rect clamp.
- `TESR_RenderScale` (RenderRect.hlsl): rect-frame coordinate mapping and read clamps.
- `TESR_ViewSpaceLightDir.xyz`: march direction and contact facing (normalised here).
- `TESR_SmoothedSunDir.xyz`: N.L for the cascade and character normal offset and slope slack (the sun the maps were drawn for).
- `TESR_ShadowScreenSpaceData`: x contact on, y BlurRadius (texels between blur taps), z RenderDistance, w Intensity.
- `TESR_ShadowScreenSpaceFade`: x NearFade start, y NearFade end (y = 0: no fade).
- `TESR_ShadowForwardData`: x 1 = object shaders' cascade lookups off (with FORWARD_SHADOWS compiled, 0 here means the apply skips
  the maps); w HandoverBlend.
- `TESR_ShadowFade.y`: maps drawn (0: no map term). (x, z: the apply's / the lamps'; not read.)
- `TESR_ShadowFormatData`: x must be 3 (PCF) and z 1 (outdoors), else no map term (as `GetSunShadow`).
- `TESR_ShadowBlur`: x 1 / atlas size (texel, floored at 1/16384), z SmoothFilter (cubic 4 x 4 instead of quadratic 3 x 3).
- `TESR_ShadowCameraToLightTransform{Near,Middle,Far,Lod}`, `TESR_Shadow{Near,Middle,Far,Lod}Center` (xyz centre, w radius).
- `TESR_RtSunReplace.x`: RT range R in Mode 1 (replace), else 0.
- With `CHARACTER_SHADOW_MAP`: `TESR_CharShadowCameraToLight`, `TESR_CharShadowCenter` (w half-extent), `TESR_CharShadowData`
  (x 1/res, y band share, z depth bias, w = 1 + CharacterShadowMapDebug, 0 = not drawn this frame).
- `TESR_BackDepthData` (BackDepth.hlsl): x drawn, y/z depth -> view distance.
- Not declared (the apply's, or unused by this effect): `TESR_ShadowData`, `TESR_SunAmbient`, `TESR_WorldViewProjectionTransform`,
  `TESR_ShadowCrossFade` and the `Old` cascades (CrossFade needs ForwardShadows), `TESR_ContactHardeningData` (needs ForwardShadows).

#### 3.3 Macros

`FORWARD_SHADOWS` 0/1 (`[Main] ForwardShadows` at load), `CHARACTER_SHADOW_MAP` (defined 1 when `[ShadowMaps] CharacterShadowMap`
was on at load), `REVERSED_DEPTH` and `PHYSICAL_SKY_ON` (no effect here; compiles with all of them).

#### 3.4 Includes

`Includes/Depth.hlsl` (with RenderRect.hlsl; for the declarations), `Includes/BackDepth.hlsl`. The PCF and character lookups are
written in the effect, following `Shaders/Includes/Shadow.hlsl`.

### 4. Settings

| Section | Key | Default | Reaches | Effect here |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ScreenSpace | Enabled | true | ScreenSpaceData.x (and the effect's enable) | Off: red = 1 from the march; blurs and apply still run. |
| ScreenSpace | BlurRadius | 1.0 | ScreenSpaceData.y | Texels between the 12 blur taps. 0 = no blur. Not used by Sharp. |
| ScreenSpace | RenderDistance | 180000 | ScreenSpaceData.z | No contact past it; fades to none over its last 20 %. Blur leaves pixels past it untouched. |
| ScreenSpace | Intensity | 0.315 | ScreenSpaceData.w | Exponent on red and green in every technique's last pass (below 1 lighter). |
| ScreenSpace | NearFade | 400 | ScreenSpaceFade (x = half, y = full; pushed out by the land displacement fade) | Contacts none nearer than x, full from y. 0 = everywhere. |
| ScreenSpace | Sharp | false | technique 2 | One 16-step march + small finish instead of march + two blurs. |
| ScreenSpace | FastPaths | true | techniques 3 / 4 | The march's skips as real branches. Same picture. |
| Main | ForwardShadows | true | macro + ForwardData.x + technique choice | On: object shaders do the cascades (techniques 1, 2, 4). Off: technique 0/3 apply does them. |
| Main | Enabled | true | ShadowFade.y | Off: no map term. |
| Main | SkipWhenFaded | true | C++ skip (section 2) | |
| ShadowMaps | HandoverBlend | 70 | ForwardData.w | Width of the cascade cross-fade band (at least a tenth of the radius, at most the radius). |
| ShadowMaps | SmoothFilter | false | ShadowBlur.z | Cubic 4 x 4 PCF instead of quadratic 3 x 3, as the object shaders. |
| ShadowMaps | CharacterShadowMap | true | macro (restart) | Adds the character map lookup to the deferred apply. |
| ShadowMaps | CharacterShadowMapResolution / Bias / Blend / Margin / Fit / Target / Others | | CharShadowData, box | Map texel, bias, hand-over band, fit (producer side). |
| ShadowMaps | CharacterShadowMapDebug | 0 | CharShadowData.w - 1 | 1: map verdict, 2: map depth, 3: point depth, inside the box, replacing red. |
| ShadowMaps | Anisotropy | 0 | SetCT stage 1 | Dead (the assignment in ShadowsExterior.cpp is commented out); harmless. |
| ShadowMaps | Distance, CascadeResolution, CascadeLambda, PlayerInNear, FitToDepth | | cascade centres, radii, matrices | Producer side; read through the constants. |
| RT | Enabled, Mode, Range | false, 0, 6000 | RtSunReplace.x (Mode 1) | Mode 1: maps = 1 out to 0.85 R, back linearly to full at R. |
| Main.Main.Performance | NvrDxvkPasses | true | DxvkCompute::PassesOn | Vulkan passes (section 7). |
| Main | Darkness, NightMinDarkness, UsePointShadowsDay/Night | | the apply / lamps | Not read here. |

### 5. Passes and outputs

All passes draw the frame quad (`FrameVS` passes position and uv through) into a G16R16 target of the screen size, viewport set to the
drawn part in rect frames. Ping-pong (SunShadows.cpp): pass 1 reads the buffer and writes scratch 0; each next pass reads the
previous target and writes the other scratch; the last writes the buffer. No pass reads its own target. Output: (red, green, 0, 1).

| Index | Name | Passes (pixel function) |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | (none) | ContactMarchPS(false), BlurAcrossPS, BlurDownPS, ApplySunMapsPS |
| 1 | ForwardContactShadows | ContactMarchPS(false), BlurAcrossPS, BlurDownFinalPS |
| 2 | ForwardContactSharp | SharpMarchPS, SharpFinishPS |
| 3 | ContactFast | ContactMarchPS(true), BlurAcrossPS, BlurDownPS, ApplySunMapsPS |
| 4 | ForwardContactShadowsFast | ContactMarchPS(true), BlurAcrossPS, BlurDownFinalPS |

Instruction slots (fxc ps_3_0): march ~376, blur ~365, apply ~770 (~925 with the character map), sharp ~184 (loop), finish ~76.

### 6. Behaviour

#### 6.1 Positions
View position from linear depth z: `x = (ndc.x - P31) z / P11`, `y = (ndc.y - P32) z / P22`, ndc from the screen position of the
texture coordinate (rect-aware). Projection back is the inverse. Camera-relative world position = view position through
`TESR_InvViewTransform` (w = 1). Sky: stored depth >= 0.9999.

#### 6.2 Contact march (ContactMarchPS)
- No contact (red 1) when contacts are off, z >= RenderDistance, sky, or facing <= 0, where facing = saturate(8 N.L) with the view
  normal and the view-space sun. FastPaths: real branch; otherwise computed and selected (same result).
- Four samples at `i * step`, i = 1..4, along the view-space sun from the pixel. step = 400 (= 2000 / 5) x (z / far)^0.6 x
  lerp(min(0.8, z / RenderDistance), 1, blue noise). Weight 1 / i.
- A sample counts (RayBlocked) by how far the ray point lies behind the depth seen at its texel centre: 0 below the bias
  (max(0.01, 0.002 z)), ramping to 1 over one more bias, back to 0 over the last quarter of the thickness. Thickness: 20 units, or
  BackDepth's back face minus the front when it drew one there (never over 20). Points off the drawn part or nearer than the near
  plane count 0. The ramps (instead of steps) keep sub-pixel motion and TAA jitter from flipping samples.
- Contact = 1 - occ^0.3 x facing x NearFade, faded to 1 by smoothstep(0.8 RD, RD, z). Green copied from the source.

#### 6.3 Sharp march (SharpMarchPS) and finish (SharpFinishPS)
- Ray end = pixel + sun x 4000 (z / far)^0.6, pulled back along the ray to the near plane if it would cross it. 16 even screen-space
  steps (t = k / 16) between the pixel and the end's projection, ray depth = 1 / lerp(1/z0, 1/zEnd, t) (perspective-correct),
  weight 1 / (1 + 4t), same RayBlocked and contact formula, no noise.
- Finish: centre red counted twice plus the four direct neighbours whose depth is within max(2 % z, 1); then red AND green raised to
  Intensity.

#### 6.4 Blur (BlurAcrossPS, BlurDownPS, BlurDownFinalPS)
12 taps at k = +-1..+-6 texels x BlurRadius along x or y (rect-clamped), weight exp(-k^2 / 18) x saturate(1 - |plane gap| / tol),
plane gap = distance of the tap's view position from the centre's plane (centre normal), tol = max(1, 0.012 z). The centre counts 1.
Blurs red and green. Sky and pixels at or past RenderDistance are kept. BlurDownFinalPS raises the result to Intensity.

#### 6.5 Apply (ApplySunMapsPS)
`contact = pow(max(src.rg, 1e-6), Intensity)`; `red = min(contact.r, maps)`; a character-map debug view replaces red. Green = contact.g.

#### 6.6 Map term (SunMapsVisibility), = Shadow.hlsl GetSunShadow (PCF) except the fade
- 1 when FORWARD_SHADOWS is compiled and ForwardData.x < 0.5, ShadowFade.y <= 0.5, FormatData.z <= 0.5, FormatData.x != 3, or sky.
- N.L with the world normal and SmoothedSunDir; offsetScale = saturate(1 - N.L).
- Per cascade: texel (world) = 4 R x atlas texel; normal offset = 1.5 texels x offsetScale; slope slack = 2 x atlas texel x
  min(tan, 5) (tan from N.L floored at 0.05); bias = 0.0002 (1 + offsetScale). Compare depth = light z - bias - 1.5 slack.
- Cascade = innermost whose sphere (centre, radius) holds the point; band starts at R - max(0.1 R, min(HandoverBlend, R)),
  fade = smoothstep over the band. Inside the band both this cascade and the next are read at the SAME point (normal offset lerped
  from this cascade's to the next's) and blended by fade; the Middle read inside Near's band keeps Near's slack in world units
  (slack x lerp(R_near / R_middle, 1, fade)). The Lod cascade fades to 1. Past it: 1.
- PCF: quadratic B-spline over the 3 x 3 texels round the point (cubic B-spline 4 x 4, half a texel more slack, with SmoothFilter);
  taps clamped half a texel inside the quadrant.
- RT Mode 1 (RtSunReplace.x = R > 0): maps = lerp(1, maps, saturate((z - 0.85 R) / (0.15 R))). The traced visibility fades to 1
  linearly over the same last 15 % (Rt/RtSunShadow.comp), so cascades and rays hand over without a double shadow or a gap.
- Character map (CHARACTER_SHADOW_MAP, CharShadowData.w > 0), after the RT fade (the rays do not trace actors): point offset by
  1.5 x 2 x halfExtent x map texel x offsetScale; inside the box (edge = max |x|, |y| of its clip position) weight = 1 -
  smoothstep(1 - max(band, 0.01), 1, edge); quadratic 3 x 3 PCF on the map with depth = min(z, 0.9999) - bias - 1.5 x texel x slope;
  result lerp(maps, min(maps, lit), weight). Debug views (w > 1.5) inside the box: 2 verdict, 3 map depth, 4 point depth.
- No sun fade (TESR_ShadowFade.x) here: the exterior apply fades red; applying it here too would fade twice.

#### 6.7 Edge cases
- Sky: contact 1, maps 1, blur keeps it.
- RenderScale rect frames: positions use rectToScreen / screenToRect; every off-centre read is rect-clamped or (march) dropped
  when outside the drawn part.
- TAA jitter: the effects see the unjittered projection while the depth was drawn jittered (TAA.cpp BeginJitter); no jitter
  constant reaches this effect, so nothing is undone. Stability comes from: static (screen-fixed) blue noise, ramps instead of steps
  in the ray test, texel-snapped depth reads, plane-weighted blur, texel-snapped cascades with B-spline PCF and band blending.
- Reversed depth: not relevant (linear depth and projection terms only).
- NaN guards: texel floors (1/16384), radius / band floors, max(..., 1e-6) before pow, non-zero blur weight sum (centre 1),
  1 / max(z, 1e-3) in the projection, ray end clamp at the near plane, normal fallback.
- First-person weapon: whatever CombineDepth gives its pixels; treated as any surface.

### 7. Vulkan path

- `SunShadowsEffect::ExtPingPong` (when `DxvkCompute::PassesOn()`: NvrDxvkPasses on, Vegas Shaders DXVK present, not a rect frame) runs the
  same techniques through the same targets: March (techniques 0, 1, 3, 4 pass 1), Blur (push pass 0 across, 1 down, 2 down +
  Intensity), Apply (`SunShadowsApply-c1` when CHARACTER_SHADOW_MAP was compiled; variants.txt line
  `SunShadowsApply c1 -DCHARACTER_SHADOW_MAP=1`), Sharp, SharpFinish.
- Bindings: set 1 binding 0 depth, 1 atlas, 2 normals, 3 source, 4 noise, 5 character map (depth as stand-in when off); set 2
  binding 0 the 832-byte std140 block (4 cascades, projection, invProjection, view, invView, reciprocalResolution, smoothedSunDir,
  viewSpaceLightDir, formatData, screenSpaceData, shadowFade, shadowBlur, forwardData, center[4], cameraData, flags (x FORWARD_SHADOWS
  compiled, y/z NearFade, w RT range), charToLight, charCenter, charData). The C++ comment "eight matrices: 736 bytes" counts only the
  first block; 832 is right. Push (16 bytes): linear sampler heap index, noise sampler index, blur pass, pad.
- Matrices as stored: GLSL `M[i][j]` = HLSL `M[i][j]`, HLSL `mul(v, M)` = `M * v`, `mul(M, v)` = `v * M`.
- The GLSL is the D3D9 arithmetic; the March is the FastPaths form for every technique (same picture). Differences: no back-face
  thickness (not bound by the C++: always 20 units, as D3D9 with BackDepth off, its default); the rect clamp is the plain half-texel
  clamp to the texture (no TESR_RenderScale in the block, and PassesOn excludes rect frames, so the drawn part is the whole texture).
  The character map is read with the LINEAR sampler at texel centres (= POINT).
- Build: `bash tools/nvr-dxvk/compile-compute.sh SunShadowsMarch SunShadowsBlur SunShadowsApply SunShadowsSharp SunShadowsSharpFinish`.

### 8. Look and cost

Correct look: soft contact darkening where things meet the ground and walls, fading in past NearFade and out toward
RenderDistance, none on faces turned from the sun; cascade shadow edges matching the object shaders' (same filter, bias, bands)
with no pop at cascade borders; the player's face and hair self-shadow sharp in third person with the character map in the
deferred path; no crawling under TAA or camera motion.
Wrong-look signs: bands on lit ground (bias), shadows on the far side of thin walls (thickness), halos across silhouettes (blur
weights), double or missing shadow in RT Mode 1 near R (fade), pops at cascade borders (bands), acne (normal offset / slack).
Cost: 3-4 full-screen passes; march 4 samples, sharp 16; blur 12 depth reads + 12 source reads per direction; apply 9 atlas reads
(18 in a band, 16/32 with SmoothFilter) + 9 for the character map inside its box.

### 9. Fixed and open items

Fixed in this rewrite:
1. Character shadow map was not sampled: now read in the deferred apply (D3D9 and the Vulkan c1 variant), as `CharacterMapShadow`.
2. RT replace range: the two old statements disagreed; decided from RtSunShadows.h ("the deferred apply fades the cascades in only
   where the rays fade out"), the toml (Mode 1: "the cascades fade in only over the last 15 % of Range") and Rt/RtSunShadow.comp
   (rays fade linearly over the last 15 %): maps are 1 inside 0.85 R and come back linearly to full at R.
3. The cascade lookup now matches the object shaders (3 x 3 B-spline, SmoothFilter, slope slack, 1.5-texel offset, bands, slack
   scale) instead of a 2 x 2 compare with a different bias.
4. Normals: the buffer holds VIEW-space normals; the facing test uses them directly and the map lookups the world normal (the old
   spec had them the other way round).
5. `TESR_NoiseSampler` declares its ResourceName, so the blue noise really loads (D3D9 and `ExtFileTexture`).
6. `TESR_CharShadowMap` is declared unconditionally, so `TESR_BackDepthBuffer` is the 7th sampler and binds to stage 6 whatever
   the macro says (stage = declaration index).
7. The apply's map term is gated like `GetSunShadow` (FormatData.x = 3, FormatData.z outdoors, ShadowFade.y).
8. Divide-by-zero guards (texels, radii, bands, projection), texel-snapped depth reads, ramps in the ray test, plane-weighted blur.
9. Vulkan: rewritten for every pass; the rect clamp is present (full-texture clamp, see 7).
10. (upstream 1.1.2, ported 2026-11-22) Exploded casters: weapon animation mods (MMP TOZ-34 / TOZ-66) hide a sub-mesh by
   scaling its bone by 1e-9 and the child by 1e+9; on an NPC not playing that animation the child keeps a ~1e9 world scale and a
   bound billions of units wide, and drew a ~24 m dark circle into the near cascades (Forms.Actors on). `ShadowManager.cpp`
   `IsExplodedShadowCaster` now drops any caster other than land / LOD land whose world scale is non-finite or above 1000, or
   whose world bound is non-finite or wider than 1e6 units (`AccumObject`, `WalkSunObject`; the lamp cube maps too).
   CachedDistantShadows' mover spheres (`CollectMovers`) take only finite bounds of radius <= 2000 and otherwise a 128-unit
   sphere 64 units above the actor's position.

Open / decided, for review:
1. Sharp finish raises green to Intensity too (the old file raised only red). Chosen for one behaviour across techniques, per the
   C++ comment that the passes raise the lamp term to Intensity; with Sharp on, lamp shadows outdoors look as with Sharp off.
2. Vulkan back-face thickness: the C++ binds no TESR_BackDepthBuffer / TESR_BackDepthData for the Vulkan passes; with
   `[Main.Main.Quality] BackDepth` on, D3D9 and Vulkan differ on thin occluders. Needs a C++ binding (slot 6 + one vec4) to close.
3. Ray lengths read "distance" in the old formulas as the depth's share of the far distance (400 x (z/far)^0.6 per step, 4000 x
   (z/far)^0.6 for Sharp), giving contact rays of tens of units at mid range; with raw view distance they would be kilometres.
   They therefore change with the far distance.
4. No sun fade here (the apply fades); if a reader of red other than the apply needs the faded value, it must fade itself (as the
   apply and its copies do).
5. TAA jitter is not undone (no constant); fine under TAA, a sub-pixel offset of the contact term otherwise.
6. `NvrDxvkKeepD3D9` is not checked by `ExtPingPong` (C++, unchanged).
7. `[ShadowMaps] Anisotropy` is dead in the C++ (unchanged).
8. Not measured in game (no game runs in this wave).

## Underwater

*README title: Underwater effect spec*

Status 2026-11-02 (branch claude/rw3-lens): the D3D9 effect `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/Underwater.fx.hlsl` and the four Vulkan passes (`Compute/UnderwaterGodrays.frag`, `UnderwaterBlur.frag`, `UnderwaterWater.frag`, `UnderwaterDistortion.frag`, shared `Underwater.glsl`) were written fresh from this spec. The text below describes the new shader. Fixed from the old one: caustic projection plane (now the water surface height, TESR_WaterSettings x, not the underwater flag z: open question 1); sun vertical component guarded (open question 3); no horizontal-view division (open question 4). TESR_FogColor is no longer declared (it was never used; open question 7). Not changed: the settings, the pass order, the wobble clock (open question 2).

Source read: `the source tree` (read only). Covers the D3D9 effect, its Vulkan (Vegas Shaders DXVK) port, and the settings it reads.

### 1. Purpose

When the camera is under water, or the player is in water, the picture is turned into what water looks like from inside. The colour drains toward the water's deep and shallow tints, distant scenery fades into that murk, and surfaces below the waterline shimmer and drift. Sunlit floors show moving caustic light (only where the point-light shadow map says the light reaches), and faint shafts of light fall through the water. The whole picture gets a very small wobble that reads as ripples. Anything above the surface is left exactly as it was before this effect ran.

### 2. When it runs

- Switched by `[_Shaders.Underwater.Status]` `Enabled` (bool, default true).
- Draws when the sky reports the camera as underwater (the game's sky flag) or the player is in water (the player-in-water flag). No other condition: the effect has no night, interior, exterior, menu or dialogue gate of its own.
- Interiors: it runs too. The water settings come from the interior section (section 4). The point-shadow buffer is cleared to "no shadow" indoors for this effect (ShaderManager, interior forward-shadow block).
- Night: no special rule. The sun vector and sun colour are used as they are at that time.
- Frame order (`src/core/ShaderManager.cpp`, scene-effects block): it runs after the lighting and shading effects (sun shadows, skin scattering, clouds, interior and exterior shadows, snow accumulation, ambient occlusion, ambient light, bounce light, blood flow, wet world, flashlight, specular) and after the combined bounce/specular step. It runs before reflections, volumetric fog, volumetric light, sandstorm, god rays, exposure, lens and LUT. The volumetric fog therefore hazes its output afterwards.
- It is listed in the effects that stop the interior forward-shadow fold from being used when it draws (`effectsBetween`).
- The render path checks the enabled state and ShouldRender. If either is false, the effect draws nothing and the frame is unchanged.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures (D3D9 file; sampler registers are fixed)

| Slot | Name | Holds | Filter / address |
|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | The current frame (the chain's image). Later passes read the god-ray image from it. | linear, clamp |
| s1 | TESR_SourceBuffer | The frame as it was when this effect started. The render path refreshes it for this effect because the effect samples it. | linear, clamp |
| s2 | TESR_DepthBuffer | Linear depth from CombineDepth (view distance divided by the far distance; the effect multiplies by the far distance). | linear, clamp |
| s3 | TESR_WavesSampler | Water normal texture `Water\water_NRM.dds`. Declared and loaded, but not read by any pass (kept so the C++ binding still fits). | anisotropic, wrap |
| s4 | TESR_NormalsBuffer | Per-pixel normals from the Normals effect (view space, packed to 0..1). | point, clamp |
| s5 | TESR_BlueNoiseSampler | `Effects\bluenoise256.dds` (noise for ray jitter). | point, wrap |
| s6 | TESR_CausticsSampler | `Water\caust_001.dds` (caustic pattern; red channel used). | anisotropic, wrap |
| s7 | TESR_PointShadowBuffer | Point-light shadow mask: red = the light reaches the point (caustics); green = a second shadow term for surface absorption. | anisotropic, wrap |

The depth, normals and point-shadow textures are the same objects other effects use: CombineDepth's depth, the Normals effect's normals, and the point-shadow buffer.

#### Constants (D3D9 names; registers come from the constant table, not fixed)

Units: game units for distances and heights. Colours are the game's own values; the effect linearises the colour inputs itself.

- TESR_ReciprocalResolution: xy = 1 / frame width and height (texel size).
- TESR_SunDirection: xyz = unit vector to the sun (z up).
- TESR_SunColor: rgb = sun colour; w = sun glare (not read here).
- TESR_GameTime: x = game time (drives caustic drift); z = the frame value used for the wobble (see section 9).
- TESR_WaterCoefficients: xyz = light extinction per channel (from `inExtCoeff_R/G/B`); w = scattering amount (from `inScattCoeff`).
- TESR_WaterFog: x = near distance of the underwater fog; y = far distance (both from the water form's underwater fog values). z and w are not read.
- TESR_WaterVolume: x = caustic strength (from `causticsStrength`, multiplied by the weather's sun glare); y = shore factor (not read here); z = turbidity (from `turbidity`); w = god-ray strength (from `causticsStrengthS`).
- TESR_WaveParams: x = choppiness (not read by the active passes); y = wave width (not read by the active passes); z = wave speed (from `waveSpeed`; sets caustic drift and wobble rate); w = reflectivity (not read here).
- TESR_WaterSettings: x = water surface height (from the water height at the player); y = depth darkness (from `depthDarkness`); z = the underwater flag (0 or 1), read as the height the caustic projection is taken to (see section 9); w = refraction power (not read here).
- TESR_WaterShallowColor, TESR_WaterDeepColor: rgb = the water form's shallow and deep tints.
- TESR_HorizonColor, TESR_SkyColor: rgb = the game's horizon and zenith sky colours.
- TESR_FogColor: no longer declared in the new shader (it had no effect on the picture).
- TESR_CameraPosition: xyz = camera world position.
- TESR_CameraData: x = near distance (not read here); y = far distance (the depth scale).
- TESR_ProjectionTransform, TESR_ViewTransform: 4x4 matrices as the engine stores them.
- TESR_RenderScale (rect frames only): xy = the drawn part's share of the frame. Used by the coordinate helpers and the clips (section 6).
- Not read by this effect: TESR_WaterLODColor, the TESR_Placed* water constants, TESR_DebugVar.

The water constants are registered by the Water shader (`src/effects/Water/Water.cpp`). This effect has no constants of its own; its own constant block and settings update are empty. The effect's constants are refreshed from the Water shader's values even when the Water shader itself is disabled (ShaderManager, underwater check).

### 4. Settings

Settings the effect reads, by TOML section. The defaults file does not state ranges, so none are given.

| Section | Key | Type | Default | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[_Shaders.Underwater.Status]` | Enabled | bool | true | Switches the effect. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Default]` | causticsStrength | float | 3.0 | Brightness of caustic light on sunlit floors (also multiplied by the weather's sun glare). High values wash the floor out. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Default]` | causticsStrengthS | float | 0.2 | Strength of the god-ray shafts added to the water. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Default]` | inExtCoeff_R, inExtCoeff_G, inExtCoeff_B | float | 1.0 each | Per-channel tint: how fast each colour is absorbed. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Default]` | inScattCoeff | float | 1.0 | Light scattered into the murk. Higher = darker, less bright water. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Default]` | depthDarkness | float | 1.0 | How much light is absorbed with depth (darkening of the bottom and far water). |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Default]` | turbidity | float | 3.0 | Murkiness. Higher = thicker fog and shorter sight distance. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Default]` | waveSpeed | float | 0.7 | Speed of caustic drift and wobble. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Interiors]` | depthDarkness | float | 10.0 | Depth darkness indoors. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Interiors]` | turbidity | float | 1.0 | Murkiness indoors. |
| `[_Shaders.Water.Interiors]` | waveSpeed | float | 0.7 | Wave speed indoors. |
| `[Main.Main.Performance]` | NvrDxvkPasses | bool | false | Runs the Vulkan port instead of the D3D9 passes (section 7). |
| `[Main.Main.Performance]` | NvrDxvkKeepD3D9 | text | empty | Names effects that keep their D3D9 passes while NvrDxvkPasses is on. Listing this effect forces D3D9. |
| `[Main.Main.Water]` | RemoveUnderwater | bool | false | Removes the game's own underwater fog. The defaults file says it is not compatible with this effect. |

Keys in the Water sections that this effect does not read: choppiness, waveWidth, reflectivity, shoreFactor, shoreMovement, refractionPower.

Section choice: the water constants come from `[_Shaders.Water.Default]` outdoors and `[_Shaders.Water.Interiors]` indoors. Blood and lava water types use their own sections. Those sections are not in the defaults file (see section 9). The Interiors section does not define causticsStrength, causticsStrengthS, inExtCoeff_R/G/B or inScattCoeff, so indoors those values come from whatever the settings reader returns for a missing key (see section 9).

### 5. Passes and outputs

The C++ asks for technique index 0 only. The D3D9 technique is unnamed and has five passes (index 0 to 4). The Vulkan port has the same five passes in the same order.

| Pass | D3D9 pass index | Vulkan pass | Reads | Writes | Region |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1, god rays | 0 | UnderwaterGodrays | current image (outside the region), depth, blue noise, caustics | the chain's next image | upper-left quarter (the ray image) |
| 2, blur vertical | 1 | UnderwaterBlur, mask (0, 1) | current image | the chain's next image | upper-left quarter; the rest keeps the current image |
| 3, blur horizontal | 2 | UnderwaterBlur, mask (1, 0) | current image | the chain's next image | same as pass 2 |
| 4, water | 3 | UnderwaterWater | current image (blurred rays), source frame, depth, normals, caustics, point shadows | the chain's next image | whole frame |
| 5, wobble | 4 | UnderwaterDistortion | current image (the water result), source frame, depth | the game's render target (last pass, written directly) | whole frame |

Format: the chain's images and the target use the frame chain's format (not checked here). Alpha is 1 on every pass output.

Pass 1 detail: the ray image is computed at half resolution per axis and stored in the upper-left quarter of the frame. Its own 0 to 1 coordinates cover that quarter. Outside the quarter the pass returns the current image unchanged.

Passes 2 and 3 detail: a 12-tap blur along one axis, taps at 1 to 6 steps either side of the centre with the step set to two full-resolution texels (so the reach is up to 12 texels). The centre weight is about 0.115, and the twelve tap weights are fixed in the shader file. Taps that land outside the quarter are dropped and the weights renormalised. The blur only changes the quarter.

Pass 5 returns the source frame unchanged where the pixel is above water.

The source frame copy: the render path copies the current frame into the source buffer before this effect's passes (the Vulkan port does the same copy in its first pass).

### 6. Behaviour details

- Above-water test, per pixel. Let E = camera height, W = water surface height (TESR_WaterSettings x), P = the pixel's world height. If E is below W - 4, every pixel is treated as underwater. Otherwise, if E is above W + 4, the pixel is above water. Otherwise (camera near the surface), the pixel is above water only if P is above W - 0.6. Above-water pixels return the source frame in passes 4 and 5, so the effect is a no-op there. Passes 1 to 3 still compute (cost) but are not seen above water.
- Sky: there is no sky mask. Sky pixels read the far-plane depth, so the far fog treatment (fade to the fog colour past 5000 units) turns them into fog colour when the camera is under water.
- Ray reading: each full-frame pixel reads the god-ray image at half its own coordinates. The ray image covers the whole picture once, computed at half resolution per axis. The shafts follow the whole screen and are never seen as a quarter block.
- Water maths in words: the fog colour is the deep tint for the lower view and the shallow tint (plus a little sky) toward the horizon. It is scaled by the sun's brightness, a sun-direction factor (base 0.4, rising with alignment to the sun), and the extinction tint. A height fog that thins with height (density 0.08, falloff 0.00225, fixed in the shader) darkens water below the camera. The sky colour for the fog tint is built from the horizon and zenith colours with a sun-glow lobe (power 12).
- Caustics: a point is projected toward the sun onto the water surface plane (TESR_WaterSettings x; was the underwater flag, see section 9). The sun's vertical component is floored at 0.05 for the projection, and caustics and god rays fade out as the sun goes below about 0.17 (saturate(sun z x 6)), and the caustic pattern is sampled twice (two scales, the second 1.2 times the first, drifting in opposite directions at a speed from game time and wave speed). The lower of the two red values is raised to 1.3 and multiplied by 4. Caustic light is added only where the floor faces up and faces the sun (smooth steps on the normal), where it is close below the surface (fades to nothing 800 units down), within 6000 units of the camera, and where the point-shadow red channel is lit.
- Surface absorption uses the point-shadow green channel: lit water keeps its colour, shadowed water darkens toward the deep tint. The strength fades out by 6000 units.
- Scattering: the scattering amount (one minus the fog colour, times the scattering coefficient) mixes into the fog colour by a linear distance factor between TESR_WaterFog x and y. A thickness exponent from turbidity sets how sharp the falloff is, and a turbidity weight saturates as turbidity grows.
- Far fog: past about 5000 units a steep curve pushes the colour fully to the fog colour, so far geometry loses its detail.
- God-ray add: the ray value (red channel of the ray image) times the sun colour times the god-ray strength multiplies the colour. It is applied after scattering and before the encode step.
- Encode and dither: the colour is linearised at the start and encoded back at the end. A fixed 4x4 ordered-dither threshold pattern (values about 1/17 to 16/17) is added as a fraction of one 8-bit step, to hide banding. The pattern index follows the pixel position modulo 4.
- Wobble (pass 5): inside the water, the source sample is shifted by a small sine offset in x and a cosine offset in y, amplitude 0.001 of the frame. The phase is the wobble value times 3, plus the screen position on the other axis times 60, so the ripple pattern moves across the picture. In a rect frame the amplitude is multiplied by RenderScale's x and y (D3D9 only), so its on-screen size stays the same.
- Rect frames (RenderScale below 1): the depth and view-ray helpers map texture coordinates to the drawn part before reading. The god-ray pass and the blur are clipped to the drawn part. The Vulkan port is not used in rect frames (its pass check needs RenderScale at 1), so this is D3D9 only.
- Jitter (TAA, FSR, DLSS): the effect does no jitter correction. It reads depth and the current frame at the uv it is given. Not verified visually.
- Reversed depth: none. The depth input is CombineDepth's linear value; the effect never reads raw projected depth.
- HDR vs LDR: the effect works on sRGB-encoded values with linearisation at the boundaries. There is no HDR path. Values above 1 are not clamped after the god-ray add or caustic add, so bright results may clip at the encode step.
- NaN and infinity: the new shader has no division by the view ray's vertical component (the fog uses dot products only), and the sun's vertical component is floored at 0.05 in the caustic projection. The Vulkan side clamps the fog amount; the caustic power keeps the sign of its input.
- First-person weapon: no mask. Whatever is in the frame at this point is tinted and wobbled. Not verified.
- Interior underwater: the water values come from the interior section. Sun direction and colour indoors come from whatever the sky constants hold. Not verified.

### 7. Vulkan path

Files (`src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/`):
- `Underwater.glsl`: shared part (the std140 constant block, texel helpers, depth and normal helpers, caustic function, sky-colour function, above-water test). Each pass includes it after the common shader include.
- `UnderwaterGodrays.frag`: pass 1.
- `UnderwaterBlur.frag`: passes 2 and 3 (one file; the mask is pushed per pass).
- `UnderwaterWater.frag`: pass 4.
- `UnderwaterDistortion.frag`: pass 5.
- Compiled `.spv` files exist next to the four `.frag` passes. The shared `.glsl` is not compiled on its own.

Selection (the frame-chain effect draw path in `src/core/EffectRecord.cpp`): the Vulkan port runs only when all of these hold: `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses` is on; the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is available; no rect frame is active (RenderScale at 1); the effect is not named in `NvrDxvkKeepD3D9`; the technique is not covered by the SM6 switch; the effect has not failed earlier in the session; the target belongs to the frame chain; and the technique index is 0 with no target clear. If any check fails, the effect's Vulkan routine returns false and the D3D9 technique runs. Pipelines are created once, on first use, with the pass names above. If a pipeline, sampler or required texture is missing, the frame is left as it was and the D3D9 technique runs. Required textures for the port: the depth, normals and point-shadow textures, and the caustic and blue-noise file textures (the caustic texture is sampled with mips; the blue noise at level 0).

Constants: one uniform buffer (set 2, binding 0, std140), filled every frame from the same registered constants the D3D9 technique uploads, in this order: reciprocal resolution, sun direction, game time, water coefficients, sun colour, water fog, water volume, wave params, water settings, shallow colour, deep colour, horizon colour, sky colour, camera position, camera data (15 vec4s, 240 bytes), then projection (4x4, 64 bytes at offset 240) and view (4x4, 64 bytes at offset 304). Total 368 bytes. The matrices are copied as the engine stores them.

Bindings per pass:
- Godrays: set 1 bindings 0 to 3 = current image, depth, blue noise, caustics (sampled images). Set 2 binding 0 = constants. Samplers come from the sampler heap by index in push data: offset 0 = linear clamp, offset 4 = point wrap, offset 8 = anisotropic wrap. Push block 16 bytes: linearIndex (0), noiseIndex (4), causticsIndex (8), pad (12).
- Blur: set 1 binding 0 = current image; set 2 binding 0 = constants; sampler index at push offset 8 = linear clamp. Push block 16 bytes: offset mask as two floats (0 and 4), samplerIndex (8), pad (12).
- Water: set 1 bindings 0 to 5 = current image, source frame, depth, normals, caustics, point shadows. Set 2 binding 0 = constants. Samplers: offset 0 = linear clamp, offset 4 = point clamp, offset 8 = anisotropic wrap. Push block 16 bytes: linearIndex (0), pointIndex (4), anisoIndex (8), pad (12).
- Distortion: set 1 bindings 0 to 2 = current image, source frame, depth. Set 2 binding 0 = constants. Sampler: offset 0 = linear clamp. Push block 16 bytes: linearIndex (0), pad (4 to 15).

Differences from D3D9 that the port makes: the ray pass copies the current image outside the quarter with a direct read instead of the D3D9 pre-fill; the wobble is not scaled by RenderScale (the port is not used in rect frames); the ray region test is done directly, not with a clip.

### 8. Look and cost

Correct result:
- Murky water with a clear blue-green (or the water form's) cast, darker with depth and distance. Far scenery fades into the fog colour and loses detail.
- Sunlit floor shows soft, moving caustic net where the light reaches. In shade it is absent.
- Faint, moving light shafts over the whole picture, not confined to a corner.
- Subtle ripple wobble, no tearing or streaks.
- Above the surface: identical to the frame without the effect. Near the surface there is a short transition band (about 4 units either side of the water height), not a hard seam.
- No visible banding in the murk gradients.

Typical wrong results:
- Rays confined to one quarter of the screen, or a hard edge at the middle of the screen.
- Caustics far too bright, especially with a high causticsStrength on a bright (high sun glare) day.
- Sky visible under water as sky colour (it should go to fog colour).
- Surface pixels tinted from above the water.
- Tearing or streaks from the wobble.
- Murk too clean (scattering or turbidity missing), or too dark (depth darkness too high), especially at interior and exterior changes.
- Black or noisy pixels from divisions by zero at horizontal views or with the sun below the horizon.

Cost class: multi-pass (five passes), per-pixel loop. Pass 1 is a 49-step loop over the quarter of the frame, with two caustic reads and one noise read per step. Passes 2 and 3 are 12-tap blurs over the quarter. Pass 4 is a full-frame pass with about 10 texture reads (the caustic adds two more). Pass 5 is full-frame with about 3 reads. Passes 1 and 4 dominate. The passes run whenever the effect is enabled and its condition holds, even if most of the view is above water.

### 9. Open questions

1. FIXED 2026-11-02: caustic projection now uses the water surface height (TESR_WaterSettings x). The old use of z (the underwater flag) placed caustics wrongly at every water height.
2. TESR_GameTime z: the wobble phase multiplies this value by the wave speed and 1.5. That is a smooth clock only if z is an accumulating time. The Vulkan file calls it "frame time". Which is it?
3. GUARDED 2026-11-02: the projection divides by max(sun z, 0.05), and caustics and shafts fade out with saturate(sun z x 6). The sun vector at night and indoors is still not confirmed.
4. FIXED 2026-11-02: no division by the view ray's vertical component in the new shader.
5. Interior and fallback values: `[_Shaders.Water.Interiors]` does not define causticsStrength, causticsStrengthS, inExtCoeff_R/G/B or inScattCoeff. What does the settings reader return for a missing key? Same for the blood and lava sections, which are not in the defaults file.
6. Ranges for the water keys are not stated in the defaults file, and no clamping was confirmed in the reader.
7. Unused values (TESR_FogColor is now dropped from the shader; the others are unchanged): WaterVolume y, WaveParams x, y and w, WaterSettings w, and the water shader's shore and refraction values are not used by the active passes. Confirm no other pass depends on them before a new shader drops them.
8. The wave normal texture (sampler register 3) is still declared and loaded in the new D3D9 file but no pass reads it. Vulkan does not bind it. Whether the C++ needs it at all is still open.
9. Vulkan vertex stage: the Compute folder has no vertex shader; the full-screen stage is assumed to come from the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension. Confirm its name and coordinate convention.
10. First-person weapon and held items: whether they are drawn before this effect (and so get tinted and wobbled). The effect has no mask. Not checked.
11. The scene-effects block that calls this effect may have an enclosing condition beyond this effect's own. Not traced.
12. TAA, FSR and DLSS jitter: no jitter correction on the depth read. Whether this shows as shimmer on the wobble or caustics has not been checked in the picture.
13. Partial failure in the Vulkan port: if passes 2, 3 or 4 fail after pass 1 has drawn, the frame keeps a partial result, and the D3D9 technique would then run the god rays again on top. Whether this happens in practice is not known.

## VolumetricClouds

*README title: VolumetricClouds - notes*

The shader's own header (`src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/VolumetricClouds.fx.hlsl`) describes the passes. This file holds what the header does
not: how the composite tells sky from surface, and the game's MSAA.

### Sky or surface: the composite

The composite (technique 0, full resolution) puts the clouds over a pixel only where it is sky. Without the game's MSAA that is the depth
alone: `TESR_DepthBuffer` at the far plane. An alpha-tested cutout (a pylon's lattice, foliage) writes depth only where its texture is kept,
so the holes read as sky and get the clouds; that case is exact.

With the game's MSAA (`iMultiSample` above 1; since 1.0.4 it is no longer blocked) and transparency multisampling
(`bTransparencyMultisampling`, the TMS shaders), the game draws cutouts with alpha to coverage: a pixel where the texture's alpha is 0.4
gets about 40 % of its samples. Far off, where the texture's smaller mips average the holes into the bars, the whole outline of a lattice or
a bush is such mixed pixels. One depth per pixel cannot describe that: the NVR DXVK fork resolves the nearest sample (the whole mixed area
reads as surface), stock DXVK and native D3D9 sample 0 (each pixel surface or sky by chance).

- 2026-11-04 (PR #5): `TESR_SkyCoverage` (core/SkyCoverage.h): a far-plane quad drawn against the multisampled depth before the resolve
  gives each pixel's exact sky share. The composite gave the clouds over that share, but only to pixels that are surface by depth, and it
  took the colour of the sky behind from pixels 1-2 px away that are sky by depth, else the pixel's own colour (part bar).
- 2026-11-05 `[Main.Main.Quality] MsaaEdgeFix` (on by default, live; off = the 2026-11-04 behaviour): every pixel by its exact share
  (`Includes/MsaaSkyEdge.hlsl`): all sky (share >= 0.998) the clouds as before, none (<= 0.002) the cloud shadow as before, mixed the clouds
  over the share only, also for pixels that are sky by depth (sample-0 resolves gave them the clouds whole, over the bars). The sky behind
  comes from pixels all sky by their share, 1, 2, 4 and 8 px away along the axes (a lattice far off is all mixed pixels: within 2 px there
  was often none). Only while this frame's shares were made (`TESR_GameMultisample.y`, set by SkyCoverage::Draw).
- `[Main.Main.Quality] MsaaEdgeDebugView 1` (test only): the composite shows red where the depth says sky, green the exact sky share.
- The Vulkan composite (`Compute/VolumetricCloudsComposite.frag`, only with `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkClouds`) had no sky shares at
  all; since 2026-11-05 it has MsaaEdgeFix (binding 5 `TESR_SkyCoverage`, `TESR_GameMultisample` at the end of the uniform block), not
  the 2026-11-04 form.

Bench 2026-11-05 (og-desert, cloudy, lattice pylon heads against a cloud band, `bench/foundations/lists/alphacut-fix.txt`): with MSAA 4
the bars under the cloud brightened +56 (fork) / +64 (native) luma with the 2026-11-04 composite, +44 / +54 with MsaaEdgeFix (the bars
keep their dark lattice instead of turning cream). Without MSAA on vs off differ only by noise (|diff| 0.12).

Left: a mixed pixel takes no cloud shadow on its surface part (as before).

## VolumetricFog

*README title: VolumetricFog - effect spec (rewritten 2026-11-02 for the wave-3 shader on branch claude/rw3-fog-vf)*

Describes the shader in `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/VolumetricFog.fx.hlsl` and its Vulkan passes `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/VolumetricFog.glsl`,
`VolumetricFogEstimate.frag`, `VolumetricFogApply.frag` (written from this spec's first version and `src/effects/VolumetricFog/VolumetricFog.cpp/.h`; no
upstream shader code was read). Values are the shipped defaults from `resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml` unless a code clamp
or fallback is stated. The defaults file writes its section headers with a leading underscore (`[_Shaders.VolumetricFog.Main]`); the
code asks for the same sections without it. Distances are game units (70 units = 1 m).

---

### 1. Purpose

The fog is the atmospheric haze over the whole picture. It blends each pixel toward a fog colour according to how far away it is, how
high it sits and how high the camera is, the weather, the time of day and a drifting density pattern, so distant terrain fades into the
sky and the air has patches of thicker fog that drift with the wind. Sunlight in the fog is shaded by the sun's shadow (the cascades, or
the volumetric light's froxel grid when it is on), so fog in shade is darker and glows toward a low sun. Near the horizon it also hides
the join between terrain and sky. It is the last large picture-wide effect before the volumetric light, sandstorm, bloom and the rest of
the post chain, and its pass can also apply the sun-shadow, interior darkening, AO and volumetric-light composites that come right
before or after it, saving their own full-screen passes.

### 2. When it runs

**Registration and enable.** Registered as `VolumetricFog`; the on/off switch is `[_Shaders.VolumetricFog.Status] Enabled` (default
true, read by the generic effect loader).

**Global gates (ShaderManager).** Nothing runs when the main render-effects switch is off, when the player has no parent cell, or while
the overlay (terminal / lockpicking) background is showing.

**Effect gate.** `ShouldRender` is false only when the camera is underwater. Render also returns at once when the effect is not enabled
or the effect file did not load.

**Exterior and interior.** `GameState.isExterior` picks the settings section (`Main` outdoors, `Interiors` indoors). Outdoor-only keys
are forced to 0 indoors by the C++ (see 4). The shader's `isExterior` (TESR_VolumetricFogWeather.y) is 0 indoors, so the sun, sky SH,
physical sky, aerial tint, horizon fog, EdgeAA, night terms, game fog floor, height layer and froxel-grid air all drop out; indoors the
fog's sky colours are the cell's fog colour (the C++ comment in UpdateSettings), so the shader uses TESR_FogSkyColor for all of them.

**Day and night.** `nightShare` = isExterior x (1 - smoothstep(0.4, 0.8, sun amount)): 0 by day and indoors, 1 at night. Every Night key
fades in with it. The sunlight terms use `dayFade` = smoothstep(0.1, 0.6, sun amount).

**Moon.** C++ derives the night ambient floor (TESR_VolumetricFogGlobal.z) from the days passed and the moon's phase length (the same
cosine as the exterior shadows) times 1 - `[Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] NightMinDarkness`.

**Weather.** `WeatherFilterBlend` (TESR_VolumetricFogWeather.x, animated over 0.1 game hours) drops to 0 when it rains with
RainyDisablesSkyFilter on or is cloudy with CloudyDisablesSkyFilter on; at 0 the sky dome is fogged too (overcast look).

**Frame order (ShaderManager.cpp).** After the physical sky table and the volumetric clouds; after AmbientLight / AO and the sun-shadow
apply (which may be folded into the fog pass); after underwater and reflections; after the volumetric light's froxel grid when its
Froxels mode is on (technique 8). Before the volumetric light's composite (skipped when the fog did it: `gridApplied`), the sandstorm,
TAA, bloom and the later effects.

**Composite folding.** When nothing between them reads the picture, ShaderManager leaves the sun-shadow apply, the interior darkening
(InteriorShadowFold) and the deferred AO combine to the fog: technique CompositeFog / CompositeFogInterior applies them in the
reconstruct pass (shadows or interior darkening, then AO and the sky take, then the fog). If the fog fails, ShaderManager runs the
separate passes and the fog again. When effects between them keep the composite out, ApplyShadowAO does the shadow and AO in one pass.

### 3. Inputs

#### 3a. Textures and samplers

TESR_ samplers bind by their declaration order (s0..s5); NVR_ ones are bound by the C++ to fixed stages. Every read is an explicit
level-0 read (tex2Dlod / tex3Dlod) so it is legal inside dynamic branches.

| Stage | Name | Holds | Declared filter / address | Read by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| s0 | TESR_SourceBuffer | The scene (gamma-encoded HDR). The dedicated, composite and ShadowAO passes bind the current image here; the full-res and packed passes the chain's copy | LINEAR, CLAMP | all passes |
| s1 | TESR_RenderedBuffer | Packed path only: the packed coefficients in its top-left quarter | POINT, CLAMP | PackedFog apply |
| s2 | TESR_DepthBuffer | CombineDepth: R = view z / far (1 = sky) | LINEAR, CLAMP | all fog passes, composites |
| s3 | TESR_ShadowAtlas | Sun cascade atlas (VSM / EVSM2 / EVSM4 / PCF by TESR_ShadowFormatData.x) | LINEAR, CLAMP | fog sun visibility |
| s4 | TESR_NormalsBuffer | View-space normals (n x 0.5 + 0.5); turned to world with GetWorldNormalLod | LINEAR, CLAMP | cascade normal offset, water test |
| s5 | TESR_PointShadowBuffer | Shadow buffer: R sun term (indoors the lamp mask), G lamp term | LINEAR, CLAMP | sun composite, interior composite |
| s6 | NVR_FogBuffer | Half size: fog multiply (rgb) and the depth it was made at (a) | POINT, CLAMP | reconstruct |
| s7 | NVR_FogBufferAdd | Half size: fog add (rgb) | POINT, CLAMP | reconstruct |
| s8 | NVR_AOBuffer | AO result: R AO, G its depth in game units | LINEAR, CLAMP | AO composite |
| s9 | NVR_FogNoise | Data\Textures\Effects\NoiseVolume.dds, green = fractal noise | LINEAR (mips), WRAP u/v/w | density noise |
| s10 | NVR_FogSkyView | Physical sky table TESR_SkyViewLUT (128 x 64), bound only while SkyView draws it | LINEAR, CLAMP | PHYSICAL_SKY_ON builds |
| s11 | NVR_FogGrid | The volumetric light's froxel grid (8 x 8 atlas of 64 slices), bound only in RenderDedicated | LINEAR, CLAMP | estimate (FogVolume), reconstruct (VL composite) |
| s12 | NVR_SkyVis | Skylighting v2: R sky factor, B sky visibility (AO target size) | POINT, CLAMP | AO composite sky take |
| s13 | NVR_SunFar | SunFar's land / cloud shadow factor (rgb) | POINT, CLAMP | sun composite |
| s14 | NVR_FogLamps | LampHaze's lamp table, 16 x 2 A32B32G32R32F (row 0: lamp relative to the camera + reach, row 1: colour linear x dimmer), bound only while LampHaze is on this frame | POINT, CLAMP | estimate, full and combine passes (LampHazeLight) |

#### 3b. Constants

**The fog's own (RegisterConstants; components as the C++ fills them):**

| Name | Components |
|---|---|
| TESR_VolumetricFogDensity | x BaseDensity, y WeatherImpact, z MorningFogDip (0 indoors), w SunriseSunsetBoost (0 indoors) |
| TESR_VolumetricFogShape | x HeightFalloff, or -1 / LayerThickness while HeightLayer is on; y MaxHeight, or the layer's base in world z (camera z added with LayerFollowsCamera, every frame); z Extinction; w Inscattering |
| TESR_VolumetricFogWind | xy cos / sin of WindAngle, z WindSpeed, w NoiseScale |
| TESR_VolumetricFogScatter | x PhaseAsymmetry (0 indoors), y ShadowStrength (0 indoors), z NoiseStrength, w HeightInfluence |
| TESR_VolumetricFogWeather | x WeatherFilterBlend, y isExterior, z the Sky effect is on (its SH is fresh), w FogSaturation |
| TESR_VolumetricFogAerial / AerialTint | AerialStrength, AerialRangeStart, AerialTintBlend, AerialDayFadeStart / manual tint rgb (all 0 indoors) |
| TESR_VolumetricFogDistant | x DistantFogRange, y DistantFogBlend, z DistantFogHeight, w EdgeAA (all 0 indoors) |
| TESR_VolumetricFogGlobal | x Amount, y Night DisableSkyMask, z moon night floor, w MinDensityFloor (0 indoors) |
| TESR_VolumetricFogNight | x DensityScale, y AmountScale, z HeightFalloffScale, w MaxHeightOffset |
| TESR_VolumetricFogNightScatter | x NoiseStrengthScale, y WindSpeedScale, z ExtinctionScale, w InscatteringScale |
| TESR_VolumetricFogNightTint | xyz night tint, w NightAmbientStrength |
| TESR_VolumetricFogLamps | x LampHaze (0 = off: outdoors, LampHaze 0, LampHazeMaxLamps 0, ClassicLook, LegacyFog, no lamp listed), y lamps in NVR_FogLamps, z LampHazeAnisotropy, w LampHazeCore |
| TESR_VolumetricFogSun | x SunGlow (0-2; 0 indoors), y WeatherFogColor (0 indoors), z GameFogFloor this frame (its Night scale faded in; 0 off / indoors), w the weather's raw fog near |
| TESR_VolumetricFogLow / High / Simple / Blend / Height / Data | Legacy keys (filled only while LegacyFog is on): Low x FogSaturation y WeatherImpact z DistantFogRange w SunPower; High x HeightFogDensity y HeightFogFalloff z HeightFogDist w HeightFogSkyColor; Simple x Extinction y Inscattering z FogNight (1 indoors) w SimpleFogSkyColor; Blend x DistantFogBlend y HeightFogBlend z HeightFogRolloff w SimpleFogBlend; Height x DistantFogHeight y HeightFogHeight z SimpleFogHeight w isExterior; Data x MinimumBaseFog y HeightFogSaturation z Amount w HeightFogInscattering |

**Registered by other systems:** TESR_FogData (x near, y far, z sun glare, w fog power), TESR_FogWeatherColor, TESR_FogHorizonColor,
TESR_FogSkyLowColor, TESR_FogSkyColor (the fog's copies with the sky look; Sky.cpp UpdateFogSky), TESR_ReciprocalResolution,
TESR_GameTime (x ms), TESR_SmoothedSunDir, TESR_SunPosition, TESR_SunColor, TESR_SunsetColor, TESR_SunAmbient, TESR_SkyData (x
atmosphere), TESR_SunAmount (x), TESR_PBRData (z direct scale, w ambient scale), TESR_SkyIrradiance[9] / TESR_SkyIrradianceGame[9]
(order-2 SH with band weights and 1/pi folded in: evaluating them gives radiance), the four cascade matrices and centres,
TESR_ShadowFormatData, TESR_ShadowBlur, TESR_ShadowFade (x sun-shadow fade, y maps on, z lamp shadows on, w interior distance),
TESR_ShadowData (y Darkness), TESR_WaterSettings, TESR_AmbientOcclusionAOData (z floor), TESR_AmbientOcclusionData (y LumThreshold,
z BlurDropThreshold), TESR_VolumetricLightData (x strength, y darkening, z air density, w march distance), TESR_VolumetricLightData2
(y g, z fade), TESR_VolumetricLightGridLayout (xy cells, zw atlas texel), TESR_VolumetricLightGridData (x FogVolume sums written, y lit
scale), TESR_PhysSky / TESR_PhysSkySun (declared only with PHYSICAL_SKY_ON: the C++ uses that parameter's presence to know the build),
TESR_CameraPosition, TESR_CameraData, TESR_ViewTransform, TESR_ProjectionTransform, TESR_RenderScale, and Depth.hlsl's other names.

**Set per draw by the C++:** NVR_FogLayout (xy half / full extent, zw 1 / half size), NVR_CompositeFlags (x sun shadow, y AO, z sky
visibility, w the interior fold's 16-bit rounding: 0 nearest even, 1 none, 2 toward zero), NVR_CompositeAOTexel (xy 1 / AO size, zw
SkyTake cap and no-limit), NVR_FogGridFlags (x grid bound, y the volumetric light's composite is done here; reset to 0 after the
dedicated draw), NVR_SunFarFlags (x SunFar bound).

Units: linear light with the piecewise sRGB curve for the fog; the shadow / interior / AO composites use the 2.2 power of the passes
they replace.

### 4. Settings

"Ext" = read outdoors only (forced to 0 indoors by the C++); "Int" = read from both sections. Clamps are the code's.

#### 4a. `[Shaders.VolumetricFog.Main]` (and `Interiors` for the Int keys)

| Key | Range | Default | What it does in this shader |
|---|---|---|---|
| ClassicLook | 0-2 | 0 | 1: the *Classic techniques: noise sampled at the surface point (3 octaves), sun shadow at the surface, the game's SH sky, SunGlow fixed at 1, no physical sky, no WeatherFogColor, no grid air, no game fog floor. 2: VolumetricFogClassic at full resolution (C++). LegacyFog wins. Int. |
| FogVolume | bool | true | Read by the volumetric light (TESR_VolumetricLightGridData.x). With the grid bound, outdoors, modern look: the noise and the sun visibility come from the grid's height-weighted air sums along the view (the part inside the march distance), the rest from the four-point march / the surface. |
| Amount | max 0 | 0.4 (Int 0.25) | Final strength: mul = lerp(1, mul, a), add x a, a = saturate(Amount x lerp(1, AmountScale, night)). Int. |
| FogSaturation | max 0 | 1.7 (Int 0.5) | lerp(luma, colour, FogSaturation / (1 + density)). Int. |
| BaseDensity | max 0 | 0.2 (Int 0.3) | Vegas Shaders' own density. Int. |
| WeatherImpact | max 0 | 0.5 (Int 0) | Adds the weather's density (from its fog near / far), pulls the fog power toward the weather's (lerp(1, FogData.w, WI), as the froxel grid), and blends the colour toward the weather side. Int. |
| GameFogFloor | 0-2 | 0 | Ext, modern only. After Amount, tops coverage up to the game's own fog curve x this (raw near, far, power), never on the sky, with the fog colour without glow. |
| WeatherFogColor | 0-1 (missing 1) | 1 | Ext, modern only. Share of the weather's fog colour (instead of the sky toward the horizon) in the weather side; the sky dome keeps the sky colour. |
| MorningFogDip | 0-1 | 0.5 | Ext. Base density x (1 - dip x sun height x dayFade). |
| SunriseSunsetBoost | max 0 | 0.3 | Ext. Density + boost x 4 a (1 - a), a = sun amount. |
| HeightFalloff | min 1e-4 | 50 | Scale of the height curve exp(-k (z - base)), k = 1.5e-4 / (fogPower x HeightFalloff x lerp(1, HeightFalloffScale, night)) (the grid's formula). Higher = thins more slowly. Not used while HeightLayer is on. |
| MaxHeight | - | 3 (Int 0) | Height curve base = -10000 + 1000 x (MaxHeight + MaxHeightOffset x night). |
| HeightLayer, LayerBase, LayerThickness, LayerFollowsCamera | | off, 0, 2000, off | Ext, modern (the C++ writes Shape.xy only then). Density 1 below the base, exp(-(z - base) / thickness) above, averaged along the view in closed form. |
| HeightInfluence | 0-1 | 1 (Int 0) | Blend between flat (0) and the height path mean (1); also weighs the noise taps. Int. |
| Extinction | max 0 | 3 (Int 0.4) | Optical depth per unit = density x Extinction x 0.1 x 1e-4. Int. |
| Inscattering | max 0 | 1.6 (Int 0.35) | Albedo = saturate(Inscattering / Extinction). Int. |
| WindAngle, WindSpeed | | 45, 0.3 (Int 0, 0.1) | Noise drift = (cos, sin) x speed x lerp(1, WindSpeedScale, night) x game ms x 0.002 units. Int. |
| NoiseScale | min 1e-4 | 1 | Noise tile = 6000 x NoiseScale units (4 cells of 1500, as the grid). Int. |
| NoiseStrength | 0-1 | 0.7 (Int 0.5) | Density x max(1 + k (2n - 1), 0), n the contrast-stretched noise: mean-preserving. Int. |
| PhaseAsymmetry | 0-1 | 0.6 | Ext. Henyey-Greenstein g of the glow lobe (0 = no glow). |
| SunGlow | 0-2 | 0.25 | Ext. Glow = SunGlow x 2 x (HG(g) - 1)+ (HG capped at 8) x (1 - sun height)^2 x (1 + glare x depth) x dayFade. |
| ShadowStrength | 0-1 | 1 | Ext. Blend of the sun visibility; 0 skips every shadow read. |
| RainyDisablesSkyFilter, CloudyDisablesSkyFilter | bool | true | Int. Drive WeatherFilterBlend (C++). |
| AerialStrength, AerialRangeStart, AerialTintBlend, AerialDayFadeStart, AerialTintR/G/B | | 0.5, 0.3, 0, 0.85, 0.6/0.7/1 | Ext, not legacy. mul x lerp(1, tint, s), tint = lerp(sky colour normalised to max 1, manual, blend), s = strength x smoothstep(start, 1, depth) x smoothstep(0.1, fadeStart, sun amount), not on the sky. |
| DistantFogRange, DistantFogBlend, DistantFogHeight | | 0.9, 0.3, 0.3 | Ext. Horizon fog toward the horizon colour: sqrt(smoothstep(exp(-4 range), 1, depth)) x blend x heightFade, heightFade = min(1, exp(-z / (80000 x height))), or 1 - sky with height 0. |
| EdgeAA | max 0 | 0.4 | Ext. Land pixels touching the sky blend toward the horizon colour by EdgeAA / 2 x the same distance ramp. |
| MinDensityFloor | 0-1 | 0 | Ext. Optical depth never below this share of the flat (height-free) one. |
| LegacyFog | bool | false | The *Legacy techniques (see 6, legacy). Int. |
| Legacy keys | | | MinimumBaseFog, SunPower, HeightFogDensity, HeightFogFalloff, HeightFogDist, HeightFogSkyColor, HeightFogHeight, HeightFogBlend, HeightFogRolloff, HeightFogSaturation, HeightFogInscattering, FogNight, SimpleFogSkyColor, SimpleFogHeight, SimpleFogBlend: used as described in 6 (legacy). |

#### 4b. `[Shaders.VolumetricFog.Night]` (all fade in with nightShare; outdoors only)

GameFogFloorScale (C++), DensityScale (base density only), AmountScale, HeightFalloffScale (multiplies HeightFalloff), MaxHeightOffset,
NoiseStrengthScale, WindSpeedScale, ExtinctionScale, InscatteringScale, TintR/G/B (multiply the fog colour), NightAmbientStrength (x the
moon floor on the ambient), DisableSkyMask (the sky dome takes fog at night).

#### 4c. `[Shaders.VolumetricFog.Interiors]`

The Int keys with indoor values, and the legacy keys. The Ext keys are forced to 0 (C++). Indoor-only keys (UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-03 LampHaze,
read by UpdateSettings, live; a missing key takes the default):

| Key | Range | Default | What it does in this shader |
|---|---|---|---|
| LampHaze | 0-4 | 0.5 | Strength of the lamps' light in the fog's air (6, LampHaze). 0 = the term is skipped by a uniform branch: the fog exactly as before. |
| LampHazeAnisotropy | 0-0.9 | 0.3 | Henyey-Greenstein g of the lamps' light in the haze (x 4 pi, capped at 8): brighter looking toward a lamp. |
| LampHazeCore | 0.05-1 | 0.25 | The glow's bright core e as a share of the lamp's reach (as [Shaders.PBR.LampFalloff] Falloff). |
| LampHazeMaxLamps | 0-16 | 8 | At most this many lamps, nearest sphere first. 0 = off. |
| LampHazeShadows | bool | false | UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-12: each lamp's light in the air times its traced visibility along the view (6, LampHazeShadows). Needs the ray tracing core running for another RT effect; otherwise, and off, the haze is unshadowed as before. |
| LampHazeShadowCell | 4-32 | 8 | One traced cell per this many screen pixels each way. Read at the RT helper's start. |
| LampHazeShadowSamples | 1-8 | 4 | Points along each view per lamp, one ray to the lamp each (+ one ray per cell and lamp to find the surface). Live. |
| LampHazeShadowTemporal | 0-0.95 | 0.8 | Last frame's weight at 60 fps (at most 0.5 while the camera moves). Live. |

#### 4d. Other keys

`[Shaders.ShadowsExteriors.Main] NightMinDarkness` (moon floor depth), `[Main.Main.Performance] NvrDxvkPasses / NvrDxvkVariants /
NvrDxvkKeepD3D9 / NvrDxvkTestSuffix` (Vulkan passes, see 7), `NvrSm6Shaders` (the SM6 build of the dedicated passes), `FogReferenceTest`
(development builds; its technique is not in this file, so the C++ draws the unnamed one), `DisableCompositeApply`, `InteriorShadowFold`
and `InteriorShadowFoldRound` (ShaderManager), `[Shaders.Sky.Physical] Enabled` (compiles PHYSICAL_SKY_ON), `[Main.Main.Quality]
MaxQuality` (the fog targets at full size: NVR_FogLayout = 1, handled).

### 5. Passes and outputs

| Technique | Passes | Pixel shaders |
|---|---|---|
| (unnamed, first) | Fog | FullPS(modern) |
| DedicatedFog | Estimate; Reconstruct | EstimatePS(modern, outdoor); ReconstructPS(modern, none) |
| CompositeFog | Estimate; Reconstruct | EstimatePS(modern, outdoor); ReconstructPS(modern, exterior composite) |
| CompositeFogInterior | Estimate; Reconstruct | EstimatePS(modern, inside); ReconstructPS(modern, interior composite) |
| ApplyShadowAO | Apply | ShadowAOPS |
| PackedFog | Estimate; Apply | PackEstimatePS(modern); PackApplyPS(modern) |
| HalfStoreProbe | Probe | ProbeVS (XYZW quad) + HalfProbePS |
| VolumetricFogLegacy, DedicatedFogLegacy, CompositeFogLegacy, CompositeFogInteriorLegacy, PackedFogLegacy | as above | legacy look |
| VolumetricFogClassic, DedicatedFogClassic, CompositeFogClassic, CompositeFogInteriorClassic | as above | classic look |

Not in the file: FogReference (diagnostic of the upstream shader), PackedFogClassic (the C++ does not ask for it).

Targets: NVR_FogBuffer0 / 1 (C++, half size rounded up, or full with MaxQuality, A16B16G16R16F): the Estimate writes (mul.rgb,
normalised depth) to COLOR0 and (add.rgb, 1) to COLOR1. Reconstruct, full, packed apply and ShadowAO write the frame's next image
(rgb; alpha see 6). PackedFog pass 1 writes float4(add.rgb, luma(mul)) into the top-left quarter of the target, which the C++ copies
to TESR_RenderedBuffer. HalfStoreProbe writes 64 x 16 texels of (value, value rounded to nearest even, value rounded toward zero, 1).

The looks, the interior estimate and the composite kind are compile-time arguments (one shader per pass); every runtime switch is a
dynamic branch on a float constant. Compiled sizes (plain ps_3_0, static slots / highest constant register): estimate 989 / c95
(1115 / c104 with the physical sky), composite reconstruct 1396 / c105 (1518 / c114), interior reconstruct 756, packed apply 1156,
classic full 784, legacy estimate 427, probe 48. The effect compiler's preshader adds registers on top; the effect compiles in all five
checked builds.

### 6. Behaviour details

**Geometry.** Camera-relative position = toWorld(uv) x view z (Depth.hlsl; the projection's TAA jitter terms are not used, so the fog
does not move with the jitter); distance = its length (true distance, so the fog does not change as the view turns); absolute height =
camera z + its z. Sky: normalised depth >= 0.9961.

**Density (modern and classic).** fogPower = max(lerp(1, FogData.w, saturate(WeatherImpact)), 0.05). anchor = (mean of saturate(1 -
FogData.x / far) and saturate(1 - FogData.y / far))^2 / (fogPower + 1). S = max(BaseDensity x timeScale x lerp(1, DensityScale,
night) + WeatherImpact x anchor + SunriseSunsetBoost x transition, 0).

**Optical depth.** flat = S x ext x ref x (dist / ref)^fogPower x 1e-4, ref = max(fog far, 1000) (the power bends the curve about the
weather's far distance; at power 1 it is the plain distance), ext = Extinction x 0.1 x lerp(1, ExtinctionScale, night). Height: the
mean of the profile along the straight path between camera height and pixel height in closed form (exponential:
rho(zc) (1 - e^-x) / x, x = k dz; layer: the antiderivative difference over dz; |dz| < 1 uses the midpoint), clamped to [0, 1e4].
plainOD = max(flat x lerp(1, mean, HeightInfluence), MinDensityFloor x flat). Then the noise factor, od = max(plainOD x max(1 + k(2n - 1),
0), floor), T = exp(-od).

**Noise.** Skipped (neutral) on the sky or when NoiseStrength x scale is 0. Modern: four points at (i + 0.5) / 4 of min(dist, 12000)
along the view, each weighed by the height profile (so patches sit in the layer); with the grid, its height-weighted noise mean
(b / g) for the part inside the march distance, the share by optical depth: (1 - e^(-od c)) / (1 - e^-od), c = march / view z.
Classic: 0.5, 0.3, 0.2 of the noise at the surface point x 1, 2.03, 4.01. The mean is contrast-stretched (x1.8 about 0.5).

**Fog colour.** skyHaze = lerp(horizon, sky low, smoothstep(0, 0.35, view up)) (outdoors; indoors the sky colour). With the physical
sky (PHYSICAL_SKY_ON, modern, outdoors, PhysSky.x > 0): skyHaze and the horizon colour are blended with the table's radiance in the view
direction / along the horizon (PhysAtmosphere::Blend: weather tint z, share x), and the sunlight with the sun's transmittance through the
air (AtmSunDepth, as the clouds). Ambient = the SH sky straight up (C0 + C2 + 2 C6; the game's SH in classic) when the Sky effect is on
and outdoors, else the sky colour; x PBRData.w; at least the weather ambient x moon floor x NightAmbientStrength x night. Colour =
lerp(ambient, lerp(skyHaze, weather colour, WeatherFogColor x (1 - sky)), WeatherImpact), then toward the weather colour (the sky colour
on the sky) by 1 - exp(-6 S), saturation, night tint.

**Sun.** sunLight = GetSunColor (Sky.hlsl: (1 + h) sun + sunset x (1 - h)^8 x day x atmosphere) x PBRData.z. Visibility (ShadowStrength
> 0, outdoors, maps on, faded by ShadowFade.x, by day): modern with the grid: lerp(1, a / g, grid share); otherwise one cascade lookup
at the surface (not for the sky) with the include's normal offset and bias (FogSunVisibility: the first cascade holding the point, lit
past the last one with a 10 % fade); lerp(1, vis, ShadowStrength). 35 % x dayFade of the fog colour counts as direct sun and is shaded;
the glow (4a SunGlow) is added on top and shaded. inscatter = (shaded colour + sunLight x glow x vis) x albedo.
(2026-11-07, ptaaa-fog) While the ray tracing core runs for another RT effect, the grid's a channel holds the TRACED sun visibility
(`[Shaders.VolumetricLight.RT]`, docs/EFFECTS.md "VolumetricLight" "RT fog visibility"): with FogVolume + Froxels the fog's sun
shading inside MaxDistance follows the RT sun shadows' casters with no change here. The surface lookup past the grid (and the ClassicLook /
no-grid paths) stays the cascades.

**Compose.** skyMask = lerp(1 - sky x WeatherFilterBlend, 1, night x DisableSkyMask); mul = lerp(1, T, skyMask), add = inscatter x (1 -
T) x skyMask; aerial tint on mul; horizon fog and EdgeAA (one blend k = hf + edge - hf x edge toward the horizon colour); Amount; game
fog floor (modern); mul saturated, add >= 0. Final colour = delinearize(max(linearize(scene) x mul + add, 0)); alpha = lerp(scene alpha,
1, night) (1 where a composite ran first, as the passes it replaces write 1).

**Legacy (LegacyFog; an approximation of the pre-rewrite Simple + Height fog from its keys).** Distances x lerp(1, FogNight, night).
Simple fog: coverage 1 - exp(-MinimumBaseFog x ext x dist x 1e-4 x heightMean(SimpleFogHeight x 1e-3, relative to the camera)) x (1 -
weather curve x WeatherImpact), x SimpleFogBlend. Height fog: HeightFogDensity x ext x far x (dist x HeightFogDist / far)^HeightFogRolloff
x 1e-4 x heightMean(HeightFogFalloff curve from HeightFogHeight), coverage x HeightFogBlend. Colours: sky toward weather colour by
coverage x 10 / SimpleFogSkyColor or / HeightFogSkyColor, saturation FogSaturation / HeightFogSaturation, half ambient, night tint;
albedos Inscattering / Extinction and HeightFogInscattering / Extinction; SunPower x sunlight x ((1 + cos) / 2)^4. Horizon fog from the
legacy distant keys, Amount from LegacyData.z. No aerial, EdgeAA, layer, floor, grid or physical sky.

**Estimate.** Each half texel takes the fog of the nearest (smallest depth) full pixel it covers (2 x 2, or the one pixel with
MaxQuality), at that pixel's centre: thin near objects keep their own fog.

**Reconstruct.** The composites first (below), then: the four nearest half texels, bilinear x saturate(2 - |depth - pixel depth| / tol),
tol = max(0.02 x depth, 0.0005). Where the weights sum below 0.25 the pixel's own fog is computed (dynamic branch) and blended in by
weight x 4. Then, with NVR_FogGridFlags.y, the volumetric light's composite on the linear result (VolumetricLight's CompositeAir: lit
air from the grid's r sums / grid scale, air 1 - exp(-density x |ray| x planar), HG phase capped at 8, x strength x fade).

**Composites.** Sun (CompositeFlags.x): ShadowsExteriors' apply: SunFar factor (if bound), f = lerp(floor, 1, saturate(lerp(R, 1,
ShadowFade.x) + G x ShadowFade.z)), floor = max(0, 1 - Darkness), shade tinted toward the sky (blend by saturate(f + 0.5)), gamma 2.2,
the underwater water-plane exception. Interior (CompositeFogInterior): InteriorShadowApply.hlsl with the raw lamp mask (R), then
rounded to 16-bit float as NVR_CompositeFlags.w says (RoundToHalf: nearest even, toward zero, or none), alpha 1. AO (flags.y):
AmbientOcclusion's dedicated combine: 2 x 2 AO taps, bilinear / (1 + |depth diff| / BlurDrop), lerp(floor, 1, AO), lighter where
luma > LumThreshold, frame x AO^(1/2.2); with flags.z the sky take (SkyTake with the same weights over NVR_SkyVis rb); nothing past 8000.

**LampHaze (UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-03, indoors).** Before, indoors the fog's air was lit by the ambient (and the cell's fog colour) only. Now
ModernFogTerms adds the light of the room's lamps scattered toward the camera to the add term, after the sky mask and before Amount:
add += LampHaze x S x Inscattering x skyMask x sum over lamps of colour x J x P, S the density with its noise (as the rest of the fog).
Lamps (C++ SetLampHaze, each frame in Render): the shadow-slot lamps (all slots, BatchLightPosition) and the tracked unshadowed ones
(LightPosition) that ShaderManager::GetNearbyLights listed this frame (the list AmbientLight's grid and the path tracer use), each once,
spheres wholly behind the camera left out, nearest sphere first, at most LampHazeMaxLamps; reach = the list's radius / [Shaders.
ShadowsInteriors.Main] LightRadiusMult (the game's Spec.r, where the lamp's light on the walls ends); colour = (game colour)^2.2 x dimmer.
Light in the air: f(d) = (e^2 / (d^2 + e^2) - e^2 / (r^2 + e^2)) / (1 - e^2 / (r^2 + e^2)), e = LampHazeCore x r: inverse square with a core,
1 at the lamp, 0 at its reach. J = the integral of f along the visible view inside the sphere (clipped to the camera and the surface), in
core lengths (about 2.3 through a lamp's centre at the default core, whatever its size), closed form: with s along the view from the point nearest the lamp, h that point's distance to it, h' = sqrt(h^2 + e^2),
phi = atan(s / h'): (e^2 / h' (phi_b - phi_a) - e^2 / (r^2 + e^2) (s_b - s_a)) / ((1 - e^2 / (r^2 + e^2)) e).
P = the mean of HG(g) (x 4 pi, capped at 8) x exp(-extinction x distance) at the two Gauss-Legendre points of
[phi_a, phi_b] (the remaining factors are smooth in phi: no march, no banding however close the view passes to a lamp). Rough size with the
defaults (S 0.3, Inscattering 0.35, Amount 0.25): a view through a lamp's centre gains about 0.03 x its linear colour, half its reach off
the centre about a fifth of that. Passes: the estimates (DedicatedFog, CompositeFog, CompositeFogInterior), the full-resolution and packed
techniques, and DedicatedFogCombine's per-pixel fallback. NOT in the per-pixel fallback of the CompositeFog and CompositeFogInterior
reconstructs (both went over the 224 constant registers with it): those rare edge pixels keep the fog without the lamps' light (a lamp's
own silhouette may show a one-pixel rim with less glow). ClassicLook and LegacyFog never have it (their techniques compile to exactly the
same bytecode as before). No shadows by itself: a lamp's sphere that reaches through a wall glows on the other side too, unless LampHazeShadows
(below, 2026-11-12, traced) is on; the lamp cube maps would need a march and a cube read per step and lamp, and no sampler is left for them. Big invisible fill lights (FillLightRadius) glow like any
lamp. Not taken: TESR_ExtraLights (no colour), ManyLamps' game lamp table, spot lights (the flashlight).

**LampHazeShadows (UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-12, indoors, off by default).** The lamp term above has no march, so a shadow cannot be read per step:
instead the RT core traces ONE number per lamp and screen cell, the share of the lamp's light along that view that reaches the air, weighed
exactly as J weighs it, and the shader multiplies the lamp's J x P by it. Parts: `core/RtFogVisibility.cpp` (namespace RtFogLamps: a second
rider of the RT core, registered by RtFogVisibility::Register, Order 3, indoors only, only while another RT effect keeps the helper running),
`core/RtFogLampGrid.h` (the pass constants, the tile layout, the lamps' slots; plain C++, shared with the RT host), `Compute/Rt/RtFogLamps.comp`
(the trace). Per frame: SetLampHaze hands the frame's lamps (world position, reach) to RtFogLamps::Submit; at the NEXT frame's RT point the
rider puts them in slots (a lamp keeps its slot, so its tile's history), traces, and copies the result out into its own R16F texture, 4 x 4
tiles of ceil(width / Cell) x ceil(height / Cell), tile (slot mod 4, slot / 4); SetLampHaze then writes each lamp's code into the lamp table's
row 1 alpha (0 = no trace in the last 8 frames: unshadowed, the shader's branch is not taken; tile + 1 + 32 x Cell; -1 = the trace does not
hold this lamp yet: no light in the air for that frame) and Render binds the tiles on stage 11, read through NVR_FogGrid's sampler (linear,
clamp; the volumetric light's grid is outdoors only, and stage 15, the last one, is the sky shares'). No new constant register: the cell
size rides in the code, the half-cell clamp inside a tile uses TESR_ReciprocalResolution.
The trace, per lamp and cell: the view clipped to the lamp's sphere and to the first solid surface (one camera ray, mask 0x8B: land, objects,
lamp models, far LOD; people, the hidden first-person body and cut-outs never end it); Samples points spread evenly in phi (the density of
the glow's main term), each weighing 1 - w (d^2 + e^2) / e^2 (f over that density) and casting one ray to the lamp under the RT lamp
shadows' rules ([Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.RT] LampStop, SkipLampModels with [Shaders.ShadowsInteriors.Forward] LampModelDistance,
SkipLampGlass, alpha-tested casters by their texture, decals and glass never block, the hidden body passed over near the eye). History: last
frame's value of the same texel (a whole view's number, nothing to reproject), weight Temporal standing still, at most 0.5 moving, none for
