a slot that changed its lamp or after a frame without a trace. Cost: lamps x cells their spheres cover x (Samples + 1) short rays a frame
(1080p, Cell 8: 32 400 cells a tile, so one lamp filling the screen is 162 000 rays, eight of them 1.3 million); in the fog one more point
read per lamp whose sphere the view passes through. Not measured in game. Limits: the value is one per 8 x 8 pixels and per whole view, so
a thin foreground object (a pillar in front of a lit room) takes its neighbours' value over a few pixels; the traced lamps are last
frame's (a lamp new to the list has no light in the air for one frame); the phase and the fog's own extinction are not re-weighed by
the visibility (they are smooth along the view). The Vulkan and SM6 fog passes still stand down while LampHaze is on.

**PackedFog.** Pass 1 at half size into the target's top-left quarter: (add, luma(mul)). Pass 2: the four half texels' coefficients, their
depths found again (nearest of their 2 x 2), the same weights and own-fog fallback; the multiply is grey in this path.

**HalfStoreProbe.** r = (1024 + i + q) / 1024 x 2^(i mod 7 - 3), q in {0.25, 0.5, 0.75} (ties included), i = texel index; g, b =
RoundToHalf(r, 0 / 2). The C++ compares the stored r with g and b.

**Stability.** No per-frame noise anywhere (positions ignore jitter, noise is world-space and moves only with the wind); the half-size
estimate takes whole pixels (no shimmer from sub-pixel resampling); every division is guarded; smoothstep edges are kept apart.

**Bugs of the old spec and status.**
- Solid white wall near the camera (height term divided by the distance): fixed, closed-form path mean.
- Fog going to infinity below z = 0 (uncapped horizon height fade): fixed, capped at 1.
- Thin objects taking the sky's fog: fixed, nearest-pixel estimate.
- Edge pixels with no matching half texel: now get their own fog (the earlier wave-3 shader took the nearest texel's).
- smoothstep with equal edges (AerialRangeStart 1, DistantFogRange 0 indoors): guarded.
- The froxel grid, the physical sky table, the interior rounding and HalfStoreProbe, PackedFog: implemented (were missing).

### 7. Vulkan path

Files: `Compute/VolumetricFog.glsl` (bindings, uniform block, push block, the whole FogTerms and composites, ported from the .fx),
`VolumetricFogEstimate.frag` (two outputs), `VolumetricFogApply.frag` (push.mode 0-4). `bash tools/nvr-dxvk/compile-compute.sh` builds
`VolumetricFogEstimate.spv`, `VolumetricFogApply.spv` and the variants of `Compute/variants.txt` (Estimate l c i p, Apply m l c p, the
test suffixes n / s / k / f).

- Selected by `NvrDxvkPasses` on Vegas Shaders DXVK (not in rect frames, not for the diagnostic overrides or SM6); ExtDedicated (both passes),
  ExtShadowAO (mode 3), ExtRender (mode 4, full-resolution techniques). PackedFog and HalfStoreProbe stay D3D9.
- Set 1 bindings: 0 scene, 1 depth, 2 atlas, 3 normals, 4 point shadow, 5 noise (3D), 6 sky table, 7 grid; Apply also 8 fog multiply, 9
  fog add, 10 AO, 11 sky visibility, 12 SunFar, 13 the sky shares (TESR_SkyCoverage, UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-05 MsaaEdgeFix: read while
  NVR_FogGridFlags.z is 1). Stand-ins (the depth buffer) are never read while their flags are 0.
- UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-05 MsaaEdgeFix ([Main.Main.Quality], on by default; only with the game's MSAA): where the volumetric light's froxel
  grid is applied here (FogGridLight), a pixel's grid depth is blended from the surface's toward the march distance by the pixel's exact sky
  share (core/SkyCoverage.h; D3D9: NVR_FogSkyCoverage on stage 15, bound by hand, nothing bound = 0; Vulkan: binding 13 and
  NVR_FogGridFlags.z). With transparency multisampling a see-through cutout far off (a pylon's lattice, a bush) covers only part of each
  pixel's samples, but the depth read here is one sample's (the nearest under the DXVK fork), so the see-through part took the air only
  up to the cutout. Blending the depth instead of the two airs: a second grid lookup took the D3D9 composites past ps_3_0's 224 constants
  (the air builds up almost evenly along a view, so the two differ by a few percent). Off, or without MSAA: the lookup is the one before
  (lerp by 0). Bench 2026-11-05 og-desert, MsaaEdgeFix on vs off, VL MaxDistance 15000: pylon head +1.2 (fork, Vulkan) / +1.5 (native D3D9)
  luma, the rest of the frame |diff| 0.07 (noise); without MSAA on vs off |diff| 0.10 = noise.
- UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-03 LampHaze: not ported. While its term is on this frame (TESR_VolumetricFogLamps.x > 0: indoors, lamps listed), the
  Vulkan passes (ExtDedicated, ExtRender for the modern full-resolution technique) and the SM6 passes are not used and the D3D9 technique
  draws (logged once: "UNOFFICIAL LampHaze: ..."); outdoors, with LampHaze 0, ClassicLook or LegacyFog they run as before. Porting means
  the lamp table as a set 1 binding (14; 13 is the sky shares since 2026-11-05), TESR_VolumetricFogLamps in FogExtConstants, LampHazeLight in VolumetricFog.glsl and the .spv
  rebuilt with tools/nvr-dxvk/compile-compute.sh.
- Set 2 binding 0: FogExtConstants (6 matrices, 54 vectors in FogExtVectorNames order, 9 + 9 SH, 9 colours linearized on the CPU).
- Push (112 bytes): sampler heap indices (point, linear, wrap), mode, legacy, classic, interior, physicalSky, then NVR_FogLayout,
  NVR_CompositeFlags, NVR_CompositeAOTexel, NVR_FogGridFlags, NVR_SunFarFlags.
- The C++ asks for the variant (FOG_VARIANT: the switches become constants) and falls back to the general shader.

### 8. Look and cost

Expected look (not yet checked in game): haze growing with distance and toward the weather's fog colour, a clean horizon, a soft glow
toward a low sun (bright at SunGlow 1), fog darker in sun shadow (and shaded through the air with Froxels + FogVolume), patches that drift
with the wind and sit in the height layer, the sky untouched unless the weather or the night lets the fog in, dark-but-not-black fog at
night. Cost: the half-size estimate (four depth reads, up to four noise reads or two grid reads, one cascade lookup, the closed-form
height terms; branches skip what is off) and the full-size reconstruct (four half texels x 2, the composites when folded, the own-fog
fallback only on edge pixels, two grid reads for the folded volumetric light).

LampHaze (indoors, lamps listed): estimate +165-175 static slots (DedicatedFog estimate 1132 -> 1301, CompositeFogInterior estimate 1254 ->
1423, full 1124 -> 1293, with the physical sky; highest constant c184 -> c195), DedicatedFog reconstruct 1309 -> 1478 (fallback only). Per
half-size pixel a loop of up to LampHazeMaxLamps lamps: about 12 instructions and one point read for a lamp whose sphere the view misses,
about 120 and two reads for one it passes through (two atan, two sincos, two exp, the phase). Not measured in game.

### 9. Open questions

- LampHaze (2026-11-03): not seen in game. Its scale (the 0.03 x lamp colour through a lamp with the defaults), the core size, fill lights
  glowing, glow through walls (no shadows), the rim on edge pixels in the composite passes, and the cost with 8 lamps are all to check.
  Follow-ups: lamp shadows in the air for the strongest 1-2 shadowed lamps (TESR_ShadowCubeMapBuffer, s15); the interior's directional
  light (the cell's or its lighting template's directional colour, read by AmbientLight.cpp ~940; it has no position or window, so it would
  be an even tint, not light through windows, and was left out); the Vulkan port.

- Not checked in game: every magnitude (the glow x 2 scale, the 35 % sun share of the fog colour, the noise contrast, the legacy fog's
  look, the physical-sky blend in the fog colour).
- Settings whose meaning was kept as the live code has it although a description says otherwise: HeightFalloffScale multiplies
  HeightFalloff (the toml says raising it hugs the ground; the froxel grid also multiplies); WeatherImpact pulls the fog power from 1
  toward the weather's (the earlier spec said the reverse; the grid does this); the height scale is HeightFalloff x fog power / 1.5e-4
  (the toml says "divided by the weather's fog power"; the grid multiplies).
- The sun composite's shade tint uses TESR_FogSkyColor (the fog's copy with the sky look) where ShadowsExteriors.fx uses TESR_SkyColor:
  the Vulkan constant block has only the fog's copy, and both paths must match. A small difference between folded and separate shadows.
- FogSunVisibility takes one cascade (no cross-cascade blend; the include's blended lookup does not fit the composite pass's
  constants once the effect compiler adds its preshader).
- LegacyFog and ClassicLook are rebuilt from their key descriptions, not from the old shaders (which were not read): presets made for
  them will look similar, not identical.
- FogReference (development diagnostic) is not provided.
- compile-compute.sh (full run) stops at `SunShadowsApply c1` (variants.txt names `SunShadowsApply.frag`, which is not in this tree since
  the wave-3 SunShadows rewrite); every other shader and variant, the fog's included, compiles and validates.
- The loader for `[_Shaders.VolumetricFog.Status] Enabled` was not traced (unchanged).

### Idle targets (2026-11-12)
With `[Main.Main.Performance] TargetReleaseSeconds` above 0 (default 20) the texture manager keeps 1 x 1 stand-ins in place of this effect's render targets while it is off (not made at start-up when it starts off; released that many seconds after it is switched off; made again, zero filled, before the frame draws when it is switched on). Which targets and why that is safe here: the two fog buffers are used only from Render (RenderDedicated), behind the switch. A new reader or writer of these targets outside the effect's own switch, or a copy of their pointers kept past a frame, breaks this: then answer false in `TargetParkable` (the header). See `core/TextureManager.h`.

### FoggyWeatherImpact (UNOFFICIAL 2026-11-13, modern lighting)

One WeatherImpact for every weather cannot serve both ends: a clear weather whose fog far is 45000 with a power of 0.6 already greys trees at 50 m at 0.5, and at 0.2 a foggy weather loses its fog. With `[Shaders.VolumetricFog.Main] FoggyWeatherImpact` at 0 or more (default -1: off, as before), the share of the weather's own fog follows the weather's fog distance, outdoors:

- `Sky::fogFarPlane` (the game's own blend of day / night and old / new weather) at `ClearWeatherFar` (26000) or farther: WeatherImpact as set.
- At `FoggyWeatherFar` (12000) or nearer: FoggyWeatherImpact.
- Between: eased (smoothstep on the distance's logarithm).

It is worked out on the CPU each frame (`VolumetricFogEffect::UpdateConstants`, `TESR_VolumetricFogDensity.y`): no shader or SPIR-V change, the D3D9 and Vulkan paths read the same constant. Indoors and with LegacyFog: WeatherImpact as set. Bench (Zone, slot A): clear, part-cloud and overcast weathers (far 28000-56000) unchanged; foggy1 (far 10500) at 1.0: ground median L 28 -> 35, shade -1.84 -> -1.31 stops, pines fade in depth steps; 0 flicker spikes in 600 foggy frames.

## VolumetricLight

*README title: VolumetricLight - notes*

The shader's own header (`src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/VolumetricLight.fx.hlsl`) describes the passes. This file holds what it does not.

### Where the light is applied

With `Froxels` on (the default) and the volumetric fog running, the fog applies the light grid in its own composite
(`VolumetricFog.fx.hlsl` FogGridLight, the Vulkan `VolumetricFogApply` under the DXVK fork); this effect's composites (technique 0 the
march, 8 CompositeGrid) draw only when the fog did not (`EffectChain.cpp` PreStep_VolumetricLight, `gridApplied`). A fix to how the light
is put on the picture has to go into both.

### TAA jitter and the history (2026-11-09, roadmap track 1 step 2)

`[Shaders.VolumetricLight.Main] TemporalJitterFix` (on; only the half-resolution march's Temporal pass, so only with `Froxels` off): the
march is drawn from this frame's jittered depth and the history from last frame's, so the history is read where the jitter's change puts
it (as AmbientLight's TemporalJitterFix). C++ only: `TemporalBackbone::ShiftReprojection` shifts `TESR_VolumetricLightReproject`, which
the .fx and `Compute/VolumetricLightTemporal.frag` both read; `lastJitter` is kept with `lastView`. Off: as before; 0 without TAA's Jitter.

### The game's MSAA and see-through cutouts

Each pixel's air is read at the one depth the effects get (`TESR_DepthBuffer`). With the game's MSAA and transparency multisampling, a
cutout far off (a pylon's lattice, a bush) covers only part of each pixel's samples (alpha to coverage), but that depth is one sample's:
the nearest under the NVR DXVK fork, sample 0 under stock DXVK and native D3D9. The see-through part then took the air only up to the
cutout, so the cutout read as a solid shape in the light.

- 2026-11-04 (PR #5): this effect's D3D9 composites blended a surface pixel's air toward the whole view's by its exact sky share
  (`TESR_SkyCoverage`, `Includes/MsaaSkyEdge.hlsl` SkyShare). The fog's grid composite and the Vulkan composites did not, and with the
  defaults it is the fog that applies the light.
- 2026-11-05 `[Main.Main.Quality] MsaaEdgeFix` (on by default, live; off = as before): the composites here (`MsaaAirMix`): a surface pixel's
  sky share takes the whole view's air; a pixel that is sky by depth but only partly sky (sample-0 resolves) takes, for its surface share,
  the air up to the nearest surface 1-2 px round it. Ported to the Vulkan composites (`Compute/VolumetricLightComposite.frag`,
  `VolumetricLightCompositeGrid.frag`, `Compute/MsaaSkyEdge.glsl`; binding 3 `TESR_SkyCoverage`, `TESR_GameMultisample` at the end of
  the uniform block). The fog's FogGridLight blends the grid depth instead (see `docs/EFFECTS.md "VolumetricFog"`, section 7).
- `[Main.Main.Quality] MsaaEdgeDebugView 1` (test only): these composites show red where the depth says sky, green the exact sky share.

Bench 2026-11-05 (og-desert, `bench/foundations/lists/alphacut-vl.txt`, MaxDistance 15000 so the pylons lie inside the march): with MSAA 4
MsaaEdgeFix on vs off moved the pylon heads by +1.2 (fork) / +1.5 (native) luma, the rest of the frame |diff| 0.07 (noise); without MSAA on
vs off = noise. At the default MaxDistance 5000 the pylons there lie beyond the march and their air is the sky's already.

Left: a cutout against nearer ground (a bush in front of the land a few metres behind) is not helped: the shares only know sky samples, not
the depth of the land behind. The air between them differs little at that range.

### RT fog visibility (2026-11-07, branch claude/ptaaa-fog, `[Shaders.VolumetricLight.RT]`)

**What.** With ray tracing running, the sun's visibility in each froxel grid cell is traced against the RT core's scene instead of read
from the cascades (`MarchShadow`). The cascades end at the shadow distance (6000 units by default, far less sharp past ~1750), hold
alpha-tested cards and the far LOD differently from the RT scene, and disagree with the RT sun shadows / the path tracer near the camera;
the traced air uses the same casters (land, objects, alpha-tested cards with their cut-outs, actors with `Actors`, the far LOD with
FarLandLOD / FarObjectLOD; decals never block). The fog reads the grid (FogVolume), so its sun shading follows too.

**Pieces.** `core/RtFogVisibility.{h,cpp}` (an RT core user, Order 2, registered last), `core/RtFogGrid.h` (the pass constants and the
history rules, plain C++: the RT test host fills the block with the same code), `Compute/Rt/RtFogVisibility.comp` (runs in the helper).
Each frame at the RT point (before the shadow passes) the pass writes one R16F texel per cell in the grid's own layout (8 x 8 tiles of
the 64 slices) into the helper's ping-pong pair; the core copies it into `TESR_RtFogVisibility` (this effect's
`Textures.RtVisibilityTexture`, made in RegisterTextures with the grid). `RenderBuffers` sets `TESR_VolumetricLightRt` from
`RtFogVisibility::Constant`: x 0 = not traced this frame (MarchShadow alone, exactly as before), 1 = traced on this frame's grid,
2 = traced with another MaxDistance (looked up by position); y the RT range, z FadeLength, w the traced MaxDistance.
(2026-11-07 ptaaa-hangfix) A sun ray that is not finite is not traced (the cell counts it lit), and a ray stops after 256 candidates
(cut-outs, decals): guards against GPU hangs, no change with sane data (RT-PATH-TRACING.md section 66).

**Use.** GridLight (Froxels): the cell's own texel replaces its two cascade reads inside the range; over the last FadeLength of the range
(distance from the eye) the cascades blend back in, past it the cascades alone. March (Froxels off, or a RenderScale rect frame):
`SunVisibility(position)` projects each step into the grid (bilinear between columns, linear between slice centres). The Vulkan passes
(`VolumetricLightGridLight.frag`, `VolumetricLightMarch.frag`, `VolumetricLight.glsl` VL_RT) bind the texture at binding 2 and take the
constant at the end of the uniform block.

**The trace.** RaysPerCell (default 2) rays toward the sun per cell (a rank-1 lattice across the column, stratified in depth; one ray =
the cell's centre), each from a point in the air (a point inside a solid meets the back face: shadowed, as the cascades). Every frame the
points move (a per-cell, per-frame shift) and last frame's value is blended in, reprojected through last frame's camera at the cell's
centre (trilinear in the old grid): an even average over the frames since a restart, held at Temporal (0.9) for a still cell and at
TemporalMoving (0.7) for one that moved a whole cell (the resampling blurs; a moving edge lags). The history restarts on a camera jump
over 500 units, a sun turn over ~2.5 degrees, a frame not traced, or a grid change. Range (8000, capped by SceneRadius) bounds the traced
cells; RayLength 20000. (2026-11-07 perf-history) Temporal and TemporalMoving are the weights AT 60 FPS: with `[Main.Main.RayTracing]
DenoiseTimeBased` (default on) the grid uses k^(60 dt), the same fade a second (0.9 -> 0.73, 0.7 -> 0.34 at 20 fps).

**When.** On by default but it only rides along: `Wants` = Enabled, the core Running, outdoors, not underwater, this effect on, and
`RtCore::OthersWant` (another RT effect traces this frame). Without the fork, a ray tracing GPU, the helper or another RT effect: nothing
changes. If the frame's RT record has no pass left (NVR_RT_MAX_PASSES 8) the frame uses the cascades (logged every 600). DebugView 1:
traced, not used (an A/B at the same cost). The REPORT block has its line ("RT fog visibility on ...").

**Cost.** One pass in the helper, columns x rows x 64 x RaysPerCell short rays (TerminateOnFirstHit): 1.8 M at 1440p with 2 rays, plus 8
history reads per cell; two R16F shared images (1280 x 768 at 1440p) and one copy out. Not measured on an RTX GPU yet. On the host's iGPU
the 32 x 32 x 64 grid (131 k rays) is under the noise of the run.

**Proof (RT test host, scene `fogvis`, no game).** A closed room with a window toward the sun, an alpha-tested checker card outside, a
32 x 32 x 64 grid: one fixed ray per cell, 65516 of 65536 cells checked against the CPU's ray (20 on an edge skipped), 0 mismatches
(63 shadowed by the card, 60 lit through its cut-outs, 150 lit in the window's shaft); four fixed rays, 0 mismatches in 65318. Two
jittered rays + the history, still camera: RMS against each cell's sunlit share (4 x 4 x 4 points) 0.0128 vs one frame's 0.0535 (partly
lit cells 0.058 vs 0.248), bias -0.0001. Then 16 frames walking 8 units and turning 0.5 degree a frame: 0.0294 vs one frame there 0.0545;
the control without the reprojection 0.0615. (Temporal 0.95 / no moving cap: 0.0379 moving; 0.75: still 0.0189.)

**Not done / unproven.** Not run in game (the bench iGPU; a tester checks): the look of the shafts and fog, the cost on RTX, the copy's
timing under real frames. Lamp light in the fog (VolumetricFog LampHaze) still has no shadows (a traced visibility for the few brightest
lamps would be the next step). The fog's surface lookup past MaxDistance (FogSunVisibility) and ClassicLook stay the cascades.

### "rt vol light = no worky" (2026-11-13, a tester on an RTX card)

Full note with the measurements, the reference photographs and the targets: `docs/next/RT-LIGHT-SHAFTS-2026-11-13.md` (not in this repo). In short:

- The RT version runs and reaches the picture (also under path tracing: it has no stand-down, the path tracer is its host), but it only
  swaps the shadow lookup of the lit air. At the default look the picture changes within its frame-to-frame noise (bench, og-goodsprings:
  mean |difference| 0.7 of 255 against 0.6 between two equal shots). With a strong look the traced air is the darker one near the ground
  and buildings (the soft shadow maps leak light).
- It does nothing, by design, indoors, without another ray-traced effect, and while no shafts are drawn (the sun's shadows faded).
  Nothing said so. Now the Ray tracing page's Status block has a "Light shafts" line (`RtFogVisibility::MenuState`), the session log an
  `RT LIGHT SHAFTS:` line when the reason changes, and the tips say "outdoors only, adds no light of its own".
- `[Main.Main.RayTracing] RidersWithSameFrame` (on, bug fix): with the RT lamp shadows (SameFrame) as the only other RT effect neither this
  rider nor the fog's lamp rider (LampHazeShadows) ever traced: `RtCore::OthersWant` was false after the lamps' early trace.
- How visible the lit air and its shafts are is `[Shaders.VolumetricLight.Main]` Strength, Density, Darken, Anisotropy: the RT section has
  no look of its own.

### The expanded look (2026-11-13, branch claude/rtvol2-1113, `[Shaders.VolumetricLight.RT] Expanded`, off by default)

**Why.** The traced version only swapped the shadow lookup, so it changed the picture by the noise (section above). What a current
game's traced / froxel volumetric light has and this did not: a lit path long enough for far casters to shade the air, dust that sits
near the ground, a forward glow that gathers round the sun, cloud shadows in the air.

**What it does** while the last trace (this frame or the one before) was made with it (`RtFogVisibility::ExpandedLook`):
- **Range.** The grid reaches `ExpandedDistance` (8000) instead of MaxDistance; the trace covers every cell (range 1.45 x the distance, the
  grid's corners; not capped by SceneRadius: past it only what the scene holds blocks the sun, the rest counts lit as the shadow maps'
  "past the shadow distance" did), and no fade back to the shadow maps inside the grid. `GridDistance()` returns it, so the trace and the
  grid agree (mode 1).
- **Density** `ExpandedDensity` (0.015 per 1000 units at the eye's height; the Night section's Density scales it as it scales the day's).
- **Height** `ExpandedHeight` (1500): density falls as exp(-h / H) above the eye (full at and below it). The optical depth along a view is
  closed form, `AirDepth` (sigma (1 - exp(-a t)) / a, a = the view's rise per planar unit / H). GridLight (D3D9 + Vulkan) and the grid
  composite's all-air share (D3D9 GridAirAt, Vulkan VolumetricLightCompositeGrid) both use it, so lit + shaded air stay consistent.
  `GridData.z` = 1 / H; 0 = exactly the old sigma * t. The march (Froxels off, and every FSR / DLSS RenderScale frame: the default FSR 0.67 runs the march, not the grid) weighs each sample by the same density and AirDepth.
- **Sun glow** `ExpandedSunGlow` (0.3) / `ExpandedGlowSharpness` (0.85): a second Henyey-Greenstein lobe, mixed in by that share
  (`AirPhase`; capped at 8 / (1 - g)). `GridData.w` = g x 100 + the share; 0 = the old phase exactly.
- **Clouds** `ExpandedClouds` (on): the volumetric clouds' shadow map goes to the helper (its own image, registered at the helper's start
  when the map exists then) and RtFogVisibility.comp multiplies each sample's visibility by the clouds' transmittance (as RtSunShadow.comp
  CloudShadows). Constants appended to the pass block (RtFogGrid.h; cloud.x 0 = binding 5 not bound, as before).
- **Who composites.** The fog's .fx is at ps_3_0's 224 constant registers (preshaders take a register per uniform-only expression; even
  reading GridData.z broke it), so in expanded frames the fog leaves its FogGridLight out (`NVR_FogGridFlags.y` / `push.gridFlags.y` 0,
  `gridApplied` false: `VolumetricLightEffect::expandedComposite`) and this effect's own grid composite draws the light. The fog's own sun
  shading from the grid (flags x, and FogVolume's g / a channels) is unchanged.

Off (Expanded false, DebugView 1, or no trace): GridData z / w are 0 and every formula is the old one; the fog composites as before.
Not covered: the game's own cloud layers' shadows (`[Shaders.CloudShadows.Main]`, drawn by SunFar from the sky's textures: not in the
helper); DustMotes still reads the grid with even air (its motes above the eye are a little dimmer with the height falloff on).

### Idle targets (2026-11-12)
With `[Main.Main.Performance] TargetReleaseSeconds` above 0 (default 20) the texture manager keeps 1 x 1 stand-ins in place of this effect's render targets while it is off (not made at start-up when it starts off; released that many seconds after it is switched off; made again, zero filled, before the frame draws when it is switched on). Which targets and why that is safe here: the march / blur / history / grid buffers and the RT fog visibility copy are written only behind the switch (EffectChain's wouldRender, RtFogVisibility::Wants); DustMotes reads the grid only when it was built this frame. A new reader or writer of these targets outside the effect's own switch, or a copy of their pointers kept past a frame, breaks this: then answer false in `TargetParkable` (the header). See `core/TextureManager.h`.

## WaterLens

*README title: WaterLens effect spec*

Status 2026-11-02 (branch claude/rw3-lens): the D3D9 effect `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/WaterLens.fx.hlsl` and the Vulkan pass `Compute/WaterLens.frag` were written fresh from this spec. The text below describes the new shader. Fixed from the old one: the sixth power is now an even power (squares, no NaN for a negative base); the normalisation of a zero vector is guarded (open question 9 fixed). The D3D9 pass computes the noise coordinates per pixel (no vertex-stage half-texel shift), so the D3D9 and Vulkan maths now match (open question 6 resolved that way). Settings and the fade are unchanged.

Source of truth for this spec: the NVR tree at `the source tree` (read only). Settings are from `resource/VegasShaders.defaults.toml`. The D3D9 shader is `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Effects/WaterLens.fx.hlsl`. The Vulkan pass is `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/WaterLens.frag` (compiled to `WaterLens.spv`, same folder). The frame order is in `src/core/ShaderManager.cpp`.

### 1. Purpose

When the player leaves water, the screen briefly looks as if seen through the surface of a lens: the whole picture wobbles and is refracted by a drifting ripple pattern, and thin bright bluish glints appear where the ripples bend light most. The ripple is strongest at the moment the player surfaces and fades away over a short span of game time. The effect is off at all other times, including while the player is underwater.

### 2. When it runs

- **Enabled:** the effect's on/off switch is `[Shaders.WaterLens.Status]` `Enabled` (default on). It is also loaded only when its shader source is present and compiled. The effect is drawn only when this switch is on.
- **Fade state (the real gate):** the effect draws only while its strength value is above zero. That value is the setting `Amount` multiplied by a fade value from 0 to 1 (see Behaviour details, fade). Outside a fade the value is zero and the pass is skipped entirely.
- **Underwater:** while the player is underwater the fade value is forced to zero at once, so nothing draws. Leaving water starts a fade from full strength.
- **Location, weather, time of day and menus:** there are no extra rules. The effect is not gated by interior or exterior, night, weather or the menu. Any fade in progress plays in all of these.
- **Renderer:** the D3D9 path always draws. The Vulkan path draws instead when the key `NvrDxvkPasses` in `[_Main.Main.Performance]` (default on) is on, the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is present, and no render rect is active (render-scale frames use the D3D9 path). If the Vulkan pass fails, the effect falls back to its D3D9 technique for the rest of the session.
- **Frame order:** the effect is in the lens group of the post-process chain. Order of the relevant steps, earlier first: TAA (if on), rain, snow, legacy bloom, heat haze, volumetric smoke, barrel haze, coloring, LUT (if after tonemapping), depth of field, motion blur, then **WaterLens**, then the low-high-frequency effect (LowHF), dither buster, SMAA, FXAA, sharpening, cinema, image adjust, body camera, sky dither, debug, cover map. The effect reads the image as it stands at its position in the chain. It can be the last effect in the chain, in which case it writes straight to the game's target.

### 3. Inputs

#### Textures and samplers

Slot numbers are the D3D9 sampler registers. Vulkan binding numbers are given in section 7.

| Order | D3D9 name | Slot | Content | Filtering and addressing |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `TESR_RenderedBuffer` | s0 | The frame as it stands at this point in the chain (the current image). Full frame, not only the render rect. | Linear min, mag and mip. Clamp on U and V. |
| 1 | `TESR_waterlensSampler` | s1 | The file texture `Effects\water_NRM_lens.dds` (on disk: `resource/Textures/Effects/water_NRM_lens.dds`). A tileable normal-style noise map. Each texel has four channels, each stored 0..1 and read back as -1..1. The alpha channel is used as a second height value. | Linear min, mag and mip. Wrap on U and V. |

The C++ binds samplers by declaration order: index 0 is the rendered frame, index 1 is the noise map. The noise map is loaded as a file texture; the effect does not create it.

The effect does not declare a source-buffer sampler, so the game's source frame is not copied for it.

#### Constants

| Name | Register | Components and meaning | Units |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TESR_GameTime` | not fixed (the D3D9 compiler assigns it; the C++ binds by name) | x: game clock in milliseconds. y: game hour of day. z: real time in seconds since the frame-rate timer started (used by this effect). w: last frame time in seconds. | ms, hours, s, s |
| `TESR_ReciprocalResolution` | not used by the new shader (not declared) | was: 1 / width and height, for the old D3D9 vertex-stage half-texel shift. | 1/pixel |
| `TESR_WaterLensData` | not fixed, bound by name | x: time multiplier A (`TimeMultA`). y: time multiplier B (`TimeMultB`). z: viscosity (`Viscosity`). w: effective strength = `Amount` times the current fade value (0 to 1). | multipliers, multiplier, multiplier, strength |

For the D3D9 path, the constant `TESR_WaterLensData` is updated each frame: its x, y and z come from settings and its w from `Amount` times the fade. Nothing else in the effect changes between frames apart from `TESR_GameTime`.

### 4. Settings

All keys are read from the section `Shaders.WaterLens.Main` (written with a leading underscore in the defaults file, as the settings loader expects for all sections). Keys are read by the effect every frame when it is enabled.

| Section | Key | Type | Range | Default | Effect on the picture |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Shaders.WaterLens.Main` | `Amount` | float | not declared in the defaults file or menu (see section 9) | 0.1 | Overall strength. Scales the whole refraction and the highlight. Zero turns the effect off. Also the top value the fade starts from. |
| `Shaders.WaterLens.Main` | `TimeMultA` | float | not declared | 0.1 | Speed of the first ripple pattern's drift and wobble. Higher means faster ripple motion. |
| `Shaders.WaterLens.Main` | `TimeMultB` | float | not declared | 0.2 | Vertical scroll speed of the second ripple pattern. Higher means the lower-frequency detail travels faster down the screen. |
| `Shaders.WaterLens.Main` | `Viscosity` | float | not declared | 0.05 | How strongly the ripple normal tilts in the screen plane before it is normalised. Higher means stronger bending of the picture and stronger glints (the name suggests the opposite; see section 9). |
| `Shaders.WaterLens.Main` | `Time` | float | not declared | 180.0 | Present in the defaults file but **not read by the effect**. Has no visible effect. |
| `Shaders.WaterLens.Status` | `Enabled` | bool | on / off | true | Turns the whole effect on or off from the menu. Read as the effect's enabled state. |

No hand-written menu entry for WaterLens was found in the ImGui code; its settings are listed from the defaults file, without ranges.

### 5. Passes and outputs

**D3D9 path.** One technique, index 0, with one pass. The C++ asks for technique index 0 only; any other index is refused. The pass draws a full-frame quad over the target rectangle (the render rect's top-left part when render-scale frames are on). The pixel output is written to the frame chain's current output target, which is the same format and size as the chain's frame (HDR or LDR as the chain is). The output alpha is 1. The shader does not set blending; the target receives the opaque colour.

**Vulkan path.** One graphics pass named `WaterLens`, writing one colour target: the chain's output target, or the game's final target when the effect is the last in the chain. Same format and size as the chain. No depth, no stencil, no blending.

Neither path needs extra render targets or a second frame copy.

### 6. Behaviour details

**The effect reads only the frame.** No depth, no sky test, no first-person weapon test, no HUD test. Everything in the frame at this point is refracted, including the sky. A new version should not try to mask the weapon or sky unless asked.

**Two noise samples, one picture sample, one highlight term.** For each pixel:
1. Sample the noise map twice. The first sample uses the frame coordinate halved and offset by a slow sine wobble in x and a drift in y, both driven by real time times `TimeMultA` (the D3D9 path computes these coordinates in the vertex stage and interpolates them; the Vulkan path computes them per pixel). The second sample is the frame coordinate offset straight down by real time times `TimeMultB`.
2. Map each sample from 0..1 to -1..1 on all four channels. Add the alpha channel of the second sample to the blue channel of the first sample.
3. Form a normal from the first sample's x, y and z, scale its x and y by `Viscosity` (z is left as is), and normalise to unit length. This gives a refraction direction.
4. Sample the frame at the pixel's own coordinate, moved by the refraction direction's x and y multiplied by 0.2 and by the effective strength. Clamp addressing on the frame.
5. Compute the highlight: take the refraction direction's x and y, multiply each by the effective strength, raise each to the sixth power, clamp each to 0..1, add them, and multiply by a bluish tint (0.85, 0.85, 1.0). This is added to the picture with no clamp.
6. Output the displaced frame plus the highlight, alpha 1.

**Even power for the highlight.** Step 5 raises a possibly negative value to the sixth power. The sixth power of a negative number is positive, and the intended result is the even power. A GPU `pow` of a negative base is undefined and can give NaN. A new implementation must produce the even power of the signed value (for example by squaring three times), not a generic power of a possibly negative base.

**Normalisation guard.** Step 3 normalises a vector that can, in principle, be zero. A zero vector must not produce NaN or inf. Use the un-normalised vector or a small epsilon in that case, and make sure the pixel is still a valid colour.

**Edge handling.** The noise map wraps, so tiling is seamless. The frame clamps, so refraction at the screen edge repeats the edge pixels rather than sampling outside.

**Half-texel convention.** The new D3D9 pass computes everything per pixel in the pixel shader, with no half-texel shift, the same as the Vulkan pass (sub-pixel; open question 6 accepted as this).

**Real time vs. game time.** The ripple motion uses `TESR_GameTime` z, which is real seconds, so it keeps moving during fades and while the game is paused. The fade itself uses game time (below).

**Fade.** The fade value is a linear ramp in game hours (game days times 24):
- When the player goes underwater (the game's underwater flag, read from the sky): the fade is set to zero at once. No fade-out.
- When the player leaves the water: the fade starts at 1 and falls linearly to 0 over 0.01 game hours (36 game seconds). The ramp is the strength `Amount` times the fade.
- If the player goes back underwater during the fade, the fade drops to zero at once.
- The fade only updates while the effect is enabled. If the effect is switched off while underwater, its fade state is not updated until it is switched on again.
- Game start is not underwater and the fade is zero, so nothing draws at load.

**HDR vs. LDR.** The effect does no tonemapping and no clamping of its output. On an HDR chain the highlight can push values above 1, which is intended. Do not clamp the output to 1.

**Render rect and render scale.** When a render rect is active (render-scale frames), the D3D9 draw is restricted to the render rect's viewport, and the Vulkan path is not used at all. The shader does not use `TESR_RenderScale` itself; its coordinates are over the whole target as drawn (see section 9).

**Reversed depth, jitter, TAA.** None used. The effect does not read depth and does not apply jitter.

**What looks wrong if missed:**
- Refraction that never fades, or ripples while swimming.
- A hard cut-off at the end of the fade instead of a ramp.
- Sample jumps or seams at the noise map's edges (the wrap addressing must be on).
- Black or speckled pixels, which come from NaN in the sixth power or the normalisation.
- Glints across the whole screen; they should appear only where the ripple bends light strongly.
- Highlight clipped to white; the tint and additive sum must stay.

### 7. Vulkan path

**Files.** `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/WaterLens.frag` (the source) and `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Compute/WaterLens.spv` (the compiled pass, loaded from the Vegas Shaders DXVK shader folder at run time as `Shaders\VegasShaders\Compute\WaterLens.spv`). The source includes `Common.glsl` for the shared sampler table. There is no compile-time variant (it is not in `variants.txt`) and no SM6 build; the generic `WaterLens.spv` is the only pass.

**Selection.** The C++ uses the Vulkan path when the Vegas Shaders DXVK extension is present, `NvrDxvkPasses` is on, no render rect is active, the effect is not named in `NvrDxvkKeepD3D9`, and the technique index is 0. Otherwise (any of those false) the D3D9 technique draws. After a failure the effect stays on D3D9 for the session and the log says so. The pass is made from the `WaterLens` shader name, with two texture bindings and two sampler bindings, and a push block of 48 bytes.

**Bindings.**
- Set 1, binding 0: the rendered frame (`TESR_RenderedBuffer` equivalent), sampled.
- Set 1, binding 1: the noise file texture (`TESR_waterlensSampler` equivalent), sampled, fetched through the extension's file-texture path under the name `TESR_waterlensSampler`.
- Set 0, binding 0: the shared sampler table (from `Common.glsl`).
- Sampler selection is by heap index in the push block. The linear/clamp sampler index is at push offset 32; the linear/wrap sampler index is at push offset 36. The C++ sets both fields to zero; the extension writes the real indices at those offsets from the binding descriptors.

**Push block layout (48 bytes, all 4-byte aligned).**
- Offset 0: `gameTime`, a vec4 equal to `TESR_GameTime` (z is the real-time value used).
- Offset 16: `waterLensData`, a vec4 equal to `TESR_WaterLensData` (x, y, z, w as in section 3).
- Offset 32: `samplerIndex`, a uint, the linear/clamp sampler heap index (written by the extension).
- Offset 36: `wrapIndex`, a uint, the linear/wrap sampler heap index (written by the extension).
- Offsets 40 to 47: padding, unused.

**Coordinates.** The pass's `uv` input is the full-screen coordinate at pixel centres, as the extension supplies it. The Vulkan shader derives the two noise coordinates from `uv` itself, so the noise moves as the D3D9 version does.

**Matching a new Vulkan version.** A new version must implement the behaviour in section 6 exactly, keep the same push offsets and binding order above, sample the frame through the clamp sampler and the noise map through the wrap sampler, and compute the sixth power with an even-power formulation (not a generic power of a possibly negative base).

### 8. Look and cost

**Correct result.** While the player is leaving water, the picture is visibly wobbled by a slowly drifting, tileable ripple, with soft refraction and small bright bluish glints where the ripples bend light most. The effect is strongest at the moment of surfacing and fades out over roughly half a minute of game time. With the effect off, or at any other time, the picture is untouched.

**Typical wrong results:**
- Constant distortion or ripples while the player is in normal play.
- Ripples that stay after the fade, or a sudden cut-off instead of a fade.
- Black or speckled pixels (NaN from the sixth power or from normalising zero).
- The whole picture washed out by glints; the glints must be limited to strong bends.
- A visible seam or stretching at the noise map's edges.
- Ripples that do not move, which happens when real time is not used.
- Highlight clipped to white instead of adding a tint.

**Cost class:** cheap. A single full-screen pass with three texture fetches and a handful of arithmetic per pixel. It runs only during the fade, so it costs nothing outside it. Not a per-pixel loop and not multi-pass.

### 9. Open questions

1. **Setting ranges.** The defaults file and the menu give no range for `Amount`, `TimeMultA`, `TimeMultB` or `Viscosity`. Ask the maintainer what a sensible range is before writing a clamp or a menu slider.
2. **Unused key.** `Time` = 180.0 in `[Shaders.WaterLens.Main]` is in the defaults file but the effect does not read it. Confirm whether it can be removed or was meant for the fade length.
3. **Viscosity direction.** `Viscosity` raises the strength of the bend (larger value, larger refraction), which is the opposite of what the name suggests. Confirm this is intended before a new version renames or inverts it.
4. **Fade duration.** 0.01 game hours is about 36 game seconds. The real-time length depends on the game's time scale. Confirm the intended feel.
5. **Fade while paused.** The fade follows game time, so it may stop while the game clock is stopped. Confirm for menus and dialogue.
6. **Half-texel and pixel-centre convention.** The D3D9 path shifts the coordinates by half a texel and the Vulkan path does not. Confirm which one is the reference, or accept the sub-pixel difference.
7. **Render rect.** Whether the frame sample should be limited to the render rect when render-scale frames are on (the D3D9 draw is restricted to its viewport, but the shader samples the whole target). Confirm the intended behaviour.
8. **HUD and weapon.** The effect is drawn in the chain before the final interface and does not mask the first-person weapon. Confirm whether the HUD is in the frame at this point.
9. **NaN handling.** FIXED 2026-11-02 in the new shader: the sixth power is formed as squares (an even power), and the normalisation guards a zero vector. Not yet confirmed on a GPU.
10. **Vulkan uv convention.** The extension's full-screen `uv` is assumed to be at pixel centres, as the Vulkan source comments say; confirm with a capture.

## WetnessEffects

*README title: Wetness Effects (rain wetness)*

### 1. What it does
Ported from Community Shaders' Wetness Effects (through Physically-Based-Reloaded): in the rain surfaces open to the sky darken and gain a
glossy water film with its own highlight and reflection, drops ripple on flat wet ground, nothing gets wet under cover (the top-down ortho
map). Puddles (`src/effects/Puddles`) builds on it for the standing water. Since 2026-11-21 it is THE rain wetness, on by default:
WetWorld, the old screen-space wet pass, is removed (owner's word; `docs/DESIGN.md "Deferred wetness design"` step 4). It is a shader collection, not
a pass: the water layer is `Shaders/Includes/Wetness.hlsl` inside the object, parallax and land pixel shaders (and, with
`[Main.Main.Quality] DeferredWetness`, the deferred wetness pass, `core/DeferredWetness.h`).

This folder also holds HudRaindrops (drops on the first-person arms, weapon and objects) and WetnessReflections (its screen-space
reflections on wet surfaces; off by default since 2026-11-21, the owner's setting).

### 2. The one rain clock (2026-11-21)
`WetnessEffectsShaders::TickWeather` follows the weather as Community Shaders does (the current rainy weather fading in over the
transition, the last one fading out) and eases three amounts at WeatherTransitionSpeed / 60 x 2 a second (TunedClockRate: the speeds were
tuned while it ran twice a frame, and keep that): Wetness (the film; about 10 s at 3), PuddleWetness (three times slower to dry), Raining
(four times faster, about 2.5 s). The drops' time (TESR_WetnessData.w) runs at the same doubled rate, as before. Interiors hold the state they were entered with.
ForceRain counts as full rain, ForceSnow as none. The Debug overrides force the amounts.

It runs every rendered frame whether or not the collection is on (`core/RainState.h` calls it once a frame, by FrameNumber), and the
rest of the mod reads it there: `RainState::Rain()` (Raining: the drops, the water shaders' rain rings through TESR_WetnessData.z,
Reflections' wetness, Specular's rain look, the volumetric light) and `RainState::Factor()` (max(Raining, Wetness): the Rain setting
groups of the land and the objects, skin, the heat haze). So the ground, skin, heat haze and ripples get wet and dry together.
Puddles' water level keeps its own game-hour clock (FillHours / DryHours): the water left after the film has dried.

### 3. Inputs and constants
TESR_WetnessData (x Wetness, y PuddleWetness, z Raining, w time for the drop hashes), TESR_WetnessParams / Params2 (the Main keys),
TESR_WetnessDrops / Ripple (the Raindrops keys), TESR_WetnessCamera, TESR_WetnessFlags (x on, z ripples, w ortho texel size),
TESR_WetnessSun (w: the ShowCover view), TESR_WetnessHeight. The ortho map (rain cover) is asked for while anything is wet.

### 4. Settings
`[Shaders.WetnessEffects.Status] Enabled` (restart; default true since 2026-11-21, settings step 91). `.Main`, `.Raindrops`, `.Debug`,
`.HudRaindrops`, `.Reflections`: see `defaults.toml` (the shipped values are the owner's: MaxRainWetness 0.5, RaindropChance 0.5,
Reflections off with EdgeFade 0.279, RoughnessCutoff 0.434, Thickness 50).

### 5. Edge cases
- ShaderFallback (native Direct3D 9, land shaders over the 4096-slot limit): Wetness Effects is left out of every family and switched off
  for the session. Since WetWorld's removal nothing draws wet ground in its place; a [WARNING] in the log says to use DXVK. The clock still
  runs, so drops, water rings and the Rain groups still follow the rain.
- The collection off (player's choice): no wet surfaces; the clock still runs for the rest.

### 6. Vulkan path
None of its own: the film is in the D3D9 surface shaders; the Vulkan reflection passes read Puddles' and the deferred film's data.

### 7. What correct and wrong look like
Correct: when rain starts, the ground, roads, rocks, skin and gear darken and gloss together, ripples start on puddles and water, the heat
haze goes; when it stops, all of them dry within about 10 s except the puddles, which shrink over game hours. Wrong: skin or the land's
Rain look staying wet while the ground is dry (two clocks), or wet ground under roofs.

## WorldSurface

Code: `src/hlsl/NewVegas/Shaders/WorldSurface.hlsl`, defaults in `resource/defaults/WorldSurface.toml` (no folder in `src/effects`; new in
upstream 1.1.5, taken whole in the 2026-11-27 merge). Not run in game on this fork; frame cost not measured.

### 1. Purpose
A deferred pass that overlays world-space micro-detail on static world objects and terrain: close-range normal relief and roughness variation,
settled Mojave dust in crevices (aged dust indoors), rust and tarnish on metal, waterline darkening, mud and algae above the water level, and
patchy moss. It skips characters, clothing, plants, trees and grass. It reads the pre-pass scene, the albedo and material G-buffer targets and
the smooth normals, so it needs the material target (GBufferV2).

### 2. On by default, but only with GBufferV2
`[Shaders.WorldSurface.Status] Enabled` is true (live, needs `DeferredRenderer` at startup). GBufferV2 is off by default here and in the owner's
file, so by default the effect does nothing. With GBufferV2 on, it changes the picture (a visible upstream default).

### 3. Settings (`resource/defaults/WorldSurface.toml`)
| Key | Default | Range |
|---|---|---|
| `[Main] Strength` | 1.0 | 0-2, overall multiplier of every layer |
| `[Main] Detail` | 0.75 | 0-2, micro-detail relief and roughness |
| `[Main] Distance` | 2400 | 400-6000 game units, where detail and weathering fade out |
| `[Main] DebugView` | 0 | 0-1, test only: 1 paints the layer masks (red rust, green moss, blue waterline, yellow dust, grey detail) |
| `[Dust] Enabled` / `Strength` | true / 0.55 | 0-2 |
| `[Rust] Enabled` / `Strength` | true / 0.65 | 0-2 |
| `[Waterline] Enabled` / `Strength` | true / 0.75 | 0-2 |
| `[Moss] Enabled` / `Strength` | true / 0.30 | 0-2 |

All live. The sections are written `_Shaders.WorldSurface.*` in the fragment.

### 4. Registers and samplers (pixel shader)
c0-c5 `WS_Cam` (camera axes, tan half-fov, viewport, position, jitter), c6 `WS_World` (camera position, w exterior), c7 `WS_Weights` (detail,
dust, rust, moss, already times strength), c8 `WS_Water` (water height minus camera z, waterline, rain wetness, DebugView), c9 `WS_Fade`, c10
`WS_Gamma` (gamma, 1/gamma, linear flag, depth mode), c11 `WS_Ambient`, c12-c20 `WS_SkySH`, **c21 `WS_Flags`** (x = 1: deferred wetness keeps
the land's height in the material fraction, so the land is left out). Samplers: s0 scene, s1 albedo, s2 material, s3 normals, s4 depth, s5
the 64^3 `NoiseVolume.dds`.

### 5. The wet land (2026-11-27, e9780e5)
With `DEFERRED_WETNESS` compiled in, the land records store their height for the water layer in the material target's fraction. The pass read
that as metal and wrote it back capped before DeferredSun read it (lead-architect review of the merge). `WS_Flags.x` now makes the pass `clip`
the land. Believed from the code and the commit; not run in game (GBufferV2 is off).

### 6. Checking it
`DebugView` 1 with GBufferV2 on: the masks should sit on rusty metal, shaded rock, shore lines and upward ledges, nowhere on actors or
plants. Wrong: rust on skin or clothing, wet ground losing its sheen or turning metal-bright (the land clip failed).
